# The Curator — Changelog Archive

> **This file is part of this project's memory, not a historical curiosity.**
> Every row here was moved out of `CLAUDE.md` **byte-for-byte** — nothing has
> been reworded, shortened or summarised. `CLAUDE.md` is auto-loaded into every
> session in this repo, and at ~183,000 tokens the changelog alone was spending
> roughly a third of the context window before anyone typed a word. The newest
> 6 releases stay in `CLAUDE.md`; these 150 live here.

**When to open this file.** Before you change anything with a history — a guard,
a prompt, a fallback chain, a money path, a write path — search this file for
the version that last touched it and read the whole row. These rows record the
measurements, the traps, and the "KNOWN AND UNFIXED" items that explain why the
code is shaped the way it is. **A row that reads like noise is very often the
only surviving record of why something is the way it is**, and the one-line
index in `CLAUDE.md` is a pointer to a row, never a substitute for it.

**How to search it.** `grep -n '| \`v3.17.0\`' CHANGELOG-ARCHIVE.md` gets you a
release by version. `grep -in 'KNOWN AND UNFIXED' CHANGELOG-ARCHIVE.md` gets
you every recorded open limit. Each row is a single (very long) line, so `grep`
returns the whole entry.

**⚠️ Why this file is at the repo root, and why it must stay here.** `docs/` is the
obvious home and it is the wrong one. These rows are preserved **byte-for-byte**,
and they contain **root-relative markdown links** — `[src/brain/files.js](src/brain/files.js)`,
`[scripts/test-css-tokens.js](scripts/test-css-tokens.js)` and ~170 more. Those
resolved correctly while the rows lived in `CLAUDE.md` at the repo root. Moving
this file into `docs/` re-bases every one of them against `docs/`, which breaks
**all ~176** of them at once — and the only way to fix them there would be to
edit the rows, which is exactly the byte-for-byte guarantee this archive exists
to keep. Measured, not assumed: a `docs/changelog-archive.md` placement produced
176 broken links against a baseline of 1 (the pre-existing, deliberately
gitignored `SHARED-BRAIN-UPGRADE.md` reference in `CLAUDE.md`). It is indexed
from [docs/README.md](docs/README.md) so it cannot rot unlinked.

**Order.** This table preserves `CLAUDE.md`'s original row order exactly, and that
order is **not** strictly chronological. It runs newest-first from the most recent
archived release down to `v3.0.7`, then a block of early commit SHAs and `v2.1.0`–`v2.4.1`
in *ascending* order, then a second descending block from `v3.0.6` down to `v2.4.2`.
Do not infer a date from a row's position; read the version.

---

## Archived releases

| Commit | What it fixed |
|---|---|
| `v3.38.0` | **The widget stopped being a list and became a widget — and the release found that the previous one had been judged in the worst configuration it has, for reasons neither the code nor the suites could see.** All of it came from the maintainer opening v3.37.0 and reporting three things: *"the pulse is barely visible"*, *"the widget is the same width as before"*, and *"it does not look like a widget yet."* **(1) THE WIDTH HAD NOT MOVED, AND THE CAUSE WAS THAT EVERY SUITE TESTED FROM THE WRITER'S SIDE.** v3.37.0 drops a row's machine name when the row is from THIS machine. On his setup that was false on EVERY row — because **the installed `.app` and a repo checkout are two different INSTALLATIONS on one computer**: the app runs under one installation id and his agents write state under another. So the app classified all of its own data as foreign, printed a machine name on every line, and the widest label was **74 characters rather than the 54 the suites had measured**. Reproduced exactly by pointing `CURATOR_TEST_USER_DATA_DIR` at the app's own Application Support directory — the READER'S view. Every existing tray fixture took the WRITER'S view, which is the single configuration in which this is invisible, and there is now a required reader's-view fixture because it is his actual configuration. **THE FIX IS A DIFFERENT QUESTION, NOT A PATCH.** Instead of asking *is this row from this machine* — which the app answers wrongly and will keep answering wrongly — it asks **does this component VARY across the visible rows**, and drops anything constant. That principle already governed the project token and the harness; extending it to the machine removes the suffix entirely on one computer and restores it the instant a genuine second machine appears. **And the review caught the trap inside the fix**: comparing raw FOLDER STRINGS would have seen a hostname-flapped laptop's two folders as two machines and reasserted the phantom computer v3.37.0 had just removed, so the comparison is on machine IDENTITY, keyed on the trailing installation id. **(2) THE PULSE WAS A GHOST BECAUSE A TRUE CONSTRAINT WAS APPLIED WHERE IT DOES NOT HOLD.** The strip shipped as an alpha-only TEMPLATE image on a DISABLED row, so macOS tinted it to the disabled text colour — the compounding-opacity defect this repo has already fixed twice, applied to the one piece of graphics the widget exists for. **Template images are mandatory for the TRAY GLYPH and irrelevant for MENU ITEM ICONS**, and that was settled by BUILDING A REAL ELECTRON MENU on the maintainer's machine rather than by re-reading documentation: a full-colour RGBA icon renders in colour, and **`type: 'header'` renders as a real section header**. Both had been assumed unavailable. §1.4 of the design roadmap says a menu can draw *"a template image per item"*; that was an understatement and it is recorded as one without editing the argument. **(3) THE PALETTE IS A MEASUREMENT, AND THE FIRST ONE FAILED.** Every shipped colour is asserted at **≥3:1 against its own theme's background** — the WCAG NON-TEXT floor, which is the right floor for an indicator — computed IN THE SUITE from the values actually shipped rather than eyeballed. Apple's own `systemGreen` is **ruled out at ~1.8:1 on light** and kept as the anti-vacuity control, because a check that cannot fail is not a check. The first attempt failed too, and the suite is what caught it: light rung 1 sat at **3.15 against EMPTY's 3.29**, so **one save read QUIETER THAN NONE**. **(4) COLOUR IS AN ACCELERATOR AND NEVER THE SIGNAL.** A separate section throws the palette away entirely and requires all three strip states and all five dot silhouettes to stay distinguishable **in the alpha channel alone**, with the strip's silhouette asserted byte-identical between light and dark. The dots carry a strictly decreasing ink ladder — filled disc, ring, smaller ring, smaller ring, tiny filled dot — so the five states survive a viewer who cannot resolve the colours at all. **(5) FIVE RECENCY STATES ONTO THREE COLOURS, ARGUED RATHER THAN PICKED.** Green covers `live` AND `warm`, not `live` alone, because the live window is **120 seconds** and a hand-opened menu essentially never lands inside it — green reserved for `live` would spend the strongest colour in the palette on the one state nobody ever sees. Grey covers `cool` and `cold` because three days and three weeks prompt the same action. The collapse costs nothing, because `formatAge` writes the exact figure on the same row: **the dot is the band, the words are the number.** `unknown` is a WRITTEN CASE that draws NO DOT — a grey dot would assert *old* about a row whose own label says the time is unknown. **(6) THE STRIP GOT SMALLER AND MORE PRESENT, WHICH WAS THE ONLY HONEST ANSWER.** *"Make it bigger"* and *"make the menu narrower"* are in direct tension, and the strip is a FIXED-WIDTH image that a per-row character budget cannot shrink. **83x11 → 55x14**: 34% narrower and **twice the ink per active cell**, by folding two of the producer's six-hour buckets into one drawn twelve-hour cell. The producer's seven-day window and six-hour buckets are UNTOUCHED — only the drawn resolution folds, and the tooltip says *saves per 12 hours* rather than quoting a number it no longer draws. **Seven daily cells were refused on the data**: per-day counts run 5–40, which pins the intensity ramp at saturation permanently and destroys the only thing the shading carries. **(7) THE WIDTH IS NOW A BUDGET RATHER THAN A HOPE.** Derived by measuring his own reference apps — iStat Menus ~270pt, Little Snitch ~240pt — against v3.37.0's own **517pt** — and that figure is a MEASUREMENT rather than the ~425 first estimated, because the documentation pass extracted the v3.37.0 modules and ran them over the same store from the same reader's view instead of accepting the number it was handed. Every label is truncated to a computed character budget with the full text in the tooltip, asserted per row against the raw input. Measured from the reader's view on his real store: **widest line 74 → 41 characters, widest item ~259pt against a 260pt target, mean 44.0 → 26.3.** `MENU_CHAR_POINTS` is an ASSUMED glyph advance with no font ever measured, so the suite reports the budget's sensitivity across 5–7 pt/char rather than pinning one number — the RENDERED width holds at ~260pt across that whole range and what moves is how much text survives. **(8) A DEFECT ONLY MEASUREMENT COULD FIND, AND THE AGENT THAT FOUND IT WAS RIGHT NOT TO FIX IT.** `boot()` pins `nativeTheme.themeSource = 'dark'` so the window's title bar matches the app's own theme — and that setter is exactly what `shouldUseDarkColors` reports, so the tray would have painted the **DARK palette onto a LIGHT menu bar on every Mac**. The owning agent recorded it as found-not-fixed and declined to route around it, because it could not answer the question offline. Answered by running Electron 43.5.0 on a light-appearance Mac: `shouldUseDarkColors` false → **TRUE** after the override, `getEffectiveAppearance()` light → **dark** (it follows the override too), and `getUserDefault('AppleInterfaceStyle')` **light in both** — the only one immune. The menu is drawn by AppKit against the SYSTEM appearance, which is a different question from what this app themes its own window as, and the two are allowed to disagree. The rebuild is driven by `AppleInterfaceThemeChangedNotification`, because `updated` does not fire while `themeSource` is pinned. **(9) A TAG IS NOT A RELEASE, AND THAT COST THE MAINTAINER A MORNING.** `release.js` tags, gates on CI and pushes main — and does **not** publish a GitHub Release. The DMG workflow uploads both installers as a **14-day workflow artifact**, and publishing them, RENAMED, had always been a manual step nobody wrote down. So v3.37.0 existed as a tag with no installer, and **every installed copy confidently reported "You're up to date" while running the previous version** — a wrong reassurance, which this project treats as worse than an error. He found it from the menu bar and reasonably concluded the updater was broken; it was telling the truth. The workflow now publishes the release itself, and **the rename is not cosmetic**: `archFromAssetName` needs a whole hyphen-delimited `arm64`/`x64` token and electron-builder's x64 output is `The Curator-<v>.dmg` with **no arch in it at all**, resolving to `null`, so publishing unrenamed would leave every Intel Mac unable to find a build, silently. The renamer **IMPORTS that matcher** rather than copying it, refuses any plan whose output names do not resolve, re-reads the directory afterwards, and the workflow then MOUNTS each image and checks `lipo -archs` against the name it is about to publish under — because an architecture in a filename is a claim, not a fact. `release.js`'s own comment justified deferring this *"before anything consumes it"*; that expired in v3.31.0 when the updater shipped, and is corrected in place. **(10) TWO AGENTS BUILT ONE FEATURE AND LEFT A THIRD FILE RED, WHICH IS THE EXPECTED OUTCOME AND WAS HANDLED AS ONE.** `test-tray-pulse-strip.js` was owned by neither and carried **12 assertions that were all correct before and all wrong after** — the pulse being disabled, the cap of eight rows, a hardcoded `setTemplateImage(true)`. Both agents refused to weaken their own work to keep a file they did not own green, and reported it instead; the orchestrator reconciled at merge. The reversed assertions now record WHY they reversed, so the next reader sees the argument rather than a bare expectation. **KNOWN AND UNFIXED.** **THE REDESIGNED MENU HAS NOT BEEN PHOTOGRAPHED.** The mockup that settled the colour and header questions WAS built and screenshotted on a real Mac, and the final build was driven through a real `Tray` and `Menu.buildFromTemplate` successfully — but `screencapture` returned a black frame both times it was tried on the finished article, because the display was asleep or its recording permission had lapsed, and macOS reports no error for either. So the shipped arrangement is proven to BUILD and its art is proven pixel by pixel; nobody has seen the two together. **`type: 'header'` ON macOS BELOW 14 IS UNVERIFIED.** Electron's type validator is JavaScript and OS-independent, so `buildFromTemplate` cannot throw there — the risk is drawing, not crashing — and every header carries `enabled: false` and no handler, so the worst case is a dimmed inert caption, which is what a section heading looks like. **THE PUBLISHING WORKFLOW HAS NEVER RUN.** Its rename and arch-verification logic are executed offline against the real matcher and the real electron-builder names, including the raw x64 name as a negative control; the YAML, the job-level `contents: write`, `gh release create --verify-tag --latest` and the entire `hdiutil`/`lipo` step are unexercised, and no disk image was opened by CI. **THE MENU-BAR GLYPH IS STILL MONOCHROME** — a coloured semaphore dot needs either a non-template glyph, losing dark-mode and tinted-wallpaper adaptation, or Electron PR 48738 (layered tray icons), which is open, WIP and confirmed absent from 43.5.0. **TWO GENUINELY DIFFERENT MACHINES whose short labels collide** still render identically, left deliberately with a fixture recording it. **VERIFICATION: 157 → 159 offline suites, 159 passed / 0 failed; 180 total (159 OFFLINE + 14 LIVE_CI + 7 LIVE_LOCAL).** Roughly 62 mutations across four agents. **TWO came back GREEN and both were chased** — an `archFromAssetName` verification loop invisible because every fixture was already correctly named, closed by driving it from a version string that makes the arm64 name carry BOTH tokens; and a clipped-notice tooltip whose reachability nothing asserted, closed with a guard plus a control that a short notice carries no tooltip. **One mutation was INVALID and discarded rather than counted** — its anchor did not match, so it never applied, the recorded shape where a suite reports "green, restored ok" for a guard that was never tested. **Four reddened by CRASHING rather than by a named assertion** and every site was guarded. One agent found a defect in ITS OWN HARNESS and reported it: a regex error killed the run before its restore, leaving a mutation on disk and silently poisoning every later result; it now refuses to start unless both files match a recorded baseline sha and applies mutations under `try`/`finally`. Real `.curator-config.json` sha256 identical before and after for every agent, the maintainer's `domains/` never written to, his running app never disturbed, and no `.app` launched. |
| `v3.37.0` | **The menubar widget got the graphics it was asked for, the menu stopped being a quarter of the screen, and — for the first time in six surfaces — somebody actually LOOKED at it before it shipped.** Everything here came from the maintainer opening the v3.35.0 widget for real and reporting what he saw. **(1) SETTINGS OPENED ON THE WRONG SECTION, AND IT WAS ONE DEFECT WEARING TWO FACES.** Clicking Settings in the tray landed on *Providers & keys*; so did clicking it in the app. That looked like two bugs and was one: both surfaces resolve to the same `navigate('settings')` -> `onEnter` -> `freshState()`, and `freshState()` hardcoded `section: 'providers'` under a comment justifying it as *"matches the design prototype's default state"* — a value nobody revisited when the sidebar order was settled with General first. It was **never a remembered-last-section feature**: the section is deliberately in-memory only, never written to storage, so there was no user intent to honour and nothing to preserve. The fix is two lines, and the interesting half is that the default is now **DERIVED** — `SETTINGS_SECTIONS[0][0]` at both the default and the mount-time prefetch — so the sidebar's order and the landing section cannot drift apart again by editing one of them. The guard asserts the same derivation rather than pinning the literal `'general'` twice, because a test that hardcodes the answer at two sites passes happily while both sites disagree with the sidebar; it carries a control proving its comparator can report a MISMATCH, and mutating either site independently reds it. **(2) THE HEARTBEAT IS DRAWN, AND THE MECHANISM IS THE FINDING.** Electron's `Tray` can only show a `Menu`, and **`NSMenuItem.setView:` — the hook Stats and iStat Menus use to draw their charts — is ABSENT from Electron: zero matches in the shipped type definitions.** A live multi-band trace is therefore impossible, and a menu is frozen once open at the AppKit level. What IS available was verified against the installed Electron 43.5.0 rather than against documentation: `MenuItemConstructorOptions.icon` accepts a `NativeImage`, Electron performs **no scaling whatsoever** (`item.image = icon.GetImage().ToNSImage()`, with no resize anywhere in the menu controller), and `nativeImage.createFromBuffer` exists. So the strip ships as a generated **83x11-point alpha-only template PNG** in a menu item's icon gutter — a **still frame redrawn per open**, stated as a limitation rather than engineered around. It reuses `encodeAlphaPng` from `tray-icon.js` rather than carrying a second encoder, and the suite DECODES the emitted bytes and asserts real pixel coverage. The one Electron call is **injected as `makeIcon`**, so every decision — whether there is a strip, what it says, where it sits, whether it is actionable — stays in a module `npm test` executes for real, and `main.js` keeps only the two lines it cannot give away. **(3) THE STRIP COSTS ZERO ADDITIONAL FILE I/O, AND THAT IS A MEASUREMENT.** `listWorkingScopes` already read and parsed every journal IN FULL on every call, and `journalFacts` then discarded every timestamp except the newest — **54 of 65 entries on the maintainer's own store, parsed and thrown away.** The pulse keeps them. `saveTimes` is strictly opt-in (`opts.withSaveTimes === true`) precisely so the MCP index payload stays byte-identical, which is proven by EXECUTING the real `getWorkingStateHandler` down the branch that forwards store rows wholesale, with a control proving the search finds the fields when they are present. Timing across 3x20 runs: p50 **2.19 ms before, 2.41 ms after**, inside the run-to-run spread of the unchanged code. **(4) THREE CELL STATES, NOT TWO, AND ON THIS STORE THE THIRD IS HALF THE PICTURE.** ACTIVE, EMPTY, and **UNKNOWN** for buckets predating the store. They differ by **SHAPE — a bar versus a baseline tick — not by opacity**, because an opacity difference does not survive 2pt of width. Measured on the real store: 28 buckets of 6 hours across 7 days, 67 events, `coversWholeWindow: false`, **`firstKnownBucket: 13`** — so 13 of 28 cells mean *this store did not exist yet*, which makes the fact-versus-absence distinction the COMMON case rather than an edge case. The label states only what it can support: **`4 days known`** rather than `7 days` when the store is younger than the window, and `at least N saves` when a journal hit its 16 KB tail cap. **(5) THE ONE ENCODING THAT WAS REFUSED, AND WHY THE REFUSAL WON OVER THE ORCHESTRATOR'S OWN INSTINCT.** Bar HEIGHT encoding the save count would read more literally as a heartbeat, and it was rejected on the design record's existing grounds: save frequency is a property of **the skill's capture cadence, not of work done** — an agent told to save early and often produces more ticks than one told to save twice, so a taller bar would say MORE PROGRESS when it means DIFFERENT INSTRUCTIONS. Alpha encodes cadence and never anything readable as quality; the refusal is asserted as copy, with an anti-vacuity control. **(6) ONLY THE AGENT'S OWN CLOCK IS COUNTED, WITH NO mtime FALLBACK AND NO 'mixed' STATE.** git rewrites mtime on checkout, so an mtime-fed chart would draw a second machine's pulled history as a spike at the moment of the pull — the v3.34.0 defect redrawn as a graph. `events` and `pairsCounted` are counted **BEFORE** the display slice and asserted at `limit: 1`, because the tray reads 11 pairs while showing 8 rows and reporting a CAP as a MEASUREMENT is this project's own twice-shipped defect. **(7) ONE LAPTOP STOPPED APPEARING AS TWO, AND THEN THE FIX'S OWN SHADOW HAD TO BE CHASED.** Two machine folders differing only in hostname — one flapped under DHCP, both carrying the SAME installation id — were classified as different computers, so the maintainer's own menu named a machine that does not exist. Rows now match on the **trailing installation id alone**, derived from the existing `INSTALL_ID_RE`; the hostname half is never compared, and foreign machines, id-less machines and hex-shaped non-ids are all asserted unaffected. **The shadow:** with the machine name gone, two rows for one scope produced IDENTICAL labels, and the collision guard restored the folder names — reasserting the phantom computer the fix had just removed, by another door. Colliding rows on ONE machine now **escalate the AGE** (day -> hour -> minute) instead, so the two read `35 hr ago` and `37 hr ago`: the same fact at finer resolution, making no claim about hardware. An escalation that buys nothing is handed back, so no row reads *60 min ago* beside one reading *1 hr ago*; genuinely different machines keep their labels; and when escalation is exhausted the folder names return, with a fixture for each arm. `formatAge` was **extended with a precision floor rather than duplicated** — two functions rendering an age is the smallest possible version of the two-surfaces drift this project keeps recording — and a one-argument call is asserted byte-identical to the existing copy across all 28 inputs. **(8) THE MENU GOT NARROWER, MEASURED PER LINE.** Widest rendered line **87 -> 54 characters**, mean 57.0 -> 39.1, lines over 56 **16 -> 0**. Three components carried zero information and were dropped **conditionally on the data at render time, never hardcoded**: the project token when one project has state, a leading `session-` on every scope, and a harness shared by every shown row (an ABSENT harness counts against dropping). `MAX_HEADLINE_CHARS` went 72 -> 54 — shortened rather than deleted, because the ask was for the headline at a glance, not for its removal. **Every dropped fact is asserted still reachable in that row's tooltip**, per row, against the raw input; for the same-machine collision case the tooltip is now the ONLY place the folder name appears at all, which is what makes dropping it safe. **(9) A FLAKY SUITE WAS DIAGNOSED TO ITS MECHANISM AND THE AGENT REFUSED TO CLAIM A PROOF IT DID NOT HAVE.** `test-first-run-domains.js` reddened inside a full run and passed standalone minutes later, touching nothing that had changed. The cause is in `src/server.js`: `ensureDefaultDomainsDir()` **returns with the folder already on disk, and only then writes its announcement**, so the suite broke its poll on the filesystem signal and asserted on the stdout signal with zero margin. It now polls for BOTH before breaking and names which one never arrived. **The honest part: 20 runs at load average ~190 across 200 busy-loop processes could not reproduce a single failure**, so the fix is reasoned from the shipped write order rather than demonstrated by forcing a red — reported as such rather than dressed up. Happy path unchanged at ~220 ms; no assertion was altered. **(10) THE DOCUMENTATION SWEEP FOUND THIRTEEN FALSE CLAIMS BY RUNNING THINGS.** Including `architecture.md` asserting `buildTrayModel()` *"never reads"* the standing brief and that it *"appears only inside a docblock"* — it is computed and rendered in the tooltip; three further tray *known gaps* that had already been fixed and were still documented as live; `api-reference.md` describing `isThisMachine` as an EXACT match, the one doc actively contradicting this release's identity fix; a `desktop/README.md` assertion count of 200 against a measured 296; and **five references to Settings sections that do not exist** (*API Keys*, *My Curator*, *System Check*). It also found a real CODE defect the same way — `pulseToolTip()` emitting *"1 older save fall outside this window"* — by executing the function rather than reading it, which is the argument for documentation agents verifying instead of transcribing, made concrete for the second release running. The roadmap's reasoning sections keep their original text byte-for-byte and the deviations are recorded in its status section, because a design record that becomes a lie about its own history is worse than one that is merely stale. 782 internal links checked with 4 planted controls of which exactly 2 are bad, both caught. **(11) AND THEN IT WAS ACTUALLY LOOKED AT.** With the maintainer's explicit permission, a throwaway probe created ONE tray icon, popped the menu, screenshotted it and was killed. **macOS accepted the whole template** — 21 items, the injected strip image, the `sublabel` second lines, the template tinting — and the strip renders legibly at 83x11 with its unknown region visibly distinct from its empty cells. Every one of those was unproven an hour earlier. The maintainer's real app was untouched, no dev Electron process survived, and the tree was clean afterwards. **KNOWN AND UNFIXED.** **THE STRIP IS DRAWN AT REDUCED CONTRAST**, because its row is `enabled: false` — the menu's existing idiom for a statement rather than an action. The screenshot makes the cost visible: the one piece of graphics the maintainer asked for is the dimmest thing above the separator, which is uncomfortably close to the compounding-opacity defect this repo has already fixed twice. Deliberately left for his verdict rather than changed on the way out of the door. **THE MENU IS STILL ROUGHLY A QUARTER OF THE SCREEN** — measured at about 850 of 3600 physical pixels with the widest row now being the pulse row itself, strip plus label. The 87 -> 54 character reduction is real and measured; what does not exist is a RENDERED before-and-after, because nothing in this widget had ever been rendered until this release. **THE MACHINE BADGE THE MAINTAINER ASKED FOR WAS NOT BUILT**, on purpose: his second computer has PULLED the state and has never SAVED any, so there is no second writer in the data and a badge would today label one laptop as two. **A COLOURED SEMAPHORE DOT WAS NOT BUILT EITHER** — it needs either a non-template glyph, losing dark-mode, tinted-wallpaper and menu-open-inverted adaptation and worse on a transparent menu bar, or Electron PR 48738 (layered tray icons), which is open, WIP and confirmed absent from 43.5.0. The filled-centre live state already ships; what remains unsettled is what *new* should MEAN, which is the maintainer's own open question. **PER-MACHINE AND PER-HARNESS HEARTBEAT LANES WERE REFUSED ON THE DATA**: `harness` is one value across all 65 journal lines and `model` likewise, so N lanes over a one-valued dimension is one lane with a legend. **TWO GENUINELY DIFFERENT MACHINES whose short labels collide** (same hostname, installation ids sharing their first four hex characters) still render identically, left deliberately with a fixture recording it, because inventing a finer age would hide a tag-length problem rather than fix it. **`MENU_CHAR_POINTS` IS AN ASSUMED GLYPH ADVANCE** — no font was ever measured — so *the strip row is not the widest line* is an argument in numbers, not a measurement. The documentation sweep's mermaid check is a **structural lint and not a parse**, because no parser was available offline. **VERIFICATION: 154 -> 157 offline suites, 157 passed / 0 failed; 178 total (157 OFFLINE + 14 LIVE_CI + 7 LIVE_LOCAL).** Roughly 56 mutations across five agents. **FOUR came back GREEN and every one was chased rather than filed** — a machine-identity guard reachable only on a degraded install with no installation id, closed by exporting the function as a test seam; a collision-guard skip invisible because no fixture had two anonymous remote rows, whose real shape would have relabelled a row from another computer with a harness running HERE; an escalation branch invisible because its fixture's two rows had IDENTICAL ages, so the escalation ran, changed nothing and looked correct, closed by giving the fixture the two REAL ages that round to the same words but genuinely differ; and an empty-cell assertion that simply did not exist. **Three reddened by CRASHING rather than by a named assertion** — the recorded v3.24.1 shape — including one where a throw inside the pulse ESCAPED `getTraySummary`, which documents itself as never throwing; that is now a named assertion at every call site. **One agent's own harness was found defective and reported**: a regex error killed it before its restore, leaving a mutation on disk and silently poisoning every later result, which presented as unrelated failures; it now refuses to start unless both files match a recorded baseline sha and applies mutations under `try`/`finally`. Real `.curator-config.json` sha256 identical before and after for every agent, the maintainer's `domains/` never written to, his running app never disturbed, and no `.app` launched. |
| `v3.36.0` | **The maintainer chose Check for Updates from the menu bar and was sent to a web browser, while Settings installed the update in place — two update paths that disagreed, both looking deliberate.** He found it within minutes of v3.35.0 shipping, by doing the one thing no test does: using the other door. **(1) THE CAUSE IS THE TWO-SURFACES DEFECT THIS PROJECT KEEPS RECORDING, AND THIS TIME BOTH HALVES SHIPPED IN ONE RELEASE.** The macOS menu was built in v3.33.0 by one agent against the check-and-tell design that was correct when it was written; the in-app update engine landed in **the same release**, built by a different agent; and nobody rewired the menu. So `update-verdict.js` still composed *"This build does not install updates by itself. Download opens the release page in your browser"* — a confident, precise sentence describing behaviour the app had stopped having. Verified before dispatching a fix: no module under `desktop/lib/` except `update-engine.js` and `state-watch.js` so much as mentioned `prepareUpdate` or `installUpdate`. **Both halves passed their own tests. The seam between them was never driven** — the same shape as v3.34.0's dropped warning, where one suite fed the route a string and the other asserted the engine's object. **(2) THE FIX MAKES THE SHELL A SECOND CLIENT OF THE ROUTE, NOT A SECOND CALLER OF THE ENGINE, AND THAT CHOICE IS THE INTERESTING PART.** `main.js` already holds the engine, so `updater.prepareUpdate()` would have been two lines. It was **rejected**, because the ROUTE owns the job record: a menu-started download that bypassed it would leave `GET /update-progress` reporting `job: null` while 140 MB came down, so Settings would say *no update running* during an update — **the identical defect being fixed, reappearing one layer up.** The route also owns the pre-engine refusals and the `beginUpdate()` marker the shell's own quit dialog reads. Going through it buys a property for free: **open Settings mid-download and it adopts the running job and draws the five-phase ring**, with nothing in the shell drawing anything. The menu now issues exactly `POST /api/config/update` then `POST /api/config/update/apply` — the same two calls Settings makes, in the same order. **(3) FOUR OUTCOMES WHERE THERE WAS ONE.** Updater attached and no job → *Download and Install*. A job already `running` → it says so, instead of letting the user click Download and meet a 409. A job already `staged` → *Install Now*, **skipping a re-download of 140 MB already verified on disk**. No updater attached, or the probe did not answer → the old release-page dialog, **word for word**, because it is still exactly true there. The whole `git-pull` arm and every other verdict are asserted **byte-identical** with and without the probe. **(4) PROGRESS GOES IN THE MENU ITEM'S OWN LABEL, and the alternatives were rejected on mechanism rather than taste.** `Downloading Update… 43%`, composed from the server's progress record. **Electron has no API to change or close an open `showMessageBox`**, so a dialog that updates means a dialog per tick. Switching the window to Settings needs a window at all — and the menu is reachable with none — plus the two ordered renderer couplings v3.33.0 already rejected; worse, the auto-continue lives in the client that STARTED the stream, so a panel that merely adopted the job would stop at *downloaded* and wait for a click nobody mentioned. `setProgressBar` is a `BrowserWindow` method and cannot name a phase or a failure. The label is throttled to whole percent, **driven with 550 real progress events producing 103 menu rebuilds** — measured, not argued. **(5) THE DIALOG TEXT IS PINNED TO SETTINGS' OWN, BY READING IT.** `INSTALL_EXPLAINER` is asserted byte-identical to the sentence `src/public/next/views/settings.js` shows before the same decision, so the two surfaces cannot drift again by wording. It says the app *"checks it arrived complete and unaltered, then restarts into it — with no security warning to click through"*, and the suite asserts that string never mentions notarization or Apple approval: the no-Gatekeeper claim is about **quarantine**, measured with a control, and the build is still not signed by Apple. **(6) THE GUARD IS TRAFFIC, NOT TEXT, AND THAT WAS THE INSTRUCTION.** A test asserting the new sentence is present would have been worthless, because **the defect WAS a correct-looking string describing behaviour that did not exist.** So `runInstall` is EXECUTED against a fake `fetch` that records every request, with real `ReadableStream`/`Response`/`TextDecoder`, and the headline assertion is the request list. The SSE stream is re-fed **one character at a time** and must yield identical events; every refusal shape is relayed verbatim; and the endpoint constants are pinned to the `router.get/post()` registrations **derived from the constant rather than typed beside it**. **(7) A MUTATION FOUND A HAZARD THE HAPPY PATH HIDES.** Deleting an entry guard came back GREEN, because on the staging path an unusable fetch throws into a catch returning the same refusal. On the **applyOnly** path it is not redundant at all: there, **a rejected apply request IS the success signal** — the app is being replaced out from under the caller — so an unreachable client would have reported an installed update that never happened. Only reachable on the *already downloaded, just install* branch. **(8) THREE DOCS WERE ALREADY DESCRIBING THIS BEHAVIOUR AND WERE FALSE AGAINST SHIPPED CODE.** `README.md` and `docs/user-guide.md` in two places said the menu item downloads, verifies and restarts. **The documentation was AHEAD of the code and nothing noticed** — which is the mirror image of the defect this release fixes, and a reminder that a doc agreeing with your intentions is not evidence. They become true here; `docs/mac-app.md`, which named Settings as the only entry point, was corrected the other way. README's *"with a progress bar"* was narrowed: the ring is the Settings route; the menu reports in its own label. **KNOWN AND UNFIXED.** **`desktop/main.js` HAS STILL NEVER BEEN IMPORTED, EVALUATED OR RUN**, here or anywhere — Electron is not an offline dependency. `Menu.buildFromTemplate` has never seen the new template and **neither dialog has ever been rendered**; everything in `main.js` is source-scanned, and both suites say so in their own headings. **No HTTP server ran**: the route's status codes, SSE frames and refusal bodies are TRANSCRIBED from `src/routes/config.js` and its URLs pinned by reading the file, but the two halves have never been connected — a route that changed its event names would red only if it also changed a path. The `install-running` and `install-staged` arms have never been reached from a real server response. **The menubar widget's tray icon has still never been rendered either**, and `maybeCheckRemote` remains source-scanned. That is now five surfaces shipped in one day without a human seeing them — the honest cost of barring agents from launching anything after seven crash dialogs reached the maintainer's desktop, and it should not become a habit. **VERIFICATION: 153 -> 154 offline suites, 154 passed / 0 failed; 175 total (154 OFFLINE + 14 LIVE_CI + 7 LIVE_LOCAL).** 35 effective mutations. **FOUR came back GREEN and every one was chased** — a `reader.cancel()` invisible because the happy path already drains the stream, closed with an assertion on the EARLY return and a control proving the stream really was mid-flight at 88 of 180 bytes; a re-download flag invisible because `main.js` cannot be executed, closed STRUCTURALLY by moving the decision into a drivable module and scanning to keep the literal out of `main.js`; the entry guard above; and a bare `return;` inserted above the failure dialog, closed by asserting the property it broke rather than the line it deleted. **One mutation was INVALID and discarded rather than counted** — it assigned to a `const` and reddened by crashing, the recorded v3.24.1 shape, and was redone properly. Real `.curator-config.json`, `.sync-config.json` and `.sharedbrain-config.json` unchanged; the maintainer's `domains/` and port 3333 untouched; no `.app` launched, no dialog opened and no tray icon created. |
| `v3.35.0` | **The menubar widget — and the documentation agent found three defects in it by RUNNING the code, one of which made the feature's whole reason for existing inert.** Built from two research passes commissioned before any code was written, which is also what surfaced v3.34.0's harness collision and mtime bug. **(1) WHAT SHIPPED.** A macOS menubar icon, **off by default**, showing a **Menu and not a popover** — Electron's `Tray` can only show a `Menu`, and Apple's HIG says a menubar extra displays one; the richer panel the maintainer's reference screenshot showed is a deliberate later phase and nothing half-builds toward it. The menu carries the last save as **item one at full contrast** (putting the single line the widget exists for into the dimmest style macOS offers is the compounding-opacity defect this repo has already fixed twice), then up to 8 scopes newest-first, a truncation line naming the TRUE total, a remote-waiting count, a harness-collision notice, and Open Agent Memory / Open The Curator / Settings / Quit. **The slot does double duty:** harness on local rows, machine on remote ones — on a local row the machine is constant noise, and the moment a row is remote it is the whole point. **No text in the menu bar itself**, argued rather than omitted: a relative age there is either stale or needs waking every minute forever, and extra width is what makes an icon vanish behind the notch on a feature whose main risk is already that its icon is silently absent. **Quit is `role: 'quit'` with NO click handler**, so structurally no path exists that could reach `app.exit()` and walk past the write guard. **Cost when closed: ~0.025% of one core**, no timer polling — it watches the state folder and does nothing until something changes. **(2) THE CONTRACT WAS FIXED IN WRITING BEFORE EITHER HALF WAS BUILT, AND IT STILL HAD A HOLE THE ORCHESTRATOR PUT THERE.** Two agents built the data layer and the shell in parallel against one specified shape. The shell found that `getTraySummary({limit: 8})` returning exactly 8 scopes **cannot distinguish "capped" from "exactly 8"** — verified `rows: 8, truncatedNote: null` — so the *"…and N more"* line silently vanishes in the case a user most needs it. That is this project's own named defect, recorded of `distinctScopeCount`: **reporting a CAP as a MEASUREMENT.** `total` is now counted before the slice, and `lastSave` **IS** `scopes[0]` by construction rather than by coincidence, asserted field-by-field and again at `limit: 1` where a "newest across the whole set" implementation would diverge. **(3) THE ORCHESTRATOR'S OWN AUDIT CAUGHT A FEATURE THAT WAS BUILT AND UNREACHABLE.** `noteRemoteStatus()` was defined and **never called**, so the remote count would have been permanently empty — a feature that looks built and silently does nothing. Wired into the check `GET /api/sync/remote-status` already makes, adding no fetch of its own. **(4) AND THEN THE DOCUMENTATION AGENT RAN THE CODE AND FOUND THREE MORE.** This is the argument for docs agents verifying rather than transcribing, made concrete. **(a) HOVERING SHOWED A STALE AGE UNDER A MOVING TIMESTAMP.** `mouse-enter` re-renders from the in-memory snapshot precisely so a hover costs no I/O — right design — but `buildTrayModel()` copied the pre-computed `writtenAgeSeconds` instead of re-deriving it. Measured: ONE snapshot rendered forty minutes apart gives the identical `"Last save · 4 min ago"` **while `renderedAtText` moves**, which inverts the one thing the absolute `Updated HH:MM` stamp exists to show. Ages are now re-derived at the render clock using `working-state.js`'s own expression **character for character**, so this is the producer's opinion evaluated later rather than a second opinion. The `Math.max(0, …)` clamp is load-bearing: without it a handoff from a machine three seconds ahead reads *time unknown* instead of *just now*. **An unknown age stays unknown** — precedence is parseable timestamp, else the snapshot's number unchanged, else `null`. **(b) A HARNESS COLLISION WAS ANNOUNCED TWICE**, and the root cause was upstream of the obvious one: `buildTrayModel` flattened warnings to message strings before anything could read a code, and the suppression then matched the prose `/harness/i` while the only producer says *"Two agent tools are writing…"*. **The suppression was dead against the sole case it existed for**, and each collision burned 2 of 4 notice slots. Matching is now structural — the code plus the warning's own `project` and `scope` fields, never its prose. **The old test passed because its fixture was a hand-written string containing "harnesses", a shape the producer never emits: the fixture was the fiction.** The new section extracts the REAL message off disk and asserts the old suppression provably could not fire. **(c) THE MULTI-MACHINE SIGNAL WAS EFFECTIVELY INERT, WHICH IS THE ONE THAT MATTERED.** The *"2 handoffs waiting on GitHub"* line had exactly one feed, and that feed is a browser check which **declines to fetch while `document.hidden`** — and a hidden window is the tray's NORMAL state. So the feature built for *notice that another machine sent you something* could essentially never fire. Nothing rendered wrongly; it silently never appeared. It now checks **on menu open only** — never on hover, never on a watch, never on a timer — through `brain/sync.js`'s own `getRemoteStatus()`, inheriting the existing TTL cache, in-flight memo and fetch gate rather than creating a parallel set, with attempts stamped BEFORE the await so a slow failing check cannot leave the window open. **(5) A FIFTH DEFECT FELL OUT OF FIXING THE SECOND, AND IT IS THE SAME SHAPE.** `remoteNotice()` branched on `remote.ok === false`, and `noteRemoteStatus()` **never emitted an `ok`** — so a FAILED check reached the menu as `{behindFiles: null}`, took the no-number exit, and rendered as **nothing, byte-identical to never having checked**. The distinction survived the store and died at the model. That mattered little while nothing ever checked; the moment the tray asks, silence reads as an answer. **(6) A PREMISE OF THE ORCHESTRATOR'S WAS NARROWED BY MEASUREMENT, AND THE MEASUREMENT REFUSED TO CERTIFY ITSELF.** He argued a tray-triggered check was safe because `gitFetch()` serialises every fetch the process issues. It serialises every fetch **that module** issues; `git pull`'s own internal fetch is git's subprocess and is outside the gate and cannot be inside it. The agent then tried to measure the overlap — 60 concurrent runs against real git 2.48.1, including the fetch-vs-fetch shape that IS the recorded incident — and got **zero failures on either side**. Rather than report that as safety it reported it as **inconclusive**: a harness that cannot reproduce the known failure cannot certify its absence. The decision rests instead on this reaching the same function, through the same gate, as a call the app **already makes on a 10-minute timer whenever the window is open** — one rarer, human-initiated trigger for a shipped call. **(7) THE DOCUMENTATION CARRIES THE SCENARIOS, WHICH WAS THE MAINTAINER'S NAMED ASK.** Three, each tied to a decision the code actually makes: two agent tools on one computer, where the `who` column answers *was that Claude Code or opencode* and the state path has **no slot for the tool** so two tools in one work-stream overwrite each other; two or three computers on one private repo, where a remote row shows the machine and the age says which clock it came from; and the low-on-context glance, where the tooltip is the answer and the age is a **band** rather than a number. Plus an explicit **"scenarios this does not serve"** — reading a handoff, watching progress, syncing by itself — because automatic sync is researched and NOT built and the richer panel is designed and NOT built. 1,526 links checked with a planted 4-link control of which exactly 2 are bad, and a mutation renaming the new heading reddening 7 links across 4 files; 36 mermaid blocks with both a must-parse and a must-fail control. **The roadmap keeps its reasoning byte-for-byte** and gains a status section naming five deviations, because a roadmap that becomes a lie about its own history is worse than one that is merely stale. **KNOWN AND UNFIXED.** **NO TRAY ICON HAS EVER BEEN RENDERED, on this machine or any other — `new Tray()` has never been called.** The row model, the menu template, the mode transitions and the glyph's actual pixels are executed by tests, and the PNG was opened by macOS's own `sips` to prove validity by something other than its author's decoder. Unproven: that Electron accepts these `role`/`sublabel` values, that macOS tints the image as a template, that `mouse-enter` fires, and `maybeCheckRemote` is source-scanned rather than executed. **That is the fourth surface in one day to ship without a human ever seeing it**, after the save-status strip, the update UI and the sync UI — the honest cost of barring agents from launching anything after seven crash dialogs reached the maintainer's desktop, and it should not become a habit. **`tray-only` is HEDGED**: it is recognised and turns the tray on but leaves the Dock alone, because the `accessory → regular` transition it depends on is reported buggy in exactly the direction that mode needs and could not be tested here. **Discovery is Settings-only** — there is no in-app offer when a project first accumulates traffic, which the research recommended. **The budget bar and the recency pips are undrawn**; an NSMenu cannot draw them and they belong to the popover phase. **A row click lands on the PROJECT, not the scope**, because the scope picker carries no routing attribute. **The four `sync.js` pins were not mutation-tested**, since that file was read-only for the agent; the raw-fetch counter carries controls proving it can return 0 and 2, so counting 1 is a measurement rather than an assumption. **VERIFICATION: 150 -> 153 offline suites, 153 passed / 0 failed; 174 total (153 OFFLINE + 14 LIVE_CI + 7 LIVE_LOCAL).** 80 mutations across four agents. **Eight came back GREEN and every one was chased** — a sort assertion whose fixture's alphabetical order accidentally equalled its recency order, so deleting the sort entirely still passed; Both were fixed: the assertion, and the harness, which now treats a non-zero exit or a missing tally as red-by-crash. Real `.curator-config.json`, `.sync-config.json` and `.sharedbrain-config.json` fingerprinted at both ends and unchanged; the maintainer's `domains/` and port 3333 untouched; no `.app` launched and no tray icon created. |
| `v3.34.0` | **Two defects the maintainer had been living inside without knowing, plus the memory layer finally telling the truth about its own clock.** All of it came out of him using v3.33.0 for real and out of two research passes commissioned before any code was written. **(1) TWO AGENT HARNESSES ON ONE COMPUTER SILENTLY OVERWRITE EACH OTHER, AND THAT IS HIS DAILY SETUP.** State lives at `state/<scope>/<machine>/` and `<machine>` is `<hostname-slug>-<install-id>` — per INSTALLATION, not per process. So opencode and Claude Code on one machine resolve to the **same folder**; `skills/curator-continuity` explicitly tells agents to *"reuse an existing scope whenever the work continues"*; and the only guard, `would-replace-larger-state`, refuses a save under about 5% of the stored one — so two FULL handoffs are both substantial and neither is refused. `test-memory-truth.js` §3 **reproduces this rather than quoting it**: three substantial saves from two harnesses, all three accepted, all three on one path, the surviving `current.md` holding only the last writer's work. **THE DETECTION IS BY ALTERNATION, NOT BY A CLOCK, AND THAT SHAPE IS THE FINDING.** A time window ("two harnesses within N minutes") needs a tuned constant that will be wrong on somebody's machine. The structural test needs none: a user who MIGRATED between tools produces exactly one transition ever (`A A A B B B`) while two tools working one scope INTERLEAVE (`A B A B`), so `harnessShared` requires ≥2 distinct harnesses AND ≥2 transitions. Entries carrying no harness are SKIPPED when counting transitions rather than treated as a third value — an unnamed save is missing evidence, and letting it break an `A…A` run would manufacture transitions out of silence — and they are counted separately so the blind half of the window is stated. **A path change was refused:** adding a `<harness>` segment alters the layout of a SYNCED store with live data on two of his machines, and the per-machine segment is load-bearing for the merge guarantee. The journal survives the collision precisely because it is append-only, so reading it costs no migration and no new failure mode. **AND THE SKILL NOW PREVENTS IT RATHER THAN THE APP REPORTING IT** — §8 gained a clause naming a second harness as parallel work needing its own scope, with the mechanism (the harness name is recorded INSIDE a save, never in the path) and an explicit instruction NOT to try to detect it, because an agent cannot see the other harness. **(2) EVERY SCOPE PULLED FROM ANOTHER MACHINE READ AS BRAND NEW.** `ageSeconds` came from filesystem mtime, and **git rewrites mtime on checkout** — measured in an isolated repo, a handoff authored in 2020 arrives on a second machine, by clone AND by incremental pull, with mtime = the moment of the pull. A live defect in the shipped Agent memory view, not a widget concern: on a multi-machine setup every pulled scope reported as just-written. The journal line's own `at` holds the true time, and `listWorkingScopes` **already parsed that line for `headline`** and discarded `at` and `harness`. The agent's clock is now returned **BESIDE** the filesystem clock and never replaces it, because *when did this arrive here* is also a real question; `savedAt` is untouched because it is on the pinned MCP contract, the same resolution `scopeCount`/`distinctScopeCount` already uses. **Three corrections to the orchestrator's premises, all in his favour:** "zero additional I/O" was true of the project index ONLY — `listScopeMachines`, which feeds the Machine picker whose entire job is *which computer and when*, read no journals at all; it was THREE sites on screen rather than one, now routed through one `effectiveSave` so they cannot disagree about what *2 min ago* means; and `writtenAt` can UNDERSTATE freshness, because the journal append is best-effort and never fails a save — the safe direction, since a handoff reported as older prompts another save, which is free and idempotent. **(3) "AM I SAVED?" IS NOW ANSWERABLE IN ABOUT A SECOND, AND IT NEVER CLAIMS YOU ARE SAVED.** His words: *"when I'm approaching the end of the context window I'm always wondering if we have updated the scope, and if the standing brief is up to date."* A strip at the TOP of the pane, above the pickers and both conditional notes, because the answer must not be the fifth thing on a busy day. **A pip of five discrete steps cut on `formatAge`'s own unit bands**, so the mark and the word change at the same instant and can never contradict each other — not a bar, because *how old* has no maximum and a half-full meter would invent one, with the pre-attentive contrast concentrated at the one-hour line so *5 minutes ago* and *4 hours ago* differ before you read them. **A null age is a dashed ring, not step 0** — *we don't know* is not *long ago*. It also surfaces the case nobody would think to check: *"Newer state in this project: `side-quest` — 12 min ago"*, which catches an agent saving beside you into a scope you are not watching. The standing brief gets a line and deliberately **no pip**, because an old brief is not a stale one. **And the label is `Last saved`, not "you are saved"** — it knows when the last save happened, not whether anything has changed since, and the inference stays with the user. **A TRIMMED SAVE MAY NEVER RENDER AS A BARE AGE:** the badge sits ON the reading rather than in a note underneath, because a completeness caveat below the figure is one the one-second glance never reaches. **(4) A PULL DELETED FOLDERS IT WAS NEVER TOLD TO DELETE, AND THE BLAST RADIUS WAS NOT LOCAL.** `pruneGhostDomainDirs()` ran on every pull and `rm -rf`'d any non-dot top-level directory without a `CLAUDE.md`. The domains folder is a **documented Obsidian vault root** — the docs tell people to point Obsidian at it — and Obsidian's own default for pasted images is an attachments folder in the vault root. Measured with nothing incoming: `[Attachments, demo, newdomain]` became `[demo]`. It was reported as *"removed N deleted domains"*, which it never was. **THE PART THE ORIGINAL REPORT NEVER NOTICED:** `push()`'s `git add -A` TRACKS a stray folder, so removing it STAGED a deletion that the next push propagated to the remote and to every other machine — measured as ` D Attachments/diagram.png`. Removal now requires ALL FOUR: this pull's merge actually deleted tracked files under that path (a folder git has never heard of cannot appear in a deletion diff, so it is unreachable regardless of its name), no `CLAUDE.md`, git tracks nothing under it any more, and nothing under it is untracked-and-not-ignored — ignored content does NOT block, because that is the v2.3.4 case the function exists for. **Every branch fails toward keeping**, and §1 keeps the pre-fix rule transcribed literally as a permanent control against real git. A user who already has a stray folder is now **permanently** safe: nominating it would require them to have pushed it and then deleted it elsewhere on purpose. **(5) A WARNING WAS BEING BUILT AND SILENTLY DROPPED, AND FIXING THE OBVIOUS SIDE WOULD HAVE BEEN WORSE THAN THE BUG.** v3.33.0's updater warns when the running app is not in `/Applications`. The engine returned `{reason, message}`; the route accepted only a string; so `warning` was **unconditionally `null`**. Neither suite saw it — one fed the route a string, the other asserted the object, and nothing drove the seam between them. **The fix went into the ENGINE, and the deciding fact is the TEXT rather than the shape:** that object's `message` is `classifyLaunchOrigin`'s sentence, written for the MCP shim, which talks about the Claude Desktop launcher and offers to set that connection up. Relaying it would have replaced `null` with **something false in front of someone updating an app**. The two features correctly share a *reason*; each must say its own consequence. The new guard manufactures no value: the real `classifyLaunchOrigin` feeds the real `classifyInstallTarget` feeds the real route feeds the real client — four hops, one value — with an anti-vacuity control that an `/Applications` install stays quiet, because a panel that warns on every update is one nobody reads. **(6) SIX STALE IN-CODE CLAIMS, EACH VERIFIED BEFORE REWRITING.** `install-mode.js` and `diagnostics.js` still described updating as something the app can only TELL you about. `settings.js` said an ingest **or sync** waits for an update — **sync is not gated**: `isUpdateInProgress()` is checked by ingest, compile, health, sharedbrain and the ingest queue, while `guardConcurrent` in both `sync.js` and `config.js` tests only `hasActiveWrites()`, which reads a different variable entirely. The documentation sweep briefly wrote the same wrong claim itself before checking. The literal *"34 named reasons"* was **36**, counted by executing the table — and rather than correcting a literal that will rot again, both sites now assert **no reason appears in both tables**, with an anti-vacuity check. `desktop-dmg.yml` still claimed the `--workflow` pin *"belongs in release.js, reported rather than edited"* when it landed in v3.30.0. **(7) THE DOCUMENTATION SWEEP FOUND THE README TELLING A PROSPECTIVE USER THE APP HAS NO AUTO-UPDATE**, in the section where someone decides whether to install — found by the maintainer reading his own page as a stranger would. The same claim was live in four more places. Worse were two others: `mcp-user-guide.md` said *"if you move your domains folder, MCP stops working"*, **false in bundle mode** where the entry carries no `--domains-path`, at five sites; and `sync-via-coding-agent.md` handed a coding agent a second-machine prompt that now **409s** AND still recommended `pull` "to absorb the remote" — a document aiming an agent at the exact path that destroyed this user's data. 18 files corrected, 1,228 internal links checked with a three-case planted control, 33 mermaid blocks with both a must-parse and a must-fail control. **(8) THE SKILL TOLD THE USER TO DO SOMETHING IMPOSSIBLE.** `curator-continuity` said the standing brief is edited *"in the app"*. The Agent memory view is **READ-ONLY by design** — that is the single-writer property the whole sync-safety argument rests on, and no MCP tool writes tier 1. Four sites corrected to *edit `state/project.md` directly, in a text editor or Obsidian*, plus the recorded rule that an agent may make that edit **only on the owner's explicit ask in that session, never on its own initiative**. **The frontmatter is byte-identical** (sha256 verified) and `description:` is **968 characters** before and after against claude.ai's 1024 cap — the three recorded upload traps (the cap, a colon-space breaking the YAML scalar, an XML-shaped placeholder rejected by the validator) all satisfied deliberately rather than by luck. **THE SKILL INSTALLS BY MANUAL UPLOAD AND MUST BE RE-UPLOADED BY HAND** for any of this to take effect. **KNOWN AND UNFIXED.** **THE SAVE-STATUS STRIP HAS NEVER BEEN RENDERED IN A BROWSER** — `preview_start` was refused by the permission classifier, so contrast is exact arithmetic over opaque values and layout was verified by running the shipped function in Node across six states. **Whether five pip steps are PERCEPTIBLY different at 12px is unverified and the suite makes no claim about it.** The update UI and the new sync UI are in the same position, three releases running. **`mcp/tools/working-state.js` still quotes `savedAt` — the FILESYSTEM time — in its human-readable report**, the sentence an agent is most likely to repeat back to the user, even though the structured `writtenAt` is forwarded correctly. **A NESTED GIT REPOSITORY IN THE DOMAINS FOLDER BREAKS SYNC OUTRIGHT:** found while building a fixture, a `.git` directory with no commits anywhere under the domains root makes `git add -A` fail, killing both `push()` and `pull()`'s auto-save. The domains folder is an Obsidian vault root, so a cloned notes repo living there is plausible. Reported, unfixed. **A domain whose `CLAUDE.md` the user deletes BY HAND is no longer swept**, and lingers hidden from the app until they remove it — a deliberate trade for never touching a folder git has not seen. **The `/old` bundle still says "removed N deleted domains"** and still has no in-app update path at all. **`test-first-run-domains.js` remains FLAKY** — §7 waits on a wall clock for a real server boot, the shape v3.29.0 recorded as a defect in `watchCi`. **VERIFICATION: 148 -> 150 offline suites, 150 passed / 0 failed; 171 total (150 OFFLINE + 14 LIVE_CI + 7 LIVE_LOCAL).** 67 mutations across five agents. **SIX came back GREEN and every one was chased rather than filed** — a rule masked by a sibling rule until a fixture was built for the shapes only it saves; a pure optimisation correctly redundant and annotated as such; a `CLAUDE.md` test subsumed by two others and kept as a git-free backstop; a `prunedKept` assertion whose fixture could not race the way production does, replaced by a deterministic record of the mechanism; a `distinct.length > 1` clause genuinely implied by the switch count, kept with the redundancy MEASURED over 340 generated sequences rather than claimed; and a quiet-on-success assertion that simply did not exist, so a panel warning on every update would have passed. **Five mutations reddened by CRASHING rather than by a named assertion** — the recorded v3.24.1 shape — including one that crashed before an *another application is not touched* assertion could run.
