| `v3.33.0` | **The Mac app updates itself, because the previous release's answer to "update" was to open a web page and the maintainer's verdict on it was one word: *"terrible."*** **(1) THE STANDARD ELECTRON UPDATER IS UNUSABLE HERE, AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN CONFIGURABLE.** Measured on the installed app, not reasoned from config: `codesign -d -r-` returns `designated => cdhash H"ff0e7bb5…"`, `Signature=adhoc`, `TeamIdentifier=not set`. Squirrel.Mac — which is what `electron-updater`'s `MacUpdater` drives — validates every download against the **RUNNING** app's designated requirement, and an ad-hoc signature has no certificate and no team, so `codesign` builds that requirement out of the only thing available: **the code-directory hash of that exact build.** Every genuine update has a different cdhash by definition, so it fails **100% of the time, deterministically**, and the check lives inside Electron's own binary rather than in electron-builder configuration, so nothing reaches it. Two further blockers, each sufficient alone: Squirrel installs from a **ZIP** while releases publish `.dmg`, and electron-updater reads `latest-mac.yml`, which electron-builder emits only when `publish` is set and ours is `publish: null`. **A CORRECTION TO THE v3.32.0 ROW, made here rather than by editing history:** it says the app "deliberately runs with `hardenedRuntime` OFF". `hardenedRuntime: true` IS present in `electron-builder.yml`; it is **inert**, because `identity: null` makes electron-builder skip signing entirely and `adhoc-sign.mjs` omits `--options runtime` — and `assertAdhocOnly` **refuses the build** if that flag ever appears. The effect was described correctly; the key is not actually off. **(2) SO THE APP DOWNLOADS AND SWAPS ITSELF, WHICH IS WHAT MAC APPS DID BEFORE NOTARIZATION EXISTED.** Three new `desktop/lib/` modules split by testability — pure decisions, release resolution, and I/O — exposed as two hooks through the existing module registry, because the shell and the server share one Node realm and there is no IPC. `prepareUpdate` resolves, downloads, verifies and **STAGES**, replacing nothing; `installUpdate` swaps and relaunches. **`installUpdate` TAKES AN OPAQUE TOKEN AND NEVER A PATH, AND THAT IS A SECURITY PROPERTY RATHER THAN A STYLE CHOICE:** the caller is a RENDERER, so a hook accepting `{stagedPath, targetPath}` would be a *replace any directory with any other* primitive reachable from a page. **Resolution is fully delegated** to `config.js`'s own `pickInstallableRelease` / `decideInstallerUpdate` / `RELEASES_*` — there is no version comparator anywhere under `desktop/` and the suite asserts it, because two answers to *what is the newest version* is how they drift. **(3) THE SWAP IS TWO RENAMES ON SAME-DEVICE SIBLINGS, AND THE FAILURE TABLE IS THE DESIGN.** macOS's atomic `renamex_np(RENAME_SWAP)` is unreachable from Node or any shipped CLI tool, so: device compared with `stat -f %d` FIRST, so `mv` cannot silently degrade to a copy; then two renames. Power lost **before** leaves the old app complete; **after** leaves the new app complete; and **in the two-syscall window between them** `The Curator.app` is absent while **both complete bundles sit beside it under known names** — neither half-written, because both were verified before the app quit and `rename` moves no bytes. Recovery is one `mv`. That window is the price of the property that a **half-replaced bundle is impossible**, which was the stated hard requirement; every other path rolls back and reopens the old app, through three separate `open "$TARGET"` exits. **(4) WHAT IS VERIFIED, AND WHAT HONESTLY CANNOT BE.** Byte length against the declared `size`; **sha256 against the `digest` GitHub publishes on the asset**, confirmed live and populated on all four DMGs of v3.31.0 and v3.32.0; then the staged bundle's `CFBundleShortVersionString` and `codesign --verify --deep --strict`. What it cannot verify is that **Apple vouches for the bytes** — `codesign --verify` on an ad-hoc bundle is an INTEGRITY check, not an authenticity one, so authenticity rests entirely on the digest and on TLS to GitHub. That is why the digest check is not optional and why the download host is allow-listed to `github.com` / `objects.githubusercontent.com`, and the UI copy was corrected so it never implies Apple checked anything. **(5) THE UPDATED APP OPENS WITH NO GATEKEEPER PROMPT, MEASURED WITH A CONTROL.** A DMG carrying `com.apple.quarantine` — what a browser download stamps — yields a quarantined app; the same DMG fetched by the app's own `fetch()` yields an unquarantined one. So the in-app path removes the Privacy & Security detour AND App Translocation, which is the whole difference from the hand-install flow he rejected. Both arms live in the macOS suite, the browser-download arm as the control. **(6) FIVE NAMED PHASES OVER THE EXISTING PROGRESS RING, NOT A NEW BAR.** `resolving / downloading / verifying / staging / installing`, with `58.2 MB of 137 MB · 43%` while downloading. **`percent` is `null`, never a number, when the total is unknown** — rendered as bytes-downloaded plus *total size unknown*, because a bar sitting at 0% is a different claim from a bar that cannot know, which is this project's recorded fact-versus-absence rule on a long operation. **Navigating away does NOT cancel:** the stream is a view, the job is module-level, and a full page reload re-finds it; proven end to end with nothing mounted. **The auto-continue to the swap is safe only because the server RE-CHECKS `hasActiveWrites()` AT THE SWAP**, so an ingest begun during the download parks the update at `staged` with a one-click finish rather than being truncated. Failures render the engine's own message from **34 named reasons**, never a raw exception, and the old app keeps working. **(7) THE INTEGRATION POINT WAS THE THING MOST LIKELY TO BREAK, AND IT NEARLY DID.** Two agents built the halves in parallel against a contract fixed in advance. The UI agent found in its own work that a hook rename **never landed** — the constants still named the old hooks while its suite only asserted the two names were *distinct strings*, so the whole feature would have refused silently at runtime. Names are now transcribed as literals rather than read back from the module under test, and the orchestrator verified the chain end to end after merging: the shell registers `prepareUpdate`/`installUpdate`, `DESKTOP_HOOKS` allows exactly those, and `config.js`'s constants resolve to the same two strings. **(8) THE HEAD-PINNING TRAP RECURRED THE SAME DAY IT WAS FIXED.** `test-update-in-app.js` pinned its before/after baseline to `HEAD`, which means *before this change* only while the change is uncommitted; once merged it compared a function against itself. It went red on the merge rather than silently green, which is luck, not design — the identical defect in `test-update-installer.js` this morning surfaced the same way. Pinned to `32cb410` with a control asserting the two texts DIFFER. **The pattern is now recorded twice in one release and should be assumed to exist in other suites.** **KNOWN AND UNFIXED.** **NO APPLICATION HAS EVER BEEN REPLACED BY AN AUTOMATED RUN** — the macOS suite swaps a real ad-hoc-signed FIXTURE bundle in a temp dir with the relaunch stubbed, and it does really replace it and the result really still passes `codesign`. What is NOT proven: the 140 MB GitHub round trip, the real width of the two-syscall window, `main.js`'s wiring (Electron is not an offline dependency), and Rosetta — an arm64-under-x64 install stays on x64 forever, because a silent architecture migration behind a progress bar is not something to do without an explicit offer. **THE UPDATE UI HAS NEVER BEEN RENDERED IN A BROWSER**, like the sync UI before it, because agents remain barred from launching anything after seven crash dialogs reached the maintainer's desktop. **While the download runs and the user is on another view there is no indicator anywhere** — a shell-level badge needs `next/app.js`, which the owning agent did not hold. **`signal` is plumbed but never fired**, documented as deliberately quiescent rather than shipped as a half-wired cancel. **`/old` has no in-app path at all** and still POSTs the git updater. **`test-first-run-domains.js` is FLAKY** — it failed once on a fresh worktree's first run and passes standalone; §7 waits on a WALL CLOCK for a real server boot, the same shape v3.29.0 recorded as a defect in `watchCi`. **The 140 MB staging directory is derived in `main.js`** rather than through a `getUpdateStagingDir()` in `paths.js`, which would follow the `getMcpLauncherDir()` precedent. **VERIFICATION: 146 -> 148 offline suites, 148 passed / 0 failed; 169 total (148 OFFLINE + 14 LIVE_CI + 7 LIVE_LOCAL), plus a LIVE_LOCAL macOS suite run for real at 31/31.** 57 mutations across the two agents. **NINE came back GREEN and every one was chased rather than filed** — an md5 assertion that could not fail because the length check rejected it first; a docblock claiming `ditto` preserves a signature where `cp -R` does not, refuted by measurement (both exit 0, both 11 xattr lines); an asset picked by `payload[0]` that would download the previous version's installer while reporting the new one; two call sites never asserted although their functions were; a `stageProgress || 0.5` make-it-look-busy tweak invisible to an isolated test; a hardcoded attachment probe; and a busy flag that had to be extracted to be drivable. **Seven mutations reddened by CRASHING rather than by a named assertion** — the recorded v3.24.1 shape — including one that crashed BEFORE the *another application is not touched* assertion could run. Four bugs were caught in the engine agent's own code before any mutation, including a filename matcher that read `TheCurator-3.arm64.0-x64-Intel.dmg` as arm64 and an `AbortSignal` that overwrote its own 8-second timeout, leaving a cancellable but unbounded request. Real `.curator-config.json`, real `.knowledge-git` and real `domains/` untouched; `/Applications` never written to; port 3333 never disturbed; no `.app` launched. |
| `v3.32.0` | **Connecting sync destroyed four hours of the maintainer's working state, and the destructive step was a guard being ROUTED AROUND BY ITS OWN ERROR HANDLER.** Everything here comes from him installing v3.31.0 from GitHub as an ordinary user would and reporting what broke. **THE INSTALL ITSELF WORKED** — *"the process is as it should be for a macOS app"* — download, drag, open, six existing domains attached, keys added, chat, Health and ingest all fine. That is the previous release's goal met. Then he connected GitHub sync. **(1) GIT REFUSED THE DESTRUCTIVE OPERATION TWICE AND THE CODE OVERRODE IT.** Reproduced against real git with no Curator code in the loop, and kept permanently as §1 of the new suite: `git checkout -b main origin/main` **REFUSED**, naming every colliding file; `git checkout main` **REFUSED**; `git reset --hard origin/main` destroyed them, **exit 0, no warning**. `setup()`'s catch-ladder swallowed both refusals and fell through to the reset — **the one tree-writing git command with no untracked-file safety check.** So the earlier diagnosis, the orchestrator's included, that *"pull checked the remote out over the work tree"* is WRONG: the pull was innocent and the reflog proves it (`13:43:16 commit … / 13:44:18 reset: moving to origin/main / 13:49:16 pull --no-rebase -X theirs`). **This was not a missing guard. The guard fired, perfectly, and the error handler said yes anyway** — and the same reset ran again on his second connect, harmless only because he had pushed first. **(2) THE DATA WAS RECOVERABLE AND THE FIRST INVESTIGATION SAID IT WAS NOT.** The orchestrator checked the working tree, both repos' `log`, and the remote, found nothing, and reported the loss as permanent. The agent's report suggested the reflog; `HEAD@{…}` named commit `89c75577` *"Initial The Curator sync"*, made 62 seconds before the reset, carrying `current.md` at its newest revision and **all seven journal entries against the two left on disk**. Nothing had been deleted — the commit was merely unreachable. **A `log` that finds nothing is not evidence of loss in a repository that has been hard-reset; only `reflog` and `fsck --lost-found` are.** **(3) TWO GIT REPOSITORIES OVER ONE WORK TREE, AND THE FEATURE SHIPPED HOURS EARLIER CREATED THE CONDITION.** `getUserDataDir()` forks on install mode; the WORK TREE does not. So pointing the app at a folder an existing install already syncs — exactly what v3.31.0's *Use existing folder* invites — produces two independent histories over the same files, neither aware of the other's commits. `setup()` now **ADOPTS** a sibling repo governing the same domains folder when it points at the same remote, writing **not one byte of the work tree** (asserted; only the managed `.gitignore` changes), stores the adoption as `gitDir` rather than recomputing it, and **refuses by name** when the remote differs. Moving the repo beside the domains folder was rejected and the reason is not aesthetic: `.knowledge-git/config` holds the PAT, so *beside* pollutes an unrelated parent for a relocated `domainsPath` and *inside* puts a credential in the sync work tree, one missing ignore rule from being pushed to GitHub. Refuse-only was rejected because it leaves an app that cannot sync at all. **The candidate path is deliberately singular** — `dirname(domainsDir)/.knowledge-git` — because in repo mode `getUserDataDir()` IS the checkout, so a pre-app install's repo is necessarily that sibling; a relocated `domainsPath` is **not covered**, stated as a limit rather than papered over with a directory walk. **(4) A REFUSAL THAT LEAVES ONLY THE DESTRUCTIVE PATH OPEN IS WORSE THAN NO REFUSAL, AND THAT IS THE DEEPER DEFECT.** The connect flow correctly detected divergence, told him *"GitHub has changes you don't have locally"* — and the only door it left open was **Pull an existing wiki**, the overwriting one. Three things now: a new `POST /api/sync/preflight` that **measures** what a checkout would overwrite and returns the count and the file list, writing nothing outside a tempdir (the work tree is fingerprinted and asserted read-only); a new **merge** mode that commits the local folder **FIRST** so the pre-merge bytes stay reachable from `HEAD^1` — that ordering is the whole recoverability property; and `setup(mode:'pull')` refusing unless `confirmOverwrite === true`, checked strictly because `Boolean('false')` is `true`. **`/old`'s connect became safe WITHOUT EDITING THE FROZEN BUNDLE**, because `/old` never sends that flag. **The `update-index --refresh` in the preflight is load-bearing and was measured:** without it a byte-identical file reports as modified, the warning fires on every connect, and people learn to click through it. **`-X ours` ON FIRST CONNECT IS ARGUED AGAINST `pull()`'s `-X theirs`, NOT AROUND IT:** `-X theirs` is the STEADY-STATE rule, where origin is shared truth and the pulling machine is expected to have pushed already; on a FIRST connect the local side has by construction never been pushed anywhere, so preferring origin prefers a revision that provably lacks the user's newest work. `pull()` is untouched. **Disclosed cost:** with no merge base every remote path is an ADD, so a page deleted locally comes back — the fail-safe direction, and `docs/sync.md` says so. **(5) THE COPY WAS STALE ON TWO COUNTS AND NAMED A BUTTON THAT IS NOT THERE.** `friendlyError` said *"Click 'Pull only' first (under Advanced)"* — but `/next` puts those controls at the TOP LEVEL of the configured view, and the connect screen has neither. Every refusal string now names an ACTION and a SCREEN, never a button label, because naming *"Merge — keep both"* would reproduce the identical defect on `/old`, which is frozen and has no merge control. **(6) THE SYNC BADGE WAS LATE, NEVER WRONG, AND IT IS A PARITY GAP.** He saw `1` while 33 files were pending. The badge and the Sync view read the SAME `changesCount` field, so two readers of one field cannot disagree except by staleness. `/next` refreshed on boot, on navigate, on a 60s timer and on the batch queue's exit — **none of which is *an operation that writes files just finished***. `/old` has refreshed from the single ingest's own completion path since **v3.0.1-beta.5**, which is precisely why he experienced the browser app as more prompt. Hooked to `beginDomainWrite`'s release — the chokepoint every file-writing operation in `/next` already takes — firing only when the LAST handle settles, because `git status --porcelain` is repo-wide and a burst has one answer rather than N. Deliberately **not** gated on `document.hidden`: v3.31.0's rule is about polling and network, and this is event-driven, local-only, at most once per completed operation. The interval stays 60s and becomes the safety net for out-of-band changes (Obsidian, MCP, another machine) rather than the mechanism. **v3.31.0's gating was EXONERATED by differential** — both revisions driven through one seam over a visible/hidden/no-document matrix, identical on every visible-window input, with the pre-change SHA pinned as a literal and asserted to be neither HEAD nor textually equal. **(7) THE ACTIVE PROVIDER GOT ITS COLOUR BACK, AND THE OBVIOUS FIX IS THE ONE THE FILE HAD ALREADY REJECTED.** The active key is the one that spends money on ingest and Health, and it was indistinguishable from a merely configured one. `/old` never coloured a per-row word — it had ONE global pill with a `--success` dot, which is the signal being remembered. And `/next` had **already tried colouring the word and reverted**: `settings.css`'s own header records `--success-text` at **3.79-4.05 in light theme**, under the 4.5:1 text floor. So the colour goes on an ICON, which is non-text and needs only 3:1 — measured **10.37 dark / 4.05 light** — and the word stays on `--text`, so colour is never the ONLY signal. **(8) THE MCP BADGE WAS NOT LYING AND WAS NOT A DETECTION BUG.** It read *"Needs re-connect"* directly above a green *"Bridge responds — 20 tools, 6 domains"*. Staleness compares the command Claude Desktop has **SAVED ON DISK** against what would be generated today; the self-test **deliberately never reads that file** and always spawns the freshly-computed command — a v3.6.1 fix, so a failure can never be blamed on a stale config. Both were true, answering different questions, and the surface now says so. **KNOWN AND UNFIXED.** **THE NEW SYNC UI HAS NEVER BEEN RENDERED IN A BROWSER** — agents were forbidden from launching anything after seven crash dialogs reached the maintainer's desktop in one session, so the markup and wiring are covered by source guards and by brain-level behaviour, not by a live click. That is the likeliest place for a rough edge. **An install already in the split state is NOT migrated automatically**, by design: `setup()` does not re-run on a configured install, and silently switching a working install onto a different repo would orphan its commits — the same class of unrequested decision that caused the loss. `getStatus()` reports `splitSyncRepo` (2 stat calls, no subprocess, because it is on the 60s badge path) and the user does Disconnect then Connect. **`getCredentialFiles()`'s 0600 startup sweep is anchored on `getSyncGitDir()` and does not follow an adopted dir** — mitigated by an explicit chmod at adoption, properly closed only by touching `paths.js`. **`/old` still cannot merge**: its connect is now safe, but a user there has no third door and is pointed at the main Sync view. **The preflight downloads the remote's objects into a tempdir and discards them**, so a connect that proceeds pays the download twice — once per install, against an unrecoverable loss class. **Compile-to-Wiki registers no CLIENT-side write gate**, so it is invisible to the cross-view write gate: `routes/compile.js` registers server-side and `POST /api/sync/push` will 409 during a compile while the Sync view's buttons still render enabled. Found in passing, not fixed, and deliberately not asserted as an absence. **`buildRemoteUrl` had ZERO coverage of `setup()` for its entire life** because it could not be pointed at a local bare repo — the function that destroyed the data was untestable offline until this release made `file://` pass through unchanged. **VERIFICATION: 143 -> 146 offline suites, 146 passed / 0 failed; 166 total (146 OFFLINE + 14 LIVE_CI + 6 LIVE_LOCAL).** Roughly 40 mutations across four agents. **Four came back GREEN and all four were chased rather than filed** — a refusal made redundant by git's own backstop, closed by tagging refusals `measured` versus `checkout-refused` and pinning which fired; a null-versus-null equality carried by a fixture that only ever tested one half; a relative-path check satisfied by a path that was not a git dir either; and an attention-tint assertion satisfied by a sibling border. **Five mutations reddened by CRASHING rather than by a named assertion** — the recorded v3.24.1 shape — and every site was guarded. `test-route-write-guards.js`'s mutating-route tripwire fired on the new `/preflight` and was bumped with a written reason rather than exempted. Real `.curator-config.json`, real `.knowledge-git` and real `domains/` fingerprinted and unchanged by every agent; port 3333 never disturbed; no `.app` launched. |

An app in that state shows *"The Curator is damaged and can't be opened. You should move it to the Trash"*, whose only buttons are Move to Trash and Cancel, and for which **no "Open Anyway" ever appears in System Settings**. The only escape is `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine` — a terminal command, i.e. the exact thing this release exists to remove. A valid ad-hoc signature moves it into the ordinary unidentified-developer class: `Sealed Resources version=2 rules=13 files=5563`, verify exit 0, and **ONE** fatal finding with the Codesign Error gone. **THE ORCHESTRATOR'S FIX WAS RIGHT AND HIS PROOF WAS NOT, AND THAT IS THE MORE USEFUL HALF.** He signed a COPY, ran `codesign --verify`, got exit 0 and reported the fix as proven — without ever LAUNCHING it. That flag sat in the config described as *"written now, inert until signing"* — the moment anything signed, it activated and broke the app. **A valid signature and a working app are different claims, and no static check can tell them apart.** Hardened runtime must return when Developer ID signing lands, because notarization requires it; this is a temporary inversion recorded as one, not a setting. **(2) THE PACKAGED APP REPORTED VERSION 0.0.0, AND THE CHANGELOG HAD RECORDED AN INTENT AS AN IMPLEMENTATION — BUT ONLY BY ONE WORD.** `Info.plist` carried `0.0.0` for both keys while the published assets were named `TheCurator-3.30.0-*`, because they were **renamed by hand at upload**. Staleness is checked BEFORE the gate, so even an opt-in run will not launch the bundle most likely to crash. **(8) TWO TIMERS RAN BEHIND A HIDDEN WINDOW, ONE OF THEM A `git fetch` TO GITHUB EVERY TEN MINUTES.** Invisible in a browser tab because people close tabs; a permanent background network call in an app that never closes. Chromium's background throttling does **not** help — its clamp is far shorter than 60s and 10min — so only a `document.hidden` check does. That is the single most load-bearing architectural fact about the shell, these docs are model-read, and an agent trusting it would build IPC for what is a plain module registry. | `v3.29.0` | **Pushing to `main` WAS the deploy, and CI was a report card that arrived afterwards. Verified against the GitHub API: `main` has **NO branch protection and NO rulesets** (`{"message":"Branch not protected","status":"404"}` and an empty array). The updater runs `git fetch origin main` + `git reset --hard origin/main`, so **whatever lands on `main` reaches every user on their next update check, green or red**, and nothing could stop it. There was also no tag step, which `electron-updater` will need. **THE GATE, agreed with the maintainer, who runs the same shape on another production app of his:** `release/vX.Y.Z` -> push -> CI (~8 min, measured: v3.28.0 took 8m16s) -> green? -> **fast-forward `main`**. This gives a real gate without a PR the solo maintainer would review on himself. **`--ff-only` is structural, not stylistic** — `main` must be byte-for-byte the SHA CI ran on, and a non-`--ff-only` merge is refused by the same guard that refuses force-push. **The tag is created AFTER the merge**, on the commit `main` points at, so a tag can never name a commit that failed CI. **Red OR UNKNOWN leaves `main` untouched** — unknown fails CLOSED — and the release branch is left in place so the fix is a commit on top; `release-branch-exists` refuses a re-cut that would bury the failure. **THE DANGER THAT WOULD HAVE MADE THE GATE PASS ON NOTHING WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED:** if `.github/workflows/test.yml` filtered `on: push:` by branch, a release branch would get NO run and the gate would silently succeed against nothing. It does not — the trigger is a bare `push:` with only `paths-ignore`, which GitHub applies to every branch. But that ABSENCE was load-bearing and unasserted, so `release.js` now refuses with `ci-not-reachable` if a `branches:` or `branches-ignore:` key ever appears there, the suite asserts it against the REAL file, and `test.yml` says so in its own text. **The gate is the `offline` job only, and that is a decision rather than a gap:** `live` is gated to push-`main` plus dispatch, spends real money, runs up to 20 minutes and is deliberately flake-tolerant through `ci-flake.js` — a required check designed to tolerate its own transient failures is the wrong thing to block a green tree on. It still runs post-merge, and **a SKIPPED job is named in the output**, so "green" never quietly means "green on the two checks that happened to run". **`scripts/release.js` — 25 REFUSALS, and the interesting ones are not the obvious ones:** `dirty-tree` permits exactly the five release-metadata files and nothing else; `lock-diff-too-large` catches npm re-resolving the tree, which is v3.24.1's recorded lesson; `changelog-row-missing` requires a FULL row, because an index line is a pointer and does not count; `version-fields-disagree` checks all four fields including BOTH lock fields; `leanness-cap-exceeded` **warns AT the cap** rather than only failing past it, so nobody discovers mid-release that the next one must archive; and `commit-message-refused` checks the `.githooks/commit-msg` shapes UP FRONT rather than after the work. One override exists, `--skip-tests`, and it is **permanently recorded in the annotated tag message** — and it does not bypass the CI gate, because nothing does. **BRANCH PROTECTION: FORCE-PUSH AND DELETION ONLY, NO REQUIRED CHECK — and the orchestrator's argument for that was CORRECTED while the conclusion held.** The claim that a required check would be flaky is **wrong**: `ci-flake.js` tolerates LIVE failures, which cannot be a PR check anyway, while the offline job is deterministic and never retried. The real case is latency and ceremony — a required check cannot gate a direct push at all, so it buys a PR the maintainer reviews on himself, while the gate already runs BEFORE the push rather than after. **Force-push is the genuinely irrecoverable one precisely BECAUSE the updater hard-resets to `origin/main`**, so rewritten history propagates to every machine. The exact `gh` commands are in `CONTRIBUTING.md`, targeting `refs/heads/main` only so `release/*` deletion still works, with the required-check recipe recorded beside it in case he changes his mind. **No GitHub setting was changed** — that is the maintainer's call. **THE GATE'S HONEST LIMIT IS STATED IN `CONTRIBUTING.md`:** it lives in a script, so a bare `git push` still bypasses it. This is a solo maintainer protecting himself from his own hurry, not a repo defending against a hostile committer. **(2) `releaseChannel` SHIPS WITH `stable` AS THE ONLY VALID VALUE, AND THE REFUSAL TO SHIP `beta` IS THE FINDING.** `git ls-remote --heads origin` returns `main`, four `release/*` snapshots and five dated topic branches — **there is no pre-release ref.** And shipping `beta` anyway would break the updater for anyone who selected it, for a reason nobody had noticed: **`install.sh` line 126 uses `git clone --depth 1`, which implies `--single-branch`**, so a real user's install has the refspec `+refs/heads/main:refs/remotes/origin/main` and nothing else. Measured against a throwaway origin that DID have a beta branch: `git fetch origin beta` prints `* branch beta -> FETCH_HEAD` and **looks fine**, then `git reset --hard origin/beta` dies with `fatal: ambiguous argument`. **The fetch succeeds and the reset kills the update.** An explicit refspec was measured working and deliberately NOT written, because nothing can reach it today; the measurement lives at the resolver and a guard fires the day someone adds a second channel. Absent or unrecognised resolves to `stable` — the same fail-safe asymmetry as `paths.js`'s install-mode detection — and the unknown name is proven to reach no git command and no URL. Equivalence with today's behaviour was proved **behaviourally rather than by diff**, because substituting a variable for a literal is invisible to `git diff -w`: driving HEAD's handlers and the new ones through the same seam gives all 7 update commands identical and in order, both URLs identical, the update body identical and the check body identical plus two additive fields. **THE REVERT PATH WAS VERIFIED BEFORE IT WAS WRITTEN**, because this project has a recorded history of false revert promises — v3.9.1 found *"anything can be reverted from the Sync tab"* at EIGHT sites for a feature that has never existed. On a standard install `git checkout v3.24.2` **FAILS**: there are no tags. What works end to end is a shallow tag fetch, then checkout, then the app's own update commands to return from detached HEAD. **No version number appears in the copy**, because the newest tag on origin is four releases behind the running version, so *"go back to the previous version"* would have been false. **(3) THE APP HAS ITS OWN LOG FILE, AND THE PATH DELIBERATELY DOES NOT FORK ON INSTALL MODE.** `/tmp/the-curator.log` existed ONLY because `build-app.sh` and `install.sh` append a shell redirect — there was no file logger in `src/` at all, yet `server.js`'s own comments treated that file as owned and two user-facing docs told people to read it. The pivot deletes the AppleScript and would therefore have deleted the entire support story. New `src/brain/logger.js` writes to `~/Library/Logs/The Curator/` via a new `getLogsDir()` in `paths.js`, which **always** resolves there in both install modes — argued rather than assumed: a repo checkout is a live git working tree, and this project has **twice** shipped machine-local operational files into a tracked or synced tree by mistake (`.DS_Store` in v3.0.16, `.write-lock` in v3.0.15). A log is diagnostic exhaust, not user data. Bounded at 5 MB with single-generation rotation (~10 MB ceiling); a second generation was considered and rejected because this log answers *what just happened*, not an audit trail. **A failing write can never fail a request** — two nested try/catch layers, and the mutation proving it is precise: removing the OUTER layer reds the *directory-exists-but-unwritable* case while the *directory-cannot-be-created* case **correctly stays green**, because the inner layer owns it. Every line passes the existing `scrubPaths` and then a new `scrubSecrets` whose patterns are kept in sync with **this repo's own pre-commit hook** — found only after the hook REFUSED the agent's first commit, because the tooling docs point at `.git/hooks/` while this repo sets `core.hooksPath` to `.githooks`. Ten synthetic credential fixtures are proven redacted both from the scrubber and from the file the real write path produces. **A GAP IN THE AGENT'S OWN TEST, CAUGHT BY MUTATION:** the PEM fixture's *does-it-reach-disk* assertion stayed green under a scrubbing-disabled mutation, because line-flattening meant the raw multi-line secret never matched verbatim regardless — the assertion could not have failed. Per-request logging is deliberately excluded: the frontend polls several endpoints and an access line per GET turns a support file into noise within a day. **(4) THE DOCS NOW NAME BOTH LOGS, BECAUSE THEY ANSWER DIFFERENT QUESTIONS.** `mac-app.md` and `user-guide.md` pointed at `/tmp/the-curator.log`. A straight swap would have been wrong: the app's own log carries startup facts, provider and model, update outcomes and caught errors — but a failure BEFORE the server starts never reaches it, because nothing exists yet to write it. That case still needs the launcher's raw stdout capture, and the docs now say which is which. **A COMMAND THAT DOES NOT WORK WAS SHIPPED INTO A TROUBLESHOOTING SECTION AND CAUGHT BEFORE COMMIT:** `cat "~/Library/Logs/..."` — **a tilde inside double quotes is not expanded** — verified failing and corrected to `$HOME`. **(5) THE STANDING BRIEF WAS UPDATED AT THE MAINTAINER'S REQUEST**, which is a deviation worth recording: `state/project.md` is tier 1, hand-authored, and **no MCP tool writes it by design** — that is the single-writer property the whole sync-safety argument rests on. He asked for it directly, so it was edited directly, and the firm decision governing it was AMENDED rather than deleted: no tool may ever write it, and direct editing is allowed only when the project owner asks in that session, never on an agent's initiative. Three things in it were stale — the current phase still said *"fine-tuning the browser app"*; the Roadmap said the Mac app was *"Not started; do not begin it while the browser app is still being fine-tuned"*, wrong on both halves; and *"the changelog table IS this project's memory"* did not mention that it had been SPLIT. The release gate is now written into the brief as the procedure agents must follow, because *"a push is not a finished release"* was exactly the thing that varied between sessions. **(6) `CLAUDE.md` STAYS LEAN.** `v3.24.1` moved to the archive byte-identically (sha256 `47681a09…`, 15,398 chars, verified before and after with a flipped-character control), keeping the file at the ratchet's cap rather than exceeding it. **KNOWN AND UNFIXED.** **THE GATE HAS NEVER BEEN RUN END TO END AGAINST A REAL REMOTE** — the agent that built it was forbidden from pushing, tagging or changing a setting, so its production path was exercised only as far as `wrong-branch`. This release is its first real use, and that is stated rather than implied. The suite drives canned `git`/`npm`/`gh` output, so **real tool behaviour is not proven**; `--ff-only` landing on the CI SHA is asserted by re-reading `rev-parse HEAD` and refusing on mismatch, but only a real repo proves the merge. New `src/brain/compile-estimate.js` plus a free, local, zero-network `GET /api/compile/estimate`. **THE INPUT HALF IS EXACT, NOT A PROXY:** unlike the batch-ingest estimator, which sees file metadata and must guess extracted length, the conversation is already on disk — so the estimator runs the **REAL** `buildCompilePrompt` over the real conversation, real domain schema and real slug inventory and measures the result character by character. That captures the driver the feature exists to surface: the same 4-turn conversation measures **5,740 prompt chars on a fresh domain and 12,431 on one holding 180 pages**. No multiplier anywhere in the file. **THE OUTPUT HALF IS A RANGE BECAUSE IT CANNOT HONESTLY BE A NUMBER, AND THAT WAS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.** Ten paid calls on `gemini-2.5-flash-lite`, **$0.009462** total: input tokenisation is **DETERMINISTIC** — 3,767 tokens on three byte-identical replicates, to the token — while **output is not**: 2,456 / 2,145 / 1,977 tokens and 19 / 18 / 18 pages from identical input, **±11%**. Output also **SATURATES**: doubling the transcript 3,219 -> 6,510 chars moved pages 21 -> 21 and output 3,189 -> 3,036. The ladder's summary-only floor measured 257 and 523 tokens, which anchors `outputLow`. **THE ESTIMATE AND THE COMPILE CANNOT DISAGREE, BY CONSTRUCTION:** `compileConversation`'s three pre-spend decisions moved into an exported `precheckCompile` that both sides call, and the suite compares their refusal strings byte for byte **IN BOTH DIRECTIONS** — refusal-agreement alone is satisfiable by an estimator that refuses everything, so a conversation the estimate calls compilable must be one the compile actually reaches its paid call on. **A FACT AND ITS ABSENCE ARE NOT COLLAPSED**, which is v3.15.0's recorded defect: three distinct `costUnknown` codes (`no-provider` / `free-model` / `no-price`), three different sentences, `usdLow`/`usdHigh` **null** and never a truthy zero on the money path — *"this costs nothing"* and *"we cannot tell you what this costs"* never share wording. **CANCEL IS PROVEN TO SPEND NOTHING** rather than assumed: offline, the real `startCompile` driven against a `confirmThen` that never confirms gives 0 compiles, 0 POSTs, 1 free GET, with a positive control that confirming gives exactly 1; in a real browser, Cancel issued **one GET, zero POSTs**, wrote **zero summaries** and left `log.md` empty, while Confirm wrote 10 pages plus the index. **MEASURED ERROR, STATED RATHER THAN IMPLIED:** two further compiles run through the shipped estimator on a 70-page domain quoted $0.000272-0.001211 against a billed **$0.000413**, and $0.000254-0.000848 against **$0.000386** — both bracketed on input tokens, output tokens and dollars independently, both in the lower fifth, which is the intended direction because over-quoting is the safe way to be wrong about money. **Eleven real compiles have now landed inside the quoted range, every one on ONE model** — stated as the limit rather than presented as calibration. **AN IMPROVEMENT WAS DECLINED AND THE REASONING WRITTEN IN-FILE:** the residual error is COMPOSITIONAL, not random — ~15% HIGH on a fresh domain (prose is about 4.07 chars/token) and ~10% LOW on a mature one (slug inventories measure 2.5-2.7, exactly what `docs/ingestion-pipeline.md` derives for slug paths). A two-rate model would be tighter and was **deliberately not built**: a second hand-tuned constant fitted to seven points, on the money path, replacing one shared constant already cross-checked by the batch estimator. **`CHARS_PER_TOKEN` was EXPORTED from `ingest-queue.js` rather than copied** — two cost surfaces reading two copies of a money constant is the v3.2.0 CRITICAL shape — and the one-word change outside the stated file list was flagged rather than buried. The confirm reuses `shared/confirm.js` and `.cfd-*` rather than becoming a sixth modal, days after v3.26.0 standardised the five. **M15 CAME BACK GREEN AND WAS CHASED:** deleting the stale-target check in `onConfirm` — so that confirming after navigating away compiles the **WRONG conversation** — was untested; §7 now moves the conversation while the dialog is open, and M15 reds. Two further mutations CRASHED the suite before its naming sections ran, the v3.24.1 shape, closed by making the guards **stop** rather than merely report. New `test-next-compile-estimate.js`, **203 assertions**; `test-next-chat-compile.js` 202 -> **214**. `docs/api-reference.md` gained the whole `/api/compile` section — **both** endpoints, since `POST /conversation` had never been documented at all. **KNOWN AND UNFIXED.** **`loading -> settled` IS NOT COVERED AND CANNOT BE IN CSS ALONE:** every settled surface (`.ing-result`, `.chat-answer`, the ring) is re-emitted by `innerHTML` replacement on unrelated interactions, so a class-keyed animation re-fires on every repaint. It needs a JS class, and was reported rather than bodged. **A DEAD DECLARATION FOUND AND LEFT:** `progress-ring.js` re-emits its whole SVG per progress event, so `.pring-fill` / `.pring-arc`'s `transition: … var(--t-layout)` can **NEVER RUN** — the module's own header explains the negative-`animation-delay` hack for exactly that reason and did not apply it two rules up. JS fix, outside a CSS pass. **12 of 200 button-role elements still have no press**, each with a stated reason (link-shaped text, menu options, the drop zone, and one covered by its ancestor). Perceptibility is **not measured offline** — which is precisely how `translateY(0.5px)` passed for four releases — and cascade is resolved only for same-file ties. **The estimator is validated on ONE model's tokenizer**; §9 is a REGRESSION guard on numbers measured once, not a calibration claim. **`/old` still POSTs a compile with no estimate at all** — the frozen bundle, gap stated rather than closed. **`scripts/visual/browser.js`'s `close()` STILL leaks Chrome** — an eight-process tree survived a clean exit this release and was reaped by its unique profile path; `test-visual-regression.js` depends on that same path and this remains unfixed across three releases. **CLAUDE.md NOW CARRIES NINE FULL ROWS AGAINST AN INTENDED FIVE** — v3.24.2 asked for `v3.22.0` to be archived and v3.25.0, v3.26.0 and this release have each declined to do it, so the debt has now grown four releases running and the file is drifting back toward the cost v3.24.2 measured and cut. So 0.68 was never drift; **the real defect was LIGHT**, split `0.42` on two surfaces and `0.5` on three, and nobody had looked at it because the audit was comparing the wrong number. And *"`--radius-xl` is used once tree-wide and not on a modal"* is FALSE as a criticism: the prototype sets `.chat-composer` to `border-radius: 14px` explicitly — **the composer was right and the modals were wrong.** **THE MECHANISM IS THE FINDING.** `--scrim` was an **UNDEFINED** custom property — referenced exactly once, with a hex fallback, defined nowhere — and `test-css-tokens.js` BASELINED that count at 1. So every surface after the first was instructed, *in its own comment*, to inline the literal rather than add a second reference; four stylesheets cite that assertion by name. **A GUARD AGAINST AN UNDEFINED NAME BECAME THE REASON FIVE FILES EACH HELD A PRIVATE COPY**, and they drifted exactly as duplication does. `--modal-scrim` is now a real definition and the `/next` baseline shrinks **1 -> 0** rather than growing. **THE FIX MADE ONE NUMBER WORSE AND THE BROWSER CAUGHT IT:** unifying light on the prototype's `0.42` degraded three modals from **3.55 to 2.80**, under WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 — reversed to `0.5` as a deliberate, measured deviation recorded in `shell.css`, the same shape as v3.25.0's checkbox where the bundle's own `--border-strong` failed a floor. Net: two surfaces up, three unchanged, none degraded. **DARK STAYS UNDER THE FLOOR AND NO SCRIM VALUE FIXES IT** — card-vs-scrim is 1.07 and the border 1.29, because the card is the LIGHT thing there; separation is carried by `--shadow-lg` and the blur, which is what the system's elevation guidance says. Recorded, not papered over. Radius 10 -> **14px** on the four true modals; `backdrop-filter: blur(6px)` added to the confirm dialog and chat-browse, which had none; `.sbw-card`'s hardcoded DARK shadow was rendering verbatim in light theme — the same class as v3.25.0's Sync rail badge. `tokens/shape.css` byte-frozen and confirmed absent from the diff. Text inside the cards: **0 of 66 nodes under 4.5** in either theme. **TWO ACCESSIBILITY FINDINGS REPORTED RATHER THAN FIXED:** `.reader-panel` declares `aria-modal="true"` with **no focus trap and no focus return**, while the rail and sidebar deliberately stay live — so the attribute is arguably false either way, and resolving it (add a trap, or drop `aria-modal`) is a design decision, not a conformance fix; and `.chat-browse` dismisses on `click` where every other modal uses `mousedown` plus an identity check. The destructive confirm was deliberately NOT made easier to dismiss. **(2) DISABLED, AND THREE CONFLICTING TALLIES WERE RECONCILED RATHER THAN PICKED BETWEEN.** The v3.24.2 audit said five values with 4 of 15 sites correct; a re-scan returned different numbers; v3.25.0 recorded **nine**. All three were counting different things, and the resolution is scope: **nine is the right count of `opacity:` declarations tree-wide** — scrim fades, the progress ring's SVG arcs, a keyframe, a chip counter, a *handled* row — while **five is the right count of ways DISABLED is implemented**, across **17 rules of which 6 were already correct**. The whole-tree scan reproduces the nine exactly, which is what identifies the discrepancy as scope and not error. **THE LIVE BUG NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR IS A COMPOUND.** `.provider-row-unavailable { opacity: 0.55 }` WRAPPED a disabled `.btn` at `0.45`. Nested opacities MULTIPLY: **effective 0.2475, measured 2.05 dark / 1.73 light, on the credentials screen.** Neither value was wrong on its own — the NESTING was, which is precisely the failure mode a spread of ad-hoc opacities produces and precisely what no per-rule scan can see. Fixed by moving the container's de-emphasis to a COLOUR token, which composites once and cannot multiply: **0.45 effective, 4.05 / 2.97** — the row is simultaneously dimmer and more legible. **A SECOND FINDING ABOUT THE BUNDLE: IT SPECIFIES DISABLED TWICE AND THE TWO DISAGREE.** `readme.md`'s *Interaction states* say `opacity: 0.45`, `cursor: not-allowed`, **NO COLOUR CHANGE**, and `Button.jsx`, `IconButton.jsx`, `Checkbox.jsx` and `primitives.jsx` each write that literal inline — while `guidelines/colors-text.card.html` captions `--text-faint` *"disabled"*. **The components win, and the argument matters:** the caption sits on a SWATCH that no component implements; *no colour change* is the point, because one grey flattens a disabled danger button and a disabled primary into the same control; and a text colour cannot dim a BORDER, a FILL or an ICON, which a disabled button is all three of. So `--text-faint` is UNTOUCHED and `test-next-contrast-ratchet.js`'s §4/§4a/§7 control — which needs a genuinely failing token to fire on — still works. `--opacity-disabled` is DERIVED and lives in `shell.css` rather than `tokens/`, because `base.css` and `shape.css` are byte-frozen and `color.css` is for colour. **CURSOR WINS OVER `pointer-events: none` AT ALL THREE SITES, PROVEN BY A CONTROLLED A/B ON THE SAME ELEMENT IN THE SAME POSITION:** shipped, `elementFromPoint` at a disabled `.btn`'s centre returns THE BUTTON; with the suppression restored it falls through to `main-inner` / `provider-row`. **`getComputedStyle` reports `not-allowed` in BOTH states** — only hit-testing can tell them apart, which is why the first probe, which compared computed styles, reported no difference. Every element those rules reach is a real `<button disabled>` the UA already refuses and already removes from the tab order. And `views/sync.css` proves the suppression was accidental: the rule DIRECTLY BELOW it cancels a `--danger-text` hover on the disabled Disconnect and **could never have matched** while pointer-events was `none` — removing the suppression made an already-written, already-correct rule live for the first time. **ONE ORCHESTRATOR FRAMING WAS A RELEASE OUT OF DATE AND IS CORRECTED:** v3.23.0's *"a `title=` on a disabled control is unreachable"* class is **already structurally fixed** — every cross-write refusal now renders as a visible sibling through `renderStatus`, and `settings.js` carries a comment explaining it removed a tooltip for exactly that reason. Nothing informational was trapped. **DELIBERATELY NOT UNIFIED:** `chat.css`'s 0.45 RESTING checkbox dim (v3.25.0 refused it and the refusal stands — it is a resting state, not a disabled one), `.dm-sem-handled` (a DONE state), `.dm-chip-count`, the progress-ring arcs, and `motion.css`'s pulse. **(3) THE INSTALL-MODE SEAM — PHASE 2 OF THE NATIVE PLAN, SHIPPED WHILE ITS OWN NEW ARM IS PROVABLY DEAD CODE.** `src/brain/install-mode.js` exports `getInstallMode()` -> `'repo' | 'bundle'` plus a frozen capability record (`canSelfUpdateViaGit`, `canRunNpmInstall`, `canRebuildAppleScriptApp`, `canWriteBesideCode`, `mcpLaunchStyle`, `restartStyle`), and `POST /api/config/update` + `GET /api/config/update-check` fork on `caps.canSelfUpdateViaGit` rather than on the install FORM. **THE ORCHESTRATOR'S JUSTIFICATION WAS CORRECTED WHILE THE DECISION SURVIVED:** today all four booleans are **PERFECTLY CORRELATED** with `mode === 'repo'` and carry **zero information** over the mode — the value is naming distinct questions and forcing a third mode to be written into ONE table, not present-tense expressiveness. The enforceable half became a guard: **no route may branch on the mode** (13 route files asserted, three controls). `mcpLaunchStyle` and `restartStyle` have **no branch behind them** and are the unwired-field shape this repo has an allergy to; they were kept and ARGUED rather than assumed, on the `getUserDataDirState()` precedent. **THE REPO ARM IS PROVEN BYTE-EQUIVALENT, WHICH IS THE WHOLE SAFETY CLAIM:** `git show -w` deletes **exactly five lines**, all route-registration wrappers plus one docblock header, and **ZERO lines of either body**; the extracted bodies are sha256-identical before and after (`update-check` `0aa4035d`, 47 lines; `update` `4dfe1e4c`, 108 lines). The command sequence is pinned as literals TRANSCRIBED INTO THE SUITE rather than read back from the route, unchanged and in order. The bundle arm returns 501 with a named capability and **runs zero subprocesses** — asserted, not assumed. **THE SEMVER FIX, AND ITS OWN FIRST DRAFT WAS WRONG.** `versionDiffers = latest !== current` is a plain inequality true in BOTH directions, so a local build AHEAD of remote reported an update whose application is a `git reset --hard` DOWNGRADE. A **24-cell A/B against the pre-change expression refuted the obvious fix**: `compareSemver` returns `0` for **UNCOMPARABLE** as well as equal, so `cmp < 0` alone moved **12** cells rather than 6 — `nightly` against `3.25.0`, and `3.0.1-beta.27` against `3.0.1`, flipped from *update offered* to *no update*, the HARMFUL direction. Uncomparable now falls back to the original string inequality, so **exactly the six local-ahead cells change**. `commitsDiffer` is SUBORDINATED rather than OR-ed, because a local-ahead checkout has differing commits by construction and `|| commitsDiffer` would have left the defect fully intact in the one state that triggers it. **`/next` is unchanged in every case** — `classifyUpdate()` runs its own local-ahead arm BEFORE reading `updateAvailable`, so there is no double-guard and no contradiction, and the suite asserts the two `compareSemver` implementations agree on 10 pairs. **`/old` DOES change, and that is the fix landing:** `app.js:6444` reads `data.updateAvailable` unguarded, so in the local-ahead state it previously offered a button that downgrades the user's checkout. `/old` was not edited. **NEW `GET /api/write-status`** exposes `hasActiveWrites()` — which existed, was tested, and was reachable only as an internal route guard — as `{ok, safeToQuit, activeWrites, updateInProgress, operations[], operationsTotal}` behind an explicit allow-list capped at 50 with the TRUE total alongside. Registered as a plain GET, NOT behind `guardConcurrent`, because a 409 would fire exactly when something is asking whether a write is in progress; on a registry throw it answers 200 with `safeToQuit: null`, because a quit handler that cannot get an answer must be TOLD so rather than handed an exception it reads as *busy*. Nothing consumes it in repo mode; Electron's `before-quit` will. **A GIT PREFLIGHT AND A CONFIDENTLY WRONG DIAGNOSIS CORRECTED:** `friendlyError` tested `msg.includes('not found')` as a bare substring, so `git: command not found` rendered as **"Repository not found. Check the URL — it must be a private repo you own."** A new branch above the catch-all covers `command not found` / `git: not found` / `spawn git ENOENT` / the Windows form, and the auth and real-repo-not-found branches below still fire. **M6 STAYED GREEN AND WAS CHASED:** restoring the original semver bug passed, because §7 tested the FUNCTION and nothing tested the CALL SITE; a wire-level local-ahead assertion now reds it. And `test-route-write-guards.js` could no longer see `POST /update`'s `hasActiveWrites` guard once registration delegated — fixed by making the scan FOLLOW the delegation, **not** by widening its window or exempting the route, because a scan that cannot follow the move would also go green on the guard's DELETION. 15 mutations, all behavioural. **(4) THE DESIGN SYSTEM IS NOW MIRRORED LOCALLY AND GITIGNORED.** The bundle is copied to `the_curator_design_system/` at the repo root so agents can read the system without a personal filesystem path appearing in a committed file — **gitignored, because the repository is public and this is the maintainer's brand work.** New `docs/design-system-source.md` records the thing that would otherwise be rediscovered: **there are three copies and exactly one master, and the master is not in this repo.** The mirror is READ-ONLY; editing it changes nothing that ships. It also records the two deliberate divergences so a future conformance audit does not "fix" them backwards — v3.25.0's text ramp, and the checkbox border that fails 3:1 **as the bundle specifies it**. **KNOWN AND UNFIXED.** **MOTION IS MEASURED, PROPOSED AND NOT APPLIED** — 244 control instances, 46 covered by an `:active` rule and ALL 46 by the single `.btn` rule, so **81% of controls do nothing on press**; the shipping press is `translateY(0.5px)` measured live at **0.4716px**, sub-pixel at 1x, and the system specifies press as THREE simultaneous changes of which the app adopted ONE. `--accent-active` still has ZERO consumers tree-wide. It was held back deliberately because it collides with the same CSS files three agents held this release. **Compile to Wiki still spends money with no estimate and no confirm**, and no estimate endpoint exists to call. **`scripts/build-app.sh` still ends in an ad-hoc `codesign --force --deep --sign -` and `xattr -rd com.apple.quarantine`** — signature-destroying in a real bundle, load-bearing for repo mode today; `canRebuildAppleScriptApp` names the hazard and the packaging release must act on it. **The bundle arm never runs end-to-end**, because no bundle exists; the four capability booleans are never independently measured; §4's mode-branch scan is TEXTUAL, so a computed `caps[someVar]` fork is invisible and an aliased `paths.js` import defeats it; and §6 pins command strings and order but **NOT** exec options, so `cwd` / `env` / `timeout` could change silently. **The design-system text-ramp patch has still NOT been applied to the master**, so `tokens/color.css` intentionally differs from it. **`scripts/visual/browser.js`'s `close()` still does not reliably reap Chrome.** **CLAUDE.md now carries EIGHT full rows against an intended five** — v3.24.2 asked for `v3.22.0` to be archived, v3.25.0 did not do it, and neither did this release, so the debt has grown three releases running and no automated guard proves the archive's rows plus this file's rows still equal the original set. **PROCESS: THE SUITE-COUNT COLLISION HAPPENED FIVE TIMES IN ONE SESSION**, twice as real merge conflicts and once in `shell.css` where two agents added different token blocks at the same point — every parallel agent measures from its own worktree base, and two of them told the orchestrator his baseline was wrong when it was theirs that was stale. Adjudicated every time by `check-doc-suite-counts.js` against `run-tests.js`, never by arithmetic. **VERIFICATION: 125 -> 128 offline suites, 128 passed / 0 failed; 148 total (128 OFFLINE + 14 LIVE_CI + 6 LIVE_LOCAL).** Every contrast figure came from a real browser composited after cascade with controls stated (identical pair 1.00, black-on-white 21.00) and a theme-settle control confirming the two reads were genuinely distinct, after v3.25.0 recorded a light pass that was silently a second dark reading. Three false positives of the v3.24.2 comment-stripping class occurred and were fixed AT SOURCE with positive controls kept, including one where a new comment quoted the very values it removed. `git diff package.json` and `package-lock.json` are 0 lines for every browser harness. Real `.curator-config.json` sha256 unchanged, real `domains/` untouched, and the maintainer's live app on port 3333 never disturbed. |
| `v3.25.0` | **The text ramp had already collapsed to two levels, and the app had spent releases routing around the broken rung rather than fixing it.** Reported from real use against a screenshot: dark-mode text should be *"a little bit lighter... not full white, but a little bit more white"*, light-mode text *"a little bit too light on the light background"*, and — the constraint that shaped everything — *"we cannot break the design system, we must improve it."* **THE DIAGNOSIS WAS HALF RIGHT AND THE CORRECTION IS THE VALUABLE HALF.** The LIGHT complaint is numerically correct (light body 5.85-7.26 against dark's 7.29-8.71). The DARK complaint is right about FEEL but dark **never fails AA**, so it is a comfort fix and is recorded as one. And *"compressed at the dim end"* is **FALSE**: measured L* steps in dark are 24.5/19.9/17.4 — the dim end is the **WIDEST**-spaced part. The largest gap is **TITLE -> BODY**, and light is worse at 30.1; body carries **199 of 357 rendered elements (56%)** and sits on the far side of it. **THE REAL FINDING IS STRUCTURAL:** `--text-3` FAILED in both themes (4.15-4.38 dark, 3.87-4.14 light) and rendered on **4 of 357** elements; `--text-faint` rendered on **ZERO** as text, its only consumers a box-shadow ring and a dot; `--text-2` carried **319** CSS consumers to `--text-3`'s 52. `shell.css` said so in its own words — `.cur-eyebrow` overridden from `--text-3` to `--text-2` *"rather than at source"* because `base.css` is byte-frozen. **Every such rescue flattened the ramp by one more role, and that is the MECHANISM by which "several shades of white" became two.** **THE FIX IS THE RETIREMENT, NOT THE SIX HEX VALUES.** `--text` is deliberately UNTOUCHED (already L* 93.9, not white — exactly the *more white but not full white* asked for); `--text-2/-3/-faint` move, and the `.cur-eyebrow` override — **the ONLY rule in the tree overriding the byte-frozen design system** — is RETIRED. Measured effect: `--text-3` text elements **4 -> 18**, `--text-2` **208 -> 194**; the fourteen eyebrows moved back to rung 3. Before, eyebrows and body were BOTH `--text-2` at ~8.3 — no hierarchy at all; after, body ~10.5 and eyebrows ~6.2. **THE RAMP WENT FROM TWO USABLE LEVELS BACK TO THREE.** Three other overrides were KEPT with reasons written in-file: `.empty-card .empty-body`, `.sidebar-hint` and `.sidebar-note` are not overrides of the SYSTEM but app declarations, and they carry the only sentence on an otherwise empty screen — second level is right for that on its own merits, while an eyebrow is a LABEL whose role IS the third level. On `--surface`: `--text-2` 8.34->10.54 dark / 7.26->9.33 light, `--text-3` 4.27->6.16 / 4.14->5.60, `--text-faint` 2.26->3.47 / 2.34->3.61. **TWO ORCHESTRATOR FIGURES DID NOT SURVIVE MEASUREMENT AND ARE CORRECTED RATHER THAN REPEATED.** *"Steps become 16.7/17.1/16.3 — even"* is true of **DARK ONLY**; light is **23.3/13.8/12.3** and cannot be made even without moving `--text`, which is frozen — recorded in the token file rather than implied away. And *"live failures dark 11->7, light 19->9"* **DID NOT REPRODUCE**: a controlled A/B over 7 views x 2 themes and 714 text elements gives **dark 6->2, light 7->3, total 13->5, 0 added**. The old figure came from diffing the committed visual baseline, last recorded at **v3.23.0 — four releases stale**; that file-level delta reads 175->5, and ~162 of those were fixed by v3.23.1-v3.24.2, not here. `contrastTotal` per view is identical across both runs (207 in settings\|light), so it is staleness and not lost coverage. Both baselines re-recorded. **A NON-TEXT FAILURE NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR:** `.mem-row-mark-off`'s 1.5px inset ring measured **2.00 dark / 2.06 light** against WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 and now passes at **3.07/3.19** — thin, and measured against the SELECTED-ROW surface, which is harsher than `--surface`. Zero non-text uses degrade. **THE GUARD ENCODED THE BUG AS AN INVARIANT AND HAD TO BE RE-BASED, NOT WEAKENED.** `test-next-contrast-ratchet.js` listed `--text-3` in `BELOW_TEXT_FLOOR`, asserted *"`--text-3` FAILS the floor"*, justified survivors at *"4.27/4.14"*, and its **§4 control planted a `--text-3` rule and expected the detector to FIRE** — a control that COLLAPSES the moment the token clears 4.5. `--text-faint` still fails 4.5 at 3.47, so **it carries the re-based control** and the detector keeps a real failing token to fire on. New **§4a cross-checks the hand-written list against the arithmetic IN BOTH DIRECTIONS** — every listed token must genuinely measure under 4.5, `--text-3` must be absent PAIRED with the measurement justifying it, and at least one token must fail in BOTH themes so the control is not a one-theme edge case. That converts a list which could silently keep banning a fixed token into one that cannot drift from measurement — the exact failure mode that had the app overriding `--text-3` app-wide. **Six assertions INVERTED, not deleted**, each carrying its old text and why it flipped, including §7's planted control (same input string, opposite expected verdict — the clearest single statement in the suite that the ramp moved). 79 -> **92** assertions. Five mutations, all RED behaviourally. **(2) SEVEN RAW OS CHECKBOXES, NOT SIX — AND THE DESIGN SYSTEM'S OWN CHECKBOX FAILS WCAG.** The seventh is `shared-brain-wizard.js`'s `renderDomainCheckboxes`, built with `document.createElement` + `cb.type = 'checkbox'` — **invisible to any scan that reads markup strings**, which is why six was the reported count. The system DOES specify the component (`components/forms/Checkbox.jsx`, whose notes call it *"the pattern used for every consent gate"* — the site that drove the work), and it was transcribed faithfully: 16x16, `--radius-xs`, `--surface-inset` fill, `--accent` when checked, the 24-viewBox polyline at stroke-width 3.2. **ONE DELIBERATE DEVIATION, AND IT IS A FINDING ABOUT THE SYSTEM:** the specified unchecked border `--border-strong` measures **1.59 dark / 1.64 light** against the 3:1 non-text floor, on the ONE boundary carrying the whole control — the `--surface-inset` fill contributes nothing at 1.01/1.07. Changed to `--text-3`: **4.27/4.14**. No other border token was touched. **A REAL `<input type="checkbox">` IS KEPT** — `appearance: none`, never a `div role="checkbox"` — so the accessible element stays the painted box, `base.css`'s global `:focus-visible` ring applies, and **no `box-shadow` is declared anywhere in the file**, per v3.24.2's rule that a shadow here fights that ring. Verified through Chrome's own input pipeline rather than synthetic events: Tab reaches, Space toggles both ways, and a click on the LABEL TEXT toggles it, with `elementFromPoint` confirming the target is the `<span>` and not the box. **TWO REFUSALS WORTH MORE THAN THE CONVERSION.** The five native **RADIOS** were NOT converted — one is the Shared Brain revoke picker, the app's most destructive surface, and changing it to make a scan uniform is not a checkbox pass's business. And `chat.css`'s deliberate 0.45 resting dim (border 1.76) was left alone, with the suite made to **REPORT that number while ASSERTING the mitigation** — ticking, keyboard focus and row hover all restore full opacity. Silently changing another release's deliberate design under cover of this one was refused. New `test-next-checkbox.js` (OFFLINE, **65**) and `test-next-checkbox-visual.js` (LIVE_LOCAL, **72**); 13 mutations all red. **(3) THE MEMORY LAYER SPLIT ONE MACHINE ACROSS TWO FOLDERS AGAIN, AND THE ORCHESTRATOR'S DIAGNOSIS WAS REFUTED.** Live evidence: `hostname` returns `Mac` while `LocalHostName` still returns `Alices-MacBook-Pro`; `.curator-machine-id` EXISTS, is 0600, gitignored, and holds the CORRECT `alices-macbook-pro-9f3c1a`; yet a save landed in `mac-9f3c1a`. The proposed cause — a durable NEGATIVE cached in `persistedMachineId()` — is **WRONG**: it has exactly one caller, which always mints and repopulates in the same call, so a null never survives to be observed. **The real cause needs no code bug at all:** `.curator-machine-id` support was committed **Aug 30 12:20**; the MCP servers were spawned **Aug 29 08:01**, twenty-eight hours EARLIER, running v3.17.2-era code with no remembered-name logic — v3.23.1's ORIGINAL bug, still resident in memory. **NO CODE CHANGE REACHES A PROCESS SPAWNED BEFORE THE CODE EXISTED**, so the split keeps growing until Claude Desktop is restarted. **BUT CHASING THE WRONG HYPOTHESIS FOUND THREE REAL AND WORSE DEFECTS.** Neither the machine-id nor the install-id file was created EXCLUSIVELY: with 8 processes minting simultaneously against one empty user-data dir, the pre-fix code produced **EIGHT DISTINCT FOLDER NAMES FOR ONE INSTALLATION, IN 6 OF 6 ROUNDS** — and because the install-id candidate is **RANDOM**, this splits every time, not only on a hostname flap. `installId()` DID carry the durable-negative shape for real, so `installIdAvailable()` reported the collision guard permanently **OFF** after any repair. And a name that could not be PERSISTED was cached anyway — `rememberMachineId` set the cache AFTER a `try/catch` that swallowed the write failure, pinning a hostname-derived name for the life of a process while siblings read the file and used the other name. Fixed by caching **only** a value read from or successfully written to disk, plus exclusive `wx` create with **adopt-on-EEXIST** (the loser reads back and takes the winner's value). Deleting the memoisation was rejected — `machineId()` runs on every read and write of state — and TTL/`statSync` revalidation was rejected for making the answer TIME-DEPENDENT, one process returning two folder names in a session for no visible reason. **NOTHING IS MIGRATED, MERGED OR DELETED:** three scopes are split on the maintainer's disk and both halves stay readable and addressable by name; only the NEXT save is stable. `docs/architecture.md` carried a line that was **flatly false** — *"what is cached is the file, not the answer to a snapshotted path"* — corrected, and both docs gained the process half with the 8-of-8 measurement. `test-working-state.js` 501 -> **508**, written first and watched fail with *"ONE folder on disk, not two — got mac, alices-macbook-pro-9f3c1a"*. **TWO GREEN MUTATIONS CHASED RATHER THAN FILED:** each cache guard is REDUNDANT WITH ITS PAIR — a negative never stored cannot be returned, and one never returned cannot matter — so only the pair is observable; and the `wx`/adopt path reds only under a concurrency harness (6/6, 2/6, 6/6 splits against 0/6 shipped) which is **deliberately NOT promoted** into `npm test`, because a flaky suite is worse than an honest gap. **(4) FOUR MORE CONFORMANCE FIXES.** Domain identity dots: `DOMAIN_DOT_PALETTE`'s six raw hex values in an inline `style=` are gone, replaced by classes a `[data-theme]` block can reach. The audit measured ONE dot at 1.85; in the browser **ALL SIX failed in light, on every row state**, and the arithmetic-vs-browser gap (2.17 against 1.85) is confirmed to be `--surface` versus `--surface-active` — the SELECTED-ROW backdrop. Now 3.44-4.96, dark byte-identical, and **separability preserved at minimum pairwise CIEDE2000 14.58 light against a ~2.3 JND** — every hue kept, only lightness moved. No token file touched: three of six light values are system tokens outright, and the three that are not continue their own family's 500->600 ratio one step further, because the bundle defines nothing darker in those families. Provider colour: **green is correct and Settings' amber was the drift** — `tokens/color.css`'s own header reserves the type triad and the status palette for data types and status, *"never used to mean a data type"* outside them, and `chat.css` already carried a documented REFUSAL of amber for providers because it collides with the picker's caution badge. Unified by moving the three `--prov-*` properties into `shell.css`, which both views load — `chat.css`'s own comment had PREDICTED this (*"if a second view ever groups by provider they should move"*) and Settings became that view. The Sync rail badge's hardcoded `#E0A33A`/`#150A31`/`999px`/`9px` become tokens: dark unchanged at 8.46, **light 5.24 where it previously rendered the dark hex verbatim**, correctly scoped as THEME PARITY and not accessibility. **FINDING 8's PREMISE WAS FALSE AND IS RECORDED AS SUCH:** `.dm-issue-main` has carried `mono` at EVERY emission site since **v3.2.0** (`git log -S` returns only the introducing commit); the real defect was the ADJACENT meta column rendering `suggests entities/foo` and `-> some-slug` in the body face. Fixed there, with the prose words deliberately left sans — monoing everything is the absence of the rule, not the rule. **(5) TWO ITEMS THAT ARE NOT DESIGN.** `skills/curator-continuity/SKILL.md`'s description was **1285 characters against claude.ai's 1024 cap**, so the skill could not be uploaded at all; cut to **968** with all nine trigger phrases, the portability thesis and the whole v3.24.0 tier split intact — only three restatements of one resume intent were dropped. **A trap the file already encoded without recording it:** a plain YAML scalar cannot contain a colon followed by a space, which is why the original used em dashes throughout and why a colon-shaped draft broke the parse. Three rules, in order: **if the selected domain is already running, adopt NOTHING** (nothing is hidden, so moving the user is pure harm — an adoption can only ever fire when the screen would otherwise be blank); otherwise the latest `startedAt`; ties on slug ascending, so the order is TOTAL and cannot vary with Map iteration order. `startedAt` arrives through `wireNum`, which returns null for anything non-finite, and the comparator maps that to `-Infinity`, so an undateable record sorts LAST rather than poisoning the sort with NaN. Adoption runs ONCE PER MOUNT and the flag is spent BEFORE the pick runs — so it is forfeited even when nothing was adopted, because leaving it armed is how a poll 15 s later starts yanking the selection out from under a reader — and `selectDomain` forfeits it too. Plus **every domain with a live run is marked in the sidebar**, which is what stops the fix recreating the invisibility it removes. Refusing to adopt when ambiguous was REJECTED: it reinstates the blank screen in exactly the case that confuses most. **THE FIX CONTAINED THE BUG IT WAS FIXING, ONE LEVEL UP:** `activitySignature`'s `if (!r) return 'none'` fast path is EXACTLY the branch this scenario takes, so a running set folded in after it would have been recomputed on every poll and never painted — the dead-data shape, reintroduced inside its own repair. Both sets are now computed above the branch and appear in BOTH arms. One pre-existing assertion pinned the old `'none'` literal and was INVERTED rather than deleted. **(2) THE FINISHED CASE — the sentence the report actually opened with.** *"The process ended, but there's basically no way I can know if this article was ingested or not."* Adoption considers RUNNING records only, so a finished ingest on a non-selected domain still surfaced nothing; on Flash Lite a document can finish in ~9 s, so that is the COMMON path, not the edge. **Adoption was deliberately NOT extended to settled records** — a terminal record lives 30 minutes under `TERMINAL_TTL_MS`, so every mount inside that window would yank the destination to a finished run. Instead two surfaces, both driven from the EXISTING `ACTIVITY_ACK_KEY` store with no second dismissal store (verified: one storage key repo-wide, and `localStorage` is touched in that file only inside its two helpers). The sidebar row marks **Ingested** or **Failed** — two words, because the question asked is literally *was this ingested or not*, and reporting a failure in a success's vocabulary answers a different question — and one main-pane line names the domain with Show me / Dismiss, emitted as a SIBLING of `body` after the five-way state chain closes, so it survives the loader, the domains-error, the empty card, the queue and the form alike. Clicking routes through the existing `selectDomain` to the outcome panel that already shipped in v3.24.0; no second panel was built. With several records the line names the newest by **`finishedAt`** — which was already on the wire, so `startedAt` did not have to be reused, and it is the honest key because a long run started first can finish last — ties on slug, with an "and N more". ONE expression drives both the class and the label, so the spin stops on FAILURE as well as success: `acting` is reset and the view repainted in a `finally`, and a spinner still turning after an error is a lie about what the app is doing. Plus an advisory dependency-audit CI job, **deliberately NOT `continue-on-error`** — measured with `gh` that `main` has no branch protection (404, *Branch not protected*) and no rulesets (an empty array), so nothing can gate a merge today; `continue-on-error` would therefore buy no safety that was not already true while SUPPRESSING GitHub's workflow-failed notification, which is the only way anyone would learn of a finding.
