Verified live on **real Gemini AND Anthropic**: a page with 3 existing facts + 1 new one now yields `new_facts` containing **exactly the new fact and none of the three unchanged** — on both providers. **(2) The roadmap's cost claim was WRONG, and measuring it changed the framing.** The plan said the dead function cost "degraded quality AND wasted tokens". The token half does not hold: reviving it makes each delta prompt **larger** by exactly the prior version's byte length — **+69.5%** measured across 25 real `articles` pages (independently reproduced at +41% on a synthetic prior). This is a **quality win that COSTS input tokens at push time**; the payoff is downstream, because synthesis consumes `new_facts` alone and without a prior the collective must dedup and Jaccard-pair-scan a full page copy every cycle. **(3) Reviving it opened a NEW silent-data-loss path, caught before it shipped.** A page in `pending_retry`/`permanent_skip` failed its last push, so NONE of its body reached the collective; diffing it against the pre-failure git version would contribute only the change since, and the never-contributed body would be dropped permanently — making Decision 3's retry path lossy, the exact inverse of its purpose. New `neverContributedPages` set, frozen off `connection` BEFORE the un-skip loop mutates the working copies. The conservative branch's failure mode is a redundant full contribution, absorbed by exact-string dedup; a gap is unrecoverable. **(4) HIGH, caught in audit — the fix closed the reported case and left the purpose broken.** The docblock argued at length that `--literal-pathspecs` turns a stray empty result into "a clean null". It does — **only for the glob-superset case it tested**. A genuinely 0-byte page still returned `''`, because `git show` legitimately emits empty stdout and that path contains no glob for the flag to engage. Downstream, `isNew` is a `=== null` check while `buildDeltaPrompt` branches on truthiness: the model was told **"IS NEW PAGE: false"** while shown the new-page block with no prior, under an instruction defining `stable_facts` as "facts UNCHANGED from prior" — and **`stable_facts` is written and read by NOTHING** (synthesis takes `new_facts` only), so anything routed there is gone permanently. Reachable: **Obsidian creates 0-byte files on "new note" and the documented vault root IS `domains/<d>/wiki/`**; 2 zero-byte `.md` files already exist in the maintainer's own domains, 1 tracked in `.knowledge-git` HEAD. Closed with `if (!content) return null`. Whitespace-only content is deliberately NOT caught (truthy → both consumers already agree). **(5) A flag that has now been over-claimed twice.** With the falsy guard in place, removing `--literal-pathspecs` no longer fails anything — the guard converts the glob case too. Every literal-vs-glob shape was probed, including delete-and-recreate where the two shas genuinely differ (`663daf5` vs `c4a103e`): **the shas diverge, the content never does**, because a globbed `git log -1` can only select a commit at or after the one that last touched the page and `git show <sha>:<path>` is snapshot-based. The flag is KEPT (it removes a parsing mode from an externally-sourced value) but the assertion was relabelled to what it actually proves, and the docblock now carries an explicit "do not over-credit this flag" note. **(6) HIGH — live coverage that was fictitious.** A new live section proving a real model handles the diff prompt passed on both providers while **silently running Anthropic twice**: `__setUserDataDirOverride` redirects ALL user-data paths including `.curator-config.json`, `GEMINI_API_KEY` exists only there (not in `.env`), and `getProviderInfo('gemini')` does not fail without a key — **it falls through to whichever provider still has one**. The tell was byte-identical output from two different models. Fixed by seeding the isolated config, plus a resolved-provider assertion so a silent fallback now fails loudly. **(7) MCP/app `DOMAINS_PATH` precedence aligned** (`mcp/storage/local.js`): env ranked ABOVE config in the MCP and BELOW it in the app. History shows **no design reason** — the MCP resolver was written independently in v2.3.0 and never cross-checked; v3.1.0 spotted the mismatch and froze it rather than fixing it. Now `CLI arg → config → DOMAINS_PATH → default`, identical to `getDomainsDir()`. Blast radius is narrow (`routes/mcp.js:35` always passes `--domains-path`). **Documented, not fixed:** the CLI arg is a SNAPSHOT taken at wizard-generation time, so a stale `claude_desktop_config.json` can still diverge — the docs' new "can never disagree" claim was an overclaim and was corrected; and MCP **reads** resolve through this adapter while MCP **writes** go through `files.js → getDomainsDir()`, which never sees the CLI arg (pre-existing; now noted in the docblock). **(8) Test-suite credential exposure closed, twice.** `test-sharedbrain-routes.js` spawns a real server and isolated only `CURATOR_TEST_DOMAINS_DIR`, so it resolved the maintainer's REAL `.sync-config.json` — **a live GitHub PAT** — and one stray Sync call would have pushed to their real repo. Migrated to `CURATOR_TEST_USER_DATA_DIR`; the on-disk `.pre-test-backup` dance is REMOVED (the audit rated this a net improvement — the old code actively `unlink`ed real credential files; the new code never writes at all) and replaced by a read-only content fingerprint guard. **`mtime` is deliberately EXCLUDED** from that guard: the maintainer's live app on :3333 rewrites `.curator-config.json` during ordinary Settings use, and a multi-minute suite would then fail with a false "isolation is broken" — the same misattribution shape that cost two investigations in v3.0.16. Separately, **`npm test` itself was DELETING the real `.curator-config.json`** (`test-beta10`/`test-beta11`, to make `DOMAINS_PATH` win) and restoring it — a crash between the two cost the maintainer their API keys. Both migrated to `__setDomainsDirOverride()`, which outranks config with no need to touch the real file; verified by hashing all four credential files across a full `npm test`. In `test-beta11` the delete was entirely **vestigial** — all 12 tests exercise pure functions. **(9) Licensing (D7) — the `sharedbrain*` relicense, before GA rather than after.** Research finding: backlash comes from RETRACTING rights, not from restrictive licences (Grafana went more restrictive with no fork; Redis/HashiCorp/Cal.com retracted and were forked within weeks). Sole authorship re-verified today across all 9 brain modules, the route, and all 14 test suites. Ten files (9 × `src/brain/sharedbrain*.js` + `src/routes/sharedbrain.js`) move to the **Curator Enterprise License v1.0** in `LICENSES/`; everything else — including all test suites and the Shared Brain UI in `app.js` — stays MIT. `package.json` → `"MIT AND LicenseRef-Curator-Enterprise"` (`AND`, not `OR`; `OR` would let a recipient take the whole work under MIT). **Three decisions:** a **grace clause** (organizational production use is free, permanently for each Release, because no key mechanism exists and an org that WANTED to pay could not); an **FSL-style clause** converting each Release's covered files to MIT two years after publication; and scope limited to brain + route. **(10) The drafted licence text contradicted the drafted README, and four contradictions were found in audit.** §4.2's draft required a key while §4.3's draft README promised GitHub "free for everyone, forever" — resolved toward the strategy (`ENTERPRISE-TIER-PLAN.md` commits to it in three places) as a permanent, non-withdrawable §3.1 grant. Then: **§9 terminated "sections 2 and 3", silently revoking the §3.1 forever-promise on any unrelated breach** (carved out, together with so much of §2 as is needed to exercise it — carving §3.1 alone would have granted a right nobody could exercise); **§3.3 was self-terminating** ("UNTIL keys are available") fourteen lines above "nothing is ever withdrawn retroactively" (re-scoped per-Release); **§7 accepted contributions with no relicensing grant**, so one accepted community PR would contaminate the salable tier; and a **fourth instance** of the same permanence-vs-termination error inside the new §3.3 text. **(11) A leak the plan would have made permanent.** §3.1 as first drafted also granted the local-folder backend free forever — and `storage_type: 'local'` is fully production-reachable (`sharedbrain-config.js:138` validates it, the factory instantiates it), so an org pointing `local_storage_path` at a mounted NFS/SMB share would have obtained the Enterprise proposition (on-premise, no GitHub, org-controlled) irrevocably free. Narrowed to GitHub only; costs nothing today because §3.3 makes every backend free for this Release. **§1.6 now defines "the GitHub storage backend"** as the shipped adapter against public `api.github.com` — GitHub Enterprise Server and data-residency Enterprise Cloud are outside the permanent grant, which **describes the software rather than carving an exception**: `sharedbrain-github-adapter.js:65` hardcodes `GITHUB_API` with no configurable endpoint, so those deployments are unreachable today. §3.1's two operative paragraphs are byte-identical to before the narrowing — the promise reads absolute first, the boundary arrives after. **(12) Anti-open-washing.** README gains a prominent Licensing section (three touchpoints, leading with what is MIT and stating "nothing is being taken away from anyone"); the `License: MIT` badge linking to opensource.org was corrected to "MIT + source-available" (the composite work is not OSI-conformant — §4.2 is a field-of-use restriction, and a badge asserting otherwise is the open-washing signal the disclosure exists to prevent); new `TRADEMARK.md` (name/logo not licensed, forks need a different name, four permitted nominative uses, enforcement never entangled with code distribution — the WP Engine anti-pattern named explicitly). **Deferred deliberately:** removing `stable_facts` from the delta schema (generated, capped, stored in every payload, **read by nothing** — a real token saving, but it changes prompt content a second time in one release); a delta-prompt size cap; unifying the MCP read/write path split; and the Cloudflare R2 → S3-compatible rename, which was implemented, found to be **2 of ~12 references** across five files that no single agent owned, and **reverted byte-clean** — half-applied reads worse than either end state. **Verification: 33 offline suites green**; `test-sharedbrain-push.js` 83 → **110** assertions (real git fixtures, a **decoy page committed under a literal `domains/` directory** so a regression fails loudly with wrong content instead of quietly with null), `test-paths.js` +82, `test-sharedbrain-routes.js` 73 → **81**, `test-sharedbrain-llm-live.js` 33 → **49** live on both providers. Every new guard was proven by **reverting the fix and confirming the suite goes red**, then restoring. Secret scan: **0 findings in new content**. First git tag since `v3.0.0-beta.1`, so §5's two-year conversion clock has a verifiable anchor. |
| `v3.1.0` | **Track 1 Foundation — every user-data path now resolves through one module. Deliberately invisible to users; that is the point.** An installed macOS `.app` is **read-only** — writing inside it invalidates the code signature and macOS refuses to launch — but all six user-data locations currently live inside the repo folder. This routes them through a new [`src/brain/paths.js`](src/brain/paths.js) so they can later move to `~/Library/Application Support/` (NOT `~/Documents`/`~/Desktop`/`~/Downloads`, which are TCC-protected — and the MCP server is spawned by *Claude Desktop*, so a permission prompt would be attributed to Claude Desktop and may never render for a headless child). It ships **one full cycle before any packaging work**, specifically so it can be proven a no-op in the only mode that exists today. **(1) Detection is POSITIVE and fail-safe.** `isBundleInstall()` requires an explicit signal (a packager-written marker file, or an `<X>.app/Contents/` path shape); **everything else is repo mode — unknown layout ⇒ repo ⇒ data stays where it is.** The asymmetry is the whole design: wrongly deciding "bundle" is catastrophic AND silent (the user opens the app to an onboarding wizard, no domains, data intact but invisible — it presents as *"the update deleted my second brain"*), while wrongly deciding "repo" is a loud first-write failure on a read-only bundle with nothing lost. **(2) HIGH, caught in audit — the first two attempts at this were both wrong, in opposite directions.** v1 was `existsSync('.git')` alone: a ZIP download, a deleted `.git`, or a copy that dropped dotfiles silently relocated everything (reproduced). v2 added data markers `['.git','.curator-config.json','domains']` — but **`domains/.gitkeep` is tracked**, so every checkout AND every tree built by `git archive` contains `domains/`, which made bundle mode **unreachable by construction**: a packaged app would take the repo branch and write into itself. Its test encoded the false premise (a synthetic tree with no markers, which no real bundle can be) and passed. The inversion kills both. The suite now builds bundles the way we would actually build one (`git archive HEAD | tar -x`) and **pins the premise itself** — three assertions that a shipped tree DOES contain `domains/` and does NOT contain `.git`/`.curator-config.json` — so a stale assumption cannot hide again. **`scripts/build-app.sh` deliberately UNCHANGED**: today's `.app` is an AppleScript wrapper that runs the checkout, so it genuinely IS a repo install, and writing the marker now would flip every live user into bundle mode. **(3) Repo-mode equivalence is the claim this release stands on, and it was verified independently three times** — by reconstructing pre-change derivations from `git show HEAD:` / `git archive`, probing the ACTUAL call-path functions (not the new module's own exports, which would be tautological), and diffing resolved absolute strings: **all anchors byte-identical**, before and again after the detection core was rewritten. **(4) An anchor the plan missed:** `sharedbrain.js` derived the sync git dir as `<domainsDir>/..`, already wrong today for anyone with a custom `domainsPath`. **(5) `__setUserDataDirOverride` was import-order dependent** — `config.js`/`sync.js`/`sharedbrain-config.js` snapshotted paths at module load, so an override set *after* importing them silently no-opped while the suite (which imports nothing else) reported success. All three now resolve **per call**, with a source guard forbidding top-level `const X = <paths getter>()`. **(6) NEW SAFETY SEAM `CURATOR_TEST_USER_DATA_DIR`.** `CURATOR_TEST_DOMAINS_DIR` isolates only `domains/`, so **every test server run during development was holding the maintainer's REAL GitHub PAT** — one stray click on Sync would have pushed to their real repo (this nearly happened; an agent avoided those buttons on judgement, not by design). The new env is checked before repo/bundle detection and re-read per call so it crosses process boundaries, isolating the four credential locations unconditionally and `domains/` too **unless** `DOMAINS_PATH`/`--domains-path` is set. Audited explicitly for privilege escalation: **not a vulnerability** — setting it already implies the user's privileges (at which point reading the config directly is easier), there is no remote or cross-user path, redirection *hides* credentials rather than exposing them, and no production code sets or forwards it. **(7) Frontend binding hardening.** `app.js` is one ES module with ~90 top-level element references; a module-scope dereference of a missing element throws, no listener binds, and the auto-updater ships a **blank white page** to every user. 33 `?.` guards added, including two bare `document.querySelector('[data-tab="…"]').addEventListener(...)` calls that would have blanked the entire app if a tab button were absent; `showEl`/`hideEl`/`showStatus` now log instead of failing silently. **Scope is honest: this prevents the LOAD-TIME blank page, not every runtime throw** — two known cross-element references inside handlers remain, named in the scanner's header. **(8) HIGH, caught in audit — the null-safety scanner was silently blind, twice.** Its lexer desynced on a nested template literal (`app.js:897`) and a regex containing raw backticks (`:3518`), so it saw **78 of 90** declarations while reporting all assertions green; the rewrite then left a **dead** regex-vs-division heuristic (`noteCodeChar` appended whitespace, so the `$`-anchored keyword test never matched), meaning `return /["']/.test(s)` was lexed as division and reopened the same class — demonstrated end-to-end with a real blank-page bug present and the suite exiting 0. Both fixed, and the durable answer is an **independent, deliberately-dumb second count cross-checked for exact set equality**, which fires even on the one residual shape the heuristic still misses. **Verification:** **33 offline suites green**; new [test-paths.js](scripts/test-paths.js) **127 assertions** and [test-frontend-null-safety.js](scripts/test-frontend-null-safety.js) **63**. Verified by hand: this checkout is repo mode with `getUserDataDir() === APP_ROOT`; a `git archive` tree placed at `Curator.app/Contents/Resources` is detected as a **bundle even though it contains `domains/`**; a `.git`-less checkout stays repo; app and MCP resolve the same 6 real domains; MCP stdout stays pure JSON; credential files remain 0600; and **no `~/Library/Application Support/The Curator` is created in repo mode**. **Deferred with reasoning:** `loadPriorContent()` in `sharedbrain.js` is **100% dead** — its pathspec carries a spurious `domains/` prefix and 0 of 5242 tracked files match, so every Shared Brain delta is generated as if every changed page were brand new. The two-line fix **changes LLM prompt content**, which must not ride in a release whose purpose is proving that nothing changed. Also deferred: the MCP-vs-app `DOMAINS_PATH` precedence split (pre-existing; now also punches a hole in the new seam, so the decision is more urgent), and migrating `test-sharedbrain-routes.js` to the new seam. |
| `v3.0.17` | **Ingest progress honesty + three silent-data-loss fixes, from live use of v3.0.16.** The maintainer ingested a large document, watched "Phase 1: planning wiki structure… 12%" sit still for minutes, and reported the app as hung. It was not — but investigating why produced a bigger release than the UX fix it started as. **(1) Phase 1 is now legible.** Phase 1 is ONE LLM call with no sub-progress, so the bar genuinely cannot move; on a mature domain the outline can also overflow the output budget and re-run the ENTIRE call, silently doubling latency and tokens. New elapsed-time clock (ticks per phase and deliberately does NOT reset on a `wait` sub-event, so time keeps accumulating visibly through retries), retry/backoff states rendered in amber (they already fired but were visually indistinguishable), and an honest note that a large document takes a minute or more per phase. **(2) Cost is visible.** `ingestFile` has returned `result.tokenUsage` since v3.0.16 but [routes/ingest.js](src/routes/ingest.js) built its SSE `done` event by picking fields explicitly and dropped it — so the v3.0.16 caching win was unobservable. It now reaches a compact footer under the change records (provider/model, calls, in/out, and the cached-read/cache-write split where the saving actually shows), degrading cleanly on absent or partial data. **(3) The report no longer drowns in its own warnings.** A real live Gemini run emitted **75 warnings, 58 of them the same class** — the stub page, the stray summary and the semantic dupes were buried under 58 near-identical `.md` lines. New `aggregateWarnings()` collapses same-class entries at ONE chokepoint before the log entry is built, so `log.md`, the SSE payload and the result panel all agree. Threshold **3** (1–2 occurrences keep the specific path, which is the useful thing; 3 matches the house convention already used by the trunk-page detector), examples capped at 3 with "…and N more". Five classes aggregate; **semantic near-duplicates and trunk-page injections deliberately do NOT** — each names a specific pair the user must decide about, and collapsing them would trade signal for tidiness. Verified against the real payload: **75 → 11**. **(4) A warning that was actively misleading.** "Phase 1 outline was too large for the AI output limit" invited exactly the reading the maintainer gave it. Measured across 180 real ingests: the outlines that OVERFLOW are the **small** ones (44 and 53 pages, ~10% of budget in real content) while **370-, 328- and 316-page outlines completed fine**. Nothing predicts it — not page count, not index size (the domain with the LARGEST index had ZERO overflows in 15 ingests), not source length. It correlates with repetitive, enumerable sources (6 of 7 overflows in `posts` are the same "X Engagement Session" log shape) — i.e. **runaway generation, not volume**, the same degeneration documented for compile in v3.0.1-beta.25. The wording now also states the trade-off it used to hide: the recovery retry explicitly asks for FEWER, BROADER pages, so a recovered plan is **coarser** than a first-attempt plan, and it reports the real extra call count and that the source is sent twice. **(5) THREE silent-data-loss fixes on the ingest path.** **(a)** Phase 2 batch responses were pushed straight into the result: `{"pages": []}` is truthy, so **an entire batch's planned pages vanished with no stub, no warning and a green result panel**, while `{"pages": {}}` threw `TypeError: batchResult.pages is not iterable` and killed the whole ingest with a raw JS error. (v3.0.16's `extractPageArray` had been applied to the *single-page fallback* — the rare path — and not to the batch path, which runs first for every batch of every multi-phase ingest.) **(b)** `parseJSON` is deliberately lenient (jsonrepair), so the bare text `not json at all` became the truthy STRING `"not json at all"`, was accepted as a valid outline, skipped the retry entirely, and `validateOutline` degraded it to a summary-only plan — **an 80,000-character source could produce a ONE-PAGE wiki**. A parsed response must now carry a usable page array; a `degenerateOutline` holder keeps the salvage so the outcome is never worse than before. **(c)** A single page whose first attempt comes back unusable now gets one brevity-directed retry before a stub is written (suffix-only, so the cacheable prefix stays byte-stable; gated on `isOutputTokenLimit`; the stub remains the last resort). **(6) `MULTI_PHASE_OUTLINE_TOKENS` stays at 24,576 — a change was implemented, measured, and REVERTED.** Lowering it to 16,384 looked right (a runaway runs *until* the cap, so the cap IS the stall) and was justified with a density statistic — but the derivation measured outline summaries from `log.md`, which records only PATHS, and a regex captured the **Type** column, yielding ~73 chars/entry. The real distribution (n=2,047 index summaries) is **mean 113 chars**, giving ~176 chars/entry. The first samples from the new logging are **57.8 tok/page (Gemini, 44-page outline), 72.5 and 68.9 (Anthropic, 29 and 27 pages)** — note these are all SMALL outlines and the per-page cost varies ~25%, so they do NOT support a clean linear extrapolation. What they do establish: every sample extrapolates to **21,400–26,800 tokens** for a 370-page outline, i.e. **all of them exceed 16,384**, so the proposed lowering was unsafe. That 24,576 suffices is established separately — by the fact that 370-, 328- and 316-page outlines have historically SUCCEEDED under it. The block comment's own bound (a component-weighted ~3.53 chars/token giving ~18,400, within a 16,250–24,576 interval) remains the more defensible derivation and is what the docs state. The change would have turned the largest legitimate ingests into double-cost retries: the exact harm it existed to prevent. Instead Phase 1's REAL usage is now logged (`[ingest] Phase 1 complete (accepted outline): N pages, X output tokens of 24,576 …`) so the constant can be sized from data. **(7) HIGH, caught in audit — that instrument was dead code and reported the OPPOSITE conclusion.** `if (outline) acceptedOutlineUsage = retryProbe.totals` sat INSIDE `if (!outline) {` and two lines BEFORE the reassignment, so it provably never ran: on a runaway ingest the "accepted outline" line reported the FAILED attempt's 24,576 tokens, i.e. "a 44-page outline cost 100% of budget" — from which the only conclusion is *raise the budget*, which the comment 40 lines above spends a paragraph disproving. The suite asserted the CALL SITE with a source regex and labelled it "the sizing datapoint", giving positive assurance for a value that was always wrong. Fixed, and replaced with an executable assertion driven by a new **test-only fake-LLM seam** on `ingestMultiPhase` (trailing defaulted param, same pattern as compile's `opts.generateText`) that runs the real Phase 1 retry ladder and Phase 2 fallback ladder offline for free — **that seam is what found (5b)**. **Verification:** **31 offline suites green**; `test-ingest-prompt-slimming.js` **269 → 488 assertions**. Live on **real Gemini AND Anthropic** against an articles-scale domain (600 entities / 2,651 concepts / 121 KB index) with an md5-frozen source: Gemini 587s / 1.8% broken / 42 calls / **1,758,011 cached-read tokens**; Anthropic 210s / 3.4% broken / **cache write 46,338 + read 278,028 = 57.3% input saving**, confirming v3.0.16's prefix stability survived every prompt edit. The concise-retry path fired **5 times** on the Gemini run, so it is no longer untested. Browser-verified on an isolated :3334 instance: elapsed clock ticking/resetting/clearing across all exit paths, amber retry state, token footer on full/partial/absent data, and the reworded warnings rendering in the correct buckets (amber `#d29922` / blue `#58a6ff`). **Also fixed:** a compile-progress CSS regression introduced earlier in this same release (`#compile-progress-pct` reuses `.progress-pct` but is not wrapped in the new `.progress-meta`, so it silently lost its 10px gap), and the concise-retry warning's UI bucket, which had been reaching amber via the negation "not a stub page" matching the `stub page` trigger — replaced with a first-class `briefer than the rest` trigger in `classifyIngestEntry`. **Known follow-ups:** two entity-dedup gaps observed live — `jetbrains`/`jet-brains` (Pass B only redirects an INCOMING path onto an existing file; it never reconciles two variants that already coexist on disk — Health's hyphen-variant scan does recover it) and `cloudfare`/`cloudflare` (a typo: hyphen-normalisation gives different keys, `redirectSemanticDuplicates` excludes entities by design and would score 0.33 anyway, so only the opt-in Health semantic-duplicate AI scan can catch it — and `entities/cloudfare-workers.md` is already on disk in the real `articles` domain). Plus the v3.0.16 list: non-ASCII `.DS_Store` self-heal, `/api/query`'s 90k slice, and sync ignoring a live `.write-lock`. |
| `v3.0.16` | **Ingest prompt caching + two data-loss fixes + sync hygiene.** Phase A Track 0 part 2. Scoped as a pure cost win; live testing turned it into a cost-AND-correctness release, and cut the riskier half. **(1) Prompt caching — the cost win.** There was **zero** prompt caching anywhere (`grep cache_control` → nothing), and `buildBatchPrompt` interleaved the per-batch page list so no stable prefix could form. `pageBatch` moved to the END via a new `buildBatchPromptParts` returning `{prefix, suffix}`: on the real `articles` domain the prefix is **145,352 chars byte-identical across every batch** with a **527-char** volatile tail. Anthropic gets an explicit `cache_control` breakpoint gated TWO ways — a size floor (`ANTHROPIC_CACHE_MIN_PREFIX_CHARS = 16_000`, since Haiku 4.5 silently declines to cache below ~4,096 tokens: no error, just `cache_creation_input_tokens: 0`) AND a reuse rule at the caller (`totalBatches >= 2`), because a cache WRITE costs 1.25× and break-even is two calls — so single-pass ingest deliberately gets NO breakpoint or the change would make it *more expensive*. Gemini needs no code change (implicit prefix caching). **Verified live on the shipping bytes, both providers**: Anthropic write=41,822 / read=250,932 → **56.6% input saving** (the prefix cached once and read back 6×); Gemini implicit cache served **1,195,620 of 1,343,434 input tokens (89%)** on a 32-call run. Canonical arithmetic (outline + 3 batches): **−30.3% total ingest input**, rising to −56% at 7 batches as the write premium amortises. **The batch prompt does NOT shrink** — it never contained the index; the entire win is caching. **(2) Real token telemetry.** `generateText` gained an additive `opts.onUsage` → `{provider, model, inputTokens, outputTokens, cachedReadTokens, cacheWriteTokens}`; return type stays a bare string so all 18 call sites are untouched. Fires once per COMPLETED provider call **inside `callProvider`**, i.e. under both the 429/503 retry loop and the fallback chain, so totals are true billed spend; a throwing callback can never break the call (v3.0.4 `onWarn` contract). **Audit-driven correction:** Gemini's `promptTokenCount` INCLUDES cached tokens while Anthropic's `input_tokens` EXCLUDES them — summing both raw would make `inputTokens + cachedReadTokens×0.1` double-count on Gemini and silently mislead the first person to meter it. `normalizeGeminiUsage` now subtracts cached, so the field means the same thing on both providers and a cost calculation never branches. `ingestFile` returns an additive `result.tokenUsage`. **(3) DATA LOSS: Phase 2 accepted unvalidated page paths.** `writtenPages.push(...batchResult.pages)` trusted whatever `path` the model returned, never checking it against the paths that batch requested — so `validateOutline`'s "exactly one summary at the canonical path" guarantee was enforced on the outline only and trivially bypassed. Found by a live ingest that produced TWO summaries. This breaks the **deterministic-summary idempotency contract**: a stray second summary means re-ingesting that source never merges again. New `reconcileGeneratedPages` redirects stray `summaries/` paths to canonical and folds them into ONE entry (authoritative page passed as `incoming` so it wins every conflict; order-independent, verified across six orderings); unplanned non-summary paths are allowed but WARNED (refusing would re-create the silent-discard failure this release exists to remove). **On the final Gemini validation run the model invented 4 extra summaries and all 4 were caught and merged** — on v3.0.15 those would have been 4 permanent duplicates. **(4) DATA LOSS: `writePage` silently discarded pages missing `.md`.** A live baseline run logged `Skipping invalid path (no filename): "concepts/concurrency-control"` — the model planned it, Phase 2 generated its content, and `files.js` threw it away with only a `console.warn` and nothing in `warnings[]`. The user paid for a page that vanished with no way to know. Now the extension is appended; genuinely unusable paths are still refused but surface via a new optional `opts.onWarn`. **The blanket "basename must end in .md" rule was doubling as an ACCIDENTAL traversal defence**, so appending blindly would have converted it into a real escape — new `isUnsafePagePath` runs on BOTH the raw input and the normalised path (necessary because `normalizePath` rewrites `/tmp/x` into `entities/tmp/x`, so checking `canonPath` alone would never fire). Independently verified against ~22 vectors; nothing escaped. Nested paths are now FLATTENED to `<folder>/<basename>` (previously `entities/companies/openai.md` was written but invisible to the flat `readdir` that ingest's inventory and all three `health.js` scans use — so the app reported its inbound links broken while Obsidian resolved them). **(5) Slug-inventory safety valve.** New `capSlugInventory`/`capExistingFilesForPrompt` + `SLUG_INVENTORY_BUDGET_CHARS = 160_000`, relevance-ranked by token overlap (reusing `tokenize` from `sharedbrain-delta.js`). **Deliberately INERT** — verified it does not fire on the real `articles` domain (600→600 entities, 2,651→2,651 concepts, order preserved) and trips only around ~3,780 pages in one folder. An earlier 24,000 value measured a 2.2× Anthropic broken-link regression and the constant carries that number in a ⚠ warning block so nobody tunes it back down. The FULL uncapped list still reaches `redirectSemanticDuplicates`. **(6) `.DS_Store` untracked + `pull()` self-heal + non-ASCII domains + stale-lock deletion** (`sync.js`, `write-registry.js`): `.DS_Store` added to `DOMAINS_GITIGNORE_RULES` as a bare pattern (matches at any depth) and untracked via `untrackStaleDSStore()`; `clearStaleLock()` deletes a `.write-lock` the EXISTING staleness rule already considers dead (never a looser one), TOCTOU-guarded by re-reading immediately before unlink; `pull()` now runs the same self-heal as `push()`; `git ls-files -z` replaces ASCII-only parsing that git's C-quoting defeated. **(7) HIGH, caught in audit — the `.DS_Store` untrack broke Sync entirely.** Running the untrack BEFORE the merge leaves the file on disk untracked; if origin still carries it tracked, git aborts (`Please move or remove them before you merge. Aborting.`) — reproduced against real git, two machines, one bare remote. `-X theirs` resolves CONTENT conflicts only. The real knowledge repo tracks three `.DS_Store` files at three depths and Finder rewrites them constantly, so this would have hit users on the first auto-update, with raw git text and no `friendlyError` mapping. Fixed by moving the untrack AFTER a successful merge, plus `recoverHygieneMergeConflict()` handling both the preflight-abort and modify/delete shapes — **gated so that if ANY implicated path is not our own junk (`.write-lock`/`.DS_Store`), the original error is re-thrown untouched**; a genuine wiki-page conflict still fails loudly, now with a friendly message. Also `--literal-pathspecs` + rejecting git pathspec-magic (`: ! * ? [ ]`) in `isSafePathSegment`, since a domain named `:!x` made the untrack silently no-op forever. **(8) CRITICAL, caught by a SECOND audit — `friendlyError` misread real conflicts as auth failures.** The re-audit (run because the first audit's diff had since changed under a late scope revert + 9 fixes) found `friendlyError`'s FIRST branch tested `msg.includes('403') || msg.includes('401')` as a bare substring against the WHOLE error string — which for a merge conflict embeds abbreviated commit SHAs and absolute temp/repo paths. A SHA like `774bb**403**e78…` therefore routed a genuine unresolvable wiki-content conflict to *"GitHub rejected the token"*, sending the user to regenerate a PAT while the broken merge sat unattended — **suppressing the exact safety message this release added**. Deterministically reproducible (`TMPDIR=/tmp/tmp403dir npm test`), ~0.7–1.8% per conflict. **This was also the root cause of the `test-sync-hygiene.js` "flake" (1 failure in 22 runs) that two separate investigations had attributed to CPU load and to ambient git config — both wrong.** Fixed by moving the two conflict branches ABOVE the auth branch (both match long, specific phrases so they cannot shadow in the other direction) and tightening the numeric test to `/\b(?:401|403)\b/`. New **section 16** regression guard, validated by reverting the fix and confirming it fails. **Known gap left documented, not fixed (fail-safe):** git C-quotes non-ASCII bytes in the merge-preflight message as octal escapes and `core.quotePath=false` does NOT affect that text, so Shape A self-heal never fires for a non-ASCII domain name — the user gets the "manual look" message for a zero-content junk file; nothing is deleted or auto-committed. A docblock falsely claiming non-ASCII was verified has been corrected. **(9) O(n²) test helper.** `stripLineComments` re-scanned the whole accumulated output on every `//`, taking **22,781 ms** on `app.js` — now **70 ms**. **Verification: 31 offline suites green** (new `test-ingest-prompt-slimming.js` **269 assertions**, new `test-sync-hygiene.js` **169**; `test-chat-model.js` 76→82, `test-css-tokens.js` extended to `app.js` template literals — all 21 `var()` refs there resolve). Live: **14 paired ingests** on an articles-scale domain (600/2,651 pages, 121 KB index) against an md5-frozen source, both providers. **A finding worth recording: the broken-wikilink rate is NOT a valid single-run gate** — measured range on UNMODIFIED v3.0.15 is Gemini 0.0–3.8% and Anthropic 2.4–6.1%, i.e. noise wider than any effect we could detect at n=3. Where a question allowed it, byte-identity beat statistics: `buildOutlinePrompt` is proven **byte-identical to v3.0.15 end-to-end** (973/973 input matrix, plus inventory order preserved through the cap), which settles in seconds that this release cannot affect page planning. **Deferred, deliberately: removing `index.md` from the outline/single-pass prompts.** Implemented and measured at **−39.1%** on the outline prompt, but it is only **−16.2%** of total ingest input versus caching's −30.3%, and it was the sole piece changing what the model sees. Reverted before shipping (byte-identity proven) because it could not be shown SAFE, not because it was shown harmful. All data and reasoning are recorded in `docs/ingestion-pipeline.md` §1b so the next attempt starts from it. **Docs (same release):** ingestion-pipeline (new prompt-assembly stage, Mermaid node, 3 failure-mode rows, §7.2 caching mechanics, §8 costs re-measured, line-range citations replaced with function names so they stop rotting), architecture (`generateText` signature — stale since v3.0.11 — and the Anthropic default corrected from `claude-sonnet-4-6` to `claude-haiku-4-5`, stale since v2.4.1), user-guide §8 + §19, sync.md, CONTRIBUTING (suite count 32→48, verified), api-reference + use-cases (pre-existing errors fixed: the Notes section named "Claude" as the provider when the default is Gemini, and `/api/query` genuinely DOES still do a flat 90,000-char slice — beta.11 only fixed `chat.js`; it is also unreachable from the UI). **Known follow-ups:** an oversized outline (one run planned 60 pages) degrades into stub pages via output-token fallback — pre-existing, and the prompt's own "5–30 pages" budget is not binding on the model; `/api/query`'s 90k slice; sync still ignores a LIVE `.write-lock` held by the MCP process. |
| `v3.0.15` | **Housekeeping + safety release — the first of Phase A "quick wins". Six fixes, three of them bugs users are hitting today, plus a new test that found them.** **(1) The Gemini fallback chain was two-thirds dead.** A live API probe showed `gemini-1.5-flash` and `gemini-1.5-flash-latest` both **404** — so the v2.4.0 model-lifecycle safety net had exactly ONE working rung. The chain also escalated *backwards in time*, which is the wrong direction for retirement risk. Repointed forward to `['gemini-3.1-flash-lite','gemini-3.5-flash-lite','gemini-2.5-flash']` (all three verified alive with our exact call shape: JSON mode + `maxOutputTokens: 65536`). **`DEFAULTS.gemini` deliberately UNCHANGED** at `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` — it is 2.5× cheaper on input and 3.75× cheaper on output than its own successor, and Google has announced no shutdown date; the chain exists to keep users *working* when the default disappears, not to move them off it early. **(2) A fallback now warns when it costs more.** New exact-id `MODEL_PRICES_USD_PER_MTOK` table (covering only the ~10 ids in `DEFAULTS` + every rung) and `compareModelCost()` returning `'costlier' | 'similar' | 'unknown'`; `getFallbackStatus()` carries `costTier` (legacy `costlier` boolean retained). **An earlier family-name heuristic shipped in this same release cycle and was caught in audit: it rated `2.5-flash-lite → 3.1-flash-lite` as "same tier" — silent on the single likeliest fallback, a 3.75× output increase — and rated `haiku-4-5 → 3-5-haiku` costlier when it is actually CHEAPER.** The family word is stable across generations; the price is not. The heuristic was deleted rather than left exported. An offline invariant now fails `npm test` if any `DEFAULTS`/`FALLBACK_CHAINS` id lacks a price, so adding a rung can't silently degrade it to `unknown`. **(3) A blank page is no longer a dead end.** `app.js` is one ES module with ~79 top-level `getElementById` consts; one missing element throws at load, no listener binds, and the auto-updater ships a blank white page to every user with no in-app recovery. New inline boot guard in `index.html`'s `<head>` (it must be outside `app.js` — a handler inside cannot catch `app.js` failing to evaluate): records pre-boot errors, and on `DOMContentLoaded` checks a `window.__curatorBooted` sentinel set on `app.js`'s last line. **Audit-driven correction:** an earlier version panicked on a missing `markdown.js`, which is NOT fatal (`app.js:754` has a designed escaped-text fallback) — and narrowing by filename wasn't enough, because the SPA fallback serves `index.html` with a **200** for any missing path, so the browser parses HTML as JS and throws a `SyntaxError` before `app.js` runs. Only the sentinel is authoritative. Verified: panel shows for a missing/throwing `app.js`, does NOT show for a missing `markdown.js`, a healthy load, or post-boot errors. **(4) Three live CSS bugs, found by a new test.** New offline [test-css-tokens.js](scripts/test-css-tokens.js) (**27 assertions**, dependency-free) fails on any `var(--x)` with no matching definition — the v3.0.12 `--text-dim` class, which fails *silently* because an undefined custom property makes the declaration invalid at computed-value time. On its first run it found 5 undefined names / 9 refs, all pre-existing. Fixed the three with no fallback: `--font-mono` → `--mono` (`.app-version`, `.progress-pct` — both were rendering in the proportional font) and `--text-1` → `--text` (`.provider-toggle-btn:hover` — the hover colour silently did nothing). **The test was itself audited and corrected: it had baselined away those exact two names, so a re-introduction would have passed silently** — now guarded by named positive assertions. The remaining three (`--text-primary`, `--text-secondary`, `--surface-1`) carry hex fallbacks, render correctly, and are deliberately left for the design-system token work. **(5) `.write-lock` files are no longer synced.** `DOMAINS_GITIGNORE_RULES` covered only `*/raw/` and `*/.mcp-write-log.jsonl`, so the cross-process lock was tracked: a crash leaves a stale lock, a sync ships it, and the pulling machine refuses writes for up to 30 minutes because `isPidAlive` sees a foreign PID. Realistically reachable because the write-registry that 409s Sync during a write is **per-process** — the MCP server is a separate process, so `compile_to_wiki` holds the file lock for 15–45s while the web server thinks nothing is happening. Added the rule; **also discovered `ensureDomainsGitignore()` was only ever called from `setup()`**, so existing installs would never have picked up any new rule — now called from `push()`/`pull()` too. Already-committed locks are untracked by `untrackStaleWriteLocks()`, which acts only on paths `git ls-files` itself reports and validates each against a single-segment slug regex before interpolation (the audit could not defeat the regex, the quoting, or git's `core.quotePath` octal escaping). **(6) HIGH, caught in audit: `push()` could fail permanently with an empty error.** `push()`'s commit was the only one of three commit sites without a `'nothing to commit'` guard — latent until the untrack made it reachable: the untrack stages a deletion, `git add -A` cancels it, and the unguarded commit throws. `friendlyError` maps that message to `null`, so the user could see an **empty** error with no way to recover. Guarded to match `pull()`/`setup()`; also hardened `ensureDomainsGitignore()` to force-rewrite on any `\r` (a CRLF `.gitignore` trim-matched as "rule present" while git, which does not strip `\r`, matched nothing — the trigger). **(7) Upload rejections return JSON, not an HTML stack trace.** There is no error-handling middleware anywhere in `src/`, and the frontend does `await res.json()` on non-streaming ingest responses — so a `.docx` or a >50MB file produced Express's HTML error page and a cryptic `Unexpected token '<'` (the same class fixed once in v2.3.3). New 4-arg middleware returns `400` for an unsupported type and `413` for oversize, whitelisting via `err.curatorUserFacing` so raw `fs` errors (`ENOSPC`/`EACCES`, which carry absolute paths) get a generic message instead. **(8) multer temp-file leak.** Every upload wrote a temp file that was never unlinked — including rejected and duplicate ones. Now cleaned on every exit path via an outer `try/finally`; verified the `fileFilter` and size-limit paths never write to disk in the first place. Plus `"license": "MIT"` added to `package.json` (it was missing). **Verification:** **29 offline suites green** (28 + the new CSS suite; `test-chat-model.js` grew 32 → 76 assertions); live on **real Gemini AND Anthropic** — provider routing 5/5, truncation guard 28/28, real multi-phase Gemini ingest 17/17 (29 pages, atomic-write contract held, no orphaned temp files); live chain-walk confirmed the fallback fires and populates `getFallbackStatus()`; browser-verified on an isolated :3334 instance (tempdir domains) for the healthy-boot negative case, the three cost states, and the CSS fixes; H1 reproduced against real git *before* the fix and confirmed resolved after; secret scan clean across 176 tracked files. **Docs:** model-lifecycle.md (chain + forward-ordering rationale + price table + release-checklist step), user-guide.md (fallback banner incl. the cost line; blank-page recovery moved to §18 Troubleshooting), sync.md (`.write-lock` row), CONTRIBUTING.md (the new suite). **Deferred to v3.0.16:** `.DS_Store` is also tracked by the knowledge repo (3 files) and adds sync noise; the local stale lock is untracked but not deleted; `pull()` doesn't self-heal; non-ASCII domain names skip the untrack silently; the CSS scanner doesn't cover `var()` inside `app.js` template literals. |
| `v3.0.14` | **Compile to Wiki no longer shrinks the chat into a "second window" (community-reported UI bug).** A user reported that clicking **Compile to Wiki** "opens a second window, restricting and compressing subsequent chat dialogues into a small, unadjustable area". Confirmed and measured: the compile outcome rendered into `#compile-result.result-panel`, a **sibling** of `#chat-thread` sitting between the thread and the composer, styled `flex-shrink: 0; max-height: 38vh; overflow-y: auto`. Since `.chat-thread` is the `flex: 1` element of the `.chat-main` column, the panel took its height straight out of the message area and never gave it back — on a 720px viewport the thread collapsed **429px → 127px** the instant a compile finished, and the chat gained a second scrollbar. Worse, it was cleared only by `showChatEmpty()` / `renderThread()` — the send handler never touched it — so **every subsequent message in that conversation stayed compressed** until the user switched threads. There was no close button and no resize handle, hence "unadjustable". **Fix:** the outcome is now an inline card (`.chat-compile-card`) appended into `#chat-thread`; the fixed panel is deleted outright (element, `compileResultEl` ref, and the `.result-panel` CSS). The card scrolls with the conversation, sits in conversation order, and costs the message area nothing — verified end-to-end with real compiles: thread height **429px → 429px**, no nested scrollbar, follow-up messages render at full size. **Independent adversarial audit (fresh agent, browser-measured) confirmed the panel was completely gone with no dangling refs and no XSS, and caught three real regressions the first browser pass missed** — all fixed and re-verified: **(1)** scrolling the *thread* to its bottom buried the card's own headline (title + ✨/✏️ counts) for any card taller than the thread — i.e. every 18+-page compile, and a 20–40-page compile is the normal case; new `scrollCardIntoView(card)` puts the card's TOP at the top of the thread viewport (verified: 463px card in a 327px thread, title visible, offset 8px). **(2)** The old panel's own `overflow` had been containing horizontal overflow; without it a long `+7 bullets in Key Facts, Related, Entities Mentioned, Concepts Introduced or Referenced` detail (`white-space: nowrap; flex-shrink: 0`) pushed the overflow out to `.chat-thread` and scrolled **every message bubble** sideways — `#app` is capped at 960px so the thread is ~667px on every machine and an 87-char detail suffices. Contained via `.chat-compile-card .change-summary { overflow-x: auto; }` — deliberately on the inner block, NOT the card, because a flex item whose overflow isn't `visible` has its automatic `min-height` resolve to **0**, which would let the thread squeeze the card back into a scroll box (the exact bug being removed). Verified: summary 635/617 overflows, thread 667/667 does not. **(3)** A compile runs 15–45s with the UI live, so switching conversations mid-compile dropped the card into an unrelated transcript, and New Chat mid-compile produced a headerless thread containing only a floating card (`appendCompileCard` was un-hiding the thread). Now the handler captures `activeConvId` + `chatDomain` at click time and the single guarded renderer `renderCompileOutcome()` refuses to append if either changed (pages are still written; wiki/domain refresh still runs); `appendCompileCard` no longer touches thread visibility. Both races re-tested live with a delayed-response fetch: 0 cards misfiled, empty state intact, button + progress reset correctly. **Verification:** new offline [test-chat-compile-card.js](scripts/test-chat-compile-card.js) (**36 assertions** — panel gone everywhere, single guarded entry point, navigated-away guard, `escHtml` on the empty-state title, and the standing invariants that the card carries no `max-height`/`overflow`/`flex-shrink` while the summary contains `overflow-x`, so this bug cannot return); all **28 offline suites green**; live [test-beta27-compile-live.js](scripts/test-beta27-compile-live.js) **16/16 on real Gemini AND Anthropic** (backend compile path untouched); browser-verified on an isolated :3334 instance (tempdir domains, real Gemini) across success / second-compile / refused / error cards, ingest-panel non-regression, and the two race conditions. **Frontend-only: no backend, schema, route, or API change.** **Docs:** ingestion-pipeline §10f (new, with the invariants), user-guide §9. **Also investigated, NOT a bug (left as designed):** the same user reported the Sync badge incrementing right after a chat message with no compile. Chat conversations are intentionally tracked by the sync repo (`domains/.gitignore` excludes only `*/raw/` and `*/.mcp-write-log.jsonl`) so threads follow you across machines — documented in [sync.md](docs/sync.md). Verified empirically: one new conversation file = exactly one `git status --porcelain` entry = badge "1". |
| `v3.0.13` | **Chat model selector now mirrors SAVED Settings keys, not `.env` (community-reported correctness bug).** A user with both keys saved **Disconnected** their Anthropic key in Settings, but (a) the chat Model dropdown still offered Claude, and (b) selecting Claude STILL used Claude — the key was gone from Settings yet the chat kept calling it. **Root cause:** the v3.0.11 selector keyed availability off `getEffectiveKey` (config **OR** `.env`), and the user's Anthropic key lived in **both** config and `.env`; Disconnect only empties the config copy, so `getEffectiveKey` still resolved the `.env` key → the provider stayed "available" and `normalizeChatProvider` still honoured the override. **Fix — the per-chat provider is now strictly config-scoped:** `normalizeChatProvider` ([chat.js](src/brain/chat.js)) gates on `getApiKeys()` (config only, NOT `getEffectiveKey`), so a Disconnected provider is refused as an override and falls back to the global provider; `/api/config/api-keys` ([routes/config.js](src/routes/config.js)) dropped the misleading `geminiUsable`/`anthropicUsable` (config-or-env) fields, and the frontend selector ([app.js](src/public/app.js)) keys off config-only `hasGeminiKey`/`hasAnthropicKey`. The selector also re-initialises at the end of `loadApiKeyStatus` so a Settings Save/Disconnect/switch reflects in the chat dropdown immediately (not just on tab entry). The `.env` fallback still drives the GLOBAL provider (documented dev behaviour); only the per-chat selector is config-scoped. **Verified end-to-end** (in-app browser on :3334, with Anthropic already Disconnected in the shared config so `.env` still holds the key): the Model dropdown is correctly hidden (only Gemini in Settings), and a chat POST with `provider:'anthropic'` echoes `provider:null` (refused → global Gemini answers), while `gemini` is honoured and garbage falls back — the exact "used Claude despite disconnect" path is closed. **Verification:** offline [test-chat-model.js](scripts/test-chat-model.js) grew to **32 assertions** (`normalizeChatProvider` is config-relative + a **.env-only key does NOT make a config-less provider chat-selectable**; source guards that chat.js uses `getApiKeys` not a `getEffectiveKey(` call, the route no longer exposes usable flags, and the frontend keys off `hasXKey` + re-inits from `loadApiKeyStatus`). All **27 offline suites green**. **No schema change; two response fields removed (additive-only consumers unaffected); behaviour is now stricter (safer).** **Docs:** ingestion-pipeline §10e rewritten (availability = saved Settings keys). |
| `v3.0.12` | **Chat composer visual polish (two user-reported CSS bugs on the v3.0.11 composer).** **(1) Unreadable dropdown text.** The Length/Model dropdown menu option titles + descriptions (and the composer button labels) rendered in near-black on the dark menu — root cause: the CSS referenced `var(--text-dim)`, a variable that **does not exist** in the theme (the real vars are `--text`/`--text-2`/`--text-muted`), so the invalid `color` declaration fell back to the `<button>`'s UA-default black. Fixed by switching those to `--text-2` (readable light-grey) and giving `.chat-dd-opt` an explicit base `color: var(--text)` so nothing inherits black. Verified computed colors: title `#cdd6f4`, desc `#a6adc8` on a `#24243a` menu. **(2) "Box within a box".** The `.chat-input` textarea sat inside `.chat-composer` but the GLOBAL `textarea:focus` rule still applied its `box-shadow` focus glow to it, drawing an inner rounded border around the placeholder when focused — so the composer looked like two nested boxes instead of the intended single Claude-style container. Fixed with `.chat-input:focus { border: none; box-shadow: none; }` (same specificity as the global rule, later in the file → wins), so only `.chat-composer:focus-within` shows the one outer accent glow. **CSS-only; no JS/backend/schema/route change** (the `--text-dim` typo affected only these new v3.0.11 composer rules). Browser-verified end-to-end on :3334: both dropdowns' text is legible and the composer is one unified box (textarea + Length/Model dropdowns + Send integrated, no inner border). All **27 offline suites green**. No doc change needed — purely cosmetic; the v3.0.11 feature docs (user-guide §9, ingestion-pipeline §10e, model-lifecycle.md) already cover the selectors. |
| `v3.0.11` | **Chat composer redesign — per-chat model selector + Length dropdown (Claude-style composer, Curator theme).** The two stacked bars (a `LENGTH` pill row above a separate input+Send strip) became ONE rounded composer with the controls tucked inside at the bottom, like Claude's composer. Two changes: **(1) Length** (Concise/Balanced/Detailed) is now a **dropdown** (opens upward, default Balanced) instead of three pills. **(2) New per-chat Model selector** — a user with BOTH provider keys can pick **Gemini** or **Claude** for a single chat WITHOUT changing the global Settings provider (great for comparing models); with one key it's hidden. The selector picks the **provider** — the model id is always the current `DEFAULTS[provider]`, so users never pick a specific version and the label auto-updates when we bump a default (directly addressing the pending Gemini `2.5-flash-lite` deprecation: the selector will reflect the successor the moment `DEFAULTS.gemini` is bumped — no frontend change). **Backend** ([llm.js](src/brain/llm.js)): `getProviderInfo(preferProvider)` gained an override (honoured only if that provider has a usable key via `getEffectiveKey`, else falls through to the global provider); new `getDefaultModel(provider)` export (applies a `LLM_MODEL` dev-override only for the active provider, so no cross-provider model mismatch); threaded through `callLLM(..., providerOverride)` and `generateText(…, { provider })`. [chat.js](src/brain/chat.js): `normalizeChatProvider` (only a keyed `gemini`/`anthropic` survives; anything else → `null` → global) → `sendMessage` passes `{ provider }` and returns the resolved provider. [routes/chat.js](src/routes/chat.js) reads `provider` from the body. [routes/config.js](src/routes/config.js): `/api/config/api-keys` now also returns `geminiUsable`/`anthropicUsable` (via `getEffectiveKey` — config OR `.env`, matching the real call path) and `models: { gemini, anthropic }`. **Frontend** ([index.html](src/public/index.html)+[app.js](src/public/app.js)+[styles.css](src/public/styles.css)): unified `.chat-composer`; a generic idempotent `wireDropdown` (a single module-level document handler for outside-click/Escape — no listener stacking); `initChatModelSelector` builds the model menu only when ≥2 usable providers, persists the choice in `localStorage`, and **re-initialises on Chat-tab entry** so a key added in Settings this session appears without a reload; the send POST carries `provider` (null → global). **Independent adversarial audit (fresh agent):** the backend override (no injection, no key bypass, no wrong-provider model, correct fallback + error text) and the menu XSS were rated **airtight**; it caught one real MEDIUM — the selector only initialised once at load (adding a 2nd key mid-session didn't reveal it until reload) and the related re-entrancy NIT (document listeners could stack). Both fixed (idempotent re-init on tab entry; module-level listeners; onSelect validates against the live provider list). **Security note:** a client value never reaches the SDK as a model — only a strictly-validated provider name; the model id is always server-chosen from `DEFAULTS`. **Verification:** offline [test-chat-model.js](scripts/test-chat-model.js) (**30 assertions** — `getDefaultModel`, `normalizeChatProvider` on every invalid input incl. prototype keys, key-backed honouring, end-to-end source guards; config-independent) + live [test-chat-model-live.js](scripts/test-chat-model-live.js) (**5 on real Gemini AND Anthropic** — routing resolves to each provider, both answer through the override, garbage falls back). **Browser-verified end-to-end** (in-app browser on :3334, both keys usable): the composer renders, both dropdowns open upward/select/close, outside-click closes, double re-init keeps exactly 2 model options (no stacking), and direct API calls confirmed `provider:'anthropic'`→Claude / `'gemini'`→Gemini / garbage→null-fallback all answer. All **27 offline suites green**; live chat sweep (truncation + intent + style + model + ordering) green on both providers; config restored; test conversations cleaned. **Additive/behavioural only:** new exports + defaulted params (back-compatible), two new api-keys response fields, one optional route field, one UI control; no schema change. **Docs:** user-guide §9 (composer + model selector), ingestion-pipeline §10e (provider-override plumbing + invariants), model-lifecycle.md (selector auto-reflects `DEFAULTS`). |
| `v3.0.10` | **Chat response-style ordering fix + Markdown rendering (two user-reported polish items on the v3.0.9 work).** After v3.0.9 shipped, live testing of the response-style selector on a content-rich question exposed two issues. **(1) Non-monotonic length ordering.** The styles are supposed to be Concise < Balanced < Detailed, but an actual run came back Concise < Detailed < **Balanced** — Balanced overshot. Root cause: `balanced.directive` was deliberately **empty** (to stay byte-identical to the pre-Tier-2 prompt), so on a rich question the model ran to its natural maximum verbosity — often longer than `comprehensive`, whose "be thorough but not a longer list" directive ironically made it more disciplined. **Fix** ([chat.js](src/brain/chat.js) `RESPONSE_STYLES`): `balanced` gained a soft directive ("cover the key points and important nuances… NOT exhaustive") and `comprehensive` was strengthened ("this should be your LONGEST, most detailed answer") — the anti-catalogue-dump rule in comprehensive is unchanged. The 4-arg `buildPrompt` still equals the 5-arg `'balanced'` (default param), so no caller/test regresses. The live suite now asserts the **full monotonic ordering** `concise < balanced < comprehensive` on both providers — verified stable across two runs (Gemini 940<1376<4612 then 976<2066<2503; Claude 1629<2072<3822 then 1808<2094<5188). **(2) Raw Markdown in the viewer.** Chat answers showed `###`, `**bold**`, `*bullets*`, and `[[wikilinks]]` literally. **New** [src/public/markdown.js](src/public/markdown.js) — a dependency-free, **XSS-safe** renderer (`window.renderChatMarkdown`) wired into `appendMessage` for assistant bubbles: it HTML-escapes the whole string FIRST, then inserts only a fixed allow-list of tags (`<strong>`, `<em>`, `<code>`, `<pre>`, `<ul>/<ol>/<li>`, `<p>`, `<div class="md-h">`, styled `<span>`s for `[source:]` chips + `[[wikilink]]`) — no user text ever lands in an attribute or URL, and there's no `href`/`src` sink. User bubbles stay plain text; CSS added for the rendered elements. **Independent adversarial audit (fresh agent)** confirmed the XSS surface is airtight and the directive change is non-regressing, and caught two real LOW bugs — a **forgeable ` CODEn ` code-span sentinel** (a literal `CODE0` in an answer got corrupted) and a **mild ReDoS** in the citation regex. Both fixed: inline code is now isolated by a **structural split** (no forgeable text sentinel), and the citation regex dropped the `\s*` adjacency (trim in the callback). **Verification:** offline [test-chat-markdown.js](scripts/test-chat-markdown.js) (**30 assertions** — XSS vectors incl. `<script>`/`<img onerror>`/attribute-breakout via `[source:]`+`[[…]]`, block+inline formatting, citation/wikilink rendering, the code-sentinel-forgery guard, defensive inputs) + [test-chat-style.js](scripts/test-chat-style.js) grew to **58** (balanced-directive-present + monotonic-intent assertions); live [test-chat-style-live.js](scripts/test-chat-style-live.js) grew to **17 on real Gemini AND Anthropic** (full ordering). **Browser-verified end-to-end** (in-app browser on :3334): a real Detailed answer rendered 3 headings + 14 bold + 14 list items with `rawMarkdownLeak: false`, and the renderer output is XSS-clean. All **26 offline suites green**; live style ordering + intent (Test 3) + Test 2 composition all green on both providers; test conversations cleaned up; config restored. **Additive/behavioural only:** one new frontend file, tuned directives, CSS; no schema/route/API change. **Known follow-up:** a few citation chips show `a.md, source: b.md` glued (the model wrote `[source: a.md, source: b.md]`) — cosmetic, tracked. |
| `v3.0.9` | **Chat response-style control — Concise / Balanced / Detailed (Tier 2 of the chat-quality work; the user-adjustable "sensitivity" feature).** A per-question **Length** selector in the Chat tab lets the user dial how much detail the answer carries. It is **ORTHOGONAL** to the v3.0.8 intent routing: intent (`detectQueryIntent`) picks the answer SHAPE (decision / enumerate / synthesis); the response style picks the DETAIL and LENGTH. **Backend** ([chat.js](src/brain/chat.js)): new exported `RESPONSE_STYLES` table — `concise` (cap 4096; "short and direct, 1–3 paragraphs, 2–3 sources"), `balanced` (cap 8192; **empty directive → byte-identical to the pre-Tier-2 prompt**, so every existing caller/test is unaffected), `comprehensive` (cap 12288; "be thorough… more citations where they add value; NEVER reproduce the catalogue — depth means better reasoning, not a longer list"). New exported `normalizeResponseStyle()` maps any value to a known style, defaulting to `balanced`; `buildPrompt(domain, pages, history, userMessage, responseStyle='balanced')` appends the style directive AFTER the intent block; `sendMessage(domain, id, message, opts={})` normalises `opts.responseStyle`, uses the style's `maxTokens` cap, and returns the resolved `responseStyle`. **Route** ([routes/chat.js](src/routes/chat.js)): reads optional `responseStyle` from the body and passes it through (normalisation makes any absent/garbage value safe). **Frontend** ([index.html](src/public/index.html)+[app.js](src/public/app.js)+[styles.css](src/public/styles.css)): a segmented **Length: Concise · Balanced · Detailed** control above the input, persisted in `localStorage`, sent with each message; "Detailed" is the friendly label for `comprehensive`. **Independent adversarial audit (fresh agent)** confirmed the feature is orthogonal and non-regressing and caught one real (currently-masked, contract-violating) bug: `normalizeResponseStyle('__proto__')` / `'constructor'` slipped through the truthiness guard because `RESPONSE_STYLES` is a plain object — fixed with an `Object.hasOwn` own-property check + regression tests. **Verification:** offline [test-chat-style.js](scripts/test-chat-style.js) (**55 assertions** — normalisation incl. prototype-key inputs + always-usable cap/directive, the cap/directive table, 4-arg `buildPrompt` ≡ 5-arg `balanced` no-regression, orthogonality to intent, comprehensive-keeps-the-anti-dump-rules, route+sendMessage wiring); live [test-chat-style-live.js](scripts/test-chat-style-live.js) (**15 assertions on real Gemini AND Anthropic** — concise < comprehensive in length on both, all focused, no catalogue echo, garbage→balanced, and a comprehensive+enumerate no-dump guard). **Browser-verified end-to-end** (in-app browser on a throwaway :3334 instance): the selector renders, toggles active state, persists, and a live API sweep showed concise 1607 / comprehensive 2408 chars with garbage→balanced. All **25 offline suites green**; the full live chat sweep — v3.0.7 truncation (28) + Tier 1 intent (16) + Tier 2 style (15) — green on both providers. Test conversations cleaned up; the dev machine's real config (active provider) restored byte-exact. **Additive only:** two new exported helpers, an extended `buildPrompt`/`sendMessage` signature (defaulted params — back-compatible), one optional route field, one new UI control; no schema change, and `balanced` reproduces the exact prior behaviour. **Known follow-up (user-flagged, not in this release):** chat answers render as raw markdown in the viewer (`###`, `**bold**`, `[[wikilinks]]` shown literally) — a rendering polish, tracked separately. |
| `v3.0.8` | **Chat gives a focused answer instead of dumping the whole domain (community-reported; Tier 1 of 2).** Right after v3.0.7 raised the chat output cap, the SAME user re-ran his decision question ("evaluate these three newsletter topic ideas and recommend one") and got a HUGE, redundant answer — ~160 sources with duplicates plus a trailing raw blob of glued-together file paths — that never actually recommended anything. v3.0.7 didn't cause this; it EXPOSED a latent problem (before, the same bloat hit 4096 and errored; now it has room to complete). **Root causes, all in [chat.js](src/brain/chat.js): (1) Intent misclassification.** The chat routes a question to an `enumerate` (list-everything) vs `synthesis` prompt via `detectQueryIntent`. His question was flagged enumerate because the word **"everything"** appeared inside his pasted topic idea ("…everything that circles around content") — a content word, not a request for a list. His follow-up ("which concepts have the **most** sources **disagreeing**?") was also flagged enumerate because it started with "which concepts". **(2) The enumerate prompt commanded a dump** ("completeness matters more than synthesis… give a COMPLETE list… do not skip them"), and the model echoed the internal page catalogue verbatim. **Fix:** `detectQueryIntent` now first calls a new `extractAsk()` to classify on the user's ACTUAL ask (interrogative sentences → command-opener sentences → whole text), so list/decision words buried in pasted CONTENT can't hijack routing; it then returns `decision | enumerate | synthesis` via an ordered, audit-hardened router: a NARROW analytical flag (genuine disagreement words + whole-word `differ`, NOT bare "most/least") → **strong list/count COMMAND anchors** (`list`/`how many`/`count`/`what|which <plural-noun>`/`all|every <noun>`), anchored to a clause boundary so they win over incidental decision words AND work after a preamble → **decision** cues (`recommend`/`should I`/`which of these`/`evaluate`/`which…best`) → **weak** list phrases. The bare "everything" trigger is gone. A superlative/disagreement question shaped like a list request diverts to synthesis. New `buildPrompt` blocks: **decision** (lead with a recommendation in sentence 1, cite 3–7 sources, no lists), **enumerate** reshaped to focused+deduped+capped (~40, "…and N more"), **synthesis** hardened to lead with the answer; **all three forbid reproducing the catalogue**, and the catalogue is now labelled "FOR YOUR REFERENCE ONLY". Safety net: new exported `stripCatalogueEcho(answer)` removes any residual run of 5+ bare `folder/slug.md` paths (spaces/tabs or glued only — comma lists, code blocks, and multi-path `[source: …]` citations are preserved; no global whitespace collapse, so code indentation survives), applied in `sendMessage` before citation extraction. **Independent adversarial audit (second agent, three passes) run before shipping** caught real regressions in the drafts — decision words unconditionally beating `^list`/`^how many`, an over-broad analytical override (`least`⊂"at least", `most`⊂"most recent", `differ`⊂"different"), and a clause-boundary anchor that let a pasted "List:" line hijack a decision ask — all fixed (the last via `extractAsk`'s questions-first ask-extraction) and covered by new tests. **Verification:** new offline [test-chat-intent.js](scripts/test-chat-intent.js) (**77 assertions** — the two reported questions, decision/enumerate/synthesis matrix, the audit regression classes, `extractAsk` focus extraction, and the echo stripper incl. comma-list/code-block/multi-path-citation preservation); new live [test-chat-intent-live.js](scripts/test-chat-intent-live.js) (**16 assertions on real Gemini AND Anthropic** — decision + analytical questions on the large `articles` domain return FOCUSED answers <9k chars with no catalogue echo; drives the prompt path with no conversation-file side effects). Robin's exact Q1 now returns a 1,308-char answer that names a single recommended topic, gives grounded reasoning, and says why the other two are weaker (vs the ~20k-char dump). Regression: all **24 offline suites green**; beta13 (chat retrieval) 62/62; live truncation (v3.0.7) still green on both providers. **Additive/behavioural only:** `detectQueryIntent` gains a `decision` return, one new exported helper, three reshaped prompts; no schema/route/API change. **Tier 2 (a user-adjustable Concise/Balanced/Comprehensive response-style control) is a separate, later release.** |
| `v3.0.7` | **Chat no longer hard-fails with a misleading ingest error on a long question (community-reported).** A user pasted an analytical multi-part question into the Chat tab ("evaluate these three newsletter topic ideas and recommend one") and got a hard error: *"⚠ Gemini hit the output token limit (4096 tokens)… split the source into smaller parts (e.g. by chapter) and ingest each separately… the Phase 2 batch size in src/brain/ingest.js may need tuning."* Two real defects, both fixed. **Defect 1 — the MAX_TOKENS message was ingest-specific but the guard is a SHARED chokepoint.** The output-token-limit guard lives in `callProvider` in [llm.js](src/brain/llm.js) — the single path ALL LLM calls flow through (chat, query, health-AI, shared-brain, compile, ingest) — yet both provider branches threw hard-coded ingest advice ("split the source by chapter", "tune the Phase 2 batch size in src/brain/ingest.js"). For a chat question there is no source, no chapters, no ingest, no Phase 2 — the user was told to edit `ingest.js`. (This is the same class of leak the v3.0.1-beta.27 fix cleaned up for *compile*, but only locally; the shared guard still leaked into chat and every other caller.) **Defect 2 — chat HARD-FAILED on truncation instead of degrading.** Chat is text mode; a truncated prose answer is still useful, but the guard treated every MAX_TOKENS as a fatal "cannot be parsed" JSON error, so the user got NOTHING instead of a 95%-complete answer. Chat also had no truncation handling at all (unlike ingest/compile, which got fallback ladders) and a tight 4096-token cap. **Fix:** new exported `handleOutputTokenLimit(providerName, maxTokens, responseFormat, partialText)` in llm.js, called from both provider branches. **JSON mode** still THROWS — the message stays context-neutral (no ingest vocabulary) but deliberately keeps the phrase *"output token limit"* so `isOutputTokenLimit(err)` (load-bearing for ingest/compile fallback ladders) keeps matching. **Text mode** RETURNS the partial answer with a clear appended note (*"⚠ This answer was cut off because it reached the response length limit… ask a more specific question to see the rest."*) instead of throwing. Chat + query output caps raised 4096 → **8192** ([chat.js](src/brain/chat.js), [query.js](src/brain/query.js)) so most analytical questions fit outright; over-long ones degrade to partial-with-note. **Blast radius:** the JSON-mode throw path is unchanged in behaviour — `isOutputTokenLimit` still returns true (verified offline AND against a real truncated Gemini/Anthropic JSON call), so ingest's page-by-page fallback, single-pass→multi-phase switch, and compile's full→concise→summary-only ladder all still fire; the only user-visible change on those paths is a cleaner final message if every fallback is exhausted. Non-token LLM errors (503/429/auth/network) are untouched. **Verification:** new offline [test-chat-truncation.js](scripts/test-chat-truncation.js) (**32 assertions** — helper contract for both modes incl. empty/null partial, `isOutputTokenLimit` still true on the new JSON throw, source-guards that ingest vocabulary is gone from llm.js and chat/query request 8192); new live [test-chat-truncation-live.js](scripts/test-chat-truncation-live.js) (**28 assertions on REAL Gemini AND Anthropic** — forced truncation at maxTokens=48: text mode returns partial+note and does NOT throw; JSON mode throws, satisfies `isOutputTokenLimit`, carries no ingest vocabulary; provider-switch config-restored byte-exact). Regression: all **23 offline suites green**; beta27 compile-live (JSON-fallback on real Anthropic) 16/16; beta8 live ingest (real Gemini multi-phase) 17/17; a real end-to-end chat turn on the `articles` domain returned a complete 3,301-char answer with 7 citations (no truncation, no vocab leak). **Additive only:** one new exported helper, two output-cap bumps; no schema/route/API change. |
