| `v2.1.0` | Remove Stop button + /api/shutdown; server runs until quit; update rebuilds .app; build-app.sh |
| `f80b2db` | Absolute node path in AppleScript — fixes "node: No such file or directory"; process.execPath in restart; CURATOR_NO_OPEN prevents double browser tabs |
| `c5eddef` | Auto-refresh UI state after ingest, sync, and tab switches — domain stats, wiki tab, and dropdowns update without manual browser reload |
| `v2.3.0`  | My Curator MCP — local stdio MCP server exposes 10 tools to Claude Desktop (7 retrieval + 3 graph-native: graph_overview, tags, backlinks). `mcp/` directory, `/api/mcp` routes, Settings-tab wizard with visual diff + self-test button. Existing wikis work as-is; no re-ingest required. Scalable-by-default responses (compact summaries + size guard); path-traversal hardening via `resolveInsideBase()` + slug/domain validators in `mcp/util.js`; `execFile` for reveal-config. Added optional step 4 to the onboarding overlay. |
| `v2.3.1`  | MCP response-budget correction. Dropped `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` 900 KB → 400 KB (~100 k tokens) so multi-turn conversations don't blow the context window on a single tool call. Reworked `get_connected_nodes` with `max_nodes` default 60, ranked by hop+degree, shorter previews, max depth 2: on the real 2116-node articles domain, depth-2 response dropped 575 KB → 39 KB. All other tools unchanged. See `docs/audits/2026-04-20.md` addendum. |
| `v2.3.2`  | Auto-updater made crash-resilient. Replaced `git pull origin main` with `git fetch origin main` + `git reset --hard origin/main` — plain pull aborted on end-user machines whenever `npm install` had regenerated `package-lock.json` with a machine-specific diff. Tracked files hard-sync to remote; gitignored user data (`domains/`, `.curator-config.json`, `.sync-config.json`) is untouched. Response now returns `from`/`to` short SHAs so the UI can show exactly what moved. |
| `v2.3.3`  | "Restart needed" detection across the UI. `/api/version` now returns `{version, onDiskVersion, restartRequired}` — compares the version cached at server startup with the current on-disk `package.json`. When these diverge (user ran the manual `git reset --hard` recovery but didn't relaunch the .app), three places surface it clearly: header badge turns amber and shows "v2.2.2 · restart" on hover; **Check for Updates** button displays "Files are updated (vX) but running app is still vY — please quit and relaunch"; the MCP section detects HTML coming back from missing `/api/mcp/*` routes (SPA fallthrough) and replaces the cryptic `Unexpected token '<'` JSON parse error with a plain-English restart prompt. |
| `v2.3.4`  | Ghost-domain fix after sync-delete. When another machine deletes a domain via sync, git-pull removes every tracked file but leaves empty directories behind (git doesn't track empty dirs), so the deleted domain's shell (`conversations/`, `raw/`, `wiki/`) appeared as a ghost in the Domains list. Fix: `listDomains()` in both `src/brain/files.js` and `mcp/storage/local.js` now requires a `CLAUDE.md` schema for a directory to count as a domain — ghosts and unrelated files (`Untitled.base`, stray `.md`) are filtered out. `sync.pull()` additionally prunes ghost directories after every pull by recursively removing any `domains/<name>/` that has no schema — sync-delete is now end-to-end. |
| `v2.3.5`  | Subprocess-PATH fix for auto-updater and sync. When The Curator is launched via the `.app` wrapper, AppleScript's `do shell script` starts the Node process with a minimal PATH (`/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`) — enough to find `git` (in `/usr/bin` from Xcode CLT) but not `npm`, which lives next to Node in `/usr/local/bin` or `/opt/homebrew/bin`. Every subprocess spawned by the updater inherited this bare PATH, so `npm install` failed with `npm: command not found`. Fix: `SUBPROCESS_ENV` prepends the Node binary's directory plus common Homebrew/system prefixes to every `execAsync` call in `src/routes/config.js` (update + pick-folder + update-check) and `src/brain/sync.js` (all git operations). Same fix pattern as the absolute-node-path trick used in `scripts/build-app.sh`. |
| `v2.3.6`  | Updater partial-success recovery. Resolves the catch-22 where users on a pre-v2.3.5 running app couldn't install v2.3.5 because the npm-not-found bug was in the very updater trying to apply the fix. Now when `npm install` fails specifically with `npm: command not found` AND the `git reset` already succeeded, the endpoint returns `{ok:true, partial:true, from, to, warning}` instead of an error. The frontend surfaces the warning in the restart banner and proceeds with the auto-restart — which loads the fixed updater in the new process. Any OTHER npm error (real dependency issues) still re-throws and is reported normally, so we never auto-restart into a broken-deps state. |
| `v2.3.7`  | Accurate sync file counts in UI. Bug: after a big ingest, push reported "6 files synced" when ~200 had actually moved. Root cause: the fallback count used `git diff --stat --name-only origin/main~1..origin/main` AFTER the push, which only counted files in the most recent commit (typically a merge commit with a tiny delta) instead of the union of files across all unpushed commits. Fix: `push()` now counts `git diff --name-only origin/main..HEAD` BEFORE the push (union across every unpushed commit); `pull()` counts `HEAD..origin/main` after fetch-but-before-merge. Both return `{filesChanged, commitsAhead/Pulled, files: [preview]}`. Frontend now shows per-direction counts in bidirectional sync (e.g. "Pulled 5 files from GitHub, pushed 197 files to GitHub") and the pruned-domain list explicitly when a sync-delete propagated. |
| `v2.3.8`  | Onboarding fixes. (1) `install.sh` was calling `bash start.sh` on a file that was removed in commit `6b0889c` ("app lifecycle redesign") — fresh installs since then fell back to the "taking longer than expected" yellow warning instead of auto-launching. Replaced with `nohup "${NODE_PATH}" src/server.js ... &` using the absolute node path we already resolve at line 141. (2) `${INSTALL_CHOICE,,}` lowercasing syntax needs bash 4+; macOS ships bash 3.2. Replaced with `tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]'` so the interactive prompt works on clean Macs that don't have Homebrew's bash. (3) README version badge switched from `github/v/release/...` (reads GitHub Releases — stale at v2.1.0 because we push commits, not tagged releases) to `github/package-json/v/...` which reads `package.json` on `main` — auto-updates with every version bump. |
| `v2.3.9`  | Wiki Health "Fix" for missing backlinks now actually writes. Bug: clicking Fix returned `{ok:true, fixed:1}` but the flagged file was unchanged. Two root causes: (a) `injectSummaryBacklinks` only did hyphen-normalised resolution after the exact-entities check, so when both `concepts/email.md` and `concepts/e-mail.md` existed, `[[email]]` fuzzy-matched `e-mail.md` first (alphabetical `Array.find`) and the backlink went to the wrong file; (b) `fixMissingBacklink` was re-running the whole bulk-resolve machinery instead of using the scan's already-resolved `issue.entity`. Fixes: resolution order in `injectSummaryBacklinks` is now `entities/exact → concepts/exact → hyphen-normalised (entities then concepts)` — matching the scan's own logic; added `injectSingleBacklink(path, slug, title)` that trusts the caller-provided path; `fixMissingBacklink` now uses it with `issue.entity`. Verified end-to-end on the real articles domain: scan → Fix → rescan returns 0 missing. |
| `v2.4.0`  | Model-lifecycle safety net. Phase 0 of the AI Wiki Health roadmap. When a provider retires the pinned default model, every call would otherwise 404. Fix: `llm.js` now wraps `callProvider()` in a chain — primary → `FALLBACK_CHAINS[provider]` — triggered only on model-not-found errors (429/503 still go through the existing retry path). Module-level `_activeFallback` tracks which fallback is in use and is exposed via `getFallbackStatus()`. `/api/config/api-keys` response grows a `fallback` field; Settings UI renders an amber "Using fallback model — run Check for Updates" banner when populated. Verified end-to-end with `LLM_MODEL=gemini-nonexistent` override: chat, ingest, MCP, health, sync all still work; fallback logged + surfaced; clears automatically when primary returns. Full user-facing guide: `docs/model-lifecycle.md`. |
| `v2.4.1`  | Anthropic default switched from `claude-sonnet-4-6` to **`claude-haiku-4-5`** — Anthropic's low-cost tier, matching the cost profile of Gemini's `gemini-2.5-flash-lite`. Users who want higher quality can opt in via `LLM_MODEL=claude-sonnet-4-5` in `.env`. Anthropic fallback chain reordered: Haiku variants first (same cost tier), then escalates to Sonnet only if the entire Haiku family is gone: `claude-3-5-haiku-latest → claude-3-5-haiku-20241022 → claude-sonnet-4-5 → claude-3-7-sonnet-latest → claude-3-5-sonnet-latest`. |
| `v3.0.6` | **Shared Brain production hardening — Phase 5 (production test program) + two REAL adapter bugs the live tests caught.** The point of Phase 5 was to run the untested production configuration — real GitHub adapter, real LLMs, real concurrency — and it immediately paid for itself. **Bug 1 (found by the new 5.4 live concurrency test): concurrent CREATE of the same new file threw.** Two writers racing to create a page both see "no file → no sha"; the loser's PUT gets GitHub's 422 `"sha" wasn't supplied`, and the adapter's single immediate retry refetched BEFORE the winner's commit was visible (GitHub contents API is eventually consistent for ~1-2s) → same sha-less PUT → threw. Fix in [sharedbrain-github-adapter.js](src/brain/sharedbrain-github-adapter.js): default `maxRetries` 1 → **3** with a growing 250ms·n backoff before each refetch. Real-world trigger: two admins synthesizing simultaneously creating the same new collective page/index. **Bug 2 (found by the new 5.1 live revoke E2E — the highest-risk gap in the whole plan): revoke could delete pages and rebuild NOTHING while reporting success.** The revoke resets `state.last-synthesis` then re-runs synthesis — but the rebuild RE-READ the meta it had just written, and GitHub's read-after-write lag served the STALE pre-reset state (old watermark + `processed_ids`), so every surviving contribution was dedup'd as already-processed: "Loading contributions since <old watermark>… No new contributions", `pages_rebuilt: 0`, `ok: true`, collective gutted. Two-part fix: [sharedbrain-revoke.js](src/brain/sharedbrain-revoke.js) resets with the FULL v3.0.3 zero-state (`watermark: null, processed_ids: []` — the old `{at: epoch}` shape also left stale `processed_ids` in play) AND passes it DIRECTLY into the rebuild via a new `runLocalSynthesis(…, {stateOverride})` ([sharedbrain-synthesis.js](src/brain/sharedbrain-synthesis.js)) — no read-after-write dependency at all. Verified live: "Loading contributions since beginning… 1 page rebuilt". **New test suites: (1)** [test-sharedbrain-scenarios.js](scripts/test-sharedbrain-scenarios.js) (OFFLINE, 34 assertions) — 5.8 pull-onto-hand-edited-mirror (edits overwritten, stray pages pruned, readonly marker intact), 5.9 late/back-dated contribution (clock skew or mid-synthesis push) picked up via the watermark window and never double-processed, 5.10 PAT degradation (401/403/network-drop → typed errors, ZERO token bytes in any message incl. an adversarial body-echo, retry bookkeeping preserved + `last_push_at` not advanced), 5.11 scale probe (10 fellows × 50 pages: all written in ~20ms, index complete, idempotent second run), 5.12 two-machine mirror × Personal Sync interplay with REAL git (mirror survives the round-trip with `readonly: true` intact, erasure propagates). **(2)** [test-sharedbrain-llm-live.js](scripts/test-sharedbrain-llm-live.js) (LIVE_CI, 33 assertions live) — the delta-generation and conflict-resolution prompts had NEVER met a real model; now both run on **real Gemini AND real Anthropic Haiku through the real GitHubStorageAdapter** (5.2/5.3): delta payloads parse with grounded string facts surviving the trust boundary on both models; the handcrafted 2017-vs-2015 contradiction got BOTH verdict shapes exercised live (Gemini → 'both' + CONFLICTING SOURCES marker + `conflict_pages`; Haiku → decisively kept 2017) — all shapes parse, no facts silently lost. Config force-switch is crash-safe (on-disk backup). **(3)** [test-sharedbrain-github-live.js](scripts/test-sharedbrain-github-live.js) extended 21 → **39 live assertions**: §6b true concurrent writers (`Promise.allSettled` same page + same meta key → no rejection, final content is exactly one writer's payload) and §6c **revoke E2E against the live repo** (contributions erased, provenance-tainted page deleted, page rebuilt from the survivor WITHOUT the revokee's fact/short-id, conflict marker dissolved, `state/revocations.jsonl` audit carries UUID + token HASH, in-progress marker cleared); read-after-write polls added where fresh commits are read back. **(4)** [test-sharedbrain-routes.js](scripts/test-sharedbrain-routes.js) 55 → **73 assertions**: §5b full admin lifecycle over HTTP — `pending_pages`, unskip (+400), members, rotate (provision → rotate → **rotated-away token 403**), masked listing, and the REVOKE SUCCESS path incl. short-form-confirmation 400 (closes plan 5.5). **(L21 fixed)** the routes suite's config backup now lives ON DISK (`<file>.pre-test-backup`, auto-recovered on the next run after a crash) — the old in-memory Map meant a SIGKILL mid-run permanently wiped the dev machine's real API keys. **(5.6) CI wiring:** `LIVE_CI` gains github-live + routes + llm-live (the workflow's `GITHUB_TEST_*` secrets are no longer dead config; each suite self-skips/degrades without them); OFFLINE gains the scenarios suite (**22 offline suites**). [test-sharedbrain-github-offline.js](scripts/test-sharedbrain-github-offline.js): persistent-conflict scenario pins `maxRetries: 1` + new guard asserting the production default stays ≥3. **Verification:** 22/22 offline green; live sweep on this machine with both providers + `gh auth token` as the GitHub PAT (see CONTRIBUTING for the recipe). **Docs:** CONTRIBUTING.md (LIVE_CI inventory, on-disk config backups, gh-token recipe), shared-brain.md (revoke stateOverride step + write-concurrency note). **App-code changes are exactly the two fixes** (adapter retry default + backoff; revoke reset shape + `stateOverride`) — both additive, no schema/API changes. Remaining for GA (v3.1.0): 5.7 Playwright wizard pass (MCP-driven, per release — a committed playwright devDependency would be installed on every end-user machine by the auto-updater), and 5.13 the structured beta pilot with a real cohort. |
| `v3.0.5` | **Shared Brain production hardening — Phase 4 (admin features; plan in the private SHARED-BRAIN-UPGRADE.md).** Everything an admin needs to actually OPERATE a brain from the UI — before this, revocation was impossible without hand-editing config (no admin token existed, no fellow_id was discoverable). **(4.1) Admin-token provisioning:** `POST /generate-invite` response gains a fresh `admin_token` (`generateAdminToken()` in [routes/sharedbrain.js](src/routes/sharedbrain.js) — `sbat_` + 40 hex, 160 bits); the admin wizard shows it ONCE on step 2 in a warning-styled block next to the invite token (copy button, "store it in your password manager" copy) and stores it on the connection at save; the FIRST generated token is kept if the admin Backs and regenerates (the invite token is deterministic, the admin token is random — silently replacing a noted-down token would strand the admin). New `POST /:id/admin-token/rotate` provisions pre-v3.0.5 connections and rotates after a leak — returns the token once (`{ok, admin_token, rotated}`), writes through `saveSharedBrain` (the single audited credential path). Card Advanced gains **Generate/Rotate admin token** (inline confirm; shown-once display in the card token box with deliberate no-auto-refresh so a re-render can't wipe it before it's copied). `validateConnection` gains `admin_token` rules (single-line 16-200 chars or null; masked-ellipsis round-trip refused — same defense as github_pat). **(4.3) Member directory:** new `groupMembers()`/`listMembers()` in [sharedbrain.js](src/brain/sharedbrain.js) — groups `listContributionsSince(null)` by the storage-PATH-derived fellow id (same trust rule as synthesis; a spoofed payload `fellow_id` cannot merge fellows), returning per-member submission/page counts, first/last contribution dates, and a newline-flattened informational display name; exposed as `GET /:id/members` (+ `self_fellow_id` so the UI can mark "YOU"). **(4.2) Revoke UI:** card Advanced gains **Revoke a contributor…** (visible only when the connection HAS an admin token; hidden for read-only members) → panel loads the member directory → radio picker → admin-token paste + typed `REVOKE-<short-id>` confirmation (the client expands to the API's full-UUID literal — deliberate typing preserved, API contract unchanged) → danger button unlocks only when all three are valid → SSE progress through the v3.0.4 in-flight registry → done message carries the follow-up checklist (contributors must Pull; remove the GitHub collaborator). **(4.4) Invite-token re-display:** connections now persist `data_handling_terms` (validated enum; both wizard paths send it) so **Show invite token** on the card regenerates the ORIGINAL deterministic token from stored metadata; pre-v3.0.5 connections get a caution that the re-displayed token defaults to contributor_retains. **(4.5) Synthesis confirm:** "Run synthesis (admin)" now shows an inline confirm explaining what it does before running (contributors who click it by accident can cancel); read-only members additionally lose the admin-token/revoke affordances. **(user-reported design fix)** the Settings "Enable Shared Brain (beta)" button now matches the Settings pill convention (`btn primary pill` — it was the odd square one out next to System Check's buttons). **Verification:** [test-sharedbrain-hardening.js](scripts/test-sharedbrain-hardening.js) grew 158 → **192 assertions** (groupMembers/listMembers incl. the spoofed-payload identity case + newline-flattened names + sort order; generateAdminToken format/uniqueness; validateConnection admin_token + data_handling_terms matrices incl. masked-ellipsis refusal; Phase-4 source-level guards incl. the button fix); all **21 offline suites green**; "never — ask your admin to run synthesis" finally explains the classic "Pull pulled 0 pages" confusion, which the empty-collective message now also spells out). **(M17)** synthesis result + done event gain additive `conflict_pages[]`; | `v3.0.3` | **Shared Brain production hardening — Phase 2 (data-integrity + trust boundary; plan in the private SHARED-BRAIN-UPGRADE.md).** The correctness core of the hardening effort. **(H3+H4) Processed-submission tracking replaces wall-clock filtering** in [sharedbrain-synthesis.js](src/brain/sharedbrain-synthesis.js): `state.last-synthesis` gains `watermark` (max `contributed_at` over FULLY-processed submissions — derived from contribution stamps, never the admin clock) + a window-bounded `processed_ids` list; contributions list from `watermark − 24h` and dedup by ID, so contributor clock skew and pushes landing mid-synthesis can no longer be silently skipped forever. A submission is marked processed ONLY when every page it touches wrote successfully — failed pages leave their submissions queued (pre-fix, `writePage` failures were swallowed while the state advanced → facts consumed forever). The watermark carries a low-water-mark clamp so it can never advance past an unprocessed submission's listing window. `readPage` errors are no longer coerced to "page missing" — a FILE_TOO_LARGE (GitHub 1 MB cap) or rate-limit throw now fails the page (retried next run) instead of silently discarding all previously accumulated facts. Back-compat: legacy state files (only `at`) are used as the initial watermark; readers check for the `watermark` KEY so `null` ("nothing processed yet") never falls back to `at`. **(H6/H6b) Server-side sanitization at the trust boundary:** new `sanitizeFellowText` (newline-flattening + caps — blocks forged `## Provenance` sections that could shield pages from Article 17 or spoof a victim's short-ID, and `\n## X` attacks that truncated other fellows' facts on the next cycle) + `isSafeLinkSlug` (no `[]|`/newlines — can't break out of `[[…]]`); contributor identity now comes from the storage path, NOT the payload's `fellow_id` field (attribution spoofing closed; mismatches logged). **(H8) Mirror pulls are true mirrors:** `writePage` gains an additive `opts.replace` flag ([files.js](src/brain/files.js) — merge skipped, every other stage unchanged, single-write-path preserved); `pullCollective` uses it AND prunes local pages absent from the collective (gated on a fully-processed pull — a page skipped over a read error never triggers pruning; the empty-collective early return can't wipe a mirror). Erasure finally propagates: revoked content disappears from every contributor's mirror on their next pull, and facts removed by conflict resolution stop being resurrected by the union merge. Pull result gains `pruned`. **(H9) GitHub tree truncation refused:** all three `_apiTree` callers throw typed `SHARED_BRAIN_TREE_TRUNCATED` instead of silently missing files — most critically `listFellowSubmissions`, where truncation meant an INCOMPLETE Article 17 erasure reported as success. **(M1/2.5) Revocation robustness:** `state/revocation-in-progress` marker written before the erasure steps — ordinary synthesis refuses while active (the revoke's own rebuild passes `allowDuringRevocation`), cleared only on full success, so an interrupted revoke can't be papered over; rebuild-synthesis failure now returns `ok:false, partial:true` with explicit re-run guidance (pre-fix the admin saw "Revocation complete" over a gutted collective); audit record gains `rebuild_ok`. **(2.9)** provenance matching is exact-token (bare short-id or full UUID) — the old `includes(shortId)` could delete an innocent page on an 8-hex-char substring collision. **(L6)** admin-token comparison is constant-time (`timingSafeEqual` over sha256 digests). **(M4)** contributions targeting a different `shared_domain` are skipped with a warning (multi-domain repos no longer cross-contaminate) and tracked as foreign-processed. **(M5/L20) Conflict-marker round-trip:** `extractSectionBullets` reconstitutes a `⚠️ CONFLICTING SOURCES` block as ONE multi-line bullet — pre-fix it re-parsed as 3 separate facts with baked-in attribution, stacking degenerate markers and burning LLM re-flagging calls every cycle; dead render branch removed. **(M7 partial)** Jaccard token sets memoized before the O(N²) pair scan (numerically identical, no more per-pair re-tokenisation); >500-facts-per-page soft-cap warning (warn-not-evict — silently dropping a contributor's fact would violate the conservation invariant). **(M8)** missing/unparseable `contributed_at` no longer silently drops a contribution (both adapters include it; garbage stamps never advance the watermark). **(L2)** push timestamp captured BEFORE the change scan (mtime-window gap closed). **(L3)** retry/skip bookkeeping persisted even when the storage write fails (without advancing `last_push_at`). **Deferred with rationale (plan 2.10):** wiring digests + contribution pruning — pruning would break revoke's rebuild-from-contributions model until digests become the per-fellow rebuild source; documented in shared-brain.md's Deferred table. **Verification:** [test-sharedbrain-hardening.js](scripts/test-sharedbrain-hardening.js) grew 61 → **113 assertions** (clock-skew survival, consumed-on-failure recovery via a planted directory blocking a page write, unparseable-stamp inclusion, Provenance-forgery + title/link injection + attribution-spoof corpus, cross-domain filtering, conflict-marker round-trip stability incl. no re-flagging, mirror replace+prune end-to-end incl. deleted-fact non-resurrection, revocation marker + exact-match innocent-page survival, truncated-tree refusal on all three GitHub listing paths via mock fetch, Phase-2 source-level guards); all **21 offline suites green** (incl. the pre-existing synthesis/pull/revoke/push suites — back-compat verified). **Docs:** shared-brain.md (synthesize section: state schema + trust boundary; Deferred table rewritten), shared-brain-admin.md (§8 force-reprocess now also clears `processed_ids`; new truncation + partial-revoke entries; §3 mirror-propagation claim now true and versioned), shared-brain-compliance.md (erasure-propagation paragraph), shared-brain-user-guide.md (pull = true mirror), api-reference.md (pull `pruned`, revoke marker/partial shape). **Additive only:** `writePage` opts param, new synthesis exports, state-file fields, result fields; no schema breaks — old state files and old payloads keep working. |
| `v3.0.2` | **Shared Brain production hardening — Phase 1 of the upgrade plan (surgical bug fixes; full findings + plan in the private SHARED-BRAIN-UPGRADE.md).** A four-track evaluation (backend, routes/security, UI/UX, tests) of the Shared Brain beta found 1 critical + ~10 high-severity issues; this release lands the "stop the bleeding" subset without schema changes. **(C1) Synthesis can no longer be bricked by one malformed contribution:** non-string facts are filtered at the trust boundary (`mergeFactsForPage` + the per-page fact build in [sharedbrain-synthesis.js](src/brain/sharedbrain-synthesis.js)), and the whole per-page body is try/caught so a poisoned payload degrades to a `pages_failed` warning instead of aborting the run (pre-fix, the exception escaped before `state.last-synthesis` advanced → the poisoned contribution re-bricked EVERY future run). Result/summary gain `pages_failed`. **(H1) Failed operations no longer render as success:** push/pull/synthesize routes now emit `ok:false` results as `error` events (previously only revoke checked); the frontend `done` branch prefers the backend's real summary ("Pushed 7 pages. 3 will retry next time.") and re-applies the final status after the post-op card re-render that used to wipe it. **(H2) Push covers ALL opted-in domains:** `POST /:id/push` without an explicit `local_domain` now iterates every `connection.local_domains` entry (previously only `[0]` — other opted-in domains silently never contributed), with per-domain progress and an aggregate done message. **(H7) Shared Brain ops joined the write-registry/file-lock system** (the beta.8 bug class, reintroduced by this newer surface): all four SSE ops check `isUpdateInProgress()` → 409, register writes for their duration (update/restart/sync/delete now 409 mid-op), pull additionally takes the mirror domain's `.write-lock` (MCP coordination), and the frontend busy-state gate covers Shared Brain buttons in both directions (SB ops disable Update/Sync/Delete; ingests disable SB buttons). **(H5) `permanent_skip` recovery — the warn message stopped lying:** a skipped page edited after the last push (mtime check) is un-skipped with a fresh strike counter, matching what the message always claimed; transient provider errors (503/429/network — new `isTransientLlmError()` export in [sharedbrain.js](src/brain/sharedbrain.js)) no longer count toward the 3-strike limit, so an outage can never permanently exclude pages; stale skip/retry entries for deleted pages are pruned. **(M2) Read-only mirror contract enforced in-app, not just MCP:** ingest route, compile route, and all six mutating Health endpoints (new `assertWritableDomain` in [routes/health.js](src/routes/health.js)) refuse `readonly: true` domains with the steer message; `GET /api/domains` gains additive `readonlyDomains[]`; the Ingest dropdown excludes mirrors (Wiki/Health dropdowns keep them — reading/scanning is fine). **(M3) Host-header guard in [server.js](src/server.js):** DNS-rebinding made same-origin GETs readable (no Origin header on GETs) — every request's `Host` must now be a loopback form or 403; closes read exfiltration of wiki content/config app-wide. **(M6) `execFile` replaces `exec`** in `loadPriorContent` — page filenames (user- or remote-controlled) no longer pass through a shell. **(L4) `shared-*` namespace reserved:** `validateConnection` + `pushDomain` refuse mirrors as contributing domains (feedback-loop + remote-filename chain closed). **(L7/L8/L9)** invite tokens capped at 8 KB before decode work; repo name `.`/`..` rejected; connection labels must be single-line (log-forgery). **UI relocation (user decision, plan §5):** the "Enable Shared Brain (beta)" opt-in moved from the Sync tab to **Settings → Shared Brain (beta)** (configuration belongs in Settings; solo users get a clean Sync tab); the Sync tab renders the operational Shared Brains block only when the flag is on, and the Settings section shows enabled-state + a link to the Sync tab. **Verification:** new offline suite [test-sharedbrain-hardening.js](scripts/test-sharedbrain-hardening.js) (61 assertions — poisoned-payload synthesis incl. second-run health, un-skip-on-edit, 5-push 503 outage never skips + recovery, genuine failures still strike out, mirror refusals, label/repo hygiene, invite cap, and source-level guards on every touched file) added to the OFFLINE manifest; all **21 offline suites green**; live-server smoke test on a throwaway port + `CURATOR_TEST_DOMAINS_DIR` tempdir confirmed: Host guard (evil Host → 403, localhost forms → 200), `readonlyDomains` payload, ingest/health-fix into a mirror → 400 with steer text, health scan on mirror → 200, cross-origin POST guard unaffected. **Docs updated in the same release:** shared-brain-user-guide (enable location, push-all-domains, permanent-skip recovery, new troubleshooting entries), shared-brain.md (Decision 7 extension), shared-brain-admin.md (§8 new entries), api-reference.md (domains `readonlyDomains`, push/pull/synthesize behaviour, Health mirror-refusal, Host guard), user-guide §15b, README, monetization doc (enable path + an honest known-limitation note that the read-only tier can't be completed through the wizard until the Phase 3 fix). **No schema changes; `isTransientLlmError` + `mergeFactsForPage` already-exported surface only; `readonlyDomains` and `pages_failed` are additive.** Phases 2-5 (data-integrity/trust-boundary, UX, admin features, production test program) remain — tracked in SHARED-BRAIN-UPGRADE.md. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.27` | **Compile-to-Wiki safety net + compile-specific error (Fix #2 of 2 for the compile-hang report).** beta.25 removed the root cause (index bloat) of the "Gemini hit the output token limit (65536 tokens)" hang; beta.27 adds the durable safety net so a genuinely large/complex conversation degrades gracefully instead of hard-failing — exactly the fallback ingest already has. **Backend** ([src/brain/compile.js](src/brain/compile.js)): `compileConversation` now runs a 3-step ladder — **full → concise → summary-only** — each step reducing output size. It escalates ONLY on `isOutputTokenLimit(err)` (now a top-level export from [ingest.js](src/brain/ingest.js)) or a JSON parse failure (a smaller response parses cleaner); **any other LLM error (503/429/auth/network) surfaces immediately** — never masked, no wasted retries on an outage (mirrors ingest's beta.15 gating). `buildCompilePrompt` gains a `mode` param: `'full'` is **byte-identical** to the beta.25 prompt (the existing 16-assertion prompt test stays green), `'concise'` injects a brevity directive (≤~10 pages, 3–5 bullets each, always include the summary), `'summary-only'` asks for ONLY the summary page (no entity/concept pages). On total overflow, the returned error is **compile-specific** — *"This conversation is too large or complex… compile a shorter conversation, or split this discussion into separate conversations by topic"* — replacing the misleading ingest text that told conversation-compilers to *"split the source into smaller parts (e.g. by chapter) and ingest each separately"* / referenced *"the Phase 2 batch size in src/brain/ingest.js"*. Degraded outcomes are surfaced via a new `warnings[]` on the result → the route emits them on the `done` event ([src/routes/compile.js](src/routes/compile.js)) → the UI renders them as a `.compile-note` above the change list ([app.js](src/public/app.js) + [styles.css](src/public/styles.css)). The summary-only page flows through the **unchanged** `writePage → syncSummaryEntities → mergeIntoIndex` pipeline (no entities to sync → no-op). **Test seam:** `compileConversation(domain, id, onProgress, opts)` accepts `opts.generateText` (defaults to the real one) so the ladder is unit-testable offline with a fake LLM. **Happy path untouched:** a successful first attempt behaves exactly as before (verified: 1 LLM call, zero warnings). **Verification:** new offline `test-beta27-compile-fallback.js` (**33 assertions** — prompt-mode contract incl. full≡default; and the orchestration via the injected-LLM seam on a throwaway domain: happy path = 1 call/no warnings, concise-recovery, summary-only-recovery writes ONLY the summary, **503 → fail fast with the real message and NO escalation**, all-overflow → compile-specific error that does NOT contain "split the PDF"/"by chapter"/"Phase 2 batch size", parse-failure escalation). New live `test-beta27-compile-live.js` (LIVE_CI, **16 assertions on REAL Gemini AND Anthropic**): a normal conversation still compiles via the full path with zero warnings (no regression), and both real models honour the new prompts (concise → valid JSON w/ summary; summary-only → exactly one summary page, no entity/concept pages). All **20 offline suites green**; `test-ingest-fixes` (94) re-run green after the `isOutputTokenLimit` export. **No schema change; one new top-level export (`isOutputTokenLimit`); `buildCompilePrompt`/`compileConversation` signatures extended with defaulted params (back-compatible); result gains an additive `warnings[]`.** |
| `v3.0.1-beta.26` | **Live-API CI gate made useful — transient provider outages no longer red the build (CI infrastructure; no app-code change).** Right after beta.25 merged, the `Tests` workflow's **live** job went red on `main` and stayed red across **three** re-runs — but NOT because of beta.25. Every failure was a **transient provider problem**: Gemini **HTTP 503** "temporarily overloaded" mid-ingest (already retried 4× with backoff in `llm.js`, provider still down), Anthropic **"Premature close"** (the streaming connection dropped), plus one non-deterministic LLM-quality miss (Haiku judged an orphan had no home). The smoking gun that it wasn't beta.25: the new `test-beta25-compile-live` suite passed **2 of its 3** Gemini compiles on the 115 KB-index domain — the fix works; run 2 just landed in a 503 window. The live job hard-failed on Google/Anthropic weather, which is useless signal. **Fix (Option 1 — the one chosen after the user re-ran the action and it failed all three times):** make the aggregator tolerant of transient provider errors. New pure module [scripts/ci-flake.js](scripts/ci-flake.js) — `hasTransientMarker(out)` (matches the exact strings `llm.js` surfaces: 503/"temporarily overloaded"/Service Unavailable, Anthropic "Premature close", 429/"Too Many Requests"/RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED, raw socket errors ECONNRESET/ETIMEDOUT/"socket hang up"/fetch-failed) + `classifyLiveOutcome({firstOk, retried, retryOk, firstTransient, retryTransient})` returning `pass`/`fail`/`inconclusive`. [scripts/run-tests.js](scripts/run-tests.js) now: **retries any failed LIVE suite once** (covers intermittent infra blips AND an occasional LLM-quality miss that passes on a second try); if it still fails but the output shows **only** transient markers, marks it **INCONCLUSIVE** (a ⚠ flake line; counted separately, does NOT fail the build); a second failure with **no** transient marker is a genuine **FAIL**. Offline suites stay deterministic and are **never retried**; a TIMEOUT is never retried (would double a 10-min wait). **Accepted, documented trade-off:** a real failure that coincides with a transient error in the same run is reported inconclusive rather than fail — acceptable for a live-API gate because the deterministic offline suite + local `test:live` still catch real regressions. **The build now goes green even while the provider is down** — the gate stops depending on Google/Anthropic recovering. **Verification:** new offline `test-ci-flake.js` (24 assertions — the marker detector against the REAL failing-run strings + the full `classifyLiveOutcome` truth table + the exact beta25/sustained-503/real-bug scenarios) and `test-runner-integration.js` (15 assertions — drives the REAL `run-tests.js` via a new test-only `RUN_TESTS_LIVE_ONLY` seam against four deterministic fake suites in `scripts/test-fixtures/`: sustained-transient→inconclusive→exit 0; real-defect→FAIL→exit 1; fail-then-recover-on-retry→pass→exit 0; mixed-batch accounting). All **19 offline suites green**. **No app code, schema, route, or `.github/workflows/test.yml` change** — the workflow keys off the `npm run test:live` exit code, which is now 0 on a pure provider outage. **Follow-up (Option 2, if a quality assertion like beta17's orphan-rescue proves persistently flaky):** move the subjective LLM-judgment assertions out of the CI gate into `LIVE_LOCAL`. The retry-once already gives them a second chance, so they only fail the gate if they miss twice in a row. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.25` | **Compile-to-Wiki no longer hangs then fails with "Gemini hit the output token limit (65536 tokens)" on large domains (community-reported bug — Fix #1 of 2).** A user ingested a PDF, cleaned up the wiki, asked a chat question (good answer), then clicked **Compile to Wiki** — it hung at ~28% and died with the output-token-limit error. Their read ("it's just Gemini, wait an hour") was only half right: the *trigger* is Gemini-side, but it is **not** a transient overload (429/503) that heals with time — it's output-token truncation, which the app treats as non-retryable (`generateText` only retries 429/503). What actually "fixes it on retry" is that Gemini's degeneration is *stochastic*, not time-based. **Root cause:** `buildCompilePrompt` in [src/brain/compile.js](src/brain/compile.js) injected the **entire `index.md`** into the request (line removed: `Current wiki index:\n${index}`). On a mature domain that table is tens of KB (96 KB on the dev machine's `articles`; the reporter's "Curation" domain similarly large). Feeding that bloated, repetitive table into a JSON-mode request pushes Gemini 2.5 Flash-Lite into a repeat/degeneration loop that fills the whole 65,536-token output budget → `finishReason: MAX_TOKENS` → the [llm.js](src/brain/llm.js) guard throws. A *single* Q&A exchange producing 65k output tokens is the tell-tale of degeneration, not legitimate volume. **The index was pure waste:** the LLM never needed it — link grounding comes from the entity/concept **filename lists** (still in the prompt), the summary path is **forced**, and the app already updates `index.md` **programmatically** via `mergeIntoIndex` AFTER the call (the LLM is explicitly told not to touch it). This is exactly why ingest's *multi-phase* batch path — which large docs take — never sends the full index either; only the single-pass path does, and that's gated to <15k-char inputs. **Fix (minimal, root-cause):** drop the index from the compile prompt; `index` is still read for `mergeIntoIndex` (unchanged). `buildCompilePrompt` is now exported for offline testing. **Blast radius is exactly `compile.js`** — `mergeIntoIndex` untouched; the MCP `compile_to_wiki` tool is self-contained (Claude composes pages itself, reads index only for its own programmatic merge) so it never had this bug; routes/compile.js calls `compileConversation` unchanged. **Verification:** new offline suite [test-beta25-compile-prompt.js](scripts/test-beta25-compile-prompt.js) (16 assertions — index excluded, grounding inputs retained, don't-touch-index guidance intact, `mergeIntoIndex` regression guard); the rebuilt prompt drops a 52 KB-index case from ~52 KB to **3.7 KB**. New live suite [test-beta25-compile-live.js](scripts/test-beta25-compile-live.js) (LIVE_CI): seeds a throwaway domain with a **115 KB synthetic index** (replicating the failure trigger) + real entity/concept pages, runs the real `compileConversation` against **live Gemini ×3** — all succeed, summary + pages written, `index.md` grows non-destructively, zero token-limit errors (prompt cut from ~119 KB to 3.7 KB). All 17 offline suites green; `test-beta15-production` re-run green on **both** Gemini (4 pages) and Anthropic Haiku (2 pages). **Fix #2 (planned, not in this release):** give compile a fallback like ingest (retry concisely on token-limit, fall back to summary-only) + a compile-specific error message (the current text wrongly tells conversation-compilers to "split the PDF by chapter"). **No schema change; one private helper now exported; the index-update path is byte-identical.** |
| `v3.0.1-beta.24` | **One-click provider toggle (Settings → API Keys) — switch the active AI provider without re-pasting or deleting a key (community-reported UX gap).** A user with BOTH a Gemini and an Anthropic key reported that pasting a Gemini key silently switched the app to Gemini (correct — the v2.4.2 *last-saved-wins* behaviour) but found **no way to switch back to Anthropic without re-pasting the full key or Disconnecting Gemini (which deletes it)**. Investigation confirmed the gap: the backend already had everything (`activeProvider` field, `getActiveProvider`, `getProviderInfo` honouring it) EXCEPT a "set active without re-saving the key" path, and the UI had only the "Active: …" badge + a one-line hint — no control to flip providers. **Fix (additive, ~70 LOC).** **Backend:** new `setActiveProvider(provider)` in [src/brain/config.js](src/brain/config.js) — writes `cfg.activeProvider` only, and **refuses to activate a provider with no stored key** (switching to a keyless provider would break every subsequent LLM call); returns the resulting active provider. New route `POST /api/config/api-keys/active` in [src/routes/config.js](src/routes/config.js) — validates the provider enum, re-checks the key exists (400 if not), returns the new `{activeProvider, activeModel}`. Inherits the beta.20 cross-origin guard (foreign-Origin POST → 403, verified). **Frontend:** a `Use: Gemini | Anthropic` segmented toggle ([index.html](src/public/index.html) under the provider badge; [styles.css](src/public/styles.css) `.provider-toggle*`) that `loadApiKeyStatus` ([app.js](src/public/app.js)) shows **only when BOTH keys are saved** (single-key users see nothing to switch) and marks the active provider; clicking the inactive side POSTs the new endpoint, toasts `✓ Switched to …`, refreshes the badge. The save-hint copy + `docs/user-guide.md` (§4 + §16 API keys) now explain last-saved-wins, the toggle, and that Disconnect *deletes* rather than deactivates. **Verification:** offline-logic test (switch persists; refuses keyless/invalid provider) green; all 16 offline suites green; a live server on a throwaway port confirmed GET keys → toggle visible, click Anthropic → `activeProvider: anthropic` / `claude-haiku-4-5`, click Gemini → restored, invalid provider → 400, foreign Origin → 403; a Playwright pass drove the real Settings UI end-to-end (toggle renders, click switches badge+toast, switch-back restores) — the dev machine's real `.curator-config.json` was left on its original `gemini`. **No schema change** (`activeProvider` exists since v2.4.2); one new additive backend function + one new route + one new UI control. Existing single-key installs are visually unchanged. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.23` | **User-facing System Check panel (Settings) — free local diagnostics + opt-in AI connectivity test + a "health"-wording overhaul.** The user-facing half of the audit's test/diagnostics work (beta.21 delivered the developer half). A new **Settings → System Check** panel lets any user confirm their install is correct in one click, and tell at a glance whether a failure is their setup or their AI provider. **Wording overhaul (resolves real confusion):** the app had three "health"-ish surfaces — the **Health** tab (wiki content), a Settings section confusingly titled **"AI Wiki Health"** (actually just AI-scan cost limits), and this new panel originally drafted as "Health Check". A community-reported confusion prompted a consistent scheme: the Settings limits section is renamed **"Wiki Health — Scan Limits"** (clearly tied to the Health tab) and the new diagnostics panel is **"System Check"** (drops "Health" entirely so app-checks and wiki-checks can't be confused). Rule of thumb baked into the copy + docs: *System Check = is the app working?* · *Health tab = is my wiki clean?* **Two tiers.** **(1) Free quick check** — new `runQuickDiagnostics()` in [src/brain/diagnostics.js](src/brain/diagnostics.js): five local checks (installed version; AI provider key configured + active model incl. fallback; knowledge folder readable/writable via a **self-deleting temp-file probe** that never touches wiki content; credential files are 0600 — validates the beta.20 hardening, POSIX-only with a Windows `info` fallback; GitHub sync configured + pending-change count). No network, no API cost. Each result is `ok`/`warn`/`fail`/`info`. **(2) Opt-in live check** — `runLiveApiCheck()` makes ONE tiny LLM call (~$0.0001) to confirm the key works and the provider is responding, returning provider/model/latency on success or the exact error (e.g. an HTTP 503) on failure — the fastest way to answer "is it my key or the provider?". The module is **read-only w.r.t. user data** (the only write is the self-deleting probe) and `runLiveApiCheck` never throws. **Routes** ([src/routes/diagnostics.js](src/routes/diagnostics.js)): `GET /api/diagnostics/quick` (free) + `POST /api/diagnostics/live` (POST so the beta.20 cross-origin guard applies; the frontend gates it behind an explicit cost-confirm dialog mirroring the Health tab's confirm-before-cost pattern). **UI** ([src/public/index.html](src/public/index.html) Settings section + [app.js](src/public/app.js) + [styles.css](src/public/styles.css)): "Run system check" → result rows with ✅/⚠️/❌/ℹ️; "Verify AI connection" → cost-confirm gate → live result. (Element IDs keep the internal `diag-*` prefix; only the user-facing labels say "System Check".) **Provider-agnostic by construction** — both the UI gate and the scan dispatch through `getProviderInfo()` / `generateText()`; verified live on BOTH Gemini AND Anthropic (claude-haiku-4-5). **Verification:** new offline suite [scripts/test-diagnostics.js](scripts/test-diagnostics.js) (12 assertions — contract shape, summary accounting, deterministic domains-writable probe incl. self-clean + missing-dir→warn) added to the `npm test` OFFLINE manifest (now 16 suites, all green); a real browser run (Playwright) drove the panel end-to-end — quick check "Everything looks good" (5 rows), live test "Works — gemini · …-flash-lite · 773 ms", foreign-origin POST → 403, no stray probe files left in `domains/`. **Additive only** — new module + new routes + new Settings section; zero changes to existing logic, schema, or routes. **Docs:** new [docs/system-check.md](docs/system-check.md) (with a 3-surface disambiguation table: Health tab vs Wiki Health — Scan Limits vs System Check); added to README features + Documentation table, `docs/README.md`, `docs/user-guide.md` §16, and `docs/api-reference.md`. Settings-path references in `docs/ai-health.md` + `docs/user-guide.md` updated for the "Wiki Health — Scan Limits" rename. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.22` | **Semantic-duplicate scan reachable again on structurally-clean wikis (community-reported regression, NOT from beta.20/21).** A community member reported the semantic-dupe scan had "disappeared" from the Health tab, and that the regular scan reported "everything OK" while a dozen real duplicates existed. **Investigation (incl. a real-Gemini production test) cleared our recent updates:** the semantic-dupe backend works end-to-end (planted two same-token duplicate pairs in a throwaway domain → `findSemanticCandidatePairs` surfaced them → real `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` judged both "high" confidence, ignored the distractor, $0.0001), and the beta.20 Origin guard doesn't block the scan (it's a same-origin browser POST). **Real root cause — introduced in beta.17, not beta.20/21:** the beta.17 Health UX overhaul moved the standalone "Scan for semantic duplicates" card into the ⚡ Quick maintenance bar as the "✨ Find duplicate pages" button — but that whole bar was rendered only when `total > 0` (structural issues: broken links, orphans, folder-prefix, cross-folder dupes, hyphen variants, missing backlinks). Semantic duplicates are a **separate, opt-in scan** that never contributes to `total`. So on a wiki that's structurally clean but has semantic dupes, the UI showed "✅ Wiki is clean" with **no entry point to the only feature that finds semantic duplicates** — exactly the report. **Fix** ([src/public/app.js](src/public/app.js) `renderHealthReport`): the maintenance bar now renders when `total > 0` **OR** there's any actionable button (`maintBtns.length > 0`). Since "✨ Find duplicate pages" is in `maintBtns` whenever an API key is configured, the semantic scan is now reachable on clean wikis too. Edge cases preserved: structural issues present → unchanged (incl. the "add an API key" hint when no key); clean wiki + no API key → no bar (nothing actionable). **Frontend-only render-logic change; no backend/route/schema change.** Verified: `node --check` clean; `npm test` 15/15 green; real-Gemini semantic scan confirmed working before + after. **For affected users:** after updating, open Health → click **Scan** → the "✨ Find duplicate pages" button now appears in the Quick maintenance bar even when the wiki is otherwise clean. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.21` | **Test aggregator (`npm test`) + a class of latent test-isolation bugs fixed (audit Tier B, part 1).** Delivers the developer-facing half of the audit's test-infrastructure recommendation, and in doing so surfaced + fixed real bugs that had been invisible because no one ran the whole suite on a configured machine. **(1) `npm test` / `npm run test:live`.** New [scripts/run-tests.js](scripts/run-tests.js) aggregator runs every battle-test suite and prints one pass/fail report (was: 25 scripts you had to run by name). `npm test` runs the **15 OFFLINE suites** (pure, deterministic, no network/API) in ~1.3s; `npm run test:live` adds the **10 LIVE suites** (real Gemini/Anthropic/GitHub — each self-skips with exit 0 if its key is absent). Pass/fail is judged by child exit code AND an output scan (case-sensitive `Failed: [1-9]` / `[1-9] failed` / bare `✗`, so the summary line `Passed: 39   Failed: 0` doesn't self-trip). **Safety net:** offline children are spawned with `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`GITHUB_TEST_*` stripped from their env, so even a mis-classified suite physically cannot make a paid call. Suite manifest (offline vs live) is an explicit list in the runner. `package.json` gains `test` + `test:live` scripts. **(2) Root-cause fix — a broken test-injection seam that was ALSO a latent production no-op.** `getDomainsDir()` ([src/brain/config.js](src/brain/config.js)) resolves config.domainsPath → env `DOMAINS_PATH` → default. Battle tests (and `pullCollective` itself) tried to redirect the domains dir to a throwaway tempdir by setting `process.env.DOMAINS_PATH` — but on any real install (which has `domainsPath` in `.curator-config.json`) **config wins, so the env override was silently ignored**. Consequences: (a) `test-sharedbrain-security.js` + 3 others (`test-ingest-fixes`, `test-sharedbrain-pull`, `-synthesis`, `-mcp-guard`) FAILED on a configured machine, and (b) worse, those suites' `writePage` calls resolved to the REAL `domains/` folder — they had been **writing test fixtures into the user's actual domains** (found + removed two stray `shared-cohort` / `shared-test-cohort` dirs; never committed, real domains untouched). The same env seam in `pullCollective` ([src/brain/sharedbrain.js](src/brain/sharedbrain.js)) carried a comment literally admitting it was *"a no-op in production where the user has a config file"* — so the documented `opts.domainsDir` test-injection never worked on configured machines. **Fix:** new `__setDomainsDirOverride()` test seam in `config.js` — a module-level override checked BEFORE config/env, **null in production** (so `getDomainsDir`'s real precedence is byte-identical; verified production still resolves to the configured path). `pullCollective` and the affected tests now redirect via this override instead of the env var. Production is unaffected — the only caller of `pullCollective`, the sync route, never passes `opts.domainsDir`. **(3) Verification:** `npm test` 15/15 green; server boots clean as beta.21 and `/api/domains` (6 real domains), `/api/sync/status`, `/api/version` all respond correctly; MCP server module loads clean; `node --check` clean on all changed files; confirmed `getDomainsDir()` returns the real configured path in production. **No production behavior change, no schema change, no API change** — this release is test infrastructure + a test-seam fix whose production code path is provably inert. **Docs:** new [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md) (developer setup, running/adding tests, the `__setDomainsDirOverride` rule, release process) — the conventional developer home the audit flagged as missing — linked from `README.md` + `docs/README.md`; the `npm test` commands are also in CLAUDE.md's Scripts Reference. The README **Security** section was corrected to reflect the beta.20 hardening (loopback-only bind + cross-origin guard + 0600 credential files) — it previously said only "do not expose on a public network (no authentication)". **Deferred to beta.22:** the user-facing "Health Check" Settings panel (safe diagnostics + opt-in live-API check) — the other half of the test/diagnostics work. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.20` | **Tier A security + hygiene bundle (from an external GLM-5.2 code audit, verified claim-by-claim before acting).** A user-commissioned audit surfaced ~30 findings; four parallel verification agents checked each against the actual code, separating real issues from outdated/incorrect claims (e.g. the audit's "chat reads the entire wiki every message" described pre-beta.11 code, since replaced by `selectRelevantPages`; its "cross-process lock is one-directional" was wrong — both ingest and MCP `acquireFileLock`). This release implements only the **verified, small, low-risk** subset. **(1) Loopback-only binding.** `app.listen(PORT, ...)` bound to `0.0.0.0` (all interfaces) — anyone on the same LAN/Wi-Fi could reach `:3333` and hit unauthenticated endpoints (config, sync, ingest). Now binds to `127.0.0.1` ([src/server.js](src/server.js) `BIND_HOST`), matching the single-user-localhost design and removing the LAN attack surface. **(2) Cross-origin guard.** New middleware (before the API routers) rejects any **mutating** request (POST/PUT/DELETE/PATCH) whose `Origin` header is present and not one of the loopback origins (`http://localhost:<PORT>` / `http://127.0.0.1:<PORT>`) → 403. Defends against a malicious open web page issuing `fetch('http://localhost:3333/...')` and against DNS-rebinding. Requests with **no** Origin (curl, scripts, the documented Shared Brain revoke flow, server-to-server) are allowed — they aren't the browser-driven CSRF vector; GETs are never blocked (static assets + SPA). **(3) Credential files 0600 + atomic.** `.curator-config.json` (API keys), `.sync-config.json` (GitHub PAT), and `.sharedbrain-config.json` (PATs + admin/fellow tokens) were written world-readable (0644), and the beta.8 atomic-write rollout had missed the latter two plus `domains/.gitignore`. Fixes: `writeFileAtomic`/`writeFileAtomicSync` gained an options form (`{encoding, mode}`) that explicitly `chmod`s the tempfile to 0600 **before** the atomic rename (so umask can't loosen it); the legacy bare-string-encoding signature is unchanged (backward-compatible). All three credential writers now route through it with `mode: 0o600`; `.gitignore` now writes atomically (a truncated `.gitignore` could let `raw/` source files get committed to GitHub). A one-shot startup sweep in [src/server.js](src/server.js) best-effort `chmod 0600`s those files plus `.env` and `.knowledge-git/config` (git embeds the sync PAT in the remote URL there) so **existing** installs are hardened immediately, not only on the next write. **(4) Stale-string + misleading-advice sweep.** The v2.6.0 Sync-tab rename ("Sync Up/Sync Down" → "Push only/Pull only/Sync now") had left dead button names in `friendlyError` ([src/brain/sync.js](src/brain/sync.js)), two `app.js` toasts, and `docs/architecture.md` + `docs/api-reference.md` — all corrected. `friendlyError`'s auth-failure message said only *"Make sure it has 'repo' scope"* (classic-token advice), wrong for the fine-grained tokens the current docs recommend; it now names **both** ("fine-grained: Contents: Read and write; classic: repo scope"). **(5) Doc hygiene.** `docs/shared-brain-admin.md` was missing section 2 (numbering jumped 1 → 3) — added **§2 Inviting collaborators** (pointer-based, cross-linked to the user-guide setup sections). Present-tense `v3.0.0-beta.1` status stamps in `shared-brain.md` / `-user-guide.md` / `-compliance.md` updated to reflect that Shared Brain was *introduced* in beta.1 but the current app is beta.20 (historical changelog entries left intact). **Verification:** all modified JS `node --check` clean; the beta.8 stress suite (70 assertions, covers atomic writes) green; a functional test confirms `{mode:0o600}` yields 0600 files on both async+sync paths while the legacy string signature still works; a live server smoke test on a throwaway port confirmed loopback-only bind, GET allowed, foreign-Origin POST → 403, no-Origin + same-Origin POST allowed. Verified on disk that all four credential files (incl. `.knowledge-git/config`) are now 0600 with the Gemini key intact. **No schema changes; one new backward-compatible `writeFileAtomic` options form; new 403 path is graceful (the only HTTP client is the same-origin frontend).** Deferred to a later decision (audit Tier B): a `node:test` aggregator + `npm test` with offline/live gating. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.19` | **Personal Sync robustness + documentation overhaul (from a real cross-machine setup failure on Windows).** A user setting up Personal Sync on a friend's Windows machine hit a confusing cascade — a `commit -m "Initial The Curator sync"` failure, then "Failed to fetch", then a stuck retry loop — surfacing both a real bug and a documentation gap. **Bug fix (load-bearing):** `git()` in [src/brain/sync.js](src/brain/sync.js) re-threw `new Error(sanitize(err.message))`, but Node's `exec` puts the failing command + **stderr** in `err.message` while git's benign `"nothing to commit, working tree clean"` status goes to **stdout** — which was dropped. So `setup()`'s `catch` check `err.message.includes('nothing to commit')` was ALWAYS false on a no-op commit, and `setup()` **crashed at the commit step whenever it ran against an already-committed repo** (exactly the state after a first push fails: files committed locally, push rejected, `.sync-config.json` never saved → every wizard retry trips the no-op commit). Fix: append `err.stdout` to the thrown error so the benign-commit detection in `setup()`, `pull()`'s auto-save, and `friendlyError` all work. Verified empirically (Node exec stdout/stderr split reproduced); never surfaced on Mac because a successful first push (files to commit, exit 0) never enters the buggy branch — only a failed-first-push-then-retry does, OS-independent. **Wizard text overhaul** ([src/public/index.html](src/public/index.html)): Step 1 now explicitly says **create an EMPTY repo — do NOT add a README/.gitignore/license** (a pre-filled repo is the #1 cause of the first-push rejection that started the cascade); Step 2 leads with a **fine-grained token** + exact permission (**Contents: Read and write**, Metadata auto-added) with a classic-token `<details>` fallback; Step 3 clarifies push-vs-pull by repo-empty state. [src/public/app.js](src/public/app.js): the wizard's network-error catch now translates "Failed to fetch"/"Load failed" into an actionable "server stopped responding — restart and retry; on Windows check for a GitHub sign-in popup" message. **Documentation consolidation:** [docs/sync.md](docs/sync.md) gained a fine-grained-vs-classic comparison table + exact permissions, two classic-token clarifications the user specifically asked about (**you cannot scope a classic token to one repo**, and **the `workflow` scope is NOT needed** — only `repo`), and four new troubleshooting entries from this exact incident (Failed-to-fetch/server-down, the commit-loop with a `.knowledge-git`/`.sync-config.json` reset workaround for pre-beta.19, Windows Git Credential Manager popup/hang with the `cmdkey` fix, and a "this is state, not OS — not a Windows problem" note). **NEW third setup method:** [docs/sync-via-coding-agent.md](docs/sync-via-coding-agent.md) — sync setup end-to-end with Claude Code/Cursor/opencode/Aider via a copy-paste prompt that creates an empty private repo (`gh repo create`), obtains a token (`gh auth token` or fine-grained PAT), and calls the app's own `POST /api/sync/setup` (reuses the tested path, no parallel logic); push + pull variants, required-tools table, security caveats. Cross-linked from [README.md](README.md) (sync section + docs table; the stale "PAT with `repo` scope" line corrected to name both token types + the empty-repo requirement), [docs/README.md](docs/README.md) (Start-here + index table), and [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md) §15 (empty-repo Step 1 + agent-method pointer). Server + app.js + sync.js load verified. No schema/API changes. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.18` | **Two Health-UX fixes from first real beta.17 use on the 1000+ broken-link articles domain.** (1) **HTTP 413 on Apply.** The broken-link/orphan apply endpoints POST the full plan as JSON; on a 1000+ entry plan the body exceeded Express's default 100kb `express.json()` limit → user saw *"Apply failed (HTTP 413)"*. Two-part fix: raised the body limit to `50mb` in [src/server.js](src/server.js) (localhost app, no DoS risk), AND the frontend now sends a SLIM plan — only the fields apply needs (`{linkText, action, target}` for broken links, `{orphanSlug, target, description}` for orphans), dropping `occurrences`/`sourceFiles`/`confidence`/`source` so a 620-entry plan drops from ~280kb to ~50kb. Either fix alone resolves it; together they're robust to 20k-entry plans. The backend `applyBrokenLinkFixes`/`applyOrphanRescue` already read only those fields, so the slim plan is byte-compatible. (2) **Health detail sections now collapsed by default.** `renderSection` rendered each `<details>` with `open`, so on a domain with 1000+ broken links the list pushed the ⚡ Quick-maintenance bar and the AI progress/preview panels far below the fold — the user clicked a fix and saw no progress. Sections now render collapsed (click the header to expand); the `Fix all` button inside the `<summary>` got `preventDefault`+`stopPropagation` so clicking it fixes without toggling the section; and each AI flow now `scrollIntoView`s its result panel on launch so the progress bar is always visible. No backend logic change; UI/transport only. Server + app.js syntax verified; offline suites green. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.17` | **Health section UX overhaul — unified AI Maintenance for large & shared brains.** Strategic upgrade (not just a feature): the Health section is the maintenance backbone, and becomes mission-critical for org-scale Shared Brains. The prior state was powerful but fragmented — batch tools scattered in separate cards at the bottom, and **orphans still required clicking "Ask AI" one page at a time** (the same pain beta.16 solved for broken links). Three coordinated additions. **(1) Bulk AI orphan rescue** — new `planOrphanRescue`/`estimateOrphanRescue` in [src/brain/health-ai.js](src/brain/health-ai.js) + `applyOrphanRescue` in [src/brain/health.js](src/brain/health.js): batches all orphans, the LLM picks the ONE existing page that should link to each (entities/concepts only — summaries are never homes), apply injects `- [[orphan]] — desc` into that page's Related via `injectRelatedLink`. Conservative by design (returns *no home* unless a genuine relationship). **Validated on the real 604-orphan articles domain: 391 got confident homes** (`breaches`←`data-leaks`, `hewlett-packard`←`dell-technologies`), 213 deliberately left for manual review, $0.10. **(2) One-click "Fix N safe issues"** — new `fixAllSafe` runs every deterministic type (cross-folder dupes, hyphen variants, folder-prefix links, missing backlinks, matched broken links) in a single write-locked pass. **(3) Unified "⚡ Quick maintenance" action bar** in `renderHealthReport` ([src/public/app.js](src/public/app.js)) — after a scan, batch tools surface at the top with live counts (🛠 Fix N safe · ✨ Fix N broken links · ✨ Rescue N orphans · ✨ Find duplicate pages), each launching a preview-then-confirm flow; the per-issue detail sections remain below for granular control. The three AI cards became result-only panels (`.ai-tool-headless`, hidden until launched). **Routes** ([src/routes/health.js](src/routes/health.js)): `GET/POST /:domain/orphans/{estimate,plan,apply}` (plan read-only, apply write-locked) + `POST /:domain/fix-all-safe`. **Pre-ship adversarial audit (3 parallel reviewers on the COMPLETE Health section) found + fixed 7 real issues before commit:** (HIGH) `applyOrphanRescue` injected an unvalidated client `orphanSlug` into a page — markdown/link injection vector; now every slug is regex+existence-validated and the description is stripped of `[[ ]]`. (HIGH) `isLexicalVariant` accepted generic single-token subsets (`ai`⊂`ai-and-weather-forecasting`); now a 1-token subset side must be ≥3 chars (keeps `iot`/`mcp`/`gpt`, rejects `ai`/`ml`). (HIGH) `fix-all-safe`/`fix-all` acquired the file lock + mkdir'd a ghost dir BEFORE `assertDomain`; validation now precedes lock acquisition. (HIGH) stale `_blPlan`/`_orphPlan` weren't reset across domains — a plan from domain A could apply to B; now cleared on every scan + domain switch. (HIGH) `runFixAllSafe` didn't disable its button and a re-scan error masqueraded as a fix error; both fixed. (MED) a transient batch-LLM error wrongly biased broken links toward `strip`; failed-batch items are now left untouched. (MED) empty AI cards rendered as bordered boxes / the API-key hint was suppressed / unguarded array access — all fixed. **Test coverage:** [scripts/test-beta16-broken-links.js](scripts/test-beta16-broken-links.js) extended to 36 offline (adds the single-token-subset M4 cases). NEW [scripts/test-beta17-production.js](scripts/test-beta17-production.js) — **22 PRODUCTION assertions on BOTH real Gemini AND real Anthropic Haiku** on throwaway domains: orphan plan+apply rescues a planted orphan on both providers (no broken links introduced), a hand-crafted malicious plan (slug injection, path traversal, phantom orphan, bad target, description injection) has 4/5 entries rejected with only the valid one written and `[[evil]]` stripped, and fix-all-safe resolves a planted folder-prefix issue. All 36 offline + 22 production green; beta.16 production (18) re-run green after the gate change; all prior suites green. **Backward compatibility:** zero schema changes; all new exports additive; READ-ONLY-planning invariant preserved. Docs: `docs/user-guide.md` §17 rewritten around the Quick maintenance bar; `docs/ai-health.md` gains bulk-orphan-rescue + fix-all-safe sections; `docs/api-reference.md` gains the new endpoints. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.16` | **Bulk AI broken-link fixer (Health tab).** A mature domain accumulates broken `[[wikilinks]]` — the user reported 1,033 on their `articles` domain (620 unique), impossible to clear with the per-link "Ask AI" button. New two-tier batch resolver mirroring the semantic-dupe-scan architecture (estimate → SSE plan → preview → apply through one chokepoint). **Tier 1 — deterministic pre-pass (free, no LLM):** new `buildLinkResolver` in [src/brain/health-ai.js](src/brain/health-ai.js) slugifies spaces (`[[artificial intelligence]]`→`artificial-intelligence`), strips `.md` (`[[tali-rezun.md]]`→`tali-rezun`), strips folder/honorific/article prefixes, hyphen-normalises, and matches against the on-disk slug inventory — resolved ~115 of the 620 for free. **Tier 2 — AI pass (batched):** `planBrokenLinkFixes` sends the remaining unique targets to the LLM with the full slug inventory (100 targets/call to amortise the inventory resend — the dominant token cost); the model maps each to an existing slug or null. **Critical quality gate — `isLexicalVariant`:** the LLM over-reaches, mapping genuinely-missing concepts to loosely-related pages ("context-window"→"agent-memory", "big-data"→"ai-and-weather-forecasting", "healthcare"→"ai-in-medicine" — all WRONG graph edges). A deterministic token-overlap gate (subset OR Jaccard ≥ 0.5) ACCEPTS a retarget only when the broken slug and target share enough words to be the SAME thing (variant/reorder/acronym, e.g. "rezun-tali"→"tali-rezun", "mcp"→"model-context-protocol-mcp", "iot"→"iot-and-ai"); everything weaker falls through to **strip the brackets** (keeping the readable text / alias label) — exactly the user's chosen behaviour for genuinely-missing pages. This gate is the load-bearing safety mechanism: without it the AI injects hundreds of wrong connections; with it, the real-data run produced only true-variant retargets. **Apply (destructive):** new `applyBrokenLinkFixes` in [src/brain/health.js](src/brain/health.js) walks every page ONCE, parses wikilinks with the SAME regex the scanner uses, and retargets (`[[X]]`→`[[Y]]`, alias preserved) or strips (`[[X|Label]]`→`Label`, `[[X]]`→`X`); skips `index.md`/`log.md` like the scanner; re-validates every retarget target against the on-disk inventory (the plan arrives from the client). **Three new routes** in [src/routes/health.js](src/routes/health.js): `GET /:domain/broken-links/estimate` (free counts + cost), `POST /:domain/broken-links/plan` (SSE, read-only, runs the LLM), `POST /:domain/broken-links/apply` (SSE, write-lock + registry + 409 like fix-all, cap 20000). **UI** ([src/public/index.html](src/public/index.html) + [app.js](src/public/app.js) + [styles.css](src/public/styles.css)): a "✨ Fix broken links with AI" card appears in Health when AI is available AND the domain has broken links → confirm dialog with the cost estimate → SSE plan with progress → **preview** (two columns: repointed-to-real-page vs brackets-removed, with samples + counts) → **Apply** button → SSE apply → auto re-scan. The whole wiki is git-tracked so the apply is revertable from the Sync tab. **Validated on the user's REAL `articles` domain (read-only plan, $0.014):** 1,033 broken → plan of 343 retarget-occurrences (to real pages) + 690 strip-occurrences = 0 broken; every AI retarget in the output was a genuine variant after the lexical gate. **Test coverage:** [scripts/test-beta16-broken-links.js](scripts/test-beta16-broken-links.js) — 32 offline assertions (deterministic resolver, the lexical gate's true-variant-vs-over-reach cases drawn from the real run, grouping, slugify). [scripts/test-beta16-production.js](scripts/test-beta16-production.js) — 18 PRODUCTION assertions against real Gemini on a throwaway domain: a page with one broken link of every category (written directly to disk to bypass writePage's own `.md` normalisation), real plan classifies each correctly, real apply retargets + strips with alias display text preserved, re-scan confirms 7→0 broken. All 32 offline + 18 production green; all prior suites unaffected (beta15 41, beta14 17, beta13 62, beta11 42, beta10 32, beta8-stress 70). Server + MCP load verified. **Backward compatibility:** zero schema changes; all new exports additive; three new routes. READ-ONLY-planning invariant preserved (health-ai.js never writes). Docs: `docs/ai-health.md` + `docs/api-reference.md` gain the broken-link-fix flow + endpoints. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.15` | **Five-fix bundle from a second community bug report (Anthropic Haiku user).** Five issues reported with screenshots. **Fix 1 — Compile/ingest merge silently wiped prose sections.** `mergeWikiPage` in [src/brain/files.js](src/brain/files.js) used the incoming page as the base and only re-injected bullets from the ACCUMULATE list — so any PROSE section (Definition, Summary, Why It Matters, Overview) the incoming page omitted was dropped. A minimal Compile/Curate edit shipping a thin version of an existing rich page therefore deleted its prose (the user's own chat output flagged "it briefly dropped the Definition and Why It Matters prose. I restored both"). New `extractSectionMap()` helper + prose-preservation pass: any `## ` section present in the existing page but ENTIRELY ABSENT from the incoming page is preserved; if the incoming page includes the heading (even a rewrite) the incoming version still wins, so ingest's full-document-context rewrite behaviour is unchanged and no prose is duplicated. `mergeWikiPage` now exported for unit testing. **Fix 2 — big files failing to import (the real cause of "I can't find what I uploaded").** Screenshot showed *"Claude hit the output token limit (16384 tokens)"* on a large PDF. Root cause in [src/brain/ingest.js](src/brain/ingest.js): in the multi-phase Phase 2 loop the `generateText` call sat OUTSIDE the try/catch (only `parseJSON` was wrapped), so a `max_tokens` error on one dense 4-page batch threw straight out of `ingestMultiPhase` and **killed the entire ingest — the whole document's information was lost**, which is why it couldn't be retrieved later. Fixes: (a) the Phase 2 batch `generateText` is now INSIDE the try/catch so ANY failure (token-limit OR malformed JSON) recovers via the existing page-by-page fallback instead of aborting; (b) same fix on the Phase 1 outline call (a `max_tokens` throw there used to escape uncaught) plus a "plan FEWER, broader pages" retry hint; (c) the single-pass outer catch now treats an output-token-limit error as recoverable and falls through to multi-phase rather than re-throwing; (d) new budget constants — page-by-page raised 4096 → 8192 (fewer dense pages get stubbed), outline 16384 → 24576, batch stays 16384. Partial failures now surface user-visible warnings instead of vanishing silently. **Fix 3 — ingested info not retrievable** is largely downstream of Fix 2 (failed ingests = data never written); the loud per-batch warnings make any residual partial failure visible. **Fix 4 — semantic duplicates had no batch merge.** New exported `fixSemanticDuplicatesBatch(domain, pairs, onProgress)` in [src/brain/health.js](src/brain/health.js) runs `fixSemanticDuplicate` sequentially (NOT parallel — each merge rewrites links + deletes a file domain-wide; concurrent merges would race), reporting per-pair `merged`/`skipped`/`error` (a pair whose file was already consumed by an earlier merge degrades to `skipped`, not a crash). New SSE endpoint `POST /api/health/:domain/semantic-dupes/merge-batch` in [src/routes/health.js](src/routes/health.js), registered as a write-op + file-lock like fix-all (concurrent sync/update/delete get 409), capped at 2000 pairs. New frontend flow in [src/public/app.js](src/public/app.js): after a scan, a **"✨ Merge all N high-confidence duplicates"** bar appears (HIGH-confidence only; medium/low stay manual) with an explicit confirm step naming the count + "you can undo it from the Sync tab", then a live progress bar marking each card merged/skipped as the SSE stream advances. Git-tracked wiki = revertable. **Fix 5 — "Compile to Wiki" missing after a single good exchange.** `COMPILE_MIN_USER_MESSAGES` (app.js) and `MIN_USER_MESSAGES` (compile.js) lowered 2 → 1, so the button appears after the first answer. **Pre-ship adversarial audit (3 parallel reviewers) surfaced and fixed three HIGH issues before commit:** (a) the beta.15 ingest change *widened* an error path — moving `generateText` inside the Phase 2 try meant a genuine rate-limit / 503 / auth / network error (not just a token-limit) would silently degrade a batch into stub pages. Fixed by separating the LLM call from the JSON parse and gating the page-by-page (and concise-outline-retry, and single-pass→multi-phase) fallbacks behind a new `isOutputTokenLimit()` predicate; every non-token LLM error now re-throws so the user sees the real failure. (b) `extractSectionMap` keyed sections case-sensitively, so a heading differing only in case between existing/incoming (`## Definition` vs `## definition` — the LLM is not case-stable) would DUPLICATE the prose section or double-inject bullets; fixed by lowercasing section keys + the ACCUMULATE set (matching the bullet helpers' existing `'i'`-flag behaviour). (c) the batch-merge UI read a frozen scan-time array, so a user's Flip / Skip / individual-Merge before clicking "Merge all" could merge the WRONG direction or re-merge a dismissed pair; fixed by deriving the batch list from the live DOM cards (`dataset.confidence` added; merged cards excluded). Plus NIT fixes: route now drops non-object `pairs[]` items; "undo from Sync" copy softened to "if you use GitHub Sync"; stale comment corrected. **Test coverage:** [scripts/test-beta15-fixes.js](scripts/test-beta15-fixes.js) — **41 offline assertions** (prose preservation incl. case-mismatch dup-guard; batch-merge orchestration incl. graceful skip/empty/null; and the `isOutputTokenLimit` classification that drives re-throw-vs-recover — verified against the REAL llm.js error strings for both providers). NEW [scripts/test-beta15-production.js](scripts/test-beta15-production.js) — **30 PRODUCTION assertions against REAL Gemini AND real Anthropic Haiku** on throwaway domains (config force-switched + restored): large 27k-char multi-phase ingest completes on both providers (the exact path that used to die on Haiku), 1-message compile succeeds on both, full semantic-scan→batch-merge surgery on Gemini (2 high-confidence pairs found, merged, files deleted, links rewritten, 0 new broken links), prose preservation through the real `writePage` on disk. All 41 offline + 30 production green. Existing offline suites unaffected (beta14 17, beta13 62, beta11 42, beta10 32, beta8-stress 70). Server + MCP module loads verified. **Backward compatibility:** zero schema changes; `mergeWikiPage` + `fixSemanticDuplicatesBatch` + `isOutputTokenLimit` are new additive exports; the merge-batch route is new. Existing wikis benefit on next ingest/merge. **Known limitation (not a regression):** on Anthropic Haiku the large multi-phase ingest still produces a higher broken-wikilink rate (~46 on the 27k-char test doc vs ~3 on Gemini) — this is the pre-existing beta.9 LLM-compliance issue (Phase 2 batches referencing slugs not on the plan), surfaced by `auditBrokenWikilinks` for the user to fix via Wiki Health; beta.15 makes the ingest *complete* where it previously crashed, which is strictly better. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.14` | **Anthropic large-output fix — Compile-to-Wiki + single-pass ingest now work on Haiku (responding to a community bug report).** A community member running Anthropic Haiku reported that clicking **Compile to Wiki** failed with *"LLM call failed: Streaming is strongly recommended for operations that may take longer than 10 minutes"*, and after they patched that, a second error appeared: `max_tokens: 65536 > 64000`.
