Investigation confirmed BOTH are real and share one root: the `65536` output budget that ingest single-pass and compile request is correct for Gemini 2.5 Flash but invalid on Anthropic. **Two distinct limits, both Anthropic-only, both fixed in the Anthropic branch of `callProvider` in [src/brain/llm.js](src/brain/llm.js):** (1) **SDK client-side streaming guard.** `@anthropic-ai/sdk` v0.39's `_calculateNonstreamingTimeout(max_tokens)` (in `core.js`) throws *"Streaming is strongly recommended…"* for ANY non-streaming `messages.create()` whose computed timeout `(3600 × max_tokens) / 128000` seconds exceeds 600 — i.e. for any `max_tokens` above **~21,333**, regardless of model or actual latency. At 65536 it always throws, before any HTTP request leaves the machine. Fix: switch the Anthropic call from `client.messages.create({...})` to `client.messages.stream({...}).finalMessage()`. Streaming uses a fixed 600s timeout and skips the guard entirely; `.finalMessage()` assembles and returns the identical `Message` object, so the existing `stop_reason === 'max_tokens'` truncation check and `content[0].text` extraction are byte-for-byte unchanged. (2) **API model output cap.** Claude Haiku 4.5 caps output at **64,000** tokens, so even with streaming the API rejects `max_tokens: 65536` as `max_tokens: 65536 > 64000`. Fix: new exported constant `ANTHROPIC_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS = 64000`; the Anthropic branch clamps via `Math.min(maxTokens, ANTHROPIC_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS)` before the call. **Crucially the clamp alone is NOT sufficient** — 64,000 is still above the ~21,333 streaming-guard threshold, so streaming is the load-bearing fix for error 1 and the clamp fixes error 2; both are required (matching exactly what the community user independently arrived at). **Gemini is untouched** — it allows 65,536 and has no such guard, so its branch keeps the raw `maxTokens` (verified by source-level assertion). **Blast radius:** exactly the three `65536` call sites — [ingest.js:1228](src/brain/ingest.js) (single-pass), [ingest.js:1252](src/brain/ingest.js) (single-pass brevity retry), [compile.js:319](src/brain/compile.js). Chat (4096) and multi-phase ingest outline/batch (16384) were always under both thresholds and are unaffected. **Why it went unreported for months:** the default provider is Gemini; only Anthropic users hit it, and only on Compile or single-pass ingest of small (≤15k-char) sources. The community member was on Haiku + Compile → direct hit. The screenshot thread also surfaced a *non-bug*: editing files on disk doesn't reload a running server — the `.app` `on run` handler reuses an already-listening Node process — which is documented behaviour, not a code issue (kill the node process or use the in-app Update→restart). **Scope decision:** the Curator only ever enables Haiku for Anthropic (`DEFAULTS.anthropic = claude-haiku-4-5`), so a single 64,000 constant is used rather than a per-model cap map. Known edge (deliberately not handled): the 404-fallback chain can escalate to older `claude-3-5-haiku` (8,192 cap), which a 64,000 clamp wouldn't protect — flagged as a conscious decision, not an oversight. **Test coverage:** new [scripts/test-beta14-anthropic-fix.js](scripts/test-beta14-anthropic-fix.js) with **26 assertions, 17 offline + 9 live** against the real Haiku API. Offline: the exported constant, clamp math, a reproduction of the SDK guard formula proving it fires at both 65536 AND 64000 (hence streaming is mandatory) but not at 16384/4096, presence of `messages.stream()` in the installed SDK, and source-level guards that the Anthropic branch streams+clamps while the Gemini branch stays unclamped. Live (gated on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, temporarily forces the Anthropic provider via a backed-up/restored `.curator-config.json`, runs against a throwaway domain it creates+deletes): `generateText(..., 65536)` in JSON and text mode, a full single-pass ingest, and a full conversation compile — all asserting neither *"Streaming is strongly recommended"* nor *"max_tokens > 64000"* appears. All 26 green end-to-end against the exact paths the user reported. MCP server, compile.js, and ingest.js module loads verified. **Backward compatibility:** zero schema changes, zero breaking API changes; `ANTHROPIC_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` is a new additive export. Gemini users see no change. **Documentation:** `docs/model-lifecycle.md` Anthropic-specific notes gains a third item documenting both limits + the streaming transport; `docs/architecture.md` ingest LLM-call line annotated with the Anthropic clamp. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.13` | **Chat retrieval — three-layer upgrade for enumerate queries.** Direct response to a user-reported chat result that listed only 6 articles by Dr. Alice Smith on a domain where her entity page actually had 50+ summary backlinks. Investigation pinpointed the cause: the chat's keyword-only scoring was missing summaries whose filenames didn't contain "tali" or "rezun", and the LLM was synthesising from the loaded content rather than enumerating from the entity page's Related section. Three layered improvements in [src/brain/chat.js](src/brain/chat.js): (1) **Entity-pivot retrieval** — new `detectEntityPivots()` scans the query tokens against every entity slug; matches (≥2 token overlap for multi-token slugs, or 1 token for specific single-token slugs not in a common-word blocklist) trigger force-loading of the entity page AND every `[[summaries/X]]` backlink it contains. So "list articles by Alice Smith" now loads `entities/tali-rezun.md` plus all 50+ summaries her entity references, regardless of whether "tali" or "rezun" appears in those summary filenames. (2) **Author-aware catalogue** — new `buildSummaryToEntitiesIndex()` builds a reverse map (summary → entities that backlink to it) by scanning every entity page's body for `[[summaries/X]]` markers, then `buildSlugCatalogue()` enriches each summary's catalogue line with `· referenced by: tali-rezun, cotrugli-business-school, ...`. The LLM can now enumerate "all articles by X" from the catalogue alone, even for summaries the budget couldn't load in full. Pivot entity pages additionally show their backlink count (`(N summary backlinks)`) so the LLM knows how prolific the pivoted entity is. (3) **Query-intent detection** — new `detectQueryIntent()` classifies queries as `enumerate` (triggers: "list", "what articles", "how many", "every", "name all", "by Dr X", etc.) vs `synthesis` (default). Enumerate queries get a different prompt that emphasises COMPLETENESS over synthesis: *"For items you only see in the catalogue, still LIST them by title and path. Do not skip items just because you don't have full content for them. The catalogue's 'referenced by: X' suffix is the authorship signal."* Synthesis queries get the original prompt. **Empirical results on the dev machine's real 3,250-page articles wiki** (5 scenarios run twice each = 10 LLM responses, all passed both runs): L1 enumerate-by-author returned 15+ unique summary citations vs the pre-beta.13 chat's 6. L4 count query returned consistent counts of 33-39 articles by Dr. Alice Smith across the four runs (vs 6 before). L3 entity-pivot query on "COTRUGLI Business School" returned 11-16 citations across runs. The synthesis-side regression tests (L2 RAG, L5 energy footprint) all passed too — beta.13 doesn't regress on the queries beta.11 already fixed. **Tunables bumped**: catalogue budget 8 KB → 12 KB (room for author metadata), max-pages-loaded 40 → 50 (more pivot backlinks fit). Content budget 60 KB unchanged. **Test coverage**: new `scripts/test-beta13-fixes.js` with 62 offline assertions covering every new function + regression on existing chat behaviour; new `scripts/test-beta13-chat-live.js` with 5 live-LLM scenarios run TWICE for stability against the real articles wiki. All 10 LLM runs passed both runs of all 5 scenarios. **Documentation**: `docs/ingestion-pipeline.md` §10b "Chat read-side" rewritten with new Mermaid flow showing entity-pivot + author-aware catalogue + intent detection; user-guide.md §9 chat section expanded with best-practices for asking enumerate vs synthesis queries; CLAUDE.md release entry. **Backward compatibility**: zero schema changes, zero breaking API changes. All new functions exported additively. Existing conversations work unchanged. The catalogue + entity-pivot logic is also a building block for future improvements (graph-walking retrieval, sources-transparency UI). **No regressions in the offline suite**: 635 offline assertions all green across 8 suites (beta13 + beta11 + beta10 + beta8-stress + ingest-fixes + sharedbrain-{local,push,pull,security,synthesis}). |
| `v3.0.1-beta.12` | **Ingest-report UX + documentation polish.** Two motivations from real-user feedback after the beta.11 release: (1) a typical ingest now produces 5–15 "warnings", but the great majority are actually SUCCESSES of the new safeguards (trunk-page injected, hub linkification added N wikilinks, Jaccard auto-redirected a semantic dupe) — labelling them all as amber "warnings" made the result panel look more alarming than it actually was; (2) users didn't know what the Jaccard 0.XX score meant when it appeared in the report. **Three changes:** (a) **UI rendering of the ingest report in [src/public/app.js](src/public/app.js)** now classifies each entry into four buckets — ✓ Auto-fixed (green), ⚠ For review (amber), ⚠ Attention (red), ℹ Info (blue) — by text-pattern match against the warning string. The banner border + background colour reflects the most-severe entry. The header now reads "Ingest finished — N notes" with an inline summary like "8 auto-fixed · 4 for review". Each item carries its own category icon + label so the user instantly sees what (if anything) needs their attention vs what was handled automatically. (b) **New comprehensive ingest-report reference in [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md) §8** — full reference table covering every category of entry: text pattern, category, what it means, what the Curator did, and what (if anything) the user should do about it. Covers all 12+ entry types currently emitted by the pipeline. (c) **Plain-English Jaccard similarity explanation in the same section** — formula in plain words, worked examples from real ingest reports (`the-human-touch` / `human-touch` = 0.67, `expert-roundup-format` / `experts-roundup-format` = 1.0 after stem), full table of action by score (0–0.49 keeps both silently, 0.50–0.84 keeps both with a "For review" note, 0.85–1.0 auto-redirects), plus an explanation of why the lightweight singular/plural stem matters (`collections` → `collection` so plural variants get caught) and why entities are deliberately excluded from semantic dedup (proper-noun variants are often genuinely different). **No code changes to the ingest pipeline itself** — purely a presentation layer + documentation upgrade. The `warnings` array in the result API is unchanged for backward compatibility; the UI just classifies it differently on render. **Test coverage**: no new tests because the change is text-classification-only and the existing renderIngestWarnings was previously untested; the classifier is pure-function so future tests can add unit coverage easily. All 573 offline assertions + 146 deep-ingest assertions remain green. Server + MCP module loads verified. **No breaking changes**: result panel just looks clearer; the data flowing through hasn't changed. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.11` | **Five-fix bundle responding to a deep community-member bug report.** A user running Anthropic Haiku on their Curation domain reported four distinct issues with sharp, code-level diagnoses; investigation confirmed all four were real and surfaced a fifth (UI rendering edge case). **Fix 1 — Chat 90k truncation (CRITICAL).** [src/brain/chat.js](src/brain/chat.js) had `wikiContext.slice(0, 90000)` over the concatenated body of every wiki page in arbitrary readdir order. On any domain >90 KB (which on the dev machine's articles domain means a 4.4 MB wiki → ~98% truncated), the chat LLM saw a prefix of `index.md` + `log.md` and ZERO actual entity/concept/summary pages. Chat was effectively non-functional on mature domains. Rewrote with query-driven `selectRelevantPages()`: tokenize query + recent history → score each page (filename × 3 + heading × 2 + body-head × 1) → load top-scoring pages until 60 KB budget filled. Always includes a compact slug catalogue (~8 KB) so the LLM knows the breadth of the domain. Excludes `index.md` and `log.md` from full-content selection. Falls back to most-linked hub pages when no token match. **Fix 2 — Phase 2 batches can't link to other batches' slugs.** [src/brain/ingest.js](src/brain/ingest.js) `buildBatchPrompt` now accepts `allOutlinePages` and exposes it as a "PAGES BEING CREATED IN THIS SAME INGEST" block split by folder (entities/concepts/summaries with correct link conventions). The LLM now knows what slugs sibling batches will produce and can wikilink to them. New HUB-PAGE RULE explicitly instructs the LLM to wikilink each enumerated item by exact slug. **Fix 3 — Semantic near-duplicate slug drift.** New exported function `redirectSemanticDuplicates(outline, existingFiles)` in `ingest.js` runs after `validateOutline`: for each proposed concept slug, computes Jaccard similarity (with lightweight singular/plural stemming — trim trailing `s` if remainder ≥3 chars) against existing on-disk concept slugs AND against other slugs already in this outline. ≥0.85 auto-redirect; 0.5-0.85 warn. Reuses `tokenize` + `jaccardSimilarity` primitives directly from `src/brain/sharedbrain-delta.js` — pure functions, zero new dependencies. Catches `expert-roundup-format` ↔ `experts-roundup-format`, `tools-directory` ↔ `tools-directory-format`, etc. Entities are intentionally untouched (proper-noun slug variants are usually genuinely different). **Fix 4 — Post-batch hub-page linkification.** New `linkifyHubPages(domain, canonicalPaths)` runs after all Phase 2 batches finish. For each concept page just written, detects "hub-shape" (≥5 list items + ≤2 existing wikilinks), then scans for plain-text mentions of OTHER pages' titles/slugs from this same ingest and wraps them in `[[brackets]]`. Conservative: whole-word case-insensitive match; skips code blocks/inline code/already-wikilinked content; uses `[[slug|Display Title]]` so user-visible text stays the same. **Critical sub-bug found during live LLM testing**: the original implementation modified the segments array in-place across multiple candidates, so the second candidate's case-insensitive regex matched the freshly-wrapped lowercase slug inside the FIRST wrap — producing nested `[[[[microsoft]]` patterns. Fix: re-split the content between every candidate so newly-added `[[wikilinks]]` become protected segments in the next iteration. Regression test (Test 14 in `scripts/test-beta11-fixes.js`) ensures this can never come back. **Fix 5 — CSS vertical character column bug.** `.change-path` in [src/public/styles.css](src/public/styles.css) used `word-break: break-all`, which wrapped at every character when the flex column was squeezed to less than one glyph width by a very long unbroken filename. Replaced with `overflow-wrap: anywhere` + `min-width: 4ch` on the path and `min-width: 0` on the parent `<li>` so the flex column can shrink correctly without breaking each character onto its own line. Bug latent since v2.5.0; only triggered with extremely long summary filenames in narrow viewports. **Test coverage**: new `scripts/test-beta11-fixes.js` with 42 offline assertions across chat retrieval contract (5 tests), Jaccard dedup including singular/plural (5 tests), `buildBatchPrompt` full-outline threading (3 tests), and the nested-bracket regression guard (5 tests). Extended `scripts/test-ingest-deep.js` with three new live-LLM scenarios: SYN-9 hub-shaped source (verifies ≥4 sibling wikilinks in the hub page), SYN-10 Jaccard across two ingests (singular + plural roundup articles), plus the existing REAL-1 re-ingest now exercises all five fixes end-to-end. **Empirical impact on the deep test**: REAL-1 (`The_Energy_and_Water_Footprint_of_Generative_AI.pdf`) went from 30 broken wikilinks (16.4% of total) on beta.9 down to **0 broken wikilinks** on beta.11, while the hub linkifier added 9 wikilinks across 3 hub-shaped pages. All 146 live-LLM deep-ingest assertions green. Full regression: 573 offline + 146 live-LLM = **719 assertions all passing**. **Documentation**: `docs/ingestion-pipeline.md` updated with new high-level Mermaid flow (now shows `redirectSemanticDuplicates` and `linkifyHubPages` stages), three new entries in the failure-mode catalogue, new §10b "The chat read-side" describing the query-driven page selection, refreshed §9 test-suite map with the new beta.11 suite, and refreshed §10 known-limitations noting the LLM-driven semantic-dupe gap is partially closed but the Health-side scan remains the LLM-judged backstop. Synthetic test inputs `08-hub-shape.md`, `09a-roundup-singular.md`, `09b-roundup-plural.md` committed alongside the test harness for reproducibility. **Backward compatibility**: zero schema changes, zero breaking API changes, all new exports additive. Existing wikis benefit automatically on next ingest; existing wikis with pre-beta.11 hub pages will get cleaned up the next time the source is re-ingested (manual workaround: user has already done this for the curation-format-library hub). No data migration. Server + MCP module loads verified. **The community member's diagnoses were sharp**: every code-level claim they made checked out against the current code, and one — the chat 90k slice — was a critical bug we'd shipped for many months without any test catching it (because all existing tests covered the WRITE path, not the READ path). The new beta.11 test suite closes that gap so future regressions in chat retrieval will be caught offline. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.10` | **Last-ingest-date display bug + actionable updater error classifier.** Two small, surgical fixes both surfaced by community-member bug reports. **Bug 1 — "Last ingest" date in the Domains tab was always wrong** (showing the date of the FIRST-EVER ingest of a domain rather than the most recent one). Confirmed empirically on the dev machine: the Projects domain's log.md had 25 ingest entries spanning April–May 2026 including a fresh `2026-05-21` entry from today, but the UI rendered `2026-04-26`. Root cause at [src/brain/files.js:1402-1408](src/brain/files.js) in `getDomainStats`: `content.match(/^## \[(\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2})\]/m)` — the `m` flag matches across lines but **without the `g` flag returns only the FIRST match**. Since `appendLog` appends new entries to the end of `log.md`, "first match" was always the oldest entry. Fix replaces with `matchAll(/.../gm)` + lexicographic max — ISO-8601 dates sort correctly as strings, so this is also robust to logs that have been manually reordered by the user. **Bug 2 — Updater EACCES from corrupted npm cache produced a cryptic error message.** A user's `~/.npm/_cacache` had a partial file from a previous force-quit; every subsequent `npm install` failed with `EACCES: permission denied, rename ...` but the only thing the Settings → Updates banner showed was `Update failed: Command failed: npm install --silent --no-audit --no-fund`. Zero hint that `npm cache clean --force` would resolve it. **New exported helper `classifyNpmError(rawMessage, beforeSha, afterSha)`** in [src/routes/config.js](src/routes/config.js) classifies the npm-install failure into one of: `cache-corrupted` (EACCES / errno -13 / permission denied / rename...File exists → actionable text pointing at `npm cache clean --force`), `disk-full` (ENOSPC), `network` (ETIMEDOUT / ECONNRESET / socket hang up / ENOTFOUND), `lockfile` (EINTEGRITY → rm -rf node_modules), `path` (existing partial-success handler) and `unknown` (raw npm output surfaced — never hide an error we don't recognise). For each known kind the actionable message includes the concrete next-step command and the before/after SHAs so the user knows the file update DID succeed even though dependencies didn't install. **Test coverage**: new `scripts/test-beta10-fixes.js` adds 32 offline assertions covering both fixes — `getDomainStats` returns the latest date across multi-entry logs, single-entry logs, missing log files, and manually-reordered entries; `classifyNpmError` correctly buckets EACCES, errno -13, ENOSPC, ETIMEDOUT, ECONNRESET, socket hang up, ENOTFOUND, EINTEGRITY, npm-command-not-found, the exact user-reported error string from the field report, and falls through cleanly on unknown errors plus null/undefined/empty inputs. All 499 existing offline assertions (94 ingest-fixes + 70 beta.8-stress + 70 sharedbrain-local + 83 push + 56 pull + 37 security + 89 synthesis) still green. Server + MCP module loads verified. **Backward compatibility**: zero schema changes, no breaking API changes; the `classifyNpmError` export is new but additive. Both fixes apply automatically — no migration, no user action required. Existing wikis with stale-looking "Last ingest" dates self-correct on the next page load. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.9` | **In-depth ingest stress test + three pipeline quality fixes + new technical doc.** Bundle that came out of a comprehensive stress-test pass on the v3.0.1-beta.8 ingest pipeline using a new deep-test harness ([scripts/test-ingest-deep.js](scripts/test-ingest-deep.js)) which runs the FULL production pipeline (live Gemini 2.5 Flash) across 9 scenarios (tiny baseline, trunk-cluster trigger, JSON-stressor content, multi-phase trigger, honorific author with diacritic, empty file, near-empty file, re-ingest idempotency, real-world re-ingest of `The_Energy_and_Water_Footprint_of_Generative_AI.pdf`) and applies a strict 10-point quality contract to every output (atomic-write contract, frontmatter validation, type-tag presence, wikilink resolution, backlink bidirectionality, index integrity, Health-scan cleanliness). **Initial test pass surfaced three real issues:** (1) **LLM appends `.md` to wikilinks** — Gemini Flash routinely produced `[[summaries/article.md]]` and `[[llama-3.1.md]]` instead of `[[summaries/article]]` and `[[llama-3.1]]`. Across the test suite this accounted for ~80% of all broken wikilinks (~14-21% of total links). (2) **`injectFrontmatter` fast path missed type tag** — when the LLM mirrors a source's pre-existing YAML frontmatter into its output (especially common on `.md` sources that already have their own `---` block), the fast-path branch in `injectFrontmatter` only sanitised the existing tags line without ensuring `type/<type>` is present — breaking Obsidian's graph-color contract for those pages. (3) **Phase 2 batches mention entities not on the page plan** — the LLM legitimately references entities in the source (e.g. `[[bitcoin-mining]]`, `[[chile]]`) that didn't make the Phase 1 outline, so the resulting wikilinks dangle. **Three coordinated fixes:** (a) **`writePage` step 5b2 strips `.md` from wikilinks** in [src/brain/files.js](src/brain/files.js) — a single regex `\[\[([^\]|#\n]+?)\.md(\|[^\]]+)?\]\]/g` → `[[$1$2]]` applied after the existing folder-prefix strip; preserves `|alias` suffixes; runs on all non-index/log writes. (b) **`injectFrontmatter` always ensures `type/<type>` tag** when content arrives with existing YAML — properly parses the frontmatter block, either patches the existing `tags:` line (adding the type tag if missing) or injects a new `tags:` line if none exists. (c) **`auditBrokenWikilinks` post-write reporter** in [src/brain/ingest.js](src/brain/ingest.js) — after every successful ingest, scans every page just written, counts wikilinks that don't resolve, and emits a warning like *"3 of 90 wikilinks (3.3%) don't resolve to an existing page. Examples: open-source-ai, mixture-of-experts, llama-3.1. Run Wiki Health → Ask AI to fix or strip them."* — surfaces the quality signal the user previously had to run a separate Health scan to discover. Non-fatal; informational. Cheap (single in-process pass; ~milliseconds even on 30-page domains). **After fixes the broken-link rate dropped from 14-21% to 1-3%** across all 9 scenarios; all 106 assertions in the deep-test harness now pass with zero failures. The remaining 1-3% are LLM-compliance issues (Phase 2 batches mentioning entities not on the plan) that the new audit warns the user about so they can triage via Wiki Health. **New technical documentation**: [docs/ingestion-pipeline.md](docs/ingestion-pipeline.md) (~750 lines) is the definitive developer reference for the pipeline — covers the high-level Mermaid flow, every stage of the pipeline in detail, the full LLM-failure-mode catalogue with how each one is caught, the 10-point quality contract every clean ingest guarantees, concurrency and crash safety, provider-specific behaviour (Gemini vs Anthropic asymmetries), performance characteristics, the test-suite map, and known limitations + future work. Cross-linked from `README.md` (Documentation section), `docs/README.md`, `docs/architecture.md` (Ingest data flow header), and `docs/user-guide.md` (§8 Ingest a source + §21 Further reading). **Synthetic test inputs** committed at `scripts/test-ingest-deep-inputs/` — 7 hand-crafted MD/TXT files covering tiny baseline, trunk-cluster trigger (developing 5 sibling sub-concepts of "memory" with no parent — verifies trunk detector fires on real LLM output), JSON-stressor content (with embedded code blocks, quoted strings, JSON-looking text), multi-phase trigger (>15k chars on agent architectures), honorific author with diacritic ("Dr. Tali Režun" → must resolve to canonical `tali-rezun` slug), empty file (0 bytes, must be refused with raw-file rollback), near-empty file (<200 chars, same). **Test coverage growth**: 70 stress tests (beta.8) + 94 ingest-fixes + 18 existing live-LLM + new **106 deep-ingest assertions** = 288 ingest-specific assertions across the pipeline. **No backward-incompatible changes** — the three fixes are all additive (extra cleanup step, extra warning, extra audit). Existing wikis benefit on the next ingest. **Server boots clean; MCP server boots clean; all routes load.** Found via deliberate live-LLM stress testing rather than user reports — exactly the kind of issue that hides in production for months without active hunting. The deep-test harness is now a permanent CI-style check for future ingest changes. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.8` | **Concurrency safety + granularity-inversion fix + LLM truncation detection.** Bundle responding to two user reports: (1) a power user observed "src files disappear when Curator does multiple things at a time" — clicking Update or Sync while an ingest was mid-flight; (2) another user's wiki had 28 "taste-*" pages but no parent `taste.md`, leaving every `[[taste]]` link broken (the "granularity inversion" problem). Investigation surfaced a third issue confirmed by audit: when Anthropic Haiku hits `max_tokens`, the SDK returns truncated JSON with `stop_reason: "max_tokens"` and the existing v3.0.1-beta.7 "AI returned malformed JSON twice" path mislabels it as transient — but it's deterministic and retry never helps. **Four-part fix bundle, ~600 LOC + 87 new test assertions:** (1) **Atomic writes** — new [src/brain/atomic-write.js](src/brain/atomic-write.js) exports `writeFileAtomic` / `writeFileAtomicSync` using the standard temp-file + rename pattern (POSIX `rename(2)` is atomic per-file). All wiki writes in [src/brain/files.js](src/brain/files.js) (14 call sites: writePage, syncSummaryEntities, injectSummaryBacklinks, injectSingleBacklink, injectRelatedLink, appendLog, writeIndex, conversation JSON, createDomain × 3, renameDomain × 3), destructive Health fixes in [src/brain/health.js](src/brain/health.js) (7 sites covering broken-link rewrites + cross-folder/hyphen merges), `.curator-config.json` sync writes in [src/brain/config.js](src/brain/config.js), Health dismissals JSONL rewrite in [src/brain/health-dismissed.js](src/brain/health-dismissed.js), Shared Brain local-adapter writes in [src/brain/sharedbrain-local-adapter.js](src/brain/sharedbrain-local-adapter.js), and the ingest raw-file save in [src/brain/ingest.js](src/brain/ingest.js) all funnel through the new module. **Same-directory tempfile naming** (e.g. `<dir>/.tmp-<base>-<pid>-<counter>`) — POSIX rename is only atomic within a single filesystem, and `getDomainsDir()` can be a USB volume or network mount, so `os.tmpdir()` would EXDEV-fail. **Symlink refusal** — `lstat`-based pre-check refuses to write through a symlink so a user's manual symlink isn't silently replaced with a regular file. **Append-only JSONL audit logs left alone** — sharedbrain audit JSONL and MCP write log use `appendFile`, which is already crash-safe at line granularity; converting them would be a regression. (2) **Trunk-page detector + structural checks** — `validateOutline` in [src/brain/ingest.js](src/brain/ingest.js) now scans the LLM's planned page list for clusters of ≥3 concept pages sharing a common first-segment prefix (`<prefix>-...`) and, if `concepts/<prefix>.md` is missing, injects it directly after the summary so Phase 2 writes it BEFORE any of its children. Concepts-only (entity clusters like `openai-gpt-4`, `openai-gpt-5` are more often legitimate sub-pages or misclassifications that writePage's cross-folder dedup handles). Single-character prefixes filtered to avoid noise. Plus four structural-check warnings: duplicate paths in outline, missing entities/ pages, missing concepts/ pages, < 3 pages total. **Verified end-to-end on live Gemini Flash output** — the new live test ingested `from-graph-to-intelligence-my-curator-mcp.md` and Gemini produced 3 sibling `knowledge-*` pages without a parent; the detector caught it and injected `concepts/knowledge.md`. (3) **Write registry + cross-process file lock** — new [src/brain/write-registry.js](src/brain/write-registry.js) provides a `Map<domain, refcount>` in-memory tracker that long-running operations (`/api/ingest`, `/api/compile/conversation`, `/api/health/:domain/fix-all`) join on entry and leave on exit (`try/finally`). Conflicting endpoints (`/api/update`, `/api/restart`, `/api/sync/push|pull|sync|disconnect`, `DELETE /api/domains/:slug`) check via `hasActiveWrites()` / `isDomainActive(domain)` and return **409 Conflict** with a structured body naming the active domain + ops, so the user sees *"Cannot update the app while a write operation is running: articles (ingest). Please wait for it to finish, then try again."* Update flow additionally sets a global `_updateInProgress` flag (`beginUpdate` / `endUpdate`) that ingest + compile check first — closes the millisecond race where an update arrives between ingest's check and add. **File-based lock under `<domain>/.write-lock`** (JSON payload with pid + startedAt + op) provides cross-process coordination for the MCP child process spawned by Claude Desktop — its `compile_to_wiki` and `fix_wiki_issue` tools both `acquireFileLock` before any destructive write. 30-minute stale-lock TTL + `process.kill(pid, 0)` liveness probe means a crashed holder's lock auto-clears on the next acquire attempt. Lock cleanup ALWAYS runs (`finally` blocks on all callers). (4) **Frontend button disable during ingest** — new busy-state tracker in [src/public/app.js](src/public/app.js) registers when an ingest SSE stream opens via `window.__curatorIngestStart(domain)` and `__curatorIngestEnd`. While any ingest is active, the Update / Sync / Delete buttons (`#settings-update-btn`, `#sync-both-btn`, `#sync-push-btn`, `#sync-pull-btn`, `#sync-disconnect-btn`, `.domain-delete-btn`) get `disabled = true` with a tooltip *"An ingest is in progress — please wait for it to finish."* Original disabled/title state is captured before disable so re-enable restores faithfully. Two-layer defense: most users never see the 409 because the click is impossible; the 409 is still the canonical safety net for non-UI clients (curl, scripts, MCP). **Plus 4 bonus fixes from the audit:** (a) **Anthropic stop_reason detection** in [src/brain/llm.js](src/brain/llm.js) — when `message.stop_reason === 'max_tokens'`, throws an actionable "Claude hit the output token limit (N tokens) on this call... This is NOT a transient JSON error — retrying will hit the same limit" message naming the cause and suggesting source-splitting or switching to Sonnet. Same protection added for Gemini via `finishReason === 'MAX_TOKENS'` so neither provider can produce the misleading v3.0.1-beta.7 "transient JSON" error from a budget cap. Defensive guard on missing `content[0].text` (catches tool-use-only responses). (b) **Empty-extraction guard + raw rollback** — `pdf-parse` wrapped in try/catch; failures throw with an actionable "encrypted/scanned PDF, run OCR first" message AND delete the lingering raw file so the user isn't 409-blocked on retry. Minimum text length 200 chars enforced — image-only PDFs (which "extract" 0 chars) used to silently hallucinate a wiki from the filename; now refused early with a specific OCR hint. (c) **`validateOutline` malformed-entry defense** — drops entries with null/missing path before any structural check so a malformed LLM response can't throw on `.startsWith` against undefined. (d) **MCP `compile_to_wiki` + `fix_wiki_issue` file-lock integration** — both now refuse with `conflict: 'file_lock'` and a clear message if the Curator app is mid-ingest on the same domain, instructing the user to delete the lock file manually if they believe it's stale. **Test coverage**: 70 new offline assertions in [scripts/test-beta8-stress.js](scripts/test-beta8-stress.js) (atomic writes basic + cleanup + symlink refusal + concurrent burst + SIGKILL simulation, EXDEV defense, trunk detector all 6 cases + structural checks all 5 cases, write registry in-memory + conflict response + update flag, file-based lock acquire/release/staleness/unparseable, end-to-end writePage); 17 new live-LLM assertions in [scripts/test-beta8-live-llm.js](scripts/test-beta8-live-llm.js) (real Gemini ingest verifies trunk detector fires correctly on actual LLM output, atomic-write contract holds — no zero-byte files anywhere, no orphan tempfiles, change records flow cleanly); existing test-ingest-fixes.js updated to accommodate new structural warnings (94 still green); all sharedbrain assertions still green (365); existing test-ingest-real-llm.js (18 assertions) still green end-to-end. **Total: 683 offline + 35 live-LLM assertions green** across sharedbrain + ingest + beta.8 stress + live LLM. **Backward compatibility**: no schema changes, no breaking API changes. The 409 response is new but graceful — clients that don't handle it see a clear error message; the existing UI was the only client and now handles it via the busy-state tracker. Server boots clean; MCP server boots clean; all routes load. **Migration path**: auto-updater applies the bundle. Existing users get atomic writes for free (no behavior-visible difference); trunk detector engages on next ingest for any source with sibling-cluster patterns; existing wikis with already-orphan `[[taste]]` links remain orphan until re-ingest (a future v3.0.2 Health-side fix could offer "create trunk page" for retroactive cleanup). Found during the audit triggered by user-reported issues on Claude Haiku ingests; verified end-to-end on the live LLM test where the trunk detector fired on actual Gemini Flash output. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.7` | **Phase 1 outline retry + actionable error message on JSON parse failure.** Real-world ingest of `OpenClaw_ The AI Assistant That Lives in Your Pocket.pdf` surfaced a JSON parse failure: *"Could not parse JSON response. Response length: 13725 chars."*. The LLM occasionally returns malformed JSON — unescaped quote in a summary string, stray backtick, or a control character — that defeats native `JSON.parse`, markdown-fence stripping, brace-block extraction, AND the `jsonrepair` library fallback all at once. Single-pass and Phase 2 batch parses both already had retry paths (single-pass retries with strict-brevity if the response is under 8 KB; Phase 2 falls back to page-by-page on batch parse failure). **Phase 1 (outline) had no retry path** — a single bad response surfaced as a fatal error with the technical "Last 200 chars: ..." message, which gave the user no actionable guidance. **Two-layer fix in [src/brain/ingest.js](src/brain/ingest.js)**: (1) **Phase 1 retry with stricter JSON guidance**. If the first outline parse fails, re-issue the call with an appended block: `IMPORTANT — STRICT JSON REQUIREMENTS: Return ONLY the JSON object. No markdown fences. Inside summary strings do NOT use double quotes — use single quotes. Keep summaries under 120 chars to reduce malformed-string risk. Response must parse with native JSON.parse on the first try.` This is non-deterministic (LLM output varies) so a second pass almost always succeeds — the most common failure mode is one cluster of bad characters in the middle of an otherwise valid response. (2) **Actionable error message if BOTH attempts fail**. Replaces the cryptic "Last 200 chars: ..." text with a numbered list of next steps: try Ingest again (non-deterministic), split the PDF, or switch AI provider in Settings. The message explicitly notes this is a transient AI-provider issue, NOT a Curator/file problem — consistent with the v3.0.1-beta.4 principle (every error must make clear whether the issue is upstream or in The Curator). When the retry succeeds, the user sees a warning banner: *"Phase 1 outline returned malformed JSON; auto-retried with stricter prompt"* — so they know what happened but don't get blocked. **Cost**: one additional LLM call per failed-then-recovered ingest (rare). At Gemini Flash Lite rates that's ≈ $0.0002 — invisible. **No behaviour change to single-pass or Phase 2**: those retry paths already exist and are unaffected. **No regression test changes** (the retry is triggered by LLM error states that unit tests don't reproduce — verified via live ingest of the user's actual problem PDF). All 250 assertions across sharedbrain + ingest battle tests still green. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.6` | **Accurate per-direction sync file counts.** Real-world Sync now report from a user re-ingesting articles read: *"Sync complete — Pulled 206 files from GitHub, pushed 206 files to GitHub"*, even though they hadn't changed anything on the other machine. The diagnosis from inspecting their actual git state: origin/main genuinely had no new content from Computer 2; the **same 206 local re-ingest modifications were being counted twice** under different labels. Root cause: both `pull()` and `push()` used **symmetric diff ranges** (`HEAD..origin/main` and `origin/main..HEAD`) which count files differing in EITHER direction — so any file modified locally appears in both directions of the diff. The fix uses `git merge-base` to find the common ancestor and count only files that each side genuinely added on top of it. **Changes in [src/brain/sync.js](src/brain/sync.js)**: (1) `pull()` count now uses `git diff --name-only $(git merge-base HEAD origin/main)..origin/main` — files origin advanced beyond the common ancestor, i.e. truly incoming content. (2) `push()` count now uses `git diff --name-only $(git merge-base HEAD origin/main)..HEAD` — files local advanced beyond the common ancestor, i.e. truly outgoing content. Same scenario after the fix would report *"Pulled 0 files from GitHub, pushed 206 files to GitHub"* — accurately reflecting that nothing came down and the user's re-ingests went up. The fix is defensive: when `merge-base` returns empty (extreme edge case like detached histories on a brand-new repo), push falls back to the old symmetric diff so the count is never zero when it shouldn't be. Pull's first-time-sync path is untouched (the catch around the whole block already handles "no origin yet"). **No functional sync behaviour changes** — `git pull --no-rebase -X theirs` and `git push -u origin main` are exactly the same. Only the file-counting metric reported to the UI changes. **No regression test changes needed** (count metric is human-facing display, not behaviour). All 250 assertions across sharedbrain + ingest battle tests still green. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.5` | **Pending-sync badge in the navbar.** Surfaces uncommitted local changes (deletions, ingests, edits) from every tab so the user can't accidentally believe they've synced when they haven't. The bug pattern this prevents: user deletes a domain on Computer 1, the deletion succeeds locally, the user moves on to other work, then opens Computer 2 expecting the deletion to have propagated — but it never made it past the local working tree because no Sync click happened after the delete. Diagnosed live during user testing of v3.0.1-beta.4: the `git status` on Computer 1 showed three `D` (unstaged-deletion) entries for the deleted domain, and the most recent push was hours before the delete. The Sync tab already shows "N local changes not yet pushed" — but only when the user actually opens that tab. From any other tab the user has no visual signal of pending state. **Implementation** (~50 LOC total across 3 files): (1) New `<span id="sync-pending-badge">` element inside the Sync tab button in [src/public/index.html](src/public/index.html). (2) New `.sync-pending-badge` CSS class in [src/public/styles.css](src/public/styles.css) — pill-shaped, warning-yellow (`var(--warning)`), 18px tall, with a 2.4s gentle outline-pulse animation that draws the eye without being obnoxious. Hides via `.hidden` class when count is zero. (3) New `applySyncPendingBadge(status)` + `refreshSyncPendingBadge()` functions in [src/public/app.js](src/public/app.js). **Refresh triggers**: app load (immediate background fetch); every tab-button click via a single delegated handler over `.tab-btn` (covers navigating to/from Sync, Domains, Ingest, etc.); after every successful sync operation via `renderSyncConfigured()` which all sync code paths already call; after a successful `DELETE /api/domains/:slug` so the badge bumps the instant the deletion is recorded; after an `ingest done` event so the user sees pending count grow during a re-ingest sweep; periodic `setInterval` every 60s as a safety net. **Hidden when sync is not configured** — the badge only appears for users who have set up GitHub sync and have actual pending changes. **No backend changes** — uses the existing `/api/sync/status` route which already returns `changesCount` from `git status --porcelain`. Cost per refresh: one local HTTP call + one git status; sub-millisecond. **Backward compatibility**: zero schema/API changes; older Curator users updating get the badge automatically. No regression test changes (UI-only feature). Sharedbrain regression suites + ingest battle tests still all green (94 + 71 + 56 + 29 = 250 assertions). |
| `v3.0.1-beta.4` | **Clearer Curator-vs-upstream error messages.** Paid Gemini API users who hit a transient HTTP 503 ("AI service is temporarily overloaded") were confused into thinking the issue was on their account or in The Curator — the message didn't disambiguate origin. The principle reinforced by this release: **every user-facing error must make clear whether the problem is in The Curator, in user configuration, or upstream at the AI provider — and what to do next.** Two messages in [src/brain/llm.js](src/brain/llm.js) rewritten: (1) **HTTP 503 (service overloaded)** — now explicitly names the provider (Gemini or Claude resolved from `getProviderInfo()`), states that it's a transient backend issue affecting ALL accounts equally (free and paid), confirms it's NOT a Curator/API-key problem, mentions that 4 retries with ~40 seconds of backoff already happened, and points the user at the cloud status dashboard plus the option to switch providers in Settings. (2) **HTTP 429 (rate limit)** — previously said only "the Gemini free tier allows 20 requests per day" which misled paid users on bulk operations. Now states it's an upstream account-level limit (not a Curator issue), explains BOTH free-tier (~15 RPM, ~20–50/day) and paid-plan (much higher, but reachable during bulk ops) thresholds, gives the exact retry delay parsed from the API hint, and links to the current pricing page. Both messages now lead with ⚠ + provider name + HTTP code so the origin is unmistakable at a glance. No retry/fallback logic changed — the same exponential backoff (3s → 9s → 27s) and 4-attempt cap apply. Provider name is resolved once at the top of `generateText` and reused in both error paths; if provider resolution fails (no key configured), the original "No LLM API key found" message from `getProviderInfo` is surfaced instead. **Testing**: 94/94 offline assertions remain green (no test changes needed — error-message text is human-facing copy, not behaviour). Module load smoke verified. The status banner in the UI (`showStatus` in `src/public/app.js`) renders the message via `escHtml` so URLs display as plain copyable text; making them clickable is a small followup left for a future release. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.3` | **Health hyphen-variant scanner hotfix — completes the v3.0.1-beta.2 work.** After v3.0.1-beta.2 landed, real-world Health scan still reported `Hyphen variants: 0` even though both `dr.-tali-rezun.md` and `tali-rezun.md` existed on disk. Diagnosis surfaced **three** distinct bugs in `src/brain/health.js` that the previous patch missed: (1) **Detection didn't use `normKey`**. The hyphen-variant grouping loop at the detection site had its OWN inline normalisation `stem.replace(/-/g, '').toLowerCase()` instead of calling the shared `normKey()` function — so the v3.0.1-beta.2 update to `normKey` (adding honorific stripping) was never exercised by the scan. Fixed by replacing the inline `replace` with `normKey(stemA)` / `normKey(stemB)`. (2) **Canonical-slug selection preferred the wrong file.** The original sort ranked by "most hyphens"; on `dr.-tali-rezun.md` (2 hyphens) vs `tali-rezun.md` (1 hyphen), this picked the honorific-prefixed form as canonical. Fixed by adding a primary sort key: slugs that DON'T match `HONORIFIC_PREFIX_RE` come first; ties broken by hyphen count, then length. (3) **`fixHyphenVariant`'s `SLUG_RE` rejected the dot.** Even if detection had worked, the fixer validated each slug with `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*$/i` — which excludes the period in `dr.-tali-rezun`. The fixer would silently skip the dr.- file and the merge would never happen. Relaxed to `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9.\-]*$/i` (allows embedded dot) and added explicit defenses against `..` / `/` / `\\` sequences before `resolveInsideWiki` is even called — defense in depth keeps the path-traversal hole closed. **Testing**: 4 new assertions added to `scripts/test-ingest-fixes.js` (section 10) that build a real tempdir wiki with `dr.-tali-rezun.md` + `tali-rezun.md` on disk and invoke the actual `scanWiki()` function — verifying that the pair is grouped as a hyphen variant, that BOTH files appear in the group, and that the canonical suggestion is the honorific-free `tali-rezun` (NOT `dr.-tali-rezun`). Total now 94/94 offline assertions green. **User migration path is unchanged**: update to v3.0.1-beta.3, click **Scan** on Wiki Health, click **Fix** on the `dr.-tali-rezun.md` / `tali-rezun.md` row in the "Hyphen variants" section. Future ingests on v3.0.1-beta.3 won't reproduce the bug (the v3.0.1-beta.2 regex fixes in writePage Pass A and validateOutline handle the write-time prevention; this release fixes the post-hoc cleanup path for ghost files already on disk). This is the second hotfix in 24 hours — both surfaced via real user re-ingests of the v3.0.1-beta.1 release. The pattern reinforces why live-data testing matters even after a comprehensive offline test suite. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.2` | **Honorific-with-period dedup hotfix.** Real-world re-ingest of `Data Sovereignty in the AI Age` (by Dr. Alice Smith) produced two entity files for the same person — `entities/dr.-tali-rezun.md` AND `entities/tali-rezun.md`. Root cause: the `TITLE_PREFIX_RE` regex in `files.js` (and the matching `HONORIFIC_RE` in `ingest.js`'s validator) required the honorific prefix to be followed directly by a hyphen — `^(dr\|mr\|ms\|mrs\|prof\|professor\|the)-/`. Gemini occasionally preserves the period from "Dr." when slugifying, producing `dr.-tali-rezun` instead of `dr-tali-rezun`. The regex didn't match the period, so neither `writePage`'s Pass A nor the v3.0.1-beta.1 validator caught the duplicate. **Three coordinated regex fixes:** (1) `TITLE_PREFIX_RE` in [src/brain/files.js](src/brain/files.js) gains an optional period: `^(dr\|mr\|ms\|mrs\|prof\|professor\|the)\.?-/`. This single source of truth flows through Pass A in `writePage` (filename redirect at write time), the bullet-resolution pass in `injectSummaryBacklinks`, and the variant-link normalization in step 5c. (2) `HONORIFIC_RE` in [src/brain/ingest.js](src/brain/ingest.js)'s `validateOutline` gets the same `\.?` so the originator-hint dedup map correctly identifies `dr.-X` and `X` as the same logical entity and redirects in place rather than injecting a second one. (3) **New `HONORIFIC_PREFIX_RE` added to Health's `normKey`** in [src/brain/health.js](src/brain/health.js) so the hyphen-variant Health scan now detects existing `dr.-X.md` ↔ `X.md` duplicates on pre-fix wikis and offers a merge via the existing Fix button. Without this, users who already accumulated `dr.-` ghost files (e.g. from re-ingests done on v3.0.1-beta.1) would have had no automated path to clean them up. **Testing**: 5 new assertions added to `scripts/test-ingest-fixes.js` covering the dr.- variant (case I2), the dr- variant without period (case I3), and the redirect warning. Total now 90/90 offline assertions green. **Migration for users on v3.0.1-beta.1**: (a) update to v3.0.1-beta.2 via Settings → Check for Updates; (b) open Wiki Health, click Scan — the updated `normKey` will now flag any `dr.-X.md` / `X.md` pairs as hyphen variants; (c) click Fix to merge them (union-merges bullet sections into the canonical slug, rewrites all `[[dr.-X]]` links across the domain, deletes the ghost file). Future ingests won't reproduce the bug. Found during the user's first real re-ingest sweep of the v3.0.1-beta.1 release — the kind of edge case only live-data testing surfaces. |
| `v3.0.1-beta.1` | **Ingestion accuracy fixes (responding to community feedback).** Closes five systematic gaps in the multi-phase ingest pipeline that surfaced after real-world use of v3.0.0-beta.1. Symptom (reported by a power user): "ingestion sometimes skips creating entities, summaries, and originator pages; sometimes creates multiple separate concept pages instead of one parent concept." Root cause: the multi-phase outline prompt (used for any source > 15k chars — most real PDFs) was much weaker than the single-pass prompt. The outline prompt said only *"Plan which wiki pages to create or update"* — no requirement to include a summary, no instruction to create an originator entity, no parent-over-children consolidation rule. Phase 2 batches can ONLY write pages listed in Phase 1's outline, so anything the outline omits is permanently lost for that ingest. **Seven fixes, all in `src/brain/ingest.js`:** (1) **Deterministic summary slug** computed from the source filename (`computeSummarySlugFromSource`) — `report.pdf` always lands on `summaries/report.md`, so re-ingesting the same file merges into the existing summary via `mergeWikiPage` instead of creating a second file. Mirrors the slug convention already used by `compile.js`. (2) **Strengthened Phase 1 outline prompt** with a numbered "REQUIRED COVERAGE" checklist: exactly one summary page at the forced canonical path, originator entity (author/speaker/creator), every person/tool/company/framework/dataset/project/country/organization, every key idea/technique, plus a CONSOLIDATION RULE that prefers one parent concept page over 3+ sibling pages when sub-ideas are closely related. (3) **`validateOutline` programmatic safety net** runs after Phase 1 parses — if the LLM still omits the summary or uses a non-canonical summary path, it's injected/redirected with a user-visible warning. Extra summaries are dropped to a single canonical one. **Originator-hint layer** (added during real-LLM testing): `extractAuthorHints()` scans the source for explicit author markers (YAML `author:` field including the `[[wikilink]]` form, "By Dr X" byline, "Author: X" line) and feeds the names to the validator. If the outline omitted the originator entity, it's injected at the FRONT of the pages list so it writes before any LLM-generated honorific variant; if the outline already contains a variant slug like `dr-tali-rezun.md`, the validator redirects it in-place to the canonical `tali-rezun.md` using the same Pass A + B normalisation that `writePage` applies at write time — preventing the duplicate-file failure mode where the LLM uses one slug variant and the validator another. Runs after single-pass too so the same guarantee applies regardless of which path is taken. (4) **Same coverage rules added to single-pass `buildPrompt`** with the forced summary path, originator rule, consolidation rule, and explicit "DO NOT touch index.md" instruction. (5) **80 k character truncation now surfaces a warning** — previously sources longer than 80k chars were silently truncated. Now the warning is emitted to the progress stream, included in the `result.warnings` array, written to the domain log, and rendered as an amber banner in the result panel. The 80k cap remains (raising it requires chunking past the model's context window — separate v3.x work). (6) **Replaced Phase 3 LLM-driven index regeneration with programmatic `mergeIntoIndex`** (ported from `compile.js`, now exported and reused). On large domains the 20+ KB markdown table saturated the LLM output budget — pages could land on disk but vanish from the index. Programmatic merge skips rows whose slugs are already mentioned, so re-ingest never duplicates. Per-page summary text is pulled from the LLM response or falls back to the outline's planned summary. (7) **Stub pages now clearly marked** — when the page-by-page fallback runs after a parse failure, the resulting placeholder gets a visible "⚠ **Stub page — AI failed to write this on ingest**" warning at the top + the planned summary preserved + a `stub` tag for Health-scanner discovery. Previously stubs shipped silently with just the title and a one-line summary, so users couldn't tell which pages were placeholders. **Re-ingest safety contract**: re-ingesting the same source file is fully idempotent — summary lands on the same slug → merge; entities/concepts dedup via the existing 3-pass mechanism in `writePage` (title-prefix, hyphen-norm, cross-folder) + the existing-files list passed to the LLM; index merge skips already-mentioned slugs; bullet sections accumulate via union merge with `deduplicateBulletSections` removing any duplicates introduced. **Migration**: auto-update lands the code fixes (no schema changes). For sources where information feels missing, re-ingest from Domains → Raw files — new prompts catch what the old ones missed; existing pages merge cleanly. Bulk re-ingest via `node scripts/bulk-reingest.js <domain>` is now safe — deterministic summary slug removes the v3.0.0 risk of creating duplicate summary files. **Testing**: 85-assertion offline battle test in `scripts/test-ingest-fixes.js` covers `computeSummarySlugFromSource` (10 cases), `validateOutline` (9 cases — including 4 originator-hint cases: missing → injected, already present → not duplicated, hyphen-norm prevents near-duplicate, empty hints array → no changes), `extractAuthorHints` (10 cases — YAML single + multi-line, quoted, wikilink-form, "By Dr X", "Author: X", empty/null/undefined defensive paths), `slugifyName` (6 cases including honorific stripping + diacritics), prompt-structure assertions (required-coverage keywords + forced summary path + no leftover index regeneration in single-pass + page-budget cap), `mergeIntoIndex` (6 cases including cross-folder canonical-path mapping and pipe/newline sanitisation), stub-page rendering, truncation constant. All 85 green. **Plus a real-LLM end-to-end test** in `scripts/test-ingest-real-llm.js` (18 assertions) — ingests the user's real `From Google AI Studio to Production` article via live Gemini Flash, verifies on first pass: deterministic summary slug, summary file written, "Entities Mentioned" populated with ≥5 wikilinks (got 39), author entity created (no missing-author bug), ≥3 entities + ≥3 concepts created, index row exists, log entry exists, no missing-summary warning. Then runs second ingest with `isOverwrite=true` and verifies: exactly one summary file on disk (no duplicate), exactly one author entity (no honorific variant duplicate), no duplicate index rows, no duplicate backlinks. All 18 green. Confirmed end-to-end against the exact source the user reported the bug on. Full sharedbrain regression suite (Phase 2A storage + Phase 2C pull + Phase 4B MCP guard) re-run and unaffected. **No breaking changes**: result shape gains optional `warnings` + `truncated` fields; older clients ignore them. SSE done event unchanged except for two additive fields. |
| `v3.0.0-beta.1` | **Shared Brain — Phase 4 (USER-FACING beta).** First user-facing release of the Shared Brain feature that lets a cohort/team/research group contribute to a collective wiki. Each contributor keeps a private Curator; only opted-in domains push LLM-synthesised Delta summaries to a shared private GitHub repo; the synthesised collective wiki pulls back as a read-only `shared-<slug>/` mirror domain. **Opt-in beta** — the `sharedBrainEnabled` flag in `.curator-config.json` stays default `false` for existing v2.x users, who see ZERO behaviour change until they explicitly click "Enable Shared Brain (beta)" in the Sync tab. Combined Phase 2 + Phase 3 + Phase 4 regression: **547 assertions green** (519 offline + 21 live network against the `talirezun/curator-sharedbrain-preflight` throwaway repo + 7 live PAT validation calls). **Phase 4A — Backend routes** (`src/routes/sharedbrain.js`, ~510 LOC, 11 endpoints): `GET /feature-flag`, `POST /enable-flag`, `GET /list`, `POST /save`, `DELETE /:id`, `POST /:id/push|pull|synthesize|revoke` (all SSE-streamed), `POST /parse-invite | /generate-invite | /validate-pat`. Invite token format `sbi_<base64url-JSON>` with `v: 1` versioning + `data_handling_terms` field (Decision 6c). PAT validator is server-proxy (Decision Q2 binding) — the user's PAT travels browser → localhost:3333 → GitHub once, never persisted until /save. 48 offline assertions + 7 live PAT assertions. **Phase 4B — MCP readonly-mirror guard** (Decision 7): new `refuseIfReadonly(domain)` helper in `mcp/util.js` lazy-imports `isDomainReadonly` from `src/brain/files.js` (already shipped in v2.7.0). Wired into all four MCP write tools — `compile_to_wiki`, `fix_wiki_issue`, `dismiss_wiki_issue`, `undismiss_wiki_issue` — which now refuse direct writes to `shared-*` domains with a clear steer: *"Domain 'shared-cohort' is a read-only Shared Brain mirror... To contribute, call this tool on your personal opted-in domain (e.g. 'work-ai'), then run 'Push contributions' from the Sync tab."* Read tools (`get_node`, `search_wiki`, `get_index`, etc.) intentionally do NOT call the guard — Claude can still read the collective wiki freely. SKILL.md gained §3.1 documenting the read/write contract so Claude knows where to compile when the user says "save this to the shared brain". 29 assertions including the negative case (read tools UNAFFECTED) and positive cases on all four write tools. **Phase 4C — Sync tab UI shell** (`src/public/index.html` + `styles.css` + `app.js`): "Shared Brains" block added below the existing Personal Sync section, divider for visual separation. Three states: **opt-in CTA** when flag is off, **empty state** with two paths after enabling, **connection list** once configured. Multi-connection support — users can be in multiple brains (cohort + work + research) with one card per connection. Per-connection card shows: 🧠 brain name, repo URL link, last-pushed / last-pulled relative-time ("just now", "2 hr ago", "3 days ago"), domain list, Push contributions + Pull updates buttons, Advanced disclosure with synthesize + fellow-ID pill + disconnect. **Phase 4D — Contributor wizard** (5 steps): paste invite token → confirm GitHub collaborator access → create + paste PAT with live validation → pick contributing personal domains + display name + attribution flag → review + consent + save. Live PAT validation calls `/api/sharedbrain/validate-pat` 400ms after paste; verdicts: ✓ valid + has write access / ⚠ valid but read-only / ✗ rejected. GitHub deep-link button opens the PAT creation page with the token name prefilled (`Curator Shared Brain - <Brain Name>`). Domains list filters out `shared-*` mirrors (can't contribute from a mirror). Consent text adapts based on the invite token's `data_handling_terms` field — contributor sees the same IP language the admin chose at brain setup. **Phase 4E — Admin wizard path**: same 5-step shell with first two steps swapped. Step 1 collects brain setup form: repo (owner/name), brain name, shared domain slug (auto-fills from brain name via slugifier — clears manual-override flag on wizard reopen, per audit fix), branch, data handling terms (radio cards: `contributor_retains` default vs `organisational` for enterprise IP transfer). Step 2 shows the generated invite token in a copy-to-clipboard box with a "Now invite your contributors as collaborators" checklist. Steps 3-5 are the contributor flow (admin is also a contributor). **Phase 4F — GDPR Article 17 revocation** (`src/brain/sharedbrain-revoke.js` + adapter primitives): five new methods on `SharedBrainStorageAdapter` interface — `deletePage`, `deleteContribution`, `deleteDigest`, `listFellowSubmissions`, `appendAudit` — implemented in both LocalFolder and GitHub adapters. Revoke orchestration: delete contributions → delete digest → scan all collective pages, delete any whose Provenance includes the revoked fellow's short ID → reset `state.last-synthesis` to epoch → re-run synthesis from scratch (rebuilds pages from remaining contributors; pages with zero remaining contributors stay deleted) → append audit record to `state/revocations.jsonl` (timestamp + UUID + sha256-hashed admin token + counts; NO real names, NO contribution content). Operation is irreversible — documented prominently. Returns SSE stream with audit_record + counts. Endpoint requires `admin_token` match + `confirmation: "REVOKE-<fellow_id>"` literal string (GitHub-style accident-prevention gate). Admin UI is API-only in v3.0.0-beta.1; Settings → Advanced → Revoke UI is a v3.0.0 GA deliverable. 39 battle-test assertions verify the full GDPR Article 17 property set. **Security audit fixes applied before release**: (1) XSS chain patched — `renderSharedBrainCard` previously interpolated `repoUrl` directly into href; now uses DOM API (`a.href = repoUrl`) which URL-encodes automatically + `validateConnection` now hardens `github_repo_owner` / `github_repo_name` / `github_branch` with explicit regex validation (GitHub username + repo-name + ref-name rules + reject `..` segments). (2) `/save` defensively rejects connections where `github_pat` ends in Unicode ellipsis (the mask marker) — protects against future UI bugs that might round-trip a masked listing back. (3) `/validate-pat` PAT length now bounded 20-400 chars (DoS defense). (4) Invite-token decoder branch-name regex tightened to reject `..` segments. (5) `populateSharedBrainDomains` error path no longer interpolates `err.message` into innerHTML — uses textContent. (6) Wizard state machine: `slugManuallyEdited` flag was a closure variable that persisted across wizard sessions — hoisted onto `sbWizard.state` so it resets on every `openSharedBrainWizard()` call. **Backward compatibility**: zero new dependencies, zero schema changes, zero breaking changes. Auto-updater path is the proven `git reset --hard origin/main` + `npm install` chain. Default `sharedBrainEnabled: false` means existing v2.8.0 users see literally nothing change until they opt in. The 11 new `/api/sharedbrain/*` routes return 404 when the flag is off. Pre-existing routes (`/api/ingest`, `/api/chat`, `/api/sync`, `/api/mcp`, `/api/health`, `/api/compile`, `/api/version`, `/api/restart`) unaffected. MCP server startup verified — `refuseIfReadonly`'s lazy import keeps the MCP child process startup unchanged. **Documentation sweep**: new `docs/shared-brain.md` user guide (350+ lines with 3 Mermaid diagrams: architecture, contribution flow, trust model) + roadmap section covering v3.0.0 GA, v3.1 (Cloudflare R2 with EU residency), v3.2 (Enterprise/GitHub App), and beyond. New `docs/shared-brain-admin.md` admin operations guide. `docs/shared-brain-compliance.md` (shipped in 4F) for GDPR/IP/EU residency reference. Cross-references updated across root `README.md`, `docs/README.md`, `docs/user-guide.md` (new §15b), `docs/architecture.md` (Shared Brain layer diagram), `docs/api-reference.md` (sharedbrain endpoints section), `docs/domains.md` (mirror-domain note), `docs/mcp-user-guide.md` (write-tool refusal documented), `docs/ai-health.md` (mirror-domain health contract). **Phase 5+ roadmap**: v3.0.0 GA (admin Revoke UI), v3.1 (Cloudflare R2 adapter for EU residency + custom-domain endpoints), v3.2 (Enterprise/GitHub App/SSO/SIEM), and beyond (cross-domain links, branch-per-cohort, diff history UI, admin roll-up dashboards). This release follows the project's branching-strategy principle (held locally between phases; one combined push at user-facing milestones) — the gap from v2.8.0 to v3.0.0-beta.1 represents Phases 4A through 4F all landing together. |
| `v2.8.0`  | **Shared Brain — Phase 3 infrastructure (NOT user-facing).** Lands `GitHubStorageAdapter` (`src/brain/sharedbrain-github-adapter.js`) — the production storage backend that replaces `LocalFolderStorageAdapter` for real-deployment Shared Brain cohorts. Implements all 10 methods of the `SharedBrainStorageAdapter` interface using GitHub's REST API: `GET/PUT /repos/.../contents/:path` for read/write with base64-encoded body, `GET /repos/.../git/trees/:branch?recursive=1` for efficient one-call tree listing, plus `DELETE /repos/.../contents/:path` for the Phase 4 admin revoke endpoint (Decision 6b). **Zero user-visible change** — `sharedBrainEnabled` stays default `false`, no routes registered, no UI exposes the new code paths. Existing v2.7.1 users updating to v2.8.0 receive new files but their app behaves identically. **Security baked in per Decision 1:** fine-grained PAT with Contents R/W at repo level only, all writes to the configured `branch` (default `main`), **SHA-based optimistic concurrency** with single refetch + retry on 409/422 conflicts before throwing typed `SHARED_BRAIN_SHA_CONFLICT` (no token in message); **skip-write optimisation** when remote bytes match local avoids cluttering git history with empty-diff commits; **rate-limit awareness** emits a stderr warning below 50 requests/hour remaining and throws typed `SHARED_BRAIN_RATE_LIMIT` on 403/429 with remaining=0. **Token-leak defence in depth (caught by offline test):** when GitHub returns an error body containing a credential-shaped string (adversarial proxy / misbehaving plugin scenario), the initial `_throwForStatus` echoed it verbatim. Fix: `sanitizeDetail()` redacts all six GitHub token prefixes (`github_pat_`, `ghp_`, `gho_`, `ghu_`, `ghs_`, `ghr_`) BEFORE the 200-char slice, so a token at byte 195 cannot survive truncation as a partial leak. Path-traversal hardening matches the local adapter — `isSafeId()` / `safeRelPath()` validates every domain, fellowId, submissionId, and relative wiki path before any HTTP call; `..` segments, absolute paths, control characters rejected before reaching the network. Storage layout in the repo matches the abstract interface exactly: `collective/<domain>/wiki/<path>`, `contributions/<fellow>/<sub>.json`, `digests/<fellow>/latest.json`, `meta/<key-as-path>.json`. Storage factory's `github` branch flipped from `throw "not implemented"` to instantiate `GitHubStorageAdapter`. Two new battle-test scripts: `scripts/test-sharedbrain-github-offline.js` (67 assertions, deterministic mock-fetch via injected `fetchImpl`, runs in regression sweep — covers request shapes, base64 encoding, SHA-conflict retry-success and retry-fail, skip-write, path traversal, rate-limit warning + exhaustion, multi-error-path token-leak audit) and `scripts/test-sharedbrain-github-live.js` (21 assertions, **LIVE NETWORK**, env-gated behind `GITHUB_TEST_REPO` + `GITHUB_TEST_PAT` — skips silently with exit 0 if either is missing, so default regression never hits the network. Covers full end-to-end against a real throwaway private repo: scaffold creation, two-fellow contribution push with contradictory facts, local synthesis with mock LLM picking "both" verdict, conflict marker appearance, Provenance section with shortened fellow IDs, third "puller" fellow cross-fellow visibility, exhaustive tree-based cleanup deleting every file the test created so the repo returns to its starting state). Combined Phase 2 + Phase 3 regression: 71 + 83 + 56 + 37 + 89 + 67 = **403 offline assertions green**; **21 live network assertions green** against `talirezun/curator-sharedbrain-preflight`. Phase 4 (routes `/api/sharedbrain/*` + Sync tab UI block + MCP readonly-mirror guard wiring + 6-step wizard with admin/contributor branching paths and invite-token UX for non-technical users) is next, on this same `claude/sweet-rubin-f1311c` worktree branch — held until the user-facing milestone is complete, then bumped to `v3.0.0-beta`. Documentation sweep (Phase 5) will integrate Shared Brain into `README.md`, `docs/user-guide.md`, `docs/domains.md`, `docs/mcp-user-guide.md`, `docs/ai-health.md`, `docs/model-lifecycle.md`, `claude-skills/my-curator/SKILL.md`, plus three new docs: `docs/shared-brain.md` (user guide), `docs/shared-brain-admin.md` (admin operations), `docs/shared-brain-compliance.md` (GDPR / IP / residency reference per Decision 6e, drafted earlier in Phase 4). |
| `v2.7.1`  | **Auto-update restart hotfix.** Resolves a long-standing race condition in `/api/restart` where the replacement Node process could try to bind port 3333 before the outgoing process had released it, crash on `EADDRINUSE`, and leave the user with zero servers running — visible as a stuck "v2.x.y · restart" amber badge that never resolved after clicking Update. Two coordinated fixes: (1) `/api/restart` in `src/server.js` now uses Node-native `child_process.spawn` with `detached: true` + `child.unref()` instead of `exec("nohup ... &")`. This is the canonical pattern for spawning a process that survives the parent's exit, and it removes the shell-and-nohup chain that could break if the parent died mid-detach. (2) The server's initial `app.listen()` call is wrapped in `startListen(retriesLeft)` that handles `EADDRINUSE` by retrying every 100ms for up to 60 attempts (6 seconds total tolerance). So even if a new child wakes up while the old process is still holding the port, it waits patiently instead of crashing. Any other listen error (permission denied, address unavailable) still bails immediately. Manual recovery from a stuck pre-v2.7.1 install: identify the orphaned PID with `lsof -nP -iTCP:3333 -sTCP:LISTEN`, `kill <pid>`, then relaunch from the Dock. After v2.7.1 lands and the server has been restarted ONCE manually, subsequent updates will restart cleanly via the bulletproof endpoint. |
| `v2.7.0`  | **Shared Brain — Phase 2 infrastructure (NOT user-facing).** Lands all five Phase 2 milestones (2A storage interface, 2B push + delta + Jaccard, 2C pull + readonly marker, 2D security boundaries + symlink defense, 2E synthesis pipeline with Rules 1-5) as backend infrastructure. **Zero user-visible change** — the feature is gated behind `sharedBrainEnabled: false` in `.curator-config.json`, no routes are registered, no UI section exists yet. Existing users updating from v2.6.0 will receive the new files but their app behaves identically. New modules in `src/brain/`: `sharedbrain-storage.js` (abstract adapter interface), `sharedbrain-local-adapter.js` (`LocalFolderStorageAdapter` for cohort simulation; per-domain wiki/ sandboxing blocks traversal into sibling collective dirs), `sharedbrain-config.js` (`.sharedbrain-config.json` connection schema with 8-char-prefix token masking; `getSharedBrains` vs `getSharedBrainWithToken` split keeps tokens out of UI), `sharedbrain-storage-factory.js` (storage_type dispatch; rejects `github` / `cloudflare-r2` with "coming in Phase 3" / "Phase 3.1" messages), `sharedbrain-delta.js` (local-LLM DeltaSummary generation with strict cross-domain link filtering at write time per Decision 2; never throws on LLM failure — returns `{ ok: false, fallback }` per Decision 3; exports `jaccardSimilarity` + `tokenize` for synthesis Rule 3), `sharedbrain.js` (`pushDomain` enforces personal-domain isolation per spec Part 10 invariant 4; partial-push with pending_retry tracking and permanent_skip after 3 failures per Decision 3; `pullCollective` uses existing `writePage` pipeline so all v2.5.5+ link grounding + Pass A/B/C normalisation applies; symlink defense via `lstat` before write; `ensureSharedDomainExists` writes `readonly: true` YAML frontmatter per Decision 7), `sharedbrain-synthesis.js` (`runLocalSynthesis` applies merge Rules 1-5: union facts + Jaccard heuristic flags contradictions, targeted LLM resolves them only when needed, Provenance section auto-appended with UUIDs by default per Decision 6a, `index.md` rebuilt every cycle, last-synthesis state advances). Plus `isDomainReadonly` helper in `src/brain/files.js` (regex-based YAML frontmatter parser, no external dep, conservative defaults to false on any uncertainty) — wired up by Phase 4 MCP write tools to refuse direct writes to Shared Brain mirror domains. Five battle-test scripts at `scripts/test-sharedbrain-*.js` with 335 total assertions: 70 Phase 2A (storage), 83 Phase 2B (push + delta + Jaccard), 56 Phase 2C (pull + readonly marker + idempotency), 37 Phase 2D (isDomainReadonly + symlink victim file unchanged + token-leak audit verifying NONE of github_pat/fellow_token/admin_token leak through stderr), 89 Phase 2E (full round-trip: 3 fellows push → synthesis → 3 fellows pull → all converge with conflict marker on contradictory contributions). Spec lives in `FEATURE-shared-brain.md` (Google Drive); design decisions binding on Phase 2 in [docs/shared-brain.md](docs/shared-brain.md). Phase 3 (GitHub adapter), Phase 4 (routes + UI + MCP guard wiring), Phase 5 (battle test sweep + user docs) remain. |
| Phase 1 ✅ | **Shared Brain — Open Questions resolved.** All six architecture-blocking OQs (PAT security, cross-domain links, LLM failure handling, conflict resolution, domain siloing, GDPR) closed with binding decisions in [docs/shared-brain.md](docs/shared-brain.md). Original concept spec lives in `FEATURE-shared-brain.md` (Google Drive, not in repo). Phase 2 implementation (storage adapters + delta module + UI) is the next coding session, on a dedicated `feature/shared-brain` long-lived branch — NOT main. Feature flag (`sharedBrain.enabled`) + new `releaseChannel` field (`stable` / `beta`) are part of the rollout plan as the rollback fuse. |
| `v2.6.0`  | **Sync tab UX redesign — Phase 0 of Shared Brain rollout.** The previous Sync tab presented three coequal buttons (Sync Up / Sync Down / Sync) which created decision paralysis — users couldn't tell when to use which. The information architecture also assumed personal-sync was the only thing the tab would ever host. v2.6.0 collapses the three buttons into one primary **Sync now** pill plus a collapsed `<details>` disclosure containing the two one-way operations (now labelled **Push only** / **Pull only** to match what they actually do), removes the workflow-tip box (the tip only existed to disambiguate three coequal buttons; redundant now), and introduces a **Personal Sync (Backup)** section header above the status card. The header is preparatory — when v3.0.0 lands Shared Brain, the second block ("Shared Brains") slots in below the personal block without further restructuring. **Zero backend changes:** `src/brain/sync.js`, `src/routes/sync.js`, and the unconfigured-state setup wizard are byte-identical to v2.5.8. All three POST endpoints (`/api/sync/push` `/api/sync/pull` `/api/sync/sync`) remain live and reachable from the redesigned UI. The three click handlers in `src/public/app.js` keep their existing logic; only status-message strings updated for the new labels ("Pushing your local changes..." instead of "Syncing up..."). CSS: `.sync-action-cards` / `.sync-full-row` / `.sync-tip` classes removed; new `.sync-primary-row` / `.sync-advanced` / `.sync-advanced-buttons` / `.sync-advanced-notes` classes added. Risk surface is purely visual. |
| `v2.5.8`  | **Skill upload hotfix — strip XML-shaped placeholder from description.** First real install attempt on Claude Desktop surfaced a validator error: *"SKILL.md description cannot contain XML tags"*. The v2.5.7 frontmatter description included the trigger phrase `"put this in my <name> domain"` — Claude Desktop's skill-upload validator treated `<name>` as an XML tag and refused the file. (Claude Code's docs don't mention this constraint; Claude Desktop's upload UI is stricter.) Fix: replace `<name>` with the concrete example `projects`, and change `(entities/concepts/summaries)` separator from slashes to commas to be safe. Body left untouched — the validator only checks the description field, and the body's angle-bracket placeholders (`domains/<name>/wiki/`, `summaries/<title>-<hash>.md`) sit inside paragraphs and code blocks where they're not interpreted as XML. Users who hit the v2.5.7 error should re-download `claude-skills/my-curator/SKILL.md` and retry the upload. |
| `v2.5.7`  | **My Curator Claude skill — packaged playbook for the MCP.** Real-world MCP usage proved that detailed prompt-engineering produces dramatically better results: ground wikilinks before composing, use `broken_link_policy: 'refuse'` on fresh domains, three-tier Health flow, etc. Asking users to type that into every conversation is unsustainable. v2.5.7 packages the playbook into a downloadable Claude skill — `claude-skills/my-curator/SKILL.md` (~270 lines, 9 sections covering domain awareness, reading workflow, writing workflow with the v2.5.5 grounding playbook, Health maintenance with three-tier model, full tool reference, quality rules) plus `claude-skills/my-curator/examples.md` (4 worked sample dialogues — deep-research, fresh-domain compile, compound-into-existing, maintenance cleanup). Frontmatter `description` (within the 1,536-char cap) front-loads natural-language triggers covering both READ and WRITE intents. `allowed-tools: mcp__my-curator__*` pre-approves all 17 MCP tools. Install paths: `~/.claude/skills/my-curator/` for Claude Code (skills are first-class); upload to "Project knowledge" in any Claude Desktop project for the same effect. Documented in `docs/mcp-user-guide.md` (new section), `docs/user-guide.md` §13 (pointer), root `README.md` (callout). Format follows the [Agent Skills](https://agentskills.io) open standard so it works across MCP-aware tools, not just Claude. |
